# Post-Phase-8 Forward Work Register

> **Status: Claude-authored register for operator planning.** Comprehensive enumeration of the integrations, implementations, and operator-led actions that remain **after** Phase-8 substitution-closure — each grounded in the design-substrate, with current production state and concrete close-out steps. **Posture: mode-agnostic** (process-substrate; reads `design-substrate/` + `harness-*/src/` + `.harness/`; authors only this `.harness/` file). Sibling to `.harness/R-700-phase-8-closure-accounting-draft.md` (the substitution accounting this register builds forward from). Compiled at HEAD `01cd0fa`, 2026-06-01.

---

## 0. Thesis — what "post-Phase-8" means for this harness

**Phase 8 closed the *substitution accounting*, not the *capability activation*.** Per the roadmap §1 + X-AL-2 (Meta-Architecture §7.7), a substitution RETIRES when (A) its cited units land **and** (B) the H_E scaffold surface is no longer invoked at the substitution site. **X-AL-2 never required the underlying capability to be exercised in production** — only that the hand-built H_T substrate displaced the Claude-Code scaffold. The ratified Phase-8 accounting was **46/54 RETIRED (85.2%) + 49/54 pipeline-advanced (90.7%)**. Batch-52 later back-flowed the live R-810/R-820 evidence for AS-8e, AS-8f, and CP-17; batch-53 then back-flowed OD-4 and CXA-4; batch-54 lands the CP→AS runtime composer; batch-55 records CXA-2 as a counted bounded residual; batch-56 retires CXA-1 after the AS→IS producer and edge-scope audit close. The current live ledger is **54/54 RETIRED (100.0%) + 54/54 pipeline-advanced (100.0%)**.

What remains is a **different axis of work**: *activation, deployment, scale, and external integration* of capabilities that are, in many cases, **library-complete / structurally present but not yet exercised at runtime** (multi-LLM routing, real sandbox isolation, non-LOCAL deployment surfaces, multi-tenant redaction, MANAGED_CLOUD secrets, external MCP/Files/managed_agents integrations). This is **not "fixing incomplete retirements"** — it is building the production frontier the MVP deliberately stopped short of.

The register has **two tiers**, kept distinct:

- **Tier A — Phase-8 closure residuals.** These have now been dispositioned: the R-700 review declared Phase 8 closed, bounded-residual sign-offs were ratified, the invisible-row `R-NNN` coverage was authored, and the config-discovery fork was ratified. Historical detail remains below for provenance.
- **Tier B — Post-Phase-8 forward activation.** The genuinely-forward surfaces (Multi-LLM IV / Multi-deployment V / Multi-tenant VI / External-integrations IX / Research X / Operator-tooling XI) + the CXA composition seams.

**This register also discharges a roadmap obligation.** Roadmap §1 marks Surfaces IV / VI / VIII–X as `decomposition-owed` (§9). This document **is** that decomposition for IV / VI / IX / X — its items can seed `R-300` / `R-500` / `R-800` / `R-900` entries, which also closes the **no-`R-NNN`-entry gap** R-700 surfaced for `CXA-1/2/3/4` + `CP-17`.

**Standing directive (operator 2026-07-14) — ground every row before build-or-block.** `Project_Roadmap_v1.md` §5.0b + `[[full-register-ground-every-arc-before-blocking]]` extends the R-FS-1 "nothing deferred" discipline to this register: `registered_finding` / `operator_gated` / `design_substrate_gated`, and the empty `B-16` speculative bucket, are not stop conditions. Every row gets an active grounding pass against the design-substrate before it is built or left open with a label — a label describes current state, not permission to skip investigation.

**Council note (answering the standing question).** Compiling *this register* is descriptive synthesis grounded in substrate — **not** a design decision with a nameable cross-domain tension, so per CLAUDE.md §13.2 + §10.9 it routes to solo + `advisor()`, not the council. But several **forward arcs below carry nameable two-voice tensions that ARE council-eligible the moment the operator opens them to execute** — each is flagged `⚖️ council-eligible` with the named voices. (The roadmap already encodes this on R-410/411/412 via `council_required: conditional:nameable-tension`.)

# TIER A — Phase-8 closure residuals

## A-1 · R-700 Phase-8 substitution review + final-integer ratification
- **What it is.** The operator-owned Phase-8 review (`R-700-phase-8-substitution-accounting`, BLOCKED) that consumes the R-700 draft and declares all 54 substitutions RETIRED or RETIRED-AS-BOUNDED-RESIDUAL with rationale (Surface VIII closure criterion, roadmap §1).
- **Current state.** CLOSED. The operator lifted the hold and Phase 8 was declared closed on 2026-06-02 at 46/54 RETIRED and 49/54 pipeline-advanced. Batch-52, batch-53, batch-54, batch-55, and batch-56 are forward live-ledger supersessions, not rewrites of that declaration: R-810/R-820 evidence moves AS-8e, AS-8f, and CP-17 to RETIRED; the OD-4/CXA-4 accounting back-flow moves two more rows to RETIRED; the CP→AS runtime composer moves CXA-3 to RETIRED; the CP→IS seam moves CXA-2 to counted bounded residual; the AS→IS seam moves CXA-1 to SUBSTANTIVE_RETIRED. The live ledger now reports 54/54 RETIRED and 54/54 pipeline-advanced.
- **Close-out steps.** None. **Council: no** — this was a ratification (advisor + AskUserQuestion), not a multi-voice design tension.

## A-2 · Bounded-residual sign-offs (AS-8e, AS-8f, OD-6)
- **What it is.** Three substitutions closed as deferred-by-design / dormant-at-MVP that need an explicit operator Phase-8 sign-off per X-AL-2 §5.3: **AS-8e** (`files.*` namespace, Files-arc Memory-only MVP scope, runtime spec v1.17 §14.C); **AS-8f** (`managed_agents.*`, production-only exclusion, `class_1_fork_as_8f_...` Q1=C); **OD-6** (local-first OTLP sqlite ingestion — `flush_to_sqlite` dormant at MVP, FIRST bounded-residual close in the ledger, batch-51).
- **Close-out steps.** None for Phase-8 accounting. The operator ratified the terminal bounded-residual dispositions. Later implementation proofs for Files and managed_agents are tracked by R-810/R-820; batch-52 is the separate accounting/back-flow action that moves AS-8e, AS-8f, and CP-17 in the live ledger. **Council: no** (sign-off).

## A-3 · `R-NNN` coverage for the 5 invisible open rows
- **What it is.** R-700 surfaced that `CXA-1/2/3/4` + `CP-17` have **no roadmap `R-NNN` entries** — the R-002 Surface-I decomposition surveyed only the per-axis `§4.1`, which excludes the CXA axis (no `§4.1` file) and didn't frame CP-17's batch-44 SB-INDEF reclassification as open.
- **Close-out steps.** None. CP-17 is tracked by R-010/R-810, and CXA-1/2/3/4 are tracked by R-CXA-1..4. **Council: no.**

# TIER B — Post-Phase-8 forward activation

## Surface IV — Multi-LLM maturity (R-300..R-399, decomposition-owed)

> **The commitment (ADR-F1 v1.2 + `Target_Stack_Commitment_v1` §5.1–5.2).** Per-provider official SDKs (`anthropic` + `openai` + `ollama`) under a **capability-aware abstraction** — NOT LiteLLM — with a layered cheapest-deterministic-first routing strategy (declarative manifest → embedding classifier → LLM-as-router) and feature-preservation (Anthropic prompt-caching / extended-thinking / batch / MCP host must remain reachable, no LCD flattening). Spec surface at CP spec `C-CP-01..C-CP-04`. These correspond to substitution rows `H_T-CP-1..CP-5` (all RETIRED-as-substitution — legitimately; the library displaced the scaffold).

### B-1 · Layered capability-aware routing activation `⚖️ council-eligible`
- **Current state (empirically verified this session).** Provider construction is **live** — all 3 adapters (`AnthropicAdapter` / `OpenAIAdapter` / `OllamaAdapter`) built at bootstrap stage-3a (`providers.py`). The retry/breaker/**fallback-chain dispatch** (`C-RT-16`, `lifecycle/retry_breaker_fallback.py`) **is wired** at the stage-5 `StepDispatcher` and iterates fallback candidates (primary → same-family → cross-family → terminal) with `advance_or_raise` on per-candidate exhaustion. **But the layered routing-*selection* path is a stub:** `routing_core_surface.infer()` raises `NotImplementedError` (`routing_core_surface.py:83`/`:97`), and `layered_routing_strategy.route()` has **zero non-test callers** (verified). So *which provider to use* is taken statically from the manifest `model_binding`, not from capability-aware layered routing.
- **What this means for the harness.** Provider plumbing + failure-time fallback exist; **capability-aware provider *selection* is not active.** Combined with B-2, the multi-provider machinery is present but **unexercised at MVP**.
- **Close-out steps.** (1) Implement the `infer()` composition seam to invoke `layered_routing_strategy.route()` (declarative-layer first) before provider dispatch (CP spec C-CP-02); (2) bind per-layer time-budgets (C-CP-03 `LayerBudget`); (3) implement capability-shortfall fallback (C-CP-04 + ADR-F1 §Consequences(c)) — fall to a capable provider *before* the step fails when the primary lacks a required capability (e.g. extended-thinking).
- **`⚖️ Council (C5 ⊥ C9 ⊥ capability-preservation).`** Designing the routing-selection policy is a genuine tension: **cost** (cheapest-deterministic-first) vs **reliability/recovery** (when to fall back / breaker thresholds) vs **capability-preservation** (don't route a thinking-required step to a non-thinking model to save cost). Convene a dyad (cost-voice + reliability-voice) when this arc opens.

- **Closure.** PR #281 added the deterministic production-path cross-family fallback fixture and the live Anthropic invalid-model → OpenAI `gpt-4o-mini` exercise (`just mvp-r300-cross-family`, live PASS 4.55s). PR #283 added the free local Ollama fallback exercise (invalid model → `llama3.2:3b`, live PASS 4.17s through `api.run`). Deterministic CI coverage remains for non-credentialed runs; the live provider halves are recorded in `.harness/roadmap_status.md`. **Council: no**.

## Surface V — Multi-deployment surfaces (R-400..R-499)

> Per ADR-D2 (per-deployment-surface sandbox provider) + ADR-F4 (4-tier blast-radius) + ADR-F5 (tier-aware secrets) + `C-AS-15 §15` + `C-RT-29 §14.18` (daemon). The **12-cell `deployment_matrix.py`** maps `(DeploymentSurface, BlastRadiusTier) → (SandboxTier, SandboxProviderClass)`: LOCAL/SELF_HOSTED use process/container/microVM/full-VM provider classes with **LOCAL keyring** secrets; **MANAGED_CLOUD** reserves FULL_VM at TIER_4 and **defers** its secrets backend to prod-tech. `multi-tenant-compliance × local-development` is a **closed cell** (raises `CellBindingViolation`). **R-410 now makes the first tier executable via a local Docker `ToolExecutionDriver`; higher tiers and deployment surfaces remain the honest frontier.**

### B-3 · Real TIER_2_CONTAINER sandbox execution (R-410) `⚖️ council-eligible`
- **What it is.** Make a tool call resolved to `TIER_2_CONTAINER` actually execute inside a container boundary (verifiable FS/network isolation), not in-process. **The honest heart of Surface V.**
- **Current state.** RESOLVED by this PR: `RuntimeToolDispatcher` now delegates execution through a `ToolExecutionDriver`, the default driver preserves prior MCP-host behavior, and `DockerToolRunnerExecutionDriver` runs TIER_2 calls inside a local-only Docker container by resolved immutable image id.
- **Close-out evidence.** The live e2e drives a TOOL_STEP resolved to `TIER_2_CONTAINER` through Docker, asserts outbound network is blocked, and asserts the host worktree path is not visible. Existing dispatcher tests cover under-tier rejection and sandbox.enter/exit provider/tech attribution. No provider credentials or paid calls required.
- **`⚖️ Council (C10 ⊥ C11) — roadmap already flags `conditional:nameable-tension`.`** Action-safety/blast-radius (C10) wants real isolation; operator-loop/local-deployment (C11) wants minimal provisioning burden. This slice resolves the tension narrowly with a local-only Docker driver that preserves the default in-process path unless explicitly injected.

### B-4 · TIER_3 microVM + TIER_4 full-VM execution (R-411 / R-412) `⚖️ council-eligible`
- **What it is.** Extend executable isolation up the ladder: TIER_3 (gVisor/Kata, EXTERNAL_REVERSIBLE) then TIER_4 (firecracker/full-VM, EXTERNAL_IRREVERSIBLE, **MANAGED_CLOUD-only** per the matrix).
- **Current state.** R-411 is closed for the selected no-taxonomy-change path. `GVisorRunscToolRunnerExecutionDriver` extends the R-410 Docker runner substrate, requires `SandboxTier.TIER_3_MICROVM`, and invokes Docker with `--runtime=runsc`. The approved live e2e targets the operator-provisioned Lima Linux VM through `R411_GVISOR_DOCKER_COMMAND`; host readiness passed, the TOOL_STEP executed under `runsc`, network egress was blocked, and the host repo path was not visible. R-412 is closed by the operator-approved managed E2B path: `E2BManagedFullVMToolRunnerExecutionDriver` requires `SandboxTier.TIER_4_FULL_VM`, creates an E2B managed sandbox with outbound internet disabled by default, and exchanges the same JSON runner shape as the local Docker/gVisor drivers. The approved live e2e (`just r412-e2b-full-vm-live-e2e`) passed on 2026-06-08 with provider `e2b-managed` and tech `e2b-firecracker`.
- **Close-out steps.** None for R-411/R-412. Local Firecracker and QEMU `microvm` remain valid future self-managed variants, but the roadmap Tier-4 managed-cloud lane is closed by E2B. **`⚖️ Council (C10 ⊥ C11)`** inherited from B-3.

### B-5 · SELF_HOSTED_SERVER + MANAGED_CLOUD deployment e2e (R-420 / R-421)
- **What it is.** First real non-LOCAL surfaces. **R-420 (SELF_HOSTED_SERVER):** harness daemon (`C-RT-29 §14.18`, FastMCP Unix-socket) against a **real OTLP collector** + tier-level secrets; tail-keep wrapping active (non-LOCAL); per-cell sampler `base_rate` = the SELF_HOSTED cell. **R-421 (MANAGED_CLOUD):** cloud env + cloud secrets + FULL_VM + managed collector; in-sandbox encrypted-fs secrets per ADR-F5; MANAGED_CLOUD per-cell sampler + redaction posture (`C-OD-13 §13.1`).
- **Current state.** R-420 is **RESOLVED** by the local single-node SELF_HOSTED_SERVER stack at `deploy/self-hosted-local/`: host-run harness daemon + Docker Compose OTel Collector Contrib, Tempo, and Grafana; `harness.selfhosted.local.example.toml` selects `SELF_HOSTED_BACKEND_COLLECTOR` and `self-hosted-keyring`; `just r420-self-hosted-stack-*` controls the backend. Closure evidence 2026-06-07: Docker stack running, `just r420-self-hosted-readiness harness.selfhosted.local.toml` passed, and `just r420-self-hosted-live-e2e harness.selfhosted.local.toml` passed with workflow `r420-self-hosted-tool-echo`, daemon status `success`, cost `0`, hosted-provider-calls `0`. The live workflow uses local Ollama plus non-secret keyring sentinel `r420_probe_key`; no hosted-provider inference is performed. R-421 is **RESOLVED** by the approved E2B + GCP Secret Manager + authenticated Cloud Run collector live e2e: `just r421-managed-cloud-readiness /private/tmp/r421-managed-cloud.live.toml --hosted-sandbox-provider e2b` passed, the hosted E2B sandbox ran the deterministic command, authenticated OTLP export reached Cloud Run, and Cloud Trace observed trace `d848a4da6622f42407a5e58c507513c5` with spans `r421.managed_cloud.root` and `sandbox.violation`.
- **Close-out steps.** None for R-420/R-421. R-420 **unblocks R-430** and removes the self-hosted prerequisite for R-421; R-421 supplied the managed-cloud prerequisite that R-412 consumed for the selected E2B full-VM provider path. **`⚖️ Council (R-421 only)`** — closed by the selected E2B + GCP path.

### B-6 · OTLP tail-keep preservation validation (R-430)
- **What it is.** Verify the `§10.2` classification-trigger preservation semantic against a **real** OTLP collector (the drop/keep decision is collector-side).
- **Status.** CLOSED by this PR. `TailKeepSpanProcessor` buffer logic remains in-process + bypassed at LOCAL by design (§9.1 head-based mandate), but the preservation semantic is now exercised against the R-420 local real-collector substrate.
- **Closure evidence.** `just r430-tail-keep-live-e2e harness.selfhosted.local.toml` passed against the local OTel Collector/Tempo stack: trigger trace `4972258a693b5d34c32c89ecd30749bc` exposed `r430.trigger.root` + `sandbox.violation` in Tempo, plain trace `364f9516e5f95cae58f4b44219981626` stayed absent through the negative window, and the command reported `trigger-trace-preserved=true`, `non-trigger-trace-exported=false`, `cost=0`, `hosted-provider-calls=0`.
- **Residual.** None for R-430. Managed-cloud collector posture remains R-421 scope; multi-tenant/non-SOLO telemetry posture remains R-500 scope.

### B-7 · Tier-level self-hosted secrets backend selector (R-440)
- **Status.** CLOSED by this PR. `ProviderSecretsConfig` now carries a `ProviderSecretBackend` selector. The default LOCAL path remains keyring + env fallback; `self-hosted-keyring` resolves through keyring only and disables ambient env fallback.
- **Closure evidence.** Focused tests prove `self-hosted-keyring` raises `SECRET_UNKNOWN` when only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set, resolves when the keyring entry exists, preserves LOCAL env fallback, and makes `just self-hosted-readiness` pass its static R-440 gate from TOML config.
- **Residual.** Live server/collector/keyring-entry exercise remains R-420. MANAGED_CLOUD bootstrap-token / in-sandbox HTTP fetch remains R-421 managed-cloud scope.

## Surface VI — Multi-tenant (R-500..R-599, decomposition-owed)

> Per `C-OD-13 §13.1` (redaction toggleability gradient) + `C-OD-10 §10.3` (per-`(persona_tier, deployment_surface)` sampler base-rate, 8-row table) + ADR-D5/D6. Three persona tiers (SOLO_DEVELOPER / TEAM_BINDING / MULTI_TENANT_COMPLIANCE) × three surfaces.

### B-8 · Non-default `tenant_id` / non-SOLO `persona_tier` deployment `⚖️ council-eligible`
- **Closure.** R-500 closes the self-hosted multi-tenant exercise on the R-420 local collector stack. `RuntimeConfig.tenant_id` now materializes as authoritative OTel `tenant.id` resource attr; `just r500-multitenant-live-e2e harness.selfhosted.local.toml` overlays two non-default tenants with `multi-tenant-compliance`, verifies `base_rate=0.2`, proves non-toggleable redaction strips default-off content before Tempo while preserving structure attributes, and exercises tenant-scoped audit-ledger reads. Closure evidence 2026-06-07: tenant A trace `5c0e5916bf84933296323f2038c6680b`, tenant B trace `a847370bbc76adc41b85e3328d3279aa`, `tenant-resource-separated=true`, `content-redacted=true`, `audit-ledger-separated=true`, `cost=0`, `hosted-provider-calls=0`.

### B-18 · U-1 — `cache_control` breakpoint emission *(NET-NEW, registered 2026-07-09)* — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-10 (stale-open correction, found 2026-07-14 at forward-register-schema grounding, out-of-family Codex)**
- **What it is.** The executable half of the **ADR-D3 §1.5 "prompt-cache breakpoint placement contract"** (per-cell breakpoint placement at parent-agent + per-sub-agent levels, composing D4 §1.8 concurrent-cache warm-up). The static-prefix/dynamic-suffix discipline + the observation attrs are built; the harness never AUTHORS a breakpoint — the runtime dispatch composer emits a **string-only** system prompt, so it cannot place `cache_control` markers.
- ~~**Current state.** COMMITTED at ADR-D3 §1.5; deferred only as runtime spec §14.5.2:3620-3621 OQ-1 (string-only) + OQ-2 (perf follow-on); **in no ledger/register** (confirmed by acknowledgment-grep). Load-bearing C2 cost lever (cache-miss = 4–10× input tokens per ADR-F2 §Rationale(b)(ii)).~~ **Superseded — see the ✅ BUILT bullet below; OQ-1 is un-deferred and the breakpoints are emitted.**
- ~~**Close-out steps.** Un-defer runtime spec OQ-1 (structured/multi-block `system`-array content) + emit `cache_control` breakpoints per ADR-D3 §1.5 placement contract at the dispatch composer; tests (cache-prefix stability, breakpoint placement, ≤4-breakpoint Anthropic limit); clearance marker. Bundled design+impl. Free acceptance witness: the observation half already exists (`llm_dispatch.py:331-358` reads `anthropic.cache_breakpoint_id`) → emit→observe round-trip.~~ **Done — see the ✅ BUILT bullet below.**
- **Probe findings (2026-07-09, advisor-guided scoping):** the §1.5 parent breakpoint sits at end-of-[system_prompt + frozen_tool_superset + template]. Two constraints gate a *sound, non-vacuous* build: (1) the stable prefix must be ≥4096 tok (Anthropic min cacheable prefix); (2) `tools[]` must be a static **frozen superset per epoch** (§1.5 line 203). **At MVP neither holds:** `active_system_prompt: str \| None = None` by default (`llm_dispatch.py:533` — no prefix to cache), and tools flow **per-step** (`payload.tools` from `step_payload.tools`, applied at `_payload_to_anthropic_kwargs:1670`), NOT frozen-per-epoch. So a naive breakpoint is **built-but-vacuous**. **Operator decision (2026-07-09): FROZEN-TOOLS PREREQUISITE FIRST** (build the §1.5-line-203 static-frozen-superset-per-epoch tool model → then the full §1.5 parent breakpoint is sound in one arc), rejecting the narrower system-prompt-only-conditional slice.
- **Refined disposition:** the frozen-tools prerequisite is a **substantial architectural change to the tool-dispatch model** (per-step tools → frozen-superset-per-(workload-class × prompt-version)-epoch), NOT a quick prerequisite — council-eligible (C4 tools ⊥ C2 caching ⊥ C6 routing/epoch). **Council: ⚖️ conditional** (frozen-tools-superset architecture + epoch model).
- **✅ BUILT 2026-07-10 — this entry's own "ACTIVE, scoped" framing above went stale the moment the arc it names was actually built, and was never corrected.** All 5 slices landed: PR #919 (frozen-tool-superset `cache_control` breakpoint, ADR-D3 §1.5), PR #920 (full §1.5 `[tools+system]` structured-content breakpoint, un-defers runtime spec OQ-1), PR #921 (child-scoped downgraded `frozen_tool_superset`, closes the F1 latent C10 condition-2 gap), PR #922 (workload-class-aware Anthropic prompt-cache TTL), PR #923 (`cache_ttl_seconds`/`breakpoint_id` tools/system-breakpoint observability). Runtime spec v1.93→v1.98 tracks these slices; see the 2026-07-10 change-note lineage relocated from root `CLAUDE.md` §2.3 to `.harness/claude-artifact-pointers.md` (B-17 / R-ICM-1, 2026-07-30). The related `B-18-3C-PREWARM` follow-on family (concurrent-cache warm-up prewarm/keepalive, PRs #927–#933) is a separate, already-complete arc tracked at `.harness/u1-3c-prewarm-design-decision-record.md` — not a residual of this entry.

### B-19 · U-2 — breaker `breaker.cause` + `breaker.cooldown_ms` attributes *(NET-NEW, registered 2026-07-09; operator-discretionary)* — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-13**
- **What it is.** The two CP-side ambient breaker-state attributes (`breaker.cause` trip-cause enum + `breaker.cooldown_ms`) dropped when the CP `breaker.*` set was replaced by the OD 7-attribute *event* schema.
- **✅ BUILT 2026-07-13 (R-FS-2 Wave 4, `B-19-BREAKER-AMBIENT-ATTRS`).** Operator ratified BUILD against the grounded skip-and-close recommendation (redundancy analysis found zero real consumers). Landed as `breaker.tripped` **event** attributes (not ambient state) at OD spec v1.32 (C-OD-07 §7.1, 7→9 attrs) + `harness-od`/`harness-cp`/`harness-runtime` code + tests, bundled-absorption. `cooldown_ms` is real (`cooldown_seconds * 1000`, populated at every trip). `cause` is a typed 4-value enum slot, **vacuous today by honest design** — no runtime call site can non-speculatively populate any of the 4 values (capability-shortfall pre-empts before the breaker is consulted; the fail-fast exception types don't map to `auth_failure`; the transient bucket is undifferentiated; the runtime's only real auth-vs-transient discriminator is a bootstrap-ping-only classifier). Full record at `.harness/b19-breaker-ambient-attrs-redundancy-analysis.md`; clearance marker `Spec_Operational_Discipline-v1_32-cleared-2026-07-13.md`.
- **Follow-on registered (NOT built).** A fine-grained provider-exception classifier (distinguishing `rate_limit` / `auth_failure` / `5xx_streak` at a real per-step dispatch failure, not just the current bootstrap-ping-only auth split) would make `harness.breaker.cause` non-vacuous. Building it now would be speculative infrastructure ahead of any consumer — open this only if a real ambient/cause-reading consumer is ever built. **Council: no** (mechanical extension of `_classify_provider_exception` once opened).

### B-20 · CP-side `harness.breaker.*` composition-mirror attribute-name drift *(NET-NEW, registered 2026-07-13 at Wave-5 hygiene grounding)* — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-14**
- **What it was.** `harness_cp.retry_fallback_namespace.HARNESS_BREAKER_NAMESPACE_SCHEMA`'s 7 pre-v1.32 entries (`harness.breaker.id` / `state` / `scope` / `trip_count` / `trip_window_seconds` / `fail_count_in_window` / `fail_threshold`) carried names + a concept-set that did NOT match OD's canonical §7.1 emission names (`scope` / `from_state` / `to_state` / `trigger_count` / `permanent_fail_repeats` / `tool_id` / `model_version`) — only `scope` overlapped directly; the rest were a different concept set entirely (a single `state` field vs OD's separate `from_state`/`to_state`; no OD equivalent for `id`/`trip_window_seconds`/`fail_count_in_window`/`fail_threshold`). Flagged Class-3 (not fixed) at B-19-BREAKER-AMBIENT-ATTRS grounding (2026-07-13) — this module's `verify_harness_breaker_namespace_inversion()` cardinality check only compares COUNT, not names, so the drift was invisible to CI.
- **✅ BUILT 2026-07-14.** Grounded via a background research agent (verified the OD canonical §7.1 schema + the CP module + the runtime cardinality-only check + confirmed the drift was self-contained to this module + its own test, no other file referencing the 7 old names). Renamed/split/dropped/added the 7 entries to byte-match OD's canonical 9-attribute set: `scope` kept (retyped `ENUM_REF`); `state` split into `from_state`/`to_state`; `trip_count` renamed `trigger_count`; `id`/`trip_window_seconds`/`fail_count_in_window`/`fail_threshold` dropped (no OD equivalent); `tool_id`/`permanent_fail_repeats`/`model_version` newly added (OD attributes CP had no entry for at all). The 2 already-correct v1.32 entries (`cause`/`cooldown_ms`) unchanged. Updated `test_retry_fallback_namespace.py`'s `_SPEC_HARNESS_BREAKER` expected set + the module's stale docstring/comment. 18/18 targeted tests + `harness-runtime/tests/test_lifecycle_od_cp_wiring.py` (14/14, cardinality-only check unaffected as expected) pass; `just check` 5788 passed/10 skip/33 deselected/1 xfail; `just overlay-check` 365/33; `just codex-review` converged clean, no findings. PR #994.

### B-21 · PARALLELIZATION nested-child-pause capability gap *(preflight HIGH #6, `workflow_driver.py:7787`)* — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-13**
- **What it was.** `PeerFanOutResumeState` (PARALLELIZATION's resume carrier) had no `paused_child_branches`-equivalent field, unlike `FanOutResumeState` (ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS/HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION, v1.45) — a PARALLELIZATION peer branch whose own dispatch was a `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step recursing into a child that itself PAUSED had nowhere to record it (the runtime `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` dispatcher is topology-agnostic, so the same `SubAgentChildPausedError` v1.45 already handles for ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS was raised here too — but fell into the generic branch-failure handler, silently dropping the child's snapshot behind a bogus terminal `completed`).
- **✅ BUILT 2026-07-13.** Witnessed empirically before any fix (a failing test proved the drop). `PeerFanOutResumeState.paused_child_branches` (additive, reuses v1.45's `PausedChildBranchResumeState` carrier verbatim — no new type) mirrors v1.45's `_execute_orchestrator_workers` capture/resume shape peer-shaped in `_execute_parallelization`: `SubAgentChildPausedError` caught in both PROCEED and PAUSE/CASCADE_CANCEL paths (incl. the in-flight cancellation-race capture) + a resume material-diff guard (bounds/identity/no-overlap **plus a step-kind-changed check v1.45's own equivalent guard lacks** — closes a hole, no compat risk). Bundled-absorption: CP spec v1.96 → v1.97 + clearance `spec-control-plane-v1-97-cleared-2026-07-13.md`. Full disposition + the byte-compat-scoping correctness argument (out-of-family Codex round 2 [P1] — a naive uniform strip would have retroactively broken v1.45's already-shipped nested-snapshot hash compat; fixed via a per-carrier-kind-scoped `strip_paused_child_branches` parameter) recorded at `.harness/beyond-mvp-capability-boundary-ledger.md`'s `B-21` entry. `test_workflow_driver_parallelization_pause.py` grew from 42 to 46 tests. `just check` 5734 passed/10 skip/33 deselected/1 xfailed; `just overlay-check` 365/31; `just codex-review` 3 rounds (round 1: 2 real findings fixed; round 2: the byte-compat scoping fix + 1 registered follow-on; round 3, two independent invocations: one clean, one surfaced 2 more registered follow-ons — see `B-31`/`B-32` below, both verified symmetric with the pre-existing v1.45 mechanism by direct read, not merely asserted).

### B-22 · F5 real audit-signature backend seam *(preflight CRITICAL #1 follow-on)* — **✅ CLOSED 2026-07-14 (seam scope only — see `B-36` for the split-off concrete-backend follow-on)**
- **What it was.** `sign_audit_entry`/`verify_audit_entry_signature` were making a false security attestation (unkeyed SHA-256 treated as `VERIFIED` — anyone who can write a ledger entry could forge one). Operator chose **fail loud**: both now raise `AuditSigningBackendUnavailableError` instead of fabricating a result.
- **✅ CLOSED 2026-07-14.** Grounding first: `ADR-F5 §Deferred D-ADRs` + `ADR-D5 v1.3 §1.4`'s signing-key-residence table (row 3) confirm the concrete prod-tech backend (AWS Secrets Manager / HashiCorp Vault / Azure Key Vault / GCP Secret Manager / Doppler / 1Password Connect) is **already explicitly deferred to a future D-ADR** by the design substrate itself — it is not merely under-specified, and needs real cloud credentials + an operator/D-ADR decision, genuinely out of Phase-7-execution scope (X-AL-3). This module is MULTI_TENANT_COMPLIANCE-exclusive and the workspace's operative persona tier is `solo-developer` (bridging-arc default) — zero production callers, inactive tier. Surfaced to the operator as a genuine fork (build the seam now vs. hold B-22 fully) via `advisor()`-corrected AskUserQuestion; **operator chose build-the-seam-now**. Landed: `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_98.md` (NEW §20.2.1 additive `SigningBackend` Protocol + optional `backend` keyword param on both functions; absent → identical fail-loud raise preserved byte-for-byte for the zero existing callers; present → real SHA-256-then-sign / recompute-then-verify per §20.3.1) + clearance `spec-control-plane-v1-98-cleared-2026-07-14.md` + `harness-cp` impl + a TEST-ONLY in-memory Ed25519 `SigningBackend` double proving the round-trip (no cloud creds, no backend-selection claim) — mutation-probed (forcing unconditional `VERIFIED` was caught by 2 of 10 tests). **`just codex-review` (4 rounds) caught 8 real findings, all fixed**: round 1 — [P1] a valid signature could be relabeled onto different `audit_signature_key_id`/`algorithm`/`key_period` metadata and still verify (the signed message covered only the content hash, not its own metadata) — fixed by binding all four values into a length-prefixed canonical message (mirrors the B-23 segment-injectivity fix); [P2] `sign_audit_entry` accepted a backend declaring an algorithm outside the §20.2 closed 3-value enum — fixed with an explicit membership check; [P2] `sign_audit_entry` accepted a negative `key_period` — fixed with an explicit non-negative check. Round 2 — [P1] the verifier still returned `VERIFIED` when the caller's `key.key_id` or the backend's declared `algorithm` disagreed with the entry's own stored metadata (a backend ignoring its `key_id` selector could attest the wrong key) — fixed with explicit equality checks before ever consulting the backend; [P2] the round-1 `key_period` check ran before the backend-presence check, changing the absent-backend exception type for negative periods — a real regression against this delta's own "byte-for-byte preserved" claim — fixed by reordering. Round 3 — [P2] `key_period=True`/`1.0` passes Python's `int` type hint by subtyping/duck-typing but signs a different textual message than what Pydantic coerces the field to, self-invalidating the entry — fixed with an exact `type(key_period) is not int` check; [P2] the read path never validated a `signed` entry's OWN stored algorithm/period against §20.2 before consulting the backend, so an externally-constructed entry with out-of-contract metadata could verify if a rogue backend agreed with the same invalid value — fixed by repeating the write-path checks at read time. Round 4 — finding-count trend (3→2→2→4) drifted from seam-contract into concrete-signature-representation territory, the `advisor()`-confirmed convergence signal; [P1] `SigningBackend.sign`/`verify` took only `key_id`, making a rotation-aware backend structurally unexpressible (a single stable `key_id` cannot distinguish keys across a rotation boundary per §20.3.1) — fixed by widening both methods to also take `key_period`, proven with a `_RotationAwareBackend` test double keying off period; 1 finding empirically probed and confirmed a no-op (Pydantic `model_validate` coercion of `key_period=True`/`1.0` produces a canonically-identical valid record, not a distinct forgery); 2 remaining findings (ECDSA-P256 DER-vs-64-byte signature encoding; `CPSignedAuditLedgerEntry.audit_signature_value` not JSON-serializable for real signature bytes — pre-existing on the shared type) are concrete-backend-representation concerns per §20.4's own "deferred to implementation discretion" framing — split off to `B-34`, not fixed inline (no backend exists yet to hold to a wire format). All 8 landed fixes mutation-probed individually. `test_f5_signing_key_resolution.py` grew 6 → 23 tests. `just check` CP suite 1576 passed/1 skip/1 xfail; `just overlay-check` 365/33 (no new cross-axis seam — CP-internal only); pyright + ruff clean. PR #976.
- **Why CLOSED, not held open.** This entry's scope was always the **seam** (the composition-root injection point + fail-loud contract), which is now fully built, reviewed to convergence, and merged. The remaining work — picking and provisioning a real prod-tech signing backend — is a *categorically different* kind of task (D-ADR + real cloud credentials, genuinely not Claude-closeable) that was always going to outlive this entry regardless of how it's labeled. Per operator direction 2026-07-14, that follow-on is split into its own entry, `B-36`, so this entry can accurately reflect "the seam is done" without either (a) mislabeling undone credential-gated work as closed, or (b) leaving the done seam work perpetually flagged as incomplete. See `B-36` for the concrete-backend tracking.

### B-23 · Cost-attribution composers fabricate their audit entry's IS-anchor *(preflight §4 "most urgent", `cp_audit_conversion.py:161`)* — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-13**
- **What it was.** `harness_cxa.cp_audit_conversion._entry_core_or_default` synthesized an opaque `cp-audit:<action_id>` marker whenever its caller omitted `entry_core`. All **4** production cost-attribution composers — `attribute_tool_dispatch_cost`, `attribute_llm_dispatch_cost`, `attribute_webhook_dispatch_cost`, `attribute_validator_dispatch_cost` — called `cp_audit_to_od_audit(...)` with no `entry_core`, unlike `sub_agent_dispatch.py`'s 8a/8b/8c pattern.
- **✅ BUILT 2026-07-13.** A shared `compose_cost_f2_entry_core` helper (`harness_runtime.lifecycle.cost_attribution_f2_write`) F2-writes the dispatch fact and returns its `StateLedgerEntryRef` as the real `entry_core`; `ledger_writer` + the R-003 `procedural_tier_snapshot_resolver` threaded into all 4 composer signatures + their 4 bootstrap construction sites (`runtime_tool_dispatcher_factory.py`, `stage_5_loop_init.py`'s `materialize_llm_dispatcher_stage` call, `webhook_delivery_composer_factory.py`, `stage_4_od.py`'s `materialize_validator_framework_stage` call) — each is the SOLE production construction site for its composer (verified by grep), so the `ledger_writer=None` fallback (preserved for unit-test ergonomics, matching the existing `cost_record_sink`/`rate_table` optional-substrate convention) is unreachable in production. Retry/REVALIDATE collisions guarded by a per-attempt disambiguator: a real per-attempt OTel `span_id` for tool/LLM/webhook (fresh per retry); for validator, a composed `(burden_count, outcome, branch_index)` string — `burden_count` alone collides on a REVALIDATE→PASS pair (PASS doesn't increment it per CP spec §25.4 invariant 5), caught by out-of-family Codex review round 1; `branch_index` guards the sibling-fan-out-branches-share-one-`parent_action_id` collision class (`sub_agent_dispatch.py`'s `child_index` precedent), added proactively per Codex's same finding. Codex round 2 flagged the webhook factory's ledger wiring landing only on the dormant (no-cost-substrate) construction path — closed by adding an integration test (`test_u_od_40_validator_webhook_integration.py`) on the pre-existing real cost-substrate-bound composition proving both surfaces produce a real `entry_core`; Codex's further "hard-require `ledger_writer`" suggestion was declined (would contradict the deliberately-tested None-fallback and force every existing cost-substrate-bound unit test to build a real `LedgerWriter` for zero production gain — production correctness is already structural: sole construction site + both params always threaded together). Codex round 3 flagged 3 findings against the shared `LedgerWriter` being cross-tenant/cross-run: **fixed both real ones** — (a) `tenant_id` folded into the F2 identity (mirroring `RuntimeAuditLedgerWriter._tenant_tag`'s convention), since the validator composer's disambiguator is deterministic (`burden_count`-derived) and two tenants could plausibly share `(workflow_id, parent_action_id, dispatch_disambiguator)`; (b) `parent_idempotency_key` folded into the F2 identity for run-scoping — `WorkflowManifestEntry.workflow_id` is operator-authored and STABLE across every invocation of "that workflow" (CP's own `run_idempotency_key = sha256(run_id, workflow_id, entry_version)` composition treats `run_id` and `workflow_id` as distinct components), so re-running the same workflow definition with an identical deterministic validator outcome would otherwise collapse cross-run via `IDEMPOTENT_NOOP`; `parent_idempotency_key` is already run-scoped (CP composes step-level keys from the run's own idempotency key) and already a parameter on all 4 composers. **Declined 1 as pre-existing/dormant, not novel to this fix** (registered separately at `B-29`, both parts still applicable): shared-clock threading with `RuntimeAuditLedgerWriter.time_source` — no production site injects a non-default clock today (confirmed by grep) and no existing test combines a custom `time_source` with any cost-attribution composer, so the flagged `NonMonotonicTimestampError` failure mode has no live trigger; the `branch_metadata` sidecar finding from round 3's first pass is likewise pre-existing (the `sub_agent_dispatch.py` precedent this fix mirrors ALSO omits it). Codex round 4 found 2 more real bugs against the round-3 tenant fix itself: (a) the tool-dispatch call site (`_attribute_tool_cost_best_effort`) hardcoded `tenant_id=None` (pre-existing since before this arc) instead of the in-scope `step_context.tenant_id` — silently defeating BOTH the new F2 tenant fix AND the pre-existing `audit_writer.append(tenant_id, ...)` OD tenant scoping for tool dispatch specifically (LLM/webhook/validator were already correct); **fixed** by passing the real value. (b) the `tenant_id or "_single"` sentinel encoding aliased `tenant_id=None` with a genuine `tenant_id="_single"` (`RuntimeConfig` doesn't forbid that literal tenant name); **fixed** with a length-prefixed injective encoding (`f"tenant:{len}:{value}"` vs a distinct `"no-tenant"` sentinel that can't collide with any wrapped value). Codex round 5 found 2 more real bugs: (a) `attribute_webhook_dispatch_cost`'s `span_id` (unlike tool/LLM) is a *synthesized* `f"webhook-deliver-{idempotency_key}"` string, not a real per-call OTel span — Codex's own probe showed 2 `deliver_webhook()` calls sharing one `idempotency_key` (a legitimate caller-side outer-retry pattern; the composer fires its cost wrapper unconditionally on every call, no idempotent skip) produced 2 audit entries but only 1 F2 entry; **fixed** with a new `dispatch_disambiguator` parameter (falls back to `span_id` for existing callers), wired at `WebhookDeliveryComposer` with a fresh `uuid.uuid4()` per invocation. (b) the length-prefixed tenant encoding still used truthiness (`if tenant_id else`), aliasing the legal-but-falsy `tenant_id=""` with `tenant_id=None`; **fixed** with `is not None`. Full-chain-witness tests (F2 entry lands in the real IS ledger AND the audit entry's `entry_core` references it, not separate seam tests) added to all 4 composer test files + the validator+webhook integration test + cross-tenant + cross-run + tool-dispatcher-real-tenant-propagation + sentinel-aliasing + webhook-repeat-idempotency-key + empty-tenant regression tests. **advisor() (round 6, after round-5 fix committed) diagnosed rounds 1-5 as one recurring defect class, not five unrelated bugs**: every round closed ONE aliasing case on the raw colon-joined F2 identity (`f"cost:{tenant_tag}:{workflow_id}:{parent_action_id}:{parent_idempotency_key}:{disambiguator}"`) without fixing the join itself — `parent_action_id` is production-shaped as `f"workflow:{workflow_id}:step:{n}"` (contains `:`), so e.g. `(workflow_id="a", parent_action_id="b:c")` and `(workflow_id="a:b", parent_action_id="c")` concatenate identically; the round-4/5 tenant fix only made the tenant segment injective, leaving the same class open on every other segment. **Fixed at the root**: `compose_cost_f2_entry_core` now length-prefixes every segment (`_length_prefixed`, `f"{len(s)}:{s}"`) including a dedicated tenant presence flag (`"1"`/`"0"`) ahead of the tenant value, replacing the bespoke `tenant_tag`/`_NO_TENANT_TAG` sentinel — each segment is self-delimiting, so the full join has exactly one parse and two distinct segment tuples always produce distinct strings (closes the whole class in one place, not per-case). New dedicated `tests/test_lifecycle_cost_attribution_f2_write.py` (4 tests) exercises the helper directly: colon-shifted `(workflow_id, parent_action_id)`, colon-shifted `(parent_idempotency_key, dispatch_disambiguator)`, `tenant_id=None` vs `tenant_id=""`, and a colon-containing tenant value shifting across the `tenant:workflow` boundary — all previously-passing composer/integration tests still pass unchanged (the encoding change is internal to the opaque `action_id` string; no caller inspects its structure). `just check` 5727 passed/10 skip/1 xfail (+4 vs round 5); `just overlay-check` 365/31.

advisor() also flagged a second-order question genuinely out of B-23's scope: the 4 composers now use 3 *different* disambiguator replay strategies (validator's is deterministic — dedupes a re-fired cost event on resume; tool/LLM/webhook's are per-attempt-random — always create a fresh F2 entry on resume) with no decided policy on whether a step re-firing on resume should be the SAME billable event or a NEW one. Registered as `B-30` rather than answered inline — it's a CP replay/fencing-semantics design decision, not a B-23 mechanical fix.

### B-24 · `secret_negative_observation.py:127` wrong `NegativeObservationSurface` label — needs a new enum member *(design-substrate)* — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-15**
- **What it was.** `verify_sole_resolution_path` hardcoded the wrong `NegativeObservationSurface` label; no existing enum value mapped to "workflow manifest" arrival. Zero production callers (latent).
- **Ratification.** Fork doc (`class_1_fork_secret_negative_observation_missing_enum_member.md`) grounded 2026-07-15. Filer's recommended readings (Q1=A `WORKFLOW_MANIFEST_ENTRY`, Q2=a bundled dispatch fix, Q3=a fork-doc-as-audit-trail) ratified as-is — mechanical, reversible, zero production callers, convention-following (member name mirrors the CP `WorkflowManifestEntry` carrier per the existing per-member naming pattern).
- **Process correction (out-of-family `just codex-review` round 2, same PR).** The first close-out pass claimed "no design-substrate edit owed," reasoning `Spec_Action_Surface_v1.md` never declares this enum — true, but incomplete: `Implementation_Plan_Action_Surface_v1.md` §1073-1078 (the PLAN, a distinct design-substrate artifact) DOES declare `NegativeObservationSurface`'s canonical 4-member signature at U-AS-21, preserved verbatim through v1.2/v1.3/v1.4. Extending it to 5 members without a plan delta was a silent design-extension under X-AL-3 even though the spec itself was silent. Corrected same-PR: `Implementation_Plan_Action_Surface_v1_5.md` + clearance marker `.harness/clearance/implementation-plan-action-surface-v1-5-cleared-2026-07-15.md`.
- **✅ BUILT 2026-07-15.** Added `NegativeObservationSurface.WORKFLOW_MANIFEST_ENTRY`; replaced the hardcoded `surface=STATIC_PROMPT_CACHE_PREFIX` with a real `_ARRIVAL_SITE_SURFACES` dispatch dict, defaulting unrecognized sites to `WORKFLOW_MANIFEST_ENTRY` (the one arrival path with no dedicated detector of its own). **Out-of-family `just codex-review` round 1** caught a real gap: the initial dict only keyed the longer-form aliases (`prompt_cache_prefix`, `log_records`, ...), not §5.3's own short-form vocabulary the docstring itself advertises ("manifest, prompt, log, ledger") — calling with `"prompt"` silently fell through to the `WORKFLOW_MANIFEST_ENTRY` default instead of `STATIC_PROMPT_CACHE_PREFIX`. Fixed by adding the 4 short-form keys alongside the existing long-form ones. Test updated to assert `.surface` (previously untested) + coverage for both the long-form and short-form dispatch branches. 9/9 tests pass. **Council: no.**

### B-25 · `sandbox_tier_floor.py:54` — `is_deterministic_inhouse=False` branch has no defined floor *(design-substrate)* — **✅ RESOLVED 2026-07-15 (Reading A via council + operator decision)**
- **What it was.** `is_deterministic_inhouse` is threaded through the types but never read; both the AS spec's §2.3 row 7 and its own upstream authority, `ADR-D2 v1.2` §1.5.1, self-contradicted — the `where:`-block's row-7 condition tuple named the field as a match input, while the adjacent "Row→argument keying" paragraph claimed row 7 keys purely on `blast_radius_tier` with no carve-out. Full grounding history (the ADR-level self-contradiction discovery) preserved at the fork doc's §1.5 addendum.
- **Resolution process, 2026-07-15 follow-on session.** Per the fork's own §2 Q3 recommendation, a dyadic council convening (C10 action-safety/blast-radius + C4 tool-contract-semantics) ran before any operator decision. Three empirical probes preceded deliberation; the decisive one — `is_deterministic_inhouse` carries **zero verification mechanism today** (a plain self-declared boolean; no code-signing, static check, or attestation) — meant a floor bump keyed on `False` would be trivially spoofable by exactly the actor it targets (a careless/dishonest author just sets the flag `True`), while its only real cost falls on honest authors who simply never opted into a newer field. Both voices converged on **Reading A** (do not branch on the field; state its non-gating status explicitly), a reversal of the filer's original Reading-B lean under the new evidence. The council synthesized 4 options for the operator; **the operator selected Option 1 — "Reading A + reserved annotation"** via `AskUserQuestion`.
- **✅ RESOLVED 2026-07-15.** `ADR-D2.md` v1.2 → v1.3 (`where:`-block row 7 corrected to the 2-element `(read-only, *)` form matching rows 8-10; explicit reserved-non-gating sentence added) + `Spec_Action_Surface_v1.md` v1.13 → v1.14 (same row-7 correction + keying-paragraph note + stale `ToolContract`/`ToolMetadata`-discriminators docstring prose corrected) — bundled-absorption arc, both carrying clearance markers (`.harness/clearance/adr-d2-v1-3-cleared-2026-07-15.md`, `.harness/clearance/spec-action-surface-v1-14-cleared-2026-07-15.md`). Stale docstrings also fixed at `harness-as/src/harness_as/tool_contract.py` (`ToolContract.is_deterministic_inhouse`), `harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/types.py` (`MCPClientConfig.default_is_deterministic_inhouse`), and `harness-as/src/harness_as/sandbox_tier_floor.py` (`ToolMetadata` class docstring). **No code behavior change** — production already implemented Reading A; this closes the documentation self-contradiction and stale-docstring drift. A new mutation-probed witness test (`test_sandbox_tier_floor_read_only_ignores_is_deterministic_inhouse`) pins `True`/`False` producing identical tiers for a read-only tool, documenting this as intentional. 18/18 `test_sandbox_tier_floor.py` tests pass; 341/341 `harness-as` suite passes. **Council: ✅ convened — see above.**
- **Process correction (out-of-family `just codex-review` round 1, same PR).** The initial ADR/spec fix left the canonical *plan* (`Implementation_Plan_Action_Surface_v1_2.md` §5.2 U-AS-06, preserved verbatim through v1.5) still describing `is_deterministic_inhouse` as a "§2.3 row-1/2/7 discriminator" at its Inputs line, Signatures comment, and AC #7 — the same category of gap caught one session earlier at the B-24 arc. Corrected same-PR: `Implementation_Plan_Action_Surface_v1_6.md` (prose-only, zero code/test/AC-count change) + clearance marker `.harness/clearance/implementation-plan-action-surface-v1-6-cleared-2026-07-15.md`.
- **Downstream note.** A separate exploratory memo (`.harness/architect_recommendation_tool_determinism_attestation.md`, reviewed by `advisor()` + out-of-family Codex) explored whether `is_deterministic_inhouse` should ever become a *verified* rather than self-declared claim — that memo's own analysis was explicitly conditional on this B-25 resolution, and its Candidate 0 (deprecate the field entirely) is the road not taken here: the operator chose to keep the field reserved-but-non-gating (Option 1) rather than remove it (Option 2). No further action owed unless the operator later wants to pursue a verification mechanism per that memo's Candidates A/C.

### B-26 · `five_axis_composition.py:320` — `verify_rotation_6_steps` hardcoded-`True` stub — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-13**
- **What it was.** Real verification needs IS hash-chain re-wiring; the function always returned `True`. Dormant — zero production callers.

Round 2 caught the CP→IS seam registration gap (fixed above) + a genesis-slice contract ambiguity (fixed via docstring contract note: caller must supply the genesis-anchored chain, not a filtered/tenant-scoped slice — `verify_chain`'s own contract, no code change needed, zero callers today). Round 3 caught the residual fake-success path: the no-args default (`audit_ledger_entries=()`) still reported `rotation_complete=True` from zero evidence — fixed (empty ledger now fails the hash-chain step: "zero entries is an absence of evidence, not proof of chain integrity"), mutation-probed. Round 4 caught a TOCTOU between the `list(audit_ledger_entries)` snapshot verified and a separate re-read for the emptiness check — fixed (single snapshot reused for both). Round 5 restated round-1(a) (rotation-boundary proof) with a concrete example — confirmed via `advisor()` as the SAME residual, not a new finding; registered as `B-33` rather than chased further (needs a rotation-correlation carrier on `StateLedgerEntry`, a separate IS-spec change, PLUS the `B-22` signing backend — neither exists; over-investing further on a dormant zero-caller function past the point where rounds stop producing new finding classes is the anti-pattern to avoid). New tests: `test_hash_chain_step_valid_multi_entry_ledger`, `test_hash_chain_step_broken_chain_fails_rotation` (mutation-probed), `test_empty_ledger_does_not_fake_success` (mutation-probed). `just check` 5739 passed/10 skip/33 deselect/1 xfail; `just overlay-check` 365/33; pyright 0/0/0. **Does NOT claim rotation-boundary verification is complete** — generic hash-chain continuity + fail-closed on empty/broken is what's real; see `B-33` for the residual.

### B-27 · `cli/app.py:347` — `paused` (HITL) workflow status shares `failed`'s exit code *(design-substrate)* — **✅ BUILT 2026-07-15**
- **What it was.** The runtime spec's §14.18.2 exit-code table never assigned `paused` a disposition (the `partial` status got an explicit invariant note when it was added; `paused` didn't), so the CLI mapped it to the same exit code as `failed`.
- **Ratification.** Fork doc (`class_1_fork_cli_exit_code_paused_status_undefined.md`) ratified 2026-07-15 per filer recommendation: Q1=A (new dedicated exit code `5`), Q2=ii (spec + CLI kept in sync, mirroring how `PARTIAL`'s addition was handled), Q3=b (documentation note only — no `resume` subcommand built). Reversible additive change (new exit code, no existing code touched); `RunResult.status`'s `Literal` enum unchanged.
- **✅ BUILT 2026-07-15.** Runtime spec `v1.99 → v1.100` — NEW §14.18.2 row (`5` / `PAUSED` / `RunResult.status == PAUSED`). `cli/app.py` gains `EXIT_PAUSED = 5`; both the daemon-mode `_CP_STATUS_TO_EXIT_CODE` dict and the one-shot `if/elif` chain now dispatch `paused` to it explicitly instead of falling through to the generic-failure branch. **Out-of-family `just codex-review` round 1** caught a real gap: the initial fix named the constant `EXIT_PAUSED_RESUMABLE` / row `PAUSED_RESUMABLE`, implying every `paused` run carries a `PauseSnapshot` resumable via `resume()` — but `workflow_driver.py:4224-4248`/`:4259-4282` (`WAL_SEGMENT`/`RECONCILER_LOOP` engine-native recovery-loop pauses) intentionally return `RunStatus.PAUSED` with `pause_snapshot=None` (durable state lives in the engine's own segment log / reconciler store, not the workflow-layer snapshot), so `api.resume(snapshot)` cannot resume them — a misleading name for automation that would attempt an impossible resume call. Fixed by renaming to the non-overclaiming `EXIT_PAUSED`/`PAUSED`. **Round 3 caught a follow-on, more serious problem (P1):** the round-1 fix's own replacement text asserted engine-native pauses ARE resumable via "a plain re-invocation of `harness run`" — false, and potentially unsafe. `mcp_server.py:357` generates a FRESH `run_id` (`uuid.uuid4().hex`) on every invocation lacking a snapshot, and the engine recovery loop's `capture_pause` records key on `(workflow_id, run_id)` (`workflow_driver.py:4234,4270`) — a fresh run_id cannot locate the prior pause record, so re-invocation silently starts an unrelated new run, risking re-execution of already-committed side effects rather than resuming anything. §14.18.2's row 5 is corrected to state plainly that engine-native (`WAL_SEGMENT`/`RECONCILER_LOOP`) pauses have NO safe resume path at v1.100 — a future `resume` subcommand must solve run_id recovery for the snapshotless case first; plain re-invocation MUST NOT be documented or implemented as a resume mechanism until then. New tests: `test_b27_workflow_paused_status_exits_five` (one-shot) + `("paused", EXIT_PAUSED)` parametrize case (daemon-mode mapping test) — unaffected by the prose correction (exit code behavior itself was always correct; only the spec's resumability claim was wrong). 28/28 tests pass; ruff clean. Clearance marker: `.harness/clearance/spec-harness-runtime-v1-100-cleared-2026-07-15.md`. **Council: no.**
