- **Downstream discovery + workspace hygiene (2026-07-14, triggered by independently re-verifying this entry's closure end-to-end — not just trusting the register's own claim).** Re-ran the actual test suite at `origin/main` HEAD (matched the claimed 5788 passed) and checked `main`'s post-merge CI, not just the merged PR's own pre-merge CI. Found `main`'s CI had been HARD-failing on every push for the entire day (20/20 recent runs, spanning the whole day's merges — confirmed unrelated to this arc's own content). Root cause: `tools/codex_context_guard.py`'s `_normalize_dashboard_snapshot` blanked the volatile `cadence` array (daily-commit sparkline) before its byte-comparison but missed the structurally identical `pr_cadence` array (PR-merge-cadence sparkline), whose "today" bucket counts any PR-merge-shaped commit at HEAD — so a refresh PR's own merge commit incremented it the instant it landed, HARD-failing `DASHBOARD_SNAPSHOT_STALE` on the very next push; invisible because the standing merge protocol only checks a PR's own pre-merge CI, never `main`'s post-merge state. Fixed in **PR #982** (mirrors the existing `cadence` normalization; new `test_dashboard_snapshot_normalization_ignores_pr_cadence_alone`, mutation-probed), refresh landed at **PR #983** — watched that exact commit's real post-merge CI run go `success`, breaking the day-long red streak. A broader sample across the full repo history (PR #1 → #983) found no other unaddressed defect — only (a) early advisory-gate failures from before `R-200-ci-lint-typecheck-blocking` flipped them blocking, and (b) the standard, always-expected `ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_DRIFT` transient every content-PR merge legitimately hits until its own follow-on refresh PR lands. Separately, the operator flagged 484 stale remote branches (visible in the GitHub branch list with historical CI-failure badges on 16 spot-checked PR numbers — all already `MERGED`, confirming those badges were frozen historical artifacts, not live problems). Cross-referenced all 484 against `gh pr list --state merged` head-refs (the squash-merge-safe discriminator — squash commits aren't ancestors of the source branch, so `--is-ancestor` alone would misclassify these as unmerged): 471 matched merged-PR head-refs and were deleted directly; the remaining 12 were individually inspected (diff-stat + `git log` vs `main`, closed-PR comment history, `git merge-base --is-ancestor`) before any deletion — 7 were pure ancestors of `main` with zero unique diff, 3 had explicit "superseded by PR #NN, unsafe to merge" closing comments (with clearance markers confirmed present in `main` for 2 of them), 1 (`r-xi-06-impeccable-polish`) was confirmed superseded by timeline reconstruction (a duplicate of the immediately-merged PR #232, itself fully replaced by PR #234's redesign the same day), and all 11 were deleted. The 12th (`add-claude-github-actions-*`, the standard Anthropic GitHub-App auto-generated `@claude`-mention-trigger workflow branch) was a genuine, still-undecided operator choice, not superseded work — surfaced via `AskUserQuestion`; **operator declined it for now** (can be re-added later via the GitHub App setup flow if wanted). `main` is now the only remote branch. No code changed by the cleanup itself (remote-ref deletions only); `main`'s HEAD (`d1cd5b3c`, PR #983) and its CI status were unaffected and reconfirmed `success` afterward.

### B-29 · Pre-existing F2-write gaps surfaced at B-23 (out-of-family Codex, not novel to B-23) *(registered 2026-07-13)*
- **What it is.** Two Codex round-3 findings against B-23's new `compose_cost_f2_entry_core` helper that, on inspection, describe a PRE-EXISTING gap shared by `sub_agent_dispatch.py`'s own established 8a/8b/8c F2-write precedent — not something B-23 introduced or made worse: (1) **`branch_metadata` sidecar never populated** at these two producer sites; (2) **no shared clock with `RuntimeAuditLedgerWriter.time_source`**.
- **Close-out, part (1) — `branch_metadata`.** CLOSED 2026-07-15. This row's own "council-eligible, conditional on a proposed reading" framing was stale by the time this row was groundtruthed: the carrier-ownership question was already resolved as a Class 1 fork on 2026-06-13 (`.harness/class_1_fork_b1_branch_causality_route_x_vs_y.md`, Route Y) — CP spec v1.32 §25.13 states verbatim "The CP driver is the **producer** (composes branch metadata at branch-spawn + cancel); the IS sidecar is the **persisted carrier**," and IS spec v1.8 §5.4 confirms `terminal_status` is "producer-supplied by the CP `WorkflowDriver`." `WorkflowDriver.append_branch_step_ledger_entry` / `append_branch_terminal_ledger_entry` (`harness-cp/src/harness_cp/workflow_driver.py:5844-5953`) already compose and populate `branch_metadata` at ~19 real production call sites — the carrier is live and spec-conformant, not globally dormant as originally framed. `sub_agent_dispatch.py`'s dispatch-fact entry and the 4 cost-attribution composers are NOT branch-spawn/terminal entries per §25.13's scope, so their `branch_metadata=None` default is correct behavior, not a gap. No council was needed — the spec already resolved it. Added a one-line clarifying comment at both producer sites (`sub_agent_dispatch.py:698-701`, `cost_attribution_f2_write.py:149-152`) citing CP spec v1.32 §25.13 / IS spec v1.8 §5.4 so a future reader doesn't re-flag the omission as a gap. No behavior change; ruff clean; 155 sub_agent_dispatch/cost_attribution tests still pass.
- **Close-out, part (2) — shared clock, split to `B-44`.** The `time_source` half genuinely has no live consumer (confirmed: `stage_4_od.py:62` always defaults to `datetime.now(UTC)`, and `stage_5_loop_init.py:596`'s dispatcher-side `time_source` is likewise always hardcoded at bootstrap) — split out as its own row, `B-44`, since it has a distinct, still-open disposition rather than remaining bundled under a now-closed finding.

### B-44 · No production bootstrap site injects a non-default `time_source` clock *(split from B-29, 2026-07-15)*
- **What it is.** `RuntimeAuditLedgerWriter` and `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher` both support test-clock injection via a `time_source` constructor field, but no production bootstrap site (`stage_4_od.py:62`, `stage_5_loop_init.py:596`) ever injects a non-default one — both always resolve to `lambda: datetime.now(UTC)`. The shared `compose_cost_f2_entry_core` helper also accepts a `time_source` parameter, but none of the 4 `attribute_*_dispatch_cost` composer functions (tool/llm/webhook/validator) expose one of their own — they call the helper without it, so it always defaults the same way. A flagged `NonMonotonicTimestampError` failure mode (from clock skew across concurrent writers) has no live trigger today because nothing ever supplies a different clock to compare against.
- **Current state.** Registered, not fixed — genuinely dormant, zero live production or test consumer exercises this. Mirrors the workspace's established B-19/B-42 discipline against building speculative infrastructure ahead of a real consumer.
- **Close-out steps — OPERATIVE (the originally-registered plan, RESTORED at out-of-family Codex round 3, 2026-07-27).** If a real deterministic-clock consumer ever surfaces (e.g. a multi-writer clock-skew test or a production need for injectable time), thread an optional `time_source` parameter through the 4 `attribute_*_dispatch_cost` composer signatures (tool/llm/webhook/validator, which currently have none) down to the already-`time_source`-accepting `compose_cost_f2_entry_core` helper, plus the bootstrap sites (`stage_4_od.py:185`, `stage_5_loop_init.py:649`, `migrate_audit_sidecar.py:151`). This is the **only** mechanism that satisfies the deterministic/injectable-clock use case, and it stays within IS spec C-IS-07 §7.6's preserved caller-supplied timestamp semantics for DIRECT surfaces — **no spec change needed**. **CONSIDERED AND REJECTED — `WRITER_OWNED_TIMESTAMP` sentinel adoption at the composer F2 write** (briefly recorded as the likely fix by the 2026-07-27 grounding pass; corrected the same day at out-of-family Codex round 3), on two spec-verified grounds: **(a)** IS spec C-IS-07 §7.6's "Surfaces that do NOT change" paragraph classifies *"runtime cost writes"* as DIRECT appends that keep *"**caller-supplied** timestamp semantics verbatim"*, and its "Registered residual" paragraph states that *"[e]xtending writer-owned authority to direct surfaces would change caller-supplied semantics for every existing direct producer and requires its own back-flow"* — an extension explicitly assigned to `B-57` (*"`B-57` covers the remaining direct writers"*), **not** to `B-44`; adopting the sentinel here at impl time would be a silent spec violation. **(b)** The sentinel cannot serve `B-44`'s own stated trigger at all — `state_ledger_write.py:316-320` resolves `WRITER_OWNED_TIMESTAMP` through a hardcoded `datetime.now(UTC)` inside the write lock, so a deterministic/injectable-clock consumer gets **no** determinism from it; only an injectable `time_source` can. The `sub_agent_dispatch.py:801` / `workflow_driver.py:2028-2030` cites describe only what B-48 did on the DRAIN/offloaded surfaces — they are not a precedent for the DIRECT composer F2 write. **Council: no** — mechanical once a consumer exists; not urgent.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-27 (grounding pass, re-verified at HEAD `25b44127`) — STILL DORMANT, and MORE dormant than registered.** (a) `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher.time_source` is now **vestigial**: still accepted as a required ctor kwarg (`sub_agent_dispatch.py:607`, docstring `:587-592` retaining it for API reasons) but **never called** — the sole remaining `self.time_source` occurrence in that module is inside a comment (`:794`), because the 8b F2 dispatch entry now stamps `timestamp=WRITER_OWNED_TIMESTAMP` (`:801`) per IS spec C-IS-07 §7.6 and lets the IS writer sample inside its own lock. (b) A **third production construction site** this row never enumerated: `harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/admin/migrate_audit_sidecar.py:139-152` builds a `RuntimeAuditLedgerWriter` with `time_source=lambda: datetime.now(UTC)` (`:151`) — the default clock, so the scope EXTENDS without the premise being falsified. (c) **Line cites drifted, refreshed here:** `stage_4_od.py:62` → the audit-writer materialization now sits at `:185` (`materialize_audit_writer_stage(...)`), which passes NO `time_source` at all and falls through to the `audit_writer.py:1639` default; `stage_5_loop_init.py:596` → `:649`. (d) All four `attribute_*_dispatch_cost` composers still expose **zero** `time_source` parameters (occurrence count 0 in each of the tool/llm/webhook/validator modules), and `compose_cost_f2_entry_core` still stamps `timestamp=time_source()` caller-side at `cost_attribution_f2_write.py:157`.
- **Close-out guidance REFRESHED, then CORRECTED BACK — the full arc (this bullet records WHEN the guidance changed; the operative instruction is the "Close-out steps — OPERATIVE" bullet above, and the committed posture is unchanged throughout).** The **2026-07-27 grounding pass** briefly flipped this row's guidance to make **`WRITER_OWNED_TIMESTAMP` sentinel adoption at the composer F2 write** the PRIMARY instruction and marked the originally-registered clock-threading plan "SUPERSEDED — do NOT follow," reasoning by analogy from `sub_agent_dispatch.py:801` / `workflow_driver.py:2028-2030`. **Out-of-family Codex round 3, the same day (2026-07-27)**, found that flip wrong on two spec-verified grounds, and it was **corrected back**: (a) C-IS-07 §7.6 preserves caller-supplied timestamp semantics for DIRECT surfaces — runtime cost writes are named as such — and its "Registered residual" paragraph requires a separate spec back-flow (`B-57`'s registered scope, not `B-44`'s) before writer-owned authority may extend to any direct surface, so following the flipped guidance at impl time would have been a silent spec violation; and (b) the sentinel resolves through a hardcoded `datetime.now(UTC)` (`state_ledger_write.py:316-320`) and therefore cannot serve a deterministic-clock consumer at all — the very trigger this row is registered against. **Clock-threading is the operative close-out again.** **Committed posture remains detect-then-refuse / register-don't-build** (B-19/B-42 discipline). **Council: no.**

### B-30 · Cost-attribution disambiguator replay-semantics inconsistency *(surfaced at B-23 round 6, advisor())*
- **What it is.** The 4 cost-attribution composers now use 3 different `dispatch_disambiguator` replay strategies: validator dispatch composes a *deterministic* `(burden_count, outcome, branch_index)` string, so a step that re-fires cost attribution on resume recomputes the SAME F2 key and `compose_cost_f2_entry_core`'s underlying `ledger_writer.append` call `IDEMPOTENT_NOOP`s at the F2 anchor only (see below — this does NOT by itself mean the billed cost event dedupes). Tool/LLM dispatch use a real per-attempt OTel `span_id`, and webhook dispatch uses a fresh `uuid.uuid4()` per call — both create a genuinely NEW F2 entry on any re-invocation, resumed or not.
- **Correction, 2026-07-15 — this is NOT an undecided policy question, but is NOT fully diagnosed either.** The original framing ("no decided policy," council-conditional) was stale. `ADR-D6_v1_2.md` §1.5 + OD spec C-OD-14 §14.5.1 already specify a complete, ratified replay-dedup algorithm (materialized at `harness_od.idempotency_join_dedup.dedupe_on_replay`) — **no production ingestion-layer caller of it exists anywhere in the codebase today** (confirmed by grep across all 5 axis packages). This is a missing consumer, not merely an unwired composer — **and (round 4 correction) it's not JUST a missing consumer either**: all 4 `attribute_*_dispatch_cost` composers hard-code `SpanCostRecord.engine_replay_disposition=_DEFAULT_REPLAY_DISPOSITION` where `_DEFAULT_REPLAY_DISPOSITION = ReplayDisposition.NO_REPLAY` (verified at `cost_attribution_llm_dispatch.py:88,239`, `cost_attribution_tool_dispatch.py:90,295`, `cost_attribution_webhook_dispatch.py:65,204`, `cost_attribution_validator_dispatch.py:86,243` — none expose a parameter to override it). So even once an `ingest_span`-shaped consumer exists, it could never see `CHECKPOINT_RESUME`/`RECONCILER_ITERATION`/`WAL_CONSUME` from any of the 4 composers today — a producer-side replay-metadata wiring gap, separate from and in addition to the missing ingestion-layer consumer.
- **Why this is still NOT buildable right now (personally verified, not just a grounding agent's recommendation).** ADR-D6 §1.5's algorithm is specified as `ingest_span(span)` — a **trace-ingestion-time** function operating on spans read from the observability pipeline, not something a per-dispatch composer calls inline while producing its own F2 write. Wiring `dedupe_on_replay` directly into the 4 `attribute_*_dispatch_cost` composers (as an initial grounding pass recommended) would be a category error: the correct call site is an ingestion-layer consumer that doesn't exist in production yet, not merely an unimplemented wiring step inside the composers. Separately, the prior `B-COST-REPLAY-DEDUP-WITNESS` closure record (`.harness/b-cost-replay-dedup-witness-closure-record.md`, CLOSED GREEN no-fix) already verified the IS-layer content-addressed ledger write independently provides the real replay-dedup guarantee for the `JOURNAL_RESUME`/`PURE_PATTERN_NO_ENGINE` engine class, and its own scope note explicitly pre-commits any adjacent replay-dedup finding to register-don't-build, not a broadening of that arc. Webhook's fresh-`uuid4`-per-attempt billing is confirmed deliberate — the actual rationale lives at `webhook_delivery_composer.py:363-371`'s inline comment ("this method fires unconditionally on every invocation — no idempotent skip at this layer. A fresh UUID per call guarantees each invocation gets its own F2 anchor"), an intentional out-of-family-Codex-round-5 fix, not an oversight to align away.
- **Out-of-family Codex flagged three further nuances in this correction itself (2026-07-15, three rounds, all addressed same-PR).** (1) The paragraph above must not be read as settling whether validator's F2-key collision actually dedupes the *billable cost event* — it only describes the F2 anchor identity write. Verified directly: `compose_cost_f2_entry_core` (`cost_attribution_f2_write.py:153-166`) discards `ledger_writer.append`'s return value entirely and always returns a `StateLedgerEntryRef`, whether the underlying append was `APPENDED` or `IDEMPOTENT_NOOP`. The caller (e.g. `attribute_validator_dispatch_cost`, `cost_attribution_validator_dispatch.py:274-292`) then unconditionally builds `audit_entry` from that `entry_core` and calls `audit_writer.append(tenant_id, audit_entry)` — a SEPARATE, content-hash-addressed ledger write whose own dedup depends on `AuditPayload` byte-identity, not on whether the F2 cost-write above it deduped. **However** (round 3 correction) — this audit-ledger layer IS already independently verified to dedupe for ONE specific combination: `test_journal_resume_completed_run_retry_does_not_double_emit_cost` (`test_b_cost_replay_dedup_witness.py`) drives the real `attribute_llm_dispatch_cost` twice with the same `run_id` under `JOURNAL_RESUME` and proves the audit-ledger entry count stays at 1 — so "unverified for any composer" was too strong; the gap is specifically the OTHER 3 composers (tool/webhook/validator) and the OTHER engine classes, not this one already-proven case. (2) A third mechanism exists: ALL 4 production cost-wrapper call sites (`llm_dispatch.py:3031-3035`, `runtime_tool_dispatcher.py:625-628`, `webhook_delivery_composer.py:373-376`, and validator's equivalent lines 400-405) unconditionally append the returned `SpanCostRecord` to a run-scoped accumulator (`cost_record_sink`), which `api.py:1123-1132`'s `_build_run_result` rolls into the user-visible `RunResult.cost_attribution` via `_rollup_cost_attribution`. **However** (round 3 correction) — each public `run()`/`resume()` invocation installs a FRESH `CostRecordAccumulator` (`api.py:900`, `COST_ACCUM_VAR.set(CostRecordAccumulator())`; also `resume()` at `api.py:901-949` does the same), so a crash-then-separate-resume-call (the actual replay scenario this finding is about) does NOT carry the prior invocation's records into the new one's `RunResult` — the accumulator is NOT a confirmed cross-invocation dedup failure. The unconditional-append behavior only risks double-counting for repeated dispatches WITHIN one invocation (e.g. an in-flight retry loop calling the composer twice inside a single `execute_workflow`/`run()` call) or from external aggregation of multiple separate `RunResult`s by a caller — neither of which is established as happening in production today. **Net effect of both corrections**: the audit-ledger layer is proven-dedupe for LLM+`JOURNAL_RESUME` specifically and unverified elsewhere; the accumulator is NOT a general dedup-failure layer, only a narrower within-invocation-retry or external-aggregation concern.
- **Close-out steps.** (1) Resolve where `ingest_span`'s production call site actually lives (or should be built) before touching any composer. (2) Verify whether double-billing is an ACTUAL exercised risk for the durable engine classes (`SAVE_POINT_CHECKPOINT`/`EVENT_SOURCED_REPLAY`/`RECONCILER_LOOP`/`WAL_SEGMENT`) whose `resume_at` skip logic may already prevent re-dispatch of completed steps, mirroring the `JOURNAL_RESUME` finding. (3) Extend the existing `JOURNAL_RESUME`+LLM audit-ledger witness pattern to the other 3 composers (tool/webhook/validator) and, separately, verify whether a within-invocation retry (not a cross-invocation resume) can actually double-append to the run-scoped accumulator in production. (4) Thread the real `engine_replay_disposition` into all 4 composers (currently hard-coded to `NO_REPLAY`) so a future ingestion consumer can actually discriminate replay dispositions — a producer-side prerequisite, not optional polish. Only after (1)-(4) decide whether composer-level wiring is even the right fix, and at which layer. **Council: no** — a specified policy already exists (ADR-D6 v1.2 §1.5); the open question is call-site + live-risk + per-composer/per-engine-class verification + producer-side metadata wiring, not a design fork. Mirrors the B-19/B-42 discipline against building speculative infrastructure ahead of a real, demonstrated consumer.
- **✅ Step (4) BUILT + steps (1)/(2) grounded 2026-07-25 (`roadmap-continue`, advisor()-checked before build).** Re-verified at HEAD before building: `engine_replay_disposition` still had zero readers anywhere outside its own write-sites + the (uncalled) `dedupe_on_replay`/`attach_idempotency_key_to_cost_record` functions (confirmed by grepping the field itself, not just `dedupe_on_replay()` callers — advisor()'s check 1). But `StepExecutionContext.run_engine_class` (sourced from `manifest_entry.engine_class`) is **non-vacuous** — it already drives production durable-auto-fence logic at `runtime_tool_dispatcher.py` + `managed_agents_dispatch.py` (advisor()'s check 2), so a hard-coded `NO_REPLAY` was a genuine persisted-audit-ledger correctness defect for any workflow declaring a durable engine class, not a no-op default. That satisfied advisor()'s build-vs-defer decision rule (no reader, but a non-vacuous persisted-audit correctness value → build). Threaded `run_engine_class` through all 3 **live, step-scoped** composers — `attribute_llm_dispatch_cost` (`llm_dispatch.py`'s `dispatch()`, `step_context` already in scope at the existing cost-attribution call site), `attribute_tool_dispatch_cost` (`runtime_tool_dispatcher.py`'s `_attribute_tool_cost_best_effort`, same pattern), `attribute_validator_dispatch_cost` (`cost_attribution_validator_dispatch.py`'s `on_post_evaluate`, same pattern) — each now resolves `engine_replay_disposition` via the existing closed-total `REPLAY_DISPOSITION_MAPPING[EngineClass]` (the same table `retry_breaker_fallback.py`/`retry_breaker_tool.py` already use at a different surface) instead of the hard-coded default; `None` (engine class not surfaced) still falls back to `NO_REPLAY`, byte-preserving prior behavior for any caller that doesn't thread it. 15 new parametrized test cases (`EngineClass` is a 5-member enum × 3 composers) prove the mapping round-trips; a dedicated default-preserved test confirms the `None` fallback. `pyright` (strict) 0 errors; `ruff` clean; full `llm_dispatch`/`runtime_tool_dispatcher`/cost-attribution suites green (211 tests).
- **Webhook (4th composer) deliberately NOT threaded — deferred to the FM-2 webhook-config arc, not an oversight.** `WebhookDeliveryComposer` is a bootstrap-**stage-5 process-singleton** (constructed once at `webhook_delivery_composer_factory.py`), not per-step like the other 3 — its `workflow_id`/`parent_action_id`/`parent_idempotency_key` are **ctor** params, not per-call, and the v1.26 empty-marker factory doesn't even bind `rate_table`/`cost_chain`/`audit_writer` (confirmed by reading the factory body: those 3 are simply absent from the `WebhookDeliveryComposer(...)` call), so `_attribute_webhook_cost_best_effort`'s guard (`if self._rate_table is None: return`) always fires in production today — webhook cost-attribution is **fully dormant at HEAD**, a pre-existing, already-documented gap (this same finding's own text: "Webhook's fresh-uuid4-per-attempt billing is confirmed deliberate"). Threading a per-run `engine_class` into a per-process singleton is a real interface change (a new per-call param on `deliver_webhook`/`deliver_webhook_for_brief`, sourced from `hitl_gate_composer.py`'s `step_context` at its `deliver_webhook_for_brief` call site) that only matters once FM-2 binds the cost substrates — building it now would be dead code exercised only by a synthetic unit test. Registered here rather than silently dropped.
- **Step (1) (`ingest_span` consumer) — re-confirmed absent; register-don't-build stands (B-19/B-42 discipline).** Grepped `ingest_span` across the full repo (incl. all worktrees) — the only near-match is `collector_daemon.py`'s unrelated `ingest_span_row` (ring-buffer intake for `trace_browser.py`'s admin CLI), itself independently documented as having "no production caller." No `ADR-D6 §1.5`-shaped `ingest_span(span)` ingestion-time consumer exists anywhere. Building the ingestion-layer consumer itself remains out of scope — no real driver exists yet (mirrors the B-19/B-42 precedent this finding already cites).
- **Step (2) (double-billing risk) — verified NOT a live bug.** Read `workflow_driver.py:4619-4620` directly: the `SINGLE_THREADED_LINEAR` step-dispatch loop "Begin[s] at `resume_at` to skip already-materialized steps from a prior crashed/drained run" — confirming the register's own suspicion. Steps `0..resume_at-1` (the durably-committed prefix, for `SAVE_POINT_CHECKPOINT`/`EVENT_SOURCED_REPLAY`/`RECONCILER_LOOP`/`WAL_SEGMENT` alike) are never re-dispatched on resume, so there is no live double-cost-attribution bug for completed steps — only the metadata-correctness gap step (4) closes. An in-flight (dispatched-but-not-committed) step at crash time DOES re-dispatch on resume, but that re-dispatch is the step's *sole* attribution event (the original in-flight attempt never completed/never got audited) — correctly informational as `CHECKPOINT_RESUME`/etc. post step-(4), not a double-bill.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-27 (roadmap-continue, `advisor()`-checked before disposition) — step (3) VERIFIED, confirmed deliberate, NOT a defect.** An `Explore` agent traced the call graph and confirmed the double-append IS reachable, for the tool composer only: `retry_breaker_tool.py:123-129`'s two retried exception classes (`ToolInvocationTimeoutError`, `MCPHostUnreachableError`) each hit `runtime_tool_dispatcher.py`'s failure-path `attribute_tool_dispatch_cost` call (~1177/~1203) before re-raising, and the eventual success-path call (~1276) appends again to the SAME run-scoped `cost_record_sink` — confirmed NOT deduped downstream by direct read of `api.py:988-990`'s `_rollup_cost_attribution` (an unconditional sum by axis, no dedup logic of any kind). NOT reachable for the LLM composer (`llm_dispatch.py:1785-1824`: attribution fires only after a successful provider response, immediately before return — no retryable exception exists past that point). NOT applicable for the validator composer (its multiple `on_post_evaluate` firings under REVALIDATE are legitimately-separate billable `evaluate()` calls by design, per CP spec §28.10.4 invariant 1 — a `dispatch_disambiguator` built specifically to keep them from idempotency-key-colliding, not an accident). **The agent's own framing ("LIVE bug") was a presence finding, not a correctness finding, and was NOT adopted** — `advisor()` caught this before any fix was drafted: the tool composer's failure-path attribution is symmetric across all 3 exception classes including `ToolInvocationProtocolError` (`runtime_tool_dispatcher.py:1186-1198`), which is NOT even retried by `retry_breaker_tool.py` and still gets attributed — the tell that the design axis is "every physical MCP attempt," not "every retried attempt." The inline comment at `runtime_tool_dispatcher.py:1174` cites `U-OD-39 AC #1` directly ("cost-attribution invoked on every dispatch, success + failure paths") and reasons explicitly about the failure-path's degraded byte-count — not an oversight. This matches this same row's already-confirmed-deliberate webhook fresh-`uuid4`-per-attempt precedent exactly (same shape, different composer); applying a different disposition to tool than to webhook absent a spec change would itself be the inconsistency `[[forward-register-consistent-dismissal-criterion]]` warns against. **No fix landed, none warranted** — suppressing failure-path attribution would contradict a cited AC and diverge tool from webhook unilaterally. **One genuinely new residual this pass surfaced:** `U-OD-39`'s own AC #1 ("every tool dispatch, success + failure") and AC #5 ("1 tool call → 1 cost-record") use different granularity vocabulary, and neither the OD spec nor plan disambiguates whether a `flat_per_invocation` rate should bill per PHYSICAL MCP attempt or per LOGICAL dispatch when a retry eventually succeeds — an OD-spec-owned semantic question, not Phase-7 impl discretion. Registered, not answered unilaterally.
- **Remaining open surface (not closed by this pass):** the AC #1/AC #5 granularity-vocabulary gap named above, and webhook's per-call threading (deferred to FM-2, see above) stay open. Not re-registered as a fresh `B-*` — tracked here as this finding's own residual.

### B-31 · Paused-child resume guard does not validate `child_workflow_id` identity *(surfaced at B-21, out-of-family Codex round 2)*
- **What it is.** The `paused_child_branches` resume material-diff guard (both `_execute_parallelization`'s NEW guard and `_execute_orchestrator_workers`'s pre-existing v1.45 guard) validates a re-supplied `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` branch's bounds, `step_id` identity, and (PARALLELIZATION only, B-21) step-kind — but not that its `step_payload` still targets the SAME `child_workflow_id` the snapshot was captured against. A same-`step_id`/same-kind edit that swaps the target child workflow would pass the guard; the production child runner (`sub_agent_dispatch.py`) would raise a workflow-ID mismatch internally, but the generic branch-failure catch records the branch terminal and re-pauses the parent with `paused_child_branches` empty — silently discarding the original child's suspended state rather than surfacing the swap.
- **Current state.** Confirmed real by direct read (`attempt_resume` validates `snapshot_hash` + the pause-context anchor material diff, never `workflow_id`). PRE-EXISTING on the v1.45 mechanism (ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS/HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION) — not introduced by or unique to B-21's PARALLELIZATION port. Not fixed at B-21; CP cannot cheaply check this today because `step_payload` is an opaque runtime-owned `Mapping[str, Any]` (axis-boundary discipline — CP does not officially know the `child_workflow_id` payload-key convention).
- **Close-out steps.** Either (a) add a typed `child_workflow_id: str` field to `PausedChildBranchResumeState` (shared carrier — fixes both PARALLELIZATION and ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS/HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION in one pass) validated at the CP resume guard, or (b) add a runtime-side identity check inside the `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` dispatcher before it threads `child_resume_snapshot`. **Council: no** (mechanical carrier-completeness extension of an already-cleared mechanism — no nameable cross-domain tension). Scope spans BOTH carriers — not PARALLELIZATION-specific.

### B-32 · Nested paused-child pause-reason propagation *(surfaced at B-21, out-of-family Codex round 3; scope narrowed after a same-session Codex review of the first attempt)*
- **What it is.** The parent's `_pause_reason` derivation (both PARALLELIZATION and ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS/HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION — verified symmetric by direct read, not merely asserted) examined only `effect_fence_paused_dispositions`, never a nested child's OWN pause reason (e.g. `HITL_PENDING`) — an operator surface keying off the top-level `pause_reason` to decide what resume action is owed would see the generic `EXPLICIT_OPERATOR` instead.
- **Current state — CLOSED (pause_reason half only).** A first attempt bundled this with per-child response ROUTING (originally coupled here) and used a naive "any paused child present → HITL_PENDING" label. Out-of-family Codex review of that attempt caught it mislabeling a nested child that paused for an ordinary `cascade_policy=pause` branch failure (itself `EXPLICIT_OPERATOR`, not a HITL gate) as `HITL_PENDING` — a real bug, confirmed by executing the fixture. Corrected to check `child_snapshot.pause_reason` directly: a nested `HITL_PENDING` pause relabels the parent; any other nested reason does not. `EFFECT_FENCE_AMBIGUOUS` keeps its existing top precedence. Mutation-probed at BOTH the PARALLELIZATION site (a contrasting-baseline test + a positive test, in `test_workflow_driver_parallelization_pause.py`) AND the ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS/HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION site (a contrasting-baseline assertion added to the existing cancellation-race test + a new positive test, in `test_workflow_driver_fanout_pause.py`) — all four confirmed load-bearing by reverting/inverting the fix and observing the corresponding test fail, then restoring. The merge-gate test-witness lens caught that the second site was originally UNWITNESSED (an earlier close_out draft over-claimed "both sites" before those tests existed) — closed as part of this same arc. impl-to-cleared-spec: the existing `HITL_PENDING` semantic ("HITL gate opened; workflow paused awaiting operator response") already covers a nested gate without a new carrier/exception/enum value; a PROSE-ONLY CP spec delta (v1.99→v1.100) was authored to correct v1.97's Change-note registered-forward-work paragraph's stale "coupled" framing (see below) — no contract amendment.
- **Response ROUTING is NOT closed here.** The original "coupled" framing (this item + response routing must land together, or the propagated reason is a false affordance) is now moot as framed: the CORRECTED reason-propagation is honest on its own (it tells the operator a HITL response is owed somewhere in this round; it doesn't over-promise routing). Per-child response delivery is tracked separately at **B-39**, split off after Codex found the first routing attempt had 3 real defects. A SECOND out-of-family Codex round re-raised the false-affordance concern against the corrected label itself; grounded via direct grep before shipping — `pause_reason` is read in exactly one non-telemetry place (this derivation) and the composer's actual resume path (`consume_and_clear`) never branches on `pause_reason` at all, so nothing auto-dispatches a response off the label. Shipped with an explicit code comment at both sites cross-referencing B-39 so the limitation is documented, not silently assumed away. The merge-gate spec-conformance lens then caught that v1.97's Change-note registered-forward-work paragraph still asserted (b)+(c) "travel together" — contradicted by shipping (c) alone without a matching delta (the exact `B-31`/v1.99 precedent this workspace already established for closing a gap registered in that same Change-note paragraph). Closed via `design-substrate/Spec_Control_Plane_v1_100.md` (prose-only correction; clearance at `.harness/clearance/spec-control-plane-v1-100-cleared-2026-07-15.md`).

### B-39 · Nested paused-child HITL-response ROUTING (per-child operator response delivery) *(split from B-32 after a same-session Codex review found the first routing attempt had 3 real defects; not shipped — CLOSED 2026-07-24, spec leg PR #1092 + impl leg Slice A PR #1094 + Slice B PR #1096/#1097)*
- **CLOSED 2026-07-24 — spec leg + both impl-leg slices landed.** Spec leg (PR #1092, CP+Runtime spec v1.106 + plan deltas, 7 same-day correction rounds). Impl leg Slice A (PR #1094): `ResumeContext.hitl_responses`/`hitl_response_for(child_run_id)` carrier, byte-exact to CP spec v1.106 §0, 34 tests, 3 codex rounds, merge-gate 2 rounds to all-approve. Impl leg Slice B (PR #1096 + terminating refresh PR #1097): `HITLDeliveryCell` per-branch one-shot delivery (replaces the retired ctx-level `ResumeContextHolder`), `compute_hitl_uniform_fallback_eligible_run_id` implementing CP spec v1.106 §1.2 properties 4/5, and `_collect_gate_owning_run_ids` correctly distinguishing B-32-relabeled containers from true gate-owning leaves — 7 `just codex-review` rounds to convergence (rounds 2-5 each a genuine, mutation-probe-verified fix), merge-gate 3-lens all-approve (test-witness surfaced + fixed 2 real witness gaps before merge). One recurring finding (an ancestor `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` gate re-firing on resume-forward, discarding a deeper-paused child's cursor) was grounded as reachable-but-pre-existing/orthogonal to this arc and registered separately as `B-72` rather than patched blind. CP suite 1753 passed/4 skipped, Runtime suite 3048 passed/40 skipped, ruff+pyright clean. The bullets below (What it is / Current state / Close-out steps) describe the FIRST, REVERTED attempt and are retained verbatim for provenance — read the CLOSED bullet above for the actual shipped design, not the bullets below.**

- **What it is.** Two or more concurrently-paused peer/nested children (PARALLELIZATION `asyncio.TaskGroup`, or nested ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS/HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION recursion) share ONE run-level `ctx.resume_context_holder`. Delivering each paused child its OWN correct operator HITL response — without one child silently starving another, and without a later unrelated resume accidentally reusing a stale response — is unresolved.
- **Current state.** A first attempt built `ResumeContext.hitl_responses: dict[str, HITLResult]` keyed by the paused child's `workflow_id` (mirroring the existing `effect_fence_resolutions` per-branch pattern), consumed via non-destructive `peek()` + keyed lookup at the runtime HITL gate composer's resume Step-0. It was mutation-probed (reverting broke the composer-level routing test) and passed the full workspace suite — but out-of-family Codex review caught 3 real defects before it shipped, all confirmed correct on direct inspection:
  1. **Never one-shot-consumes.** `peek()` is non-destructive by design (that's what avoids the original race), but nothing ever clears a KEYED entry once delivered — a LATER, unrelated HITL-composed step sharing the same `workflow_id` (e.g. the same child dispatched again, or coincidentally reused) would silently reuse the stale response instead of requiring a fresh operator gate.
  2. **`workflow_id` is not branch-unique.** Fan-out siblings can legitimately dispatch the SAME `child_workflow_id` — the runtime already uses `branch_path` (§25.16 idempotency-key composition, `compose_branch_path`) specifically to disambiguate this case. A map keyed only by `workflow_id` collapses two such paused siblings onto one entry, so both would receive the SAME operator decision.
  3. **The delivery mechanism itself is unresolved, not just the map shape.** `execute_workflow` / `child_workflow_runner` currently accept NO `resume_context` parameter at all (only `pause_snapshot_input`) — there is no existing plumbing that threads a resolved per-child value into a recursive child's OWN internal re-entry. `effect_fence_resolution`'s per-branch pattern (the thing this attempt mirrored) resolves PARENT-side and threads a single resolved value onto the CHILD's `StepExecutionContext` — but that field is consumed by TOOL-step dispatch code within the SAME step, not by a recursive child's OWN composer at a deeper recursion level. So the "mirror the effect-fence shape" premise this attempt started from does not actually carry through to the recursive-child case; the threading path needs its own design, not just a differently-keyed map.
  The attempt was fully reverted (not shipped) rather than landed with known defects.
- **Close-out steps.** Design constraints for the next attempt, per the 3 findings above: (a) key by something branch-unique — `branch_path` or an equivalent derived child-run identity, not `workflow_id` alone; (b) delivery must be genuinely one-shot per key without a destructive whole-holder clear racing concurrent siblings — `peek()` alone is insufficient once true one-shot semantics are required per key; (c) work out the actual threading mechanism first (how does a resolved per-child value reach that child's OWN recursive re-entry, given `execute_workflow` has no `resume_context` parameter today) — the map shape is the easy part. **Council:** not yet re-evaluated — the original C7/C8-HITL-fidelity-vs-C1/C5-orchestration-correctness framing may not even be the right lens once a concrete delivery mechanism is proposed (per this register's own `[[probe-resolves-fork-prescribed-council]]` discipline: ground the mechanism first, then decide if a real value-tension remains). Not urgent — open when a real multi-child concurrent-HITL-pause scenario is confirmed reachable in a live deployment (today's fixtures/tests do not exercise it in production; the B-32 fixtures that DO exercise nested pauses use plain branch-failure pauses, not HITL gates).

### B-33 · `verify_rotation_6_steps`'s hash-chain check cannot prove a rotation boundary occurred *(surfaced at B-26, out-of-family Codex rounds 1 + 5, `advisor()`-confirmed; CLOSED 2026-07-23)*
- **CLOSED 2026-07-23 — all three ratified-fork (Option A) legs landed.** (i) IS carrier: IS spec v1.11→v1.12 (NEW §5.6 D-derivative sidecar `rotation_correlation_id` on `StateLedgerEntry`/`EntryPayload`, C-IS-05; NEW §7.7 join-key read-side invariants, C-IS-07) + IS plan v2.8 (U-IS-20), code at PR #1083. (ii) CP+OD+Runtime spec+plan rider: CP spec v1.104→v1.105 (§20.3.1 row 7 amended + NEW §20.3.2), OD spec v1.34→v1.35 (NEW §24.8), Runtime spec v1.104→v1.105 (NEW §13.6), landed at PR #1084 (4 codex rounds, 24 findings fixed). (iii) the real CODE: OD `find_rotation_pair_evidence` + CP `RotationPairEvidenceProvider`/`KeyIdentityResolver` Protocols + `verify_rotation_6_steps` extension (`rotation_window_entries`/`evidence_provider`/`key_identity_resolver`, subset-membership authentication, dual `pair_present`+`signatures_verified` gate, required physical-key-distinctness attestation) + Runtime composition-root adapter, landed at PR #1087 (2 codex rounds to clean convergence + 3-lens merge-gate all-approve).
- **FORK FILED 2026-07-21, RATIFIED 2026-07-21 (option A)** — `.harness/class_1_fork_b33_rotation_correlation_carrier.md`.
- **What it was.** `VERIFY_HASH_CHAIN_LINK` (landed at `B-26`) verified **generic** `prior_event_hash` continuity via the real `harness_is.chain_verification.verify_chain` — it did not and could not confirm that a genuine §20.3.1 rotation boundary (a dual-signed sibling pair sharing a rotation-correlation id, under two consecutive key periods) was present among the supplied entries. Any intact non-rotation ledger (even a single ordinary `StateLedgerEntry`) passed the step, so `execute_key_rotation` could report `rotation_complete=True`/`ROW_2_RETIRE_OLD` without rotation-specific evidence. This is now closed: `verify_rotation_6_steps` cannot pass on absent, tampered, or structural-only-unverified evidence.
- **Two residuals REGISTERED as separate forward dependencies, NOT part of B-33's own ratified scope** (the fork's own §3 explicitly defers both): (a) `execute_key_rotation`'s real ledger-write path + a real `rotation_window_entries` producer — `verify_rotation_6_steps` still has ZERO production callers; (b) the OD §21.2.2 rotation-period-aware cryptographic signature verifier — `find_rotation_pair_evidence` always returns `signatures_verified=False` today, so `PROBE_VERIFY_AT_READ` cannot reach a genuine pass through any real caller until this lands. Neither blocks B-33's own closure; both are candidate new B-* filings for a future arc when a real rotation-execution production surface is prioritized. **Council: no** for any future such arc unless a genuine cross-domain tension surfaces at that time.

### B-34 · Concrete signature-representation gaps in the C-CP-20 §20.2.1 seam *(surfaced at B-22 round 4, out-of-family Codex, `advisor()`-confirmed non-blocking)*
- **What it is.** Out-of-family Codex round 4 on `B-22`'s `SigningBackend` seam found 2 genuine representation-layer gaps (a 3rd candidate finding was empirically probed and confirmed a no-op — see below) — deliberately NOT fixed inline, per `advisor()`'s discriminator: **seam-contract findings** (injection, absent-path-preserved, metadata-bound-into-the-signature) get fixed in the seam itself; **concrete-signature-representation findings** (encoding, serialization, strict parse-typing) are exactly what C-CP-20 §20.4 defers to "implementation discretion / the concrete backend" and get registered against the still-deferred backend arc (`B-36`), since inline-hardening a backend that doesn't exist yet is scope creep.
  1. **Algorithm-specific signature encoding not enforced.** A real `cryptography`-library ECDSA-P256 backend's `sign()` returns a DER-encoded ~70-72-byte signature, but C-CP-20 §20.4 requires a canonical 64-byte raw r‖s encoding for `ecdsa-p256`. Neither `sign_audit_entry` nor `verify_audit_entry_signature` validates the returned/stored signature's byte length against the algorithm's expected length.
  2. **`CPSignedAuditLedgerEntry.audit_signature_value: bytes` is not JSON-serializable for real (non-UTF-8) signature bytes.** Pydantic's default `bytes` field serialization assumes UTF-8-decodable content; a genuine Ed25519/ECDSA/RSA-PSS signature's raw bytes are not, so `model_dump_json()` raises `PydanticSerializationError` once a signed entry needs to cross a JSON/JSONL persistence boundary. **Pre-existing on the shared `CPSignedAuditLedgerEntry` type** (U-CP-14, `per_step_override_evaluator.py`) — `B-22` did not introduce this field; fixing it (e.g., a base64 field-serializer) touches every other consumer of that type, out of this seam's blast radius.
  3. **(Probed, confirmed NOT a bug — recorded for completeness.)** Codex flagged that Pydantic coerces `key_period=True`/`1.0` to canonical `int(1)` when a `CPSignedAuditLedgerEntry` is built via `model_validate`, potentially defeating the round-3 strict-typing check. Empirically probed: `model_validate` coercion produces a value byte-identical to the honestly-signed record (no distinct forgery — `str(1) == str(1)`), and a genuinely type-distinct relabel via `model_copy(update=...)` (which bypasses validation) is already caught by the round-1 message-binding fix (mismatched `str()` representation → `SIGNATURE_MISMATCH`, verified directly). No code change needed.
- **Current state.** Non-blocking — the module still has zero production callers, `multi-tenant-compliance` is not this workspace's active persona tier, and no real backend (ECDSA-P256 or otherwise) exists yet to trigger either gap. Round-4 finding-count trend (3→2→2→4, drifting from seam-contract into representation-layer territory) was itself the convergence signal per `advisor()` — round 1's `SigningBackend.sign`/`verify` widened to also pass `key_period` (closing round-4's genuinely in-scope P1 finding: a rotation-aware backend was structurally unexpressible without it, since a single stable `key_id` cannot distinguish keys across a rotation boundary) is the one round-4 fix that landed inline; these 2 remain registered.
- **Close-out.** CLOSED 2026-07-16 (PR #1032). Both gaps fixed per this block's own prescribed steps, now that B-36 (ADR-D8, PR #1031) supplies a real backend: (1) `_SIGNATURE_LENGTH_BY_ALGORITHM` (`{ed25519: 64, ecdsa-p256: 64 raw r‖s, rsa-pss-2048: 256}`) enforced on the write path (fail-loud `ValueError` immediately after `backend.sign`) and the read path (`SIGNATURE_MISMATCH` before ever consulting the backend) — the `ecdsa-p256` row forecloses the DER-vs-raw ambiguity by contract (fixed-width raw only; a future DER-emitting backend fails loud at sign time); (2) reversible base64 field-serializer/validator on `CPSignedAuditLedgerEntry.audit_signature_value` — consumer sweep first confirmed zero serialization consumers of the shared type outside the two edited harness-cp modules; JSON round-trip is lossless and a persisted-then-reloaded signed entry still cryptographically VERIFIES (e2e witness). 5 new witnesses, each PD-8 mutation-probed. Original close-out steps (preserved): address both when a real backend (of any concrete kind) is actually wired: (1) validate `len(signature)` against `{ed25519: 64, ecdsa-p256: 64, rsa-pss-2048: 256}` for the declared algorithm, on both the write path (immediately after `backend.sign`) and the read path (before calling `backend.verify`); (2) add a reversible byte-encoding (base64) field-serializer to `CPSignedAuditLedgerEntry.audit_signature_value` — check ALL existing consumers of that shared type first (not just this module) before changing its serialization behavior. **Council: no** (mechanical once a real backend exists to test against).

### B-35 · `tools/test_qa_evidence_matrix.py` / `test_closure_gate.py` are orphaned from CI + 12 contracts lack test-file citation *(surfaced during B-28 triage, 2026-07-14; closed same session as B-32)*
- **What it is.** Two independent gaps discovered while triaging `B-28` finding #17/#18: (1) `qa_evidence_matrix.py --check` failed against live data, reporting 12 contracts with no test-file citing their contract id anywhere in the repo (`C-IS-14`, `C-MEM-01`, `C-MEM-04/05/06/11/12/13/14/15/17/19`); (2) neither `tools/test_qa_evidence_matrix.py` nor `tools/test_closure_gate.py` was wired into any CI job, so gap 1 sat silently red with zero CI signal.
- **Current state — CLOSED.** Grounded each of the 12 individually before touching anything:
  - **`C-IS-14` — tooling false-positive.** `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md`'s v1.93 change-note confirms the IS spec defines only `C-IS-01..10` and explicitly names `C-IS-11/14/15` as confirmed-phantom ids from a prior spec version. `closure_gate.py` already had a local `DOCUMENTED_NON_CONTRACTS = {"C-IS-11"}` exclusion for exactly this reason but it only listed 1 of the 3 documented phantoms, and `qa_evidence_matrix.py` had no such exclusion at all. Moved the constant to a shared home (`tools/semantic_overlay/overlay.py::DOCUMENTED_NON_CONTRACTS = {"C-IS-11", "C-IS-14", "C-IS-15"}`, citing the same spec text) so both consumers read one source of truth instead of drifting independently.
  - **10 of 11 `C-MEM-*` contracts — citation-only.** Each already had a real, already-passing test exercising its behavior (several tests already embedded the contract id in their own function name in snake_case, e.g. `test_..._c_mem_17_...`, just never in the hyphenated `C-MEM-17` form the citation-scan regex needs). Added a one-line docstring citation to the nearest existing test for each: `C-MEM-04` (`test_memory_capture.py`), `C-MEM-05`/`C-MEM-06` (`test_memory_promotion_review.py`), `C-MEM-11`/`C-MEM-12` (`test_memory_retrieval.py`), `C-MEM-13` (`test_memory_access_mode.py`), `C-MEM-14` (`test_memory_tool_contracts.py`), `C-MEM-15` (`test_native_memory_adapter.py`), `C-MEM-17` (`test_memory_engine_durability.py`), `C-MEM-19` (`test_memory_capture.py`).
  - **`C-MEM-01` — genuine gap, now closed.** The memory-plane-boundary architectural contract (no dedicated source module by design — its own text: "atomic implementation sequencing is allowed; product completion requires the full contract family"). Added `harness-runtime/tests/test_memory_plane_boundary.py` with two new tests, both mutation-probed against real code: (1) native provider memory, standard tools, and prompt-packet fallback all write to the SAME physical canonical-store ledger — proven by instantiating each component independently at the same `MemoryRootBinding` and reading all three operation kinds back from a fifth, independently-constructed store (mutation-probe: pointing one component's store at a different root correctly drops its operation kind from the read-back set); (2) promotion and injection are independently-settable policy decisions — proven by promoting a record to `ACTIVE` while its `injection_policy` is simultaneously `NEVER` (mutation-probe: forcing production code to ignore the caller's `injection_policy` and always use a fixed value correctly fails the assertion). Also added a one-line `C-MEM-01` citation to `CanonicalMemoryStore`'s own docstring (the class that physically embodies the boundary invariant), which incidentally closed `closure_gate.py`'s separate G1.3 source-code-orphan check too (2 real → 0 real; all 5 automatable Tier-1 predicates now PASS, reconciling the tool with an already-RESOLVED R-CL-C1 certification, not a new governance decision).
  - **CI wiring.** New job `q3-evidence-and-closure-gate` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, mirroring the existing arc-ledger/substitution-ledger/semantic-overlay job pattern: runs both test modules' pytest suites + `qa_evidence_matrix.py --check` + `closure_gate.py --check`, all blocking. `test_qa_evidence_matrix.py`'s import was normalized from a `from tools import X` package-relative style (which only worked from repo root) to the same self-contained `sys.path.insert` convention every sibling `tools/test_*.py` file already uses, matching the `working-directory: tools` job shape.
  - The pinned fingerprint test (`test_q3_evidence_matrix_known_gaps_are_tracked_not_growing`) is simplified back to a direct `missing_ids == []` assertion now that the gap is empty — the elaborate SHA-256 machinery existed only to track a known-nonzero gap without letting it silently drift; with 0 missing, the honest assertion serves the same regression-catching purpose without carrying forward dead scaffolding.
- **Verification.** `just check` clean throughout (5796 passed, +2 for the new tests); `qa_evidence_matrix.py --check` → `q3 evidence OK contracts=141/141 cxa=33/33`; `closure_gate.py --check` → all automatable predicates PASS. **Council: no** (mechanical once each contract was grounded; no cross-domain tension).

### B-36 · Concrete F5 audit-signature backend selection *(split off `B-22` 2026-07-14, per operator direction — `B-22`'s seam is CLOSED, this is what remains; CLOSED 2026-07-16 via ADR-D8)*
- **What it is.** `B-22` built and closed the `SigningBackend` composition-root seam (C-CP-20 §20.2.1, PR #976) — the CP-side call surface can accept a real backend, and a TEST-ONLY in-memory Ed25519 double proves the round-trip. This row is what remained: no real backend existed.
- **Resolution (ADR-D8; real AWS KMS key provisioned, ARN not published — see `B36_KMS_KEY_ARN` in the operator's local `.env`).** The operator authorized a real AWS account for this decision. Two facts were verified before deciding: (1) AWS KMS added native Ed25519 support (`ECC_NIST_EDWARDS25519` key spec, `ED25519_SHA_512` algorithm) in November 2025 — this dissolved the assumed algorithm-vs-exposure tradeoff, since KMS can now produce ADR-D5 §1.4's own default algorithm while never exposing key material; (2) the account's ambient credentials resolved to IAM root, so a dedicated least-privilege IAM user (`arhugula-harness-cp-signing`) scoped to `kms:Sign`/`kms:Verify`/`kms:GetPublicKey`/`kms:DescribeKey` on the one provisioned key ARN was created first. A dyadic council (C10 blast-radius ⊥ C11 operator-loop) then decided the remaining live tension — whether the signing key should ever resolve into harness process memory at all, independent of algorithm. C10's grounding: `resolve_signing_key` already resolves its `SecretRef` at `SandboxTier.TIER_4_FULL_VM` (in-sandbox HTTP per AS spec §5.4, not a literal-string env-var read) and is gated exclusively to `MULTI_TENANT_COMPLIANCE` — the landed code already signals a no-exposure design intent, and the signing key's compromise blast radius (silent, retroactive invalidation of the *entire* ledger's tamper-evidence) is categorically worse than a typical secret's. C11 conceded the exposure argument but flagged that KMS moves AWS availability onto the per-signature write-time hot path — a real, separate cost for the eventual composition-root wiring arc to route through the existing C9 breaker discipline, not a reason to prefer local signing. Council ranked KMS-delegated signing over Secrets-Manager-plus-local-signing.
- **What shipped.** New `harness_cp.aws_kms_signing_backend.AwsKmsSigningBackend` implements the `SigningBackend` Protocol via `boto3` KMS `Sign`/`Verify` (`ED25519_SHA_512`, `MessageType=RAW`), constructed with an explicit `key_id → KMS key ARN` mapping supplied by the deployment-time composition root (an unmapped `key_id` fails loud, never silently signs under a default key). Zero change to `resolve_signing_key`/`sign_audit_entry`/`verify_audit_entry_signature` — the `§20.2.1` seam already accommodated this backend shape; `resolve_signing_key`'s `fetch_secret(...)` call still executes harmlessly (it is a pure `SecretRef` constructor per AS spec §5.1 that never resolves literal secret bytes) and remains unread by the KMS-backed signing path, exactly as it is today for the no-backend path. Unit-tested against a mocked KMS client (real `cryptography` Ed25519 crypto behind the mock, proving the request/response plumbing, not just call-recording); mutation-probed (the `_resolve_key_arn` fail-loud check reverted to a silent default-key fallback, confirmed the corresponding tests fail, restored). Live-e2e-proven twice against the real provisioned key (`harness-cp/tests/integration/test_b36_kms_signing_live_e2e.py`, `just b36-kms-signing-live-e2e`): real sign/verify round-trip, tamper rejection, wrong-message rejection, unmapped-`key_id` fail-loud, and confirmation the scoped IAM identity is denied S3 (`s3:ListAllMyBuckets`), IAM (`iam:ListUsers`), and any KMS action beyond the 4 granted even on its own key (`kms:GetKeyPolicy`).
- **Out-of-family Codex round-2 findings, all fixed same session.** (1) The initial landing's `key_arns` mapping accepted KMS aliases, which are mutable — a repointed alias would silently swap key material under an unchanged logical `key_id`; fixed by rejecting aliases at construction (`MutableKeyAliasRejectedError`). (2) The initial live-e2e least-privilege test called `iam.create_user(...)` directly — if the identity were ever misconfigured with broader permissions, that call would succeed and leave a persistent IAM user before the assertion could run; replaced with three read-only, not-granted-action calls (S3 list, IAM list, and `kms:GetKeyPolicy` on the identity's OWN key — the last proving action-level KMS scoping beyond the 4 granted actions, since the account has only the one KMS key and IAM policy simulation is itself not a granted permission on this identity). (3) `boto3>=1.34` didn't guarantee the botocore KMS service model included Ed25519 support; bisected empirically (`botocore==1.40.0` lacks `ED25519_SHA_512`, `1.41.0` has it) and bumped the floor to `boto3>=1.41`. (4) B-36's own `summary` field still read "no real signing backend exists yet" directly beneath `status: closed` — fixed. (5) Composition-root wiring was closed-over without a tracked follow-on — split out as `B-47`.
- **Scope carried forward.** Single-physical-key-per-logical-`key_id` proof only — multi-key fleet routing across distinct `WORKFLOW_BOUND`/`TENANT_BOUND`/`FLEET_BOUND` scopes is out of scope. `B-33` (rotation-boundary proof) now has a real backend to build the rotation-correlation carrier against — extended, not closed. `B-34` narrows: KMS Ed25519 signatures are always a fixed 64 raw bytes with no DER-wrapping ambiguity, so the DER-encoding half of that finding only remains live if a future ECDSA-P256 fallback backend is added alongside this one; the JSON-serializability half is unaffected by backend choice and still open at B-36's close (both B-34 halves subsequently CLOSED at PR 1032). `B-47` (NEW) carries the composition-root wiring gap Codex flagged. **Council: DONE** — C10 blast-radius ⊥ C11 operator-loop/deployment-surface, dyadic council 2026-07-16, ranked AWS KMS delegated signing first.

### B-47 · `AwsKmsSigningBackend` has no production composition-root call site *(surfaced at `B-36`'s own PR, out-of-family Codex review, 2026-07-16)*
- **What it is.** `B-36` (ADR-D8) selected + implemented `AwsKmsSigningBackend` and live-e2e-proved it against a real KMS key, but zero production code path constructs it and passes it as `sign_audit_entry(backend=...)` / `verify_audit_entry_signature(backend=...)`. It is reachable only from tests. A `MULTI_TENANT_COMPLIANCE` deployment cannot get real signed audit entries until a `harness_runtime` bootstrap composition-root arc wires this backend in.
- **Close-out steps.** Author a `harness_runtime` composition-root unit (mirrors the `ProviderSecretResolver`/`GcpSecretManagerResolver` precedent at R-421) that: (a) resolves a `key_id → KMS key ARN` mapping from `RuntimeConfig` at `MULTI_TENANT_COMPLIANCE` deployment-time; (b) constructs `AwsKmsSigningBackend` with a `boto3` KMS client sourced from the runtime's own credential-resolution chain; (c) threads it through to the real `sign_audit_entry`/`verify_audit_entry_signature` call sites once those exist in production code (currently zero callers — this unit may need to land together with whatever CP-axis arc first introduces a real audit-signing call site); (d) per `ADR-D8`'s own C11 concession, wires the C9 per-`{secret_backend, scope}` breaker onto the signing call itself, not just key resolution, since KMS availability now sits on the write-time hot path. **Council: conditional** — mechanical composition-root wiring once a real `sign_audit_entry` call site exists; revisit if the call-site design itself surfaces a cross-axis tension.
- **Progress (PR A, 2026-07-16).** Grounding found the REAL production audit-signing path is the OD `sign_audit_entry` (hash-only placeholder) invoked via `cp_audit_to_od_audit` at every runtime audit write — the CP-side B-22 seam has zero production callers, so a composition root had nothing to inject into. PR A landed the missing seam end-to-end short of the composition root itself — OD spec v1.33 NEW §21.2.1 (clearance filed) + `sign_audit_entry(..., backend=...)` (absent path preserved byte-identically, present path real signature over a metadata-bound canonical message, base64 value, per-algorithm length enforcement) + `cp_audit_to_od_audit(..., backend=...)` passthrough, all mutation-probed. **Remaining (PR B, the actual close):** the harness_runtime composition root per close-out items (a)-(d) — RuntimeConfig audit-signing surface, boto3-guarded factory (R-421 shape), threading `ctx` backend into the runtime converter call sites, and the C9 per-{secret_backend, scope} breaker on the signing call, **plus (e)** (out-of-family Codex round 3 on PR A, verified directly): `RuntimeAuditLedgerWriter.append` persists only the `action_id` wrap (`entry_hash`) — neither `payload` nor `signature_attrs` reach durable storage, so a real KMS signature would be produced then dropped at persistence (pre-existing shape; placeholder signatures were equally discarded). PR B must persist the signed entry or an equivalent durable sidecar before real signatures can be claimed durable — **landed at PR B1 (2026-07-16)** together with items (a)+(b) (AuditSigningConfig + boto3-guarded factory) and the token-map half of (c) (stage-4 construction + span-processor threading; full-chain stage→real-signature→sidecar witness). Remaining: rest of (c) composer threading, (d) breaker, (f). **And (f)** (Codex round 5 on PR A): the canonical signed message does not bind tenant identity (it enters only at the writer wrap), so under a DEPLOYMENT-scoped signing key a signed entry could be re-presented under another tenant; tenant-scoped key_ids already bind the tenant. Resolve jointly with (e) at PR B — payload-attr binding, a fifth message segment (spec delta), or tenant-scoped key_ids required at MTC. No live exposure at PR A (zero backends wired). **And (g)** (Codex round 24 on PR B1): the in-memory sidecar membership index grows with complete audit history; a bounded LRU cannot prove absence, so the fix is a disk-backed index — plain performance engineering within §21.2's committed Tier-5 durability CLASS (no storage engine is spec-committed; corrected 2026-07-17 per the disposition doc — the earlier SQLite-commitment phrasing was an over-reading). **And (h)** (Codex round 30): a tenant's sidecar entries interleave independent OD chain families (CXA §0.3 action-id prefixes discriminate); the PR-B2 verifier must verify per family — global re-sequencing at sign time would touch spec-committed converter prior-hash semantics (design-phase question). Reader docstring states the non-single-chain contract. **And (i)** (Codex round 32): redaction-chain tail-read + compose + append is not cross-process atomic — two processes can sign different entries against one predecessor (the sidecar lock serializes persistence only); in-process is serialized; the clean fix is widening the existing B-40 lock scope across tail-read+compose+append (no new primitive; no SQLite redesign is spec-forced — corrected 2026-07-17). In-code residual since round 13, formally registered. **PR B2a (2026-07-17):** rest of (c) + (d) landed — signing backend threaded into every remaining production audit composer (3× HITL composer, sub-agent dispatcher, 4 cost builders, `for_single_host`) with a real-bootstrap identity witness; C9 `BreakerGuardedSigningBackend` (epoch-guarded, fail-loud) wrapped at the factory; bootstrap key-id validation; dedicated daemon audit-offload executor with per-dispatch completion ack (no post-timeout audit commits); typed + loudly-surfaced signing failures (fail-closed policy = item (m)); append serialization with in-lock timestamps; item-(k) witnesses. Remaining cluster DISPOSITIONED 2026-07-17 at `.harness/b-47-pr-b2-design-disposition-v1.md`: (g)/(i) impl-discretion engineering, (h) the next buildable impl unit (per-family chain verifier), (f)+(m) the two genuine spec-delta forks (recommendations recorded), (j) rides the next runtime-spec delta; the earlier §21.2-SQLite-commitment phrasing was corrected (Tier-5 is the F2 state-ledger durability CLASS per ADR-D1 v1.2 — no engine committed). B-48 tracks the sync-inner-on-loop fork revision.

### B-49 · Per-family audit chain verifier *(split from B-47 disposition, 2026-07-17)*
- **What it is.** B-47 disposition item (h): a tenant's sidecar entries interleave independent OD entry families, so `verify_hash_chain_integrity` over the raw per-tenant sequence fails by construction — and most families do not chain at all (cost/HITL/dispatch deliberately emit genesis predecessors per entry); only the redaction-token family carries real chain positions, discriminated by its `audit.redaction_token.*` namespace keys (caller-supplied `entry_core` rows have no reliable prefix). Buildable now against cleared contracts.
- **Close-out steps.** Build `verify_per_family_chains(entries)` with a PER-PRODUCER verification policy — chain-verified = ONLY the redaction-token family, discriminated by `audit.redaction_token.*` namespace keys (prefix matching misses caller-supplied `entry_core` rows); per-entry CONTENT-HASH checks for cost (`cost_record_audit_writer.py:50`), HITL (`hitl_gate_composer.py:1235`), and sub-agent dispatch (`sub_agent_gate_level_descent.py:215`). Backend SIGNATURE verification is out of scope (§21.2.1 leaves it out; `verify_hash_chain_integrity` never touches `signature_attrs`) — a backend-aware verification API is spec surface for the fork legs. Witnessed with interleaved multi-family fixtures + mutation probes. **Council: no.**

### B-50 · Sidecar scale engineering *(split from B-47 disposition, 2026-07-17; CLOSED 2026-07-17 — item (i) PR #1042, item (g) PR #1044)*
- **What it is.** B-47 disposition items (g)+(i): the in-memory membership index refolds full history at restart (O(history) cold start); the redaction-chain tail-read+compose+append is not cross-process atomic. Both impl-discretion within Tier-5's committed properties — no spec fork (grounding corrected at `.harness/b-47-pr-b2-design-disposition-v1.md` §0).
- **Close-out steps.** Disk-backed index (stdlib `sqlite3` on the 3.12 baseline, or an offset-checkpointed snapshot); make the token map's tail-read+compose+append transactional — NOT a plain outer-lock hold (POSIX `flock` is non-reentrant across separately-opened descriptors; the inner reader/append reacquiring the sidecar lock would self-deadlock): unlocked inner variants under an outer transaction, or a lock-ownership API on the writer. POSIX-only (`_flock` is a win32 no-op; Windows closure rides B-45). **Council: no.**

(2) The retag path still copies already-tagged rows through with their **tag, nested entry content, and `entry_hash` unchanged** (`record_migration.py:516-519` — `if tag != _SINGLE_TAG:` → count, `new_lines.append(json.dumps(row))`, `continue`). (3) The typed rejections are intact at `audit_cutover_record.py:201-214` — whose message names **`B-56` verbatim** at `:212` as the carrier of "the future explicit-relabel mechanism" — and at `record_migration.py:733-739` + `:762-767` ("a relabeled wrapper; refusing"). (4) The generalized `(source_tag, entry_hash)` alias remains **absent** at `per_family_audit_verification.py:287-312`: resolution still matches only `source_tag == normalized_scope` (`:296`) or the `"_single"` tier (`:310`). No commit since 2026-07-25 touches any OD/admin surface.

### B-57 · Direct-writer caller-supplied timestamp capture-order inversion *(registered at the B-48 apply arc, 2026-07-19)*
- **What it is.** The C-IS-07 §7.6 writer-owned fix covers the buffered/branch-DRAIN path only (the ratified narrow scope). DIRECT appends keep caller-supplied timestamps with detect-then-refuse semantics — two uncoordinated concurrent direct writers can still capture outside the lock in inverted order and hit `NonMonotonicTimestampError`. The superseded defect record §8.3 phrased the fix this broadly; the filing did not ratify it (and, per the rounds-28/29 carve-out, the OFFLOADED sub-agent audit appends belong to B-48's writer-owned scope — NOT this residual).
- **Close-out steps.** If the direct-path inversion materializes in production: a narrow IS delta extending §7.6's writer-owned sampling to an opt-in direct-append mode + U-IS-11 widening. Until then detect-then-refuse is the committed posture. **Council: no.**
- **UPDATE 2026-07-27 (grounding pass, re-verified at HEAD `25b44127`) — STILL DORMANT; premise fully intact.** `_CLOCK_SKEW_TOLERANCE = timedelta(0)` (`state_ledger_write.py:69`) is unchanged; the refusal still fires at `:321-324`; writer-owned sampling remains strictly **opt-in via the `WRITER_OWNED_TIMESTAMP` sentinel** (`:316-320` — "Every other timestamp value is caller-supplied verbatim"), so the DIRECT paths keep caller-supplied semantics exactly as this row describes. The named production direct-writer sites all re-verified present and still capturing caller-side, outside the lock: `shadow_git_rollback.py:141`, `pause_resume_protocol.py:1103`/`:1190`/`:1294`, `workflow_driver.py:6038`/`:6261`, `hitl_gate_composer.py:1566`, `cost_attribution_f2_write.py:157`, `as_is_wiring.py:129`, `audit_writer.py:701`. (A `workflow_driver.py:10550` cite was dropped from this enumeration at this pass — out-of-family codex [P2]: `_append_buffered_sequential_entry` takes a `BufferingLedgerWriter` at every one of its three call sites (`:10815`, `:12214`, `:12244`), and `drain_branch_buffers` re-stamps those buffered payloads with `WRITER_OWNED_TIMESTAMP`, so the site belongs to B-48's closed DRAIN half, not this DIRECT residual. The two retained `workflow_driver` cites are genuine — both append straight to `ctx.ledger_writer` at `:6046`/`:6269`.) (No exact total is pinned: a strict count is sensitive to what qualifies as a state-ledger direct append versus any `timestamp=<clock>` construction — a broad sweep of `harness-*/src` returns ~21 such sites.) **No new direct writer was introduced by the B-72/B-78/B-79/B-80 arcs.**
- **Nuance worth recording — asymmetric caller-side recovery already exists on ONE half of this row's own named interleave.** `audit_writer.py:727-752` carries a **bounded 5-attempt resample-retry** (`attempts_left = 5`) recovering from exactly this refusal on the **AUDIT** half of the audit/cost interleave: on `NonMonotonicTimestampError` it resamples a fresh timestamp and retries the IS append, exhausting loudly rather than silently (its own comment explains why that is safe — the OD entry and sidecar identity are timestamp-independent and the idempotency key is unchanged). That is caller-side recovery layered ATOP detect-then-refuse, fully consistent with the committed posture rather than a departure from it. The **COST** half (`cost_attribution_f2_write.py`) and every other direct writer have **no** such recovery, so the row's failure mode stays live-but-unexercised for them. **Posture unchanged. Council: no.**
- **Adjacent, and explicitly NOT evidence about this row's premise.** The stale-carry comment block at `workflow_driver.py` ~`:6752-6784` still described the drain path's timestamp as captured OUTSIDE `_WRITE_LOCK`, called the resulting inversion "a known gap ... the clean fix is timestamp-authority INSIDE `_WRITE_LOCK`", and labelled `test_concurrent_sibling_drains_invert_timestamp` "(xfail, strict)" — all three false at HEAD since the B-48 apply arc. That is CP **documentation decay**, corrected in this same PR (comment-only, zero behavior change). B-48 closed the **DRAIN** path; B-57 is the **DIRECT-writer** residual — the two must not be conflated.
