### B-59 · Capacity authority not preserved across sequential bootstrap invocations *(registered at the B-48 apply arc, 2026-07-19; CLOSED 2026-07-22)*
- **FORK FILED 2026-07-21** — `.harness/class_2_fork_b59_capacity_authority_across_bootstraps.md` (Class 2: process-scoped singleton vs drain barrier vs accept-residual; rec A with the every-admission-surface rider). **RATIFIED 2026-07-21 (option A)** — apply arc is forward work.
- **What it is.** Registered at codex round-4 [P1] "preserve one capacity authority across bootstrap runs": if `api.run()`'s shutdown times out while a daemon dispatch worker is still draining (the documented abandoned-worker-keeps-its-lease-until-fence-ack behavior), the process releases its lock and permits another `api.run()` invocation — but the NEXT bootstrap constructs a FRESH `SubAgentDispatchExecutor` with the full configured frame budget. The old, still-draining worker's occupied frame is invisible to the new executor's accounting, so the process can transiently exceed the single-shared-cap promise across the boundary. The B-48 fork's own §4 verification obligations scope entirely to a SINGLE dispatch/workflow-run's offload mechanics — multiple SEQUENTIAL bootstrap invocations sharing one OS process is a genuinely new, unspecified surface the fork never anticipated.
- **Close-out steps (historical — resolved below).** A real fix needs one of two architectural choices the fork never ratified: (a) a process-scoped capacity-authority singleton that survives across `api.run()` invocations (surviving drain state, not just the frame count), or (b) block a NEW bootstrap from starting until the PRIOR executor has fully drained (a stronger cross-invocation barrier than today's per-run shutdown timeout). Route through Class 2 fork back-flow before building — this is an architectural decision on multi-bootstrap-per-process semantics, not a bounded bug fix. **Council: yes if opened** — C1 orchestration (process lifecycle) vs C9 reliability (capacity invariant integrity) is a nameable tension.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-22 (PR #1080 spec + PR #1081 impl)** — ratified option A (process-scoped capacity-authority singleton). Spec leg: CP v1.103→v1.104 (§25.11 admission-guarantee span clarification) + Runtime v1.103→v1.104 (NEW §14.8.10.6 cross-bootstrap capacity-authority continuity + `RT-FAIL-CAPACITY-AUTHORITY-BUDGET-SHRINK` taxonomy row) + Runtime plan v2.52 (NEW U-RT-146). Impl leg: `FrameLedger` split out of `SubAgentDispatchExecutor`, adopted process-lifetime via `adopt_or_create_process_capacity_ledger()` at `stage_5_loop_init.py`; typed `CapacityAuthorityBudgetShrinkError`; 14 PD-8 mutation-probed tests. Merge-gate test-witness BLOCK r1 (atomicity backstop was a substring count, not a nesting check) fixed via AST-walk assertion + 2 new ctor guard tests, re-verified APPROVE r2.

### B-60 · Cancellation fence not checked at nonlinear-topology child entry points *(registered at the B-48 apply arc, codex round-10, 2026-07-19)*
- **What it is.** Registered at codex round-10 [P1] "check cancellation before nonlinear topology dispatch": when an offloaded child workflow uses PARALLELIZATION, ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS, or HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION, `_execute_workflow_body` returns into the topology-strategy handler before ever reaching the linear step loop's `_dispatch_cancel_token.check()` (`workflow_driver.py:4357-4359`). If an ancestor timeout trips the token between fan-out phases (e.g. between warm-up Phase 1 and Phase 2, or between a HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION recursion level), the child can continue scheduling later work past the fence grace at those branch/level boundaries — the linear-path check this arc added does not extend to the nonlinear dispatch entry points. Not a regression introduced by round 9/10's admission-deferral or future-publication fixes; a pre-existing coverage gap in the round 4-8 cancellation-fence work, surfaced for the first time at round 10.
- **Close-out steps.** Needs equivalent `_dispatch_cancel_token.check()` calls at each nonlinear-topology strategy's entry point (before `_execute_parallelization` / `_execute_orchestrator_workers` / the HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION recursion re-entry begins dispatching) and likely at each fan-out phase boundary (post warm-up Phase 1, pre Phase 2) — not just once at function entry, since the fence can trip mid-fan-out. Ground against CP spec v1.102 / Runtime spec v1.102 §14.8.10.3's fence-ack contract before building: determine whether "check once at strategy entry" or "check at every phase boundary" is the spec-intended granularity. **Council: yes if opened** — C1 orchestration (fan-out phase structure) vs C9 reliability (fence completeness) is a nameable tension.

- **CLOSED 2026-07-21 (PR #1075)** — fence consults landed at EVERY nonlinear boundary per the §14.8.10.3 grounding (strategy entry; warm-up Phase1→Phase2 at both tiers × both strategies with admission-release-on-raise; orchestrator marker→dispatch + orchestrator→worker boundaries; per-branch pre/post-marker in the cancel coroutines; guarded pre-admission consults in the proceed coroutines; EO per-dispatch; decentralized per-stage; synthesis entry + dispatch boundary) PLUS the no-fold guarantees (gather-result scans incl. group-wrapped, BaseExceptionGroup unwraps at the cascade arms and Phase-0 loops — a fence trip can no longer mint a resumable PAUSED or fold to PARTIAL). 10 codex rounds (terminal rule pre-committed at r9, honored at r10) + a 2-round merge-gate: round-1 test-witness BLOCK empirically overturned the per-site PD-8 claim (10/16 sites shadowed by defense-in-depth) and its minimal closure set was implemented + mutation-verified (depth-2 group unwrap, OW worker intra-phase, OW pre-tripped entry, all-rejected joint-pin with an honesty note); round-2 all-APPROVE with the lens re-running every mutation itself incl. the non-vacuity proof of the joint pinning. Residual registered as B-66. 23 b60 witnesses; CP suite 1704 green.

### B-61 · Buffered fan-out ledger drain writes are not fenced *(registered at the B-48 apply arc, codex round-10, 2026-07-19)*
- **What it is.** Registered at codex round-10 [P1] "fence each buffered ledger append": `drain_branch_buffers`'s real `writer.append(...)` calls (`workflow_driver.py:1922-1924`) run without `effect_entry()` or `check()`. When a timed-out offloaded child is inside a nested fan-out, its cancel token can trip while branch tasks are still finishing; `_finish()` still reaches these physical ledger appends afterward, uncounted by `_effects_in_flight` and unblocked by the fence — so `wait_ack()` can classify the timeout as `ACKED_CLEAN` even though F2 ledger entries actually begin writing after the trip. Not a regression introduced by round 9/10's admission-deferral or future-publication fixes; a pre-existing coverage gap in the round 4-8 cancellation-fence work (the `effect_entry()` guard was applied to the direct dispatch write paths, not the buffered-writer drain path), surfaced for the first time at round 10.
- **Close-out steps.** Needs the same `effect_entry()`/`check()` guard `drain_branch_buffers`'s sibling direct writes already carry (per the round 6/7 fixes), applied at each real `writer.append()` inside the drain loop. Ground against whether drain time is single-threaded-post-barrier (making a coarser single guard around the whole drain loop sufficient) or whether per-append granularity is required — resolves the discriminator B-62 below shares. **Council: no** — narrow, single-site guard-application fix once the drain-loop threading model is confirmed.

- **CLOSED 2026-07-21 (PR #1072)** — every physical `real_writer.append` in `drain_branch_buffers` now enters its OWN `effect_entry()` from the ambient token (per-append granularity, resolved per the shared discriminator: the fence contract is per-effect, `append_ledger_entry` dedups by idempotency_key so a partial drain is resume-safe, and drain time is single-threaded post-barrier). Witnesses: mid-drain trip → 1 append + AMBIGUOUS ack + signal; no-token byte-unchanged; pre-tripped → zero appends (all PD-8 probed). Codex clean round 1; merge-gate 3-lens all-APPROVE (concurrency lens swept all 11 drain call sites — no stranded state/leases). Residual noted, not built: an e2e drain-trip transit witness through a real fan-out topology (test-witness lens G2, scope-arguable — the propagation machinery is pre-existing B-48 job-boundary behavior).

### B-62 · A single effect_entry() guard spans multiple independent sequential writes *(registered at the B-48 apply arc, codex round-10, 2026-07-19)*
- **What it is.** Registered at codex round-10 [P1] "split the fence guard between sequential writes": at `sub_agent_dispatch.py:744-750` (and the durable-output/ledger pair in `_execute_workflow_body`), one `effect_entry()` guard wraps a SEQUENCE of independent effects (ledger append, then signing, then audit append). If the token trips while the FIRST write is executing, the guard remains entered for the whole block — it permits the already-running operation to finish (correct), but ALSO permits the subsequent signing + audit append to begin without a fresh consultation of the token, since the guard was entered before the trip and never re-checked mid-block. Not a regression introduced by round 9/10's admission-deferral or future-publication fixes; a pre-existing granularity gap in the round 4-8 cancellation-fence work, surfaced for the first time at round 10.
- **Close-out steps.** Needs each independent effect (ledger append / signing / audit append) wrapped in its OWN `effect_entry()` call rather than one guard spanning all three, so a trip between operations is caught before the next one begins. Ground against whether signing is itself effect-bearing (network call to the KMS backend per ADR-D8) or purely computational before deciding scope — shares the drain-loop threading-model discriminator with B-61. **Council: no** — narrow, per-site guard-splitting fix once the signing effect-classification is confirmed.

- **CLOSED 2026-07-21 (PR #1072)** — both multi-effect guard sites split per-effect: `_compose_and_persist_audit` 8b/8c/8d (signing grounded effect-bearing — `cp_audit_to_od_audit` → `sign_audit_entry` → `backend.sign()` KMS network call at MTC) and `_execute_workflow_body`'s durable-output journal + step ledger append. A trip during one effect now refuses the NEXT at its own fresh consult. Witnesses: 8b-trip → 8c/8d never begin; 8c-trip → 8d never begins (added per the merge-gate test-witness lens G1 note); journal-trip → ledger append never begins (real `execute_workflow`); all PD-8 probed incl. the coarse-guard and per-site nullcontext mutations.

### B-63 · SigningBackend.verify() infra failures are not typed-availability at the concrete backend *(registered at the U-OD-55 impl arc, codex round-10, 2026-07-20)*
- **What it is.** Registered at the U-OD-55 impl arc (codex round-10 [P2] "translate verify outages into the OD availability type"): OD spec v1.34 §21.2.2 row 7(b) requires backend availability errors during verification to surface through a dedicated typed availability error. The `AwsKmsSigningBackend.verify()` concrete backend (harness-cp, B-36/ADR-D8) catches only `KMSInvalidSignatureException` and returns `False` for it; any OTHER boto3/KMS failure (credential, network, throttling, service exception) propagates as a raw vendor exception, unclassified. Confirmed architecturally cross-axis at grounding: the `SigningBackend` Protocol (`harness_cp.f5_signing_key_resolution`) documents no availability-exception contract at all (checked directly, not inferred) — and even if it did, the OD-owned `AuditVerificationBackendUnavailableError` cannot be raised by a CP-axis backend without CP importing FROM OD, which the axis-import direction (OD consumes CP; not the reverse) forbids. A blanket `except Exception` at the OD-axis verifier call site is not a substitute — it would misclassify genuine backend defects as availability, which the same row 7(b) text forbids ("raises that are NOT the typed availability error and NOT `AuditSignatureInvalid` are DEFECTS and propagate unwrapped"). U-OD-55's own `per_family_audit_verification.py` docstring previously claimed "a well-behaved backend implementation is expected to raise THIS type itself" — corrected in-place at the same PR to state the gap accurately (no such contract exists yet) rather than assert an architecturally-impossible design. **Concrete instance (codex round-12, same PR):** when a resolver returns `AwsKmsSigningBackend` for a key_id absent from that instance's OWN internal `_key_arns` mapping, `verify()` raises `UnknownSigningKeyIdError` (a `KeyError` subclass) BEFORE reaching KMS — semantically identical to "the composition root failed to supply the key" (the exact category `AuditVerificationBackendUnavailableError`'s docstring already describes), but unreachable to translate at the OD-axis verifier without importing a name from ONE SPECIFIC backend's implementation module (`harness_cp.aws_kms_signing_backend`, not the generic `SigningBackend` Protocol module) — the same wrong-direction coupling this row already forecloses, confirmed by grep: `UnknownSigningKeyIdError` is defined and used ONLY in that one backend module, not on the Protocol. Same registered gap, not a second independent defect.
- **Close-out steps.** Needs a shared/CP-side (or harness-core) typed availability-exception contract that `SigningBackend` implementations (starting with `AwsKmsSigningBackend`) raise for infra failures distinct from a genuine signature mismatch, PLUS a translation catch at the OD-axis verifier call sites (`_verify_entry_signature` / `verify_cutover_record_signature`) that maps the CP/core type to `AuditVerificationBackendUnavailableError`. Cross-axis scope (touches harness-cp production code already shipped under B-36/PR #1031) — out of U-OD-55's OD-axis-only reach; does not block U-OD-55's merge (the resolver-path availability typing — unknown `key_id` — is already correct and covers the row 7(b) example explicitly named in spec text; this residual is the real-backend infra-failure sub-case only). **Council: no** — narrow, single cross-axis contract addition once a concrete infra-failure taxonomy is confirmed against the deployed backend(s).

- **CLOSED 2026-07-21 (PR #1073)** — the shared typed availability contract landed at `harness_core.SigningBackendUnavailableError` (the close-out's harness-core branch; carrier-home discipline): `AwsKmsSigningBackend.verify()` classifies `(BotoCoreError, ClientError)` infra failures as the shared type (mismatch still `False`, caught FIRST — order pinned by a real-ClientError-subclassing fake per the merge-gate test-witness lens; `ParamValidationError` carved out as a DEFECT per codex r3), `UnknownSigningKeyIdError` gains the shared base (the row's concrete instance), and BOTH named OD verifier sites translate narrowly to `AuditVerificationBackendUnavailableError` per OD v1.34 §21.2.2 row 7(b) — defect raises still propagate unwrapped, row-9 breaker asymmetry untouched. Codex r2 extended the taxonomy to the CLI boundaries (harness-inspect → UNVERIFIED exit 3 retryable wording; migrate-audit-sidecar → typed exit-1, zero tags changed) and the runtime-local name collision was renamed (`SigningBackendSdkUnavailableError`). 4 codex rounds converged; merge-gate 2 rounds (r1 two BLOCKs fixed per prescription) to 3-lens all-APPROVE; 15 witnesses, 7 PD-8 probes all pinning. Residual noted, not built: a CP §20.2.1 Protocol-docstring cross-reference to the availability contract (spec-lens follow-on nicety); bootstrap greenfield round-trip availability reads as config-invalid (pre-existing posture, test-witness lens note).

### B-64 · Greenfield cutover-record freshness probe and publication are not atomic cross-process *(registered at the U-RT-134 impl arc, codex round-9, 2026-07-20)*

- **What it is.** `initialize_mtc_audit_signing_record`'s ledger-freshness probes (sidecar rows + IS `audit:` refs, each under its own short-lived `cross_process_read_lock`) release their locks BEFORE `_greenfield_sign_empty_record` publishes the record — a concurrent audit append landing in the probe→publish window could in principle let an empty cutover record be minted over just-written history (codex round-9 [P1], the arc's pre-committed terminal review round).
- **Why registered, not fixed.** The practical attack paths are already closed: at MTC an appender only runs POST-bootstrap, and its own bootstrap either verified an existing record or minted one (the U-RT-134 invariant itself), so a racing appender implies the record exists and the greenfield branch is never taken; simultaneous dual-greenfield bootstraps converge via the round-7 no-clobber `os.link` publication (the loser verifies the winner). The residual hardening — holding the sidecar/ledger locks across probe + publication — interacts with `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock` same-process nesting semantics and needs its own deadlock-safety analysis, not a terminal-round patch.
- **Close-out.** Hold `cross_process_write_lock(sidecar_path)` (or an equivalent single lock scope) across ALL THREE probes (the persisted-row key scan shares the same probe→publish window — merge-gate concurrency lens, PR #1063) and the no-clobber publication, after confirming the IS lock implementation's same-process nesting semantics (replace inner read-lock acquisitions with lock-free reads under the held write lock if nesting is unsafe). Witness: an interleaving test that starts an append inside the probe→publish window and asserts the greenfield mint is refused. Does not block U-RT-134's merge (terminal-round registration per the B-48 r10 precedent).

- **CLOSED 2026-07-21 (PR #1071)** — `initialize_mtc_audit_signing_record` now holds ONE `cross_process_write_lock(sidecar_path)` across the persisted-row key scan, both freshness probes, and the no-clobber `os.link` publication, exactly per the close-out; same-path inner reads run lock-free under the hold (`_sidecar_has_rows_locked` + the `_reject_record_key_used_by_persisted_rows` wrapper/`_locked` split — `record_migration.py` callers unchanged), and the nested `ledger_has_audit_refs` probe's state-ledger read lock was deadlock-safety-grounded against the dir lock's per-thread reentrant face (the B-50 composition). The existing-record verify branch releases early (codex r3 — no publication there; availability preserved). Witness: two-thread interleaving test proving an appender holding the writer's own lock cannot land inside the window at EITHER the probe or publication half (merge-gate test-witness lens strengthened the publication half pre-merge); 4 codex rounds to convergence (2 typed-error translation fixes + the verify-branch release), merge-gate 3-lens all-APPROVE, 5 PD-8 mutation probes all pinning, full runtime suite 2926 passed.

### B-65 · Post-effect signing carrier disposition at CP topology cascade handlers is unspecified *(registered at the U-RT-136/U-CP-73 impl arc, codex round-2, 2026-07-20)*

- **CLOSED 2026-07-22 (PR #1078)** — the RATIFIED option-A apply arc landed in full: CP spec v1.103 §25.15 terminal-with-result rider + Runtime spec v1.103 §14.8.11 protected result store (spec leg PR #1077; clearance markers filed), plus the CP/Runtime impl (`workflow_driver.py` name-match fence ahead of every branch-failure → PAUSED/PARTIAL conversion across all 6 topology handlers; `protected_result_store.py` encrypted/tenant-bound/ack-gated-retention store; both dispatch-side `result_ref` threading through llm_dispatch/runtime_tool_dispatcher/sub_agent_dispatch/webhook_delivery_composer). 11 out-of-family Codex rounds to terminal (round 7 introduced then round 8 self-corrected a tenant-sentinel-encoding regression; round 11's two findings closed the arc — one fixed via a collision-free tenant-scope encoding, one registered as `B-68`, a narrow TTL-dependent GC/publish race). Merge-gate 3-lens round-1 BLOCKed on missing e2e coverage for 3 of 4 post-effect raise-site families — fixed + PD-8 mutation-probed per site, APPROVE at round 2. Both packages full-green throughout (harness-runtime 3028 passed/29 skipped; harness-cp 1717 passed/4 skipped). Second facet below (payload recoverability) is DELIVERED by the same protected result store — the store IS the resolution.
- **FORK FILED 2026-07-21** — `.harness/class_2_fork_b65_post_effect_signing_carrier_cascade_disposition.md` (Class 2: §25.15 terminal-with-result rider + §3b protected result store with encrypted/tenant-bound/ack-gated-retention contract and full-strength composite keying; rec A). **RATIFIED 2026-07-21 (option A)** — apply arc is forward work.
Under `audit_signing_fail_closed=ON`, a `PostEffectAuditSigningError` (the U-RT-136 result-preserving
post-effect carrier) raised at a fan-out branch reaches the CP workflow driver's topology cascade
handling as a generic branch failure: under `cascade_policy=pause` it converts to a resumable PAUSED
result whose resume RE-DISPATCHES the same step — re-firing the already-completed PAID effect the
carrier exists to protect — and PROCEED-tier handling reduces it to PARTIAL without the `result_ref`.
The v1.101 CP rider's catch-ordering contract enumerates the per-attempt classifiers (all fenced at
this arc) but does NOT specify branch-vs-workflow terminality of the carrier per `cascade_policy`;
that disposition (terminal-for-workflow vs terminal-for-branch-with-siblings-preserved vs a
non-resumable pause variant) is a design decision over C-CP-25 §25.10+/§25.15 cascade semantics, and
`harness-cp` cannot import the runtime carrier (any driver-side fence uses the established
`type(exc).__name__` match — the `StepDispatchTimeoutError` precedent). Registered per the terminal
register discipline (B-64/B-60..62 precedents); blast radius today is bounded to flag-ON fan-out
topologies. Close-out: spec-leg the disposition, wire the name-match fence ahead of the
branch-failure → PAUSED/PARTIAL conversions, witness a flag-ON fan-out run asserting no resumable
snapshot + result_ref surfaced + no re-dispatch on resume. Second facet (codex round-3 P1, same
design surface): payload RECOVERABILITY beyond the in-memory carrier — after the driver's
string conversion the caller holds an opaque `result_ref` with no persisted lookup (the report
log is digest-only per the round-2 redaction fix); the ratified acc-1b letter requires only
report + caller-carried reference (delivered), so a protected result store keyed by
`result_ref` (or carrying the payload through the CP/runtime result model) resolves in the
same spec leg.

### B-48 · Sync sub-agent dispatch executes on the event loop *(surfaced at B-47 PR B2a, out-of-family Codex round-2, 2026-07-17)*
- **CLOSED 2026-07-19 (PR #1060)** — spec+plan deltas APPLIED (Runtime v1.102 §14.8.10 + CP v1.102 §25.11 + IS v1.11 §7.6 + plans v2.50/v2.39/v2.7 + Core v1.3 (U-CORE-03) + clearance markers; C1⊥C9 dyad 16/16 CONFIRM at `.harness/council-dyad-b48-apply-2026-07-18.md`); executor impl arc (U-CORE-03 + U-RT-140..144 + U-CP-101 + the B-39 interim constraint) LANDED — 10 successive rounds of decorrelated pre-merge review (merge-gate 3-lens + out-of-family Codex, each re-run to convergence), 35 findings fixed with PD-8 mutation-probed witnesses. Round 10 (the operator-approved final gate) converged clean on 9's fixes; codex round-10 surfaced 3 further findings against pre-existing cancellation-fence coverage from rounds 4-8 (not a round-9/10 regression), escalated to the operator per the pre-committed terminal rule, and registered separately as B-60/B-61/B-62 rather than blind-fixed (operator selected "register + merge now").
- **Fork doc FILED 2026-07-18** at `.harness/class_2_fork_b48_sync_subagent_dispatch_offload.md` — recommendation (grounded at filing rounds 1/15: the only materialized bound is HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION's per-parent fan-out cap 3; the §25.11 depth phrase is unmaterialized, generic recursion and aggregate active frames unbounded): option B = custom grow-on-demand executor + configurable hard cap + fail-fast; C1/C9 council at apply; cancellation semantics defined first. Selection RATIFIED 2026-07-18 (option B); spec-apply COMPLETE 2026-07-19 — impl arc live.
- **What it is.** `RuntimeHITLGateComposer._dispatch_inner` invokes the sync C-RT-17 sub-agent dispatcher DIRECTLY on the event loop — the U-RT-60 wrap-asymmetry fork's Q3-ratified direct-call reading. The entire sub-agent dispatch therefore runs on the loop: child workflow execution (seconds-long), its F2/ledger writes, and (since B-47 PR B2a threaded the signing backend) KMS network signing. A slow child workflow or KMS call blocks every concurrent workflow and HITL interaction. Pre-existing at the fork ratification — B2a only ADDS KMS latency to an already-blocking ratified surface, which is why this is registered as its own arc rather than patched inside B2a.
- **Close-out steps.** Class 2 fork revision of the U-RT-60 direct-call reading: offload the sync inner dispatch in `_dispatch_inner` off the loop. The offload design must handle recursion (child workflows re-enter the sync driver whose `SyncDispatcherFacade` bridges back onto the loop; a bounded pool deadlocks under recursive `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` depth — NOT the 4-worker audit-offload pool, NOT the loop default pool), and re-validate pause/resume flags, OTel span context, and B-21 fan-out under worker-thread execution; re-run the r100 e2e. The B2a-landed mitigations already cover every OTHER production signing path (async composers/builders offload via the dedicated audit executor with join-on-cancel). **Council: yes at apply leg** (condition MET at filing rounds 1/15/17 — only the fan-out cap 3 is materialized; the depth phrase names no value/carrier; generic recursion + aggregate active frames unbounded; the C1/C9 dyad CONFIRMS the filing-settled RECOMMENDATION — fail-fast + typed error + RuntimeConfig ownership + default 256 shared-budget frames (N upstream + one per sync branch) — RATIFIED 2026-07-18 (option B as recommended); it does not re-derive the default).

### B-37 · Remote MCP streamable-HTTP transport residuals *(surfaced at `B-10`'s own `B-MCP-OAUTH-RS-ENFORCE` grounding, split out 2026-07-14 at forward-register-schema grounding; corrected 2026-07-14 round-3 out-of-family Codex — the original finding had the deprecated/current function names backwards)*
- **What it is.** `B-10`'s own "Residual" bullet (2026-07-12 grounding) named two independent gaps, left unregistered as their own item. **(1), corrected:** `mcp_client_host.py:495`'s `_http_connection_context` already imports the CURRENT, non-deprecated `mcp.client.streamable_http.streamable_http_client` (verified against the pinned `mcp==1.27.1`: `streamablehttp_client`, no underscore, carries `@deprecated("Use streamable_http_client instead.")` — the ORIGINAL B-10 bullet had the two names' deprecation status backwards). The real, empirically-confirmed gap: line 507 calls `streamable_http_client(url, **kwargs)` where `kwargs` is built (lines 498-506) in the OLD deprecated function's shape (`headers`/`timeout`/`sse_read_timeout`) — but the current function's actual signature is `(url, *, http_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = None, terminate_on_close=True)`; it does not accept those kwargs at all. Harmless today only because `_build_transport_config` (`mcp_client_host_factory.py:97`) never populates `headers`/`timeout`/`sse_read_timeout` on the `transport_config` dict (confirmed by direct read — only `url` is ever set), but a latent `TypeError` the moment anything does. The docstrings at lines 203/483/544 are also stale, still naming the deprecated `streamablehttp_client` as what's used. **(2), unchanged:** `MCPClientConfig` (`types.py:618`) carries **no field at all** for auth credentials despite its own docstring's "auth-secret reference are operator-supplied" claim — there is currently no way, even manually, to authenticate a remote MCP connection.
- **Current state.** Both dormant — no live remote-OAuth deployment exists yet; neither blocks any current close. Registered here (rather than left buried inside a closed `B-10` row where a `--open`-filtered query would miss them) per the register's own "register-then-triage, not drop" discipline.
- **Close-out steps.** (1) **Keep** the current `streamable_http_client` import (do NOT migrate to the deprecated `streamablehttp_client`); build a configured `httpx.AsyncClient` from `headers`/`timeout`/`sse_read_timeout`/`auth` and pass it via `http_client=` instead of forwarding those as direct kwargs; fix the 3 stale docstrings naming the deprecated function. (2) add an auth-credential field to `MCPClientConfig` (distinct from `B-MCP-PRIMITIVE-SIG-GATE`'s tool-description-hash mechanism) and wire it into the connection context. **Council: no** — mechanical once a real remote-MCP credential-supply build opens; not urgent.

### B-38 · Fine-grained provider-exception classifier for `breaker.cause` *(surfaced at `B-19`'s own close, split out 2026-07-14 at forward-register-schema grounding, out-of-family Codex)*
- **What it is.** `B-19`'s own "Follow-on registered (NOT built)" bullet named a fine-grained provider-exception classifier (distinguishing `rate_limit` / `auth_failure` / `5xx_streak` at a real per-step dispatch failure) that would make `harness.breaker.cause` non-vacuous — every breaker-trip call site previously passed `cause=None` (the runtime's only real auth-vs-transient discriminator was a bootstrap-ping-only classifier that never reached per-step dispatch).
- **Close-out.** Added `_classify_breaker_cause(exc: BaseException) -> BreakerCause | None` (`retry_breaker_fallback.py`) — duck-typed on `.status_code` (`429`→`RATE_LIMIT`, `401`/`403`→`AUTH_FAILURE`, `500<=code<600`→`FIVE_XX_STREAK`, else `None`) rather than importing each provider SDK's exception hierarchy, matching the existing let-SDK-exceptions-propagate discipline at `llm_dispatch._dispatch_anthropic`/`_dispatch_openai`/`_dispatch_ollama`. Computed once per exception (as `breaker_cause`, distinct from the pre-existing `cause: ValidatorRetryExitClass` local that drives retry/fail-fast control flow) and threaded into all 3 `breaker.record_failure()` call sites. Verified via an isolated status-code table test AND an e2e test driving the real `RetryBreakerFallbackDispatcher.dispatch()` catch site with SDK-shaped fake exceptions, asserting the classified cause lands on the emitted `BreakerTransition` — mutation-probed against real production-code reversions (`record_failure(cause=breaker_cause)` → `record_failure()`; the `500<=code<600` bound reverted to `>=500`) confirming the tests genuinely fail without the fix, then restored.
- **Second-round Codex catch (real, not registered-away).** `emit_breaker_trip_span_event` (`harness_breaker_schema.py`) built an `EventEmission` bookkeeping record but never called the real span's `.add_event(...)` — so `harness.breaker.cause` would have stayed a Python object field, never reaching an actual OTel span, defeating this row's own purpose. Confirmed by comparison against the sibling `emit_drift_event` (`alignment_floor_drift_detection.py`), which *does* call `.add_event` — proving this was a local, comparison-provable omission, not the systemic H_T-OD-2 "OTel SDK injection absent" gap it initially looked like (`SpanRef` is a real `opentelemetry.trace.Span`, not a stub). Fixed to match the established pattern rather than registered as a follow-on, since leaving it unfixed would mean this row's stated goal ("make `harness.breaker.cause` a real signal") was not actually achieved end-to-end.
- **Split out, not folded in.** Two adjacent findings surfaced during this close were registered as their own rows rather than absorbed here: `B-41` (the pre-existing retry-routing classifier still misroutes `AuthenticationError` to `TRANSIENT_RETRY`, a real behavior-change decision with its own blast radius) and `B-42` (`FIVE_XX_STREAK` is tagged from the terminal exception only, not a verified N-consecutive streak — an acknowledged MVP simplification, caught independently by both this row's own grounding agent and out-of-family Codex reviewing the PR). Shipped at PR #1020.

### B-40 · Memory operation ledger has no cross-process write serialization *(surfaced at B-35's merge-gate concurrency lens, 2026-07-15; CLOSED at PR #1026 after a 4-round register-correction arc at PR #1025)*
- **What it is.** `append_memory_operation` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_operation_ledger.py:478-497`) reads the full ledger to find the last entry, computes `prior_event_hash` from it, then appends — serialized only by a process-local `threading.Lock` (`memory_operation_ledger.py:36`). Two independent OS processes writing to the SAME canonical store (e.g. two concurrent `harness run` invocations against the same repository — see round 4 below) can both read the same last entry before either appends, producing a `prior_event_hash` mismatch that `verify_memory_operation_ledger` would later flag as `CHAIN_LINK_MISMATCH`. A second, related gap: `read_memory_operations()` (`memory_store.py:226-229`) does an unlocked `path.read_text()` with no coordination against an in-flight writer, risking a torn read of a partially-flushed line under true concurrency. Both gaps are real and unchanged across all four correction rounds below — only the disposition framing was wrong, three times, in three different directions.
- **Round 1 self-correction (2026-07-15) — WRONG.** Claimed this was "speculative, already-decided, not fork-eligible," conflating ADR-F2 §Rationale(c)(ii)'s `[SPECULATIVE]` tag on whole-system multi-process/cross-host *workload-mix posture* with C-MEM-08's specific, unconditional ledger invariant. Out-of-family Codex reviewing PR #1025 correctly flagged this as a P2.
- **Round 2 correction (2026-07-15) — ALSO WRONG.** Reclassified `design_substrate_gated`, treating whether C-MEM-08's "concurrent writers" covers cross-process/cross-worktree writers as an open interpretive question needing a Class 1 fork ruling. Out-of-family Codex flagged this too, citing `Spec_Information_Substrate_v1.md` C-IS-07 §7.3 and C-IS-09 §9.3 directly.
- **Round 3 correction (2026-07-15, Codex + advisor()) — the settled reading, still incomplete.** Verified directly against the cited sections: C-IS-07 §7.3 states "the C3-pole append-only write contract serializes writers" (unconditional, no in-process/cross-process qualifier). C-IS-09 §9.3 "Cross-worktree writer serialization" explicitly REQUIRES "concurrent writes across sibling worktrees against the same `.git` storage backend are serialized" — this is NOT open. §9.4 defers only the *mechanism* to implementation discretion, the same pattern `Spec_Information_Substrate_v1.md` §7.4 uses for the memory ledger specifically. Round 2 conflated this settled requirement with §9.4's separate, still-open "Multi-writer scaling BEYOND worktree-isolation requires a downstream D-ADR" clause (a different, larger, true-distributed-scale question that does not gate this row). Round 3 correctly reclassified `registered_finding`, buildable now, no fork — but still claimed "no live trigger, dormant."
- **Round 4 correction (2026-07-15, Codex round 3) — "dormant" was ALSO wrong.** Grepping for internal `multiprocessing`/`subprocess.Popen`/`ProcessPoolExecutor`/`os.fork(` only rules out the harness spawning its *own* child processes — it says nothing about two independently-launched, fully-supported CLI invocations. `RuntimeConfig.memory.enabled` defaults `True` and `memory.root_path` defaults to `<repository_root>/.harness/memory` (`types.py:1504-1508`, bound at `automatic_memory.py:114`) — so two ordinary concurrent `harness run` invocations against the SAME repository (two terminals, two CI runners — no exotic multi-process code required) already resolve to the identical canonical memory ledger **today**. This is a **live, directly reachable gap under ordinary usage**, not one gated on a future architecture.
- **Corrected disposition: `registered_finding`, buildable now, live — no fork, no council.** Requirement settled (C-IS-09 §9.3 + C-MEM-08); mechanism is implementation discretion (§9.4); trigger is live (two concurrent `harness run` invocations, no internal multiprocessing needed). This is impl-to-cleared-spec, not X-AL-3. Both real gaps are in scope for the eventual fix: (1) `append_memory_operation`'s `threading.Lock` does not serialize across OS processes — needs a cross-process mechanism per §9.4's own discretion list; (2) `read_memory_operation_ledger`'s unlocked `path.read_text()` can observe a torn/partial line during a concurrent append — needs reader coordination or tolerant parsing. Axis-wide: `memory_operation_ledger.py` and `state_ledger_write.py` share the identical writer pattern (`state_ledger_write.py:26-28,61-73`), so any fix must cover both, not memory-ledger-only.
- **CLOSED at PR #1026 (2026-07-15).** Added `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock` — a same-host `fcntl.flock` helper mirroring the proven pattern already shipped at `harness_runtime.lifecycle.reconciler_pause_resume_substrate._workflow_lock` (POSIX advisory, same-host only; true cross-host distributed writers remain out of design horizon per ADR-F2 §Rationale(c)(ii), unaffected). Wired into **4 real call sites** found during grounding — 2 more than originally named: `append_ledger_entry`, `append_memory_operation`, `shadow_git_rollback`'s ledger-preserve-across-checkout window, and `jsonl_event_ledger_lifecycle.initialize_jsonl_event_ledger`/`validate_jsonl_event_ledger_format` (the last caught by the merge-gate concurrency lens — reachable on every `harness run` bootstrap/resume via `harness_runtime.lifecycle.state_ledger.materialize_state_ledger`; its pre-existing unlocked read could undercount `entry_count` and silently skip the tamper-check gate). `read_ledger`/`read_memory_operation_ledger` take a shared lock; internal call sites use new `_unlocked` variants to avoid self-deadlock (`flock` is per-open-file-description, not reentrant). Added genuine multi-process test coverage (`multiprocessing`, `fork` context) for all 3 append-side call sites — the merge-gate test-witness lens caught that the first pass covered only 2 of 3 wired sites (missing `shadow_git_rollback`), fixed before merge. Every new test mutation-probed: reverted the fix, confirmed `CHAIN_LINK_MISMATCH` failure across 3 runs, restored. Full `harness-is` suite green; `ruff`/`pyright`/`overlay-check` clean. **Council: no** — requirement was settled (C-IS-09 §9.3 + C-MEM-08), mechanism was implementation discretion (§9.4); impl-to-cleared-spec.
- **Post-merge-gate Codex round on Windows + gitignore.** Out-of-family Codex round 1 on PR #1026 caught two further issues, both fixed before merge: (1) the unconditional top-level `import fcntl` broke `import harness_is` on Windows (eagerly imported via `memory_operation_ledger`) — C-STK-10 commits to macOS/Linux/Windows host-OS support "without per-OS port forking"; fixed by gating the import behind `sys.platform == "win32"` and degrading the lock context managers to a no-op on Windows (exact pre-B-40 parity — the in-process `threading.Lock` half is unaffected and cross-platform; only the new cross-process dimension is unavailable there). (2) the new `.lock` sidecar files were not gitignored; added `.harness/**/*.lock`, verified via `git check-ignore` against both actual lock paths. The genuine Windows locking *mechanism* remains unbuilt by design (advisor()-reviewed scope call, not an oversight) — registered separately as `B-45`.
- **Round 2 out-of-family Codex catch — `cross_process_read_lock`'s own P1 fix genuinely violated read-only-CLI, fixed.** Reviewing the FULL PR diff against `main` (an earlier attempt had been invoked against an invalid git ref and produced no usable verdict), Codex found the round-1 read-path fallback (opening the sidecar with `O_CREAT` when it didn't exist yet) still `mkdir`'d the parent directory even on the "no sidecar" branch — a read against a ledger with no lock file yet on a read-only parent would raise `PermissionError`, breaking `harness-inspect`'s no-writes contract exactly the way round 1 was supposed to prevent. Fixed: when the sidecar doesn't exist, `cross_process_read_lock` now skips locking entirely (no `mkdir`, no `O_CREAT`, no side effect) rather than trying to provision it — verified with a direct probe (a read-only-parent directory, no sidecar) confirming the read succeeds and creates nothing.
- **Round 2 also found 2 non-regression residuals, registered as `B-46` rather than fixed here.** (1) The same no-sidecar-yet fallback above reintroduces a narrow TOCTOU: a reader can pass the "sidecar doesn't exist" check moments before a racing first-writer creates it, then read unguarded while that writer is mid-append. (2) The identical shape exists in `jsonl_event_ledger_lifecycle.py`'s initialization probe. advisor()-confirmed both are pre-existing (every read was unguarded, always, before B-40 existed — this is a residual of B-40's own round-1 P1 fix, not a new hole) and narrow (once any writer has ever created the sidecar, all subsequent reads/writes correctly serialize). The durable fix — lock the canonical ledger file directly instead of a lazily-created sidecar — removes the provisioning race entirely but is a larger, `harness-inspect`-compatibility-affecting redesign properly scoped to its own arc, not bolted onto this PR's third round. Softened `cross_process_read_lock`'s docstring to state the guarantee precisely (closes the race once a ledger has been written at least once under the lock, not before).
- **Also fixed (round 2), unrelated to the code review: a self-inflicted register regression.** The B-40/B-45 register update in this PR used a full-file `yaml.safe_dump` reserialization, which silently stripped `.harness/forward-register.yaml`'s entire header comment block (status enum, standing grounding directive, forward-only update rules) even though only 2 rows changed semantically. Restored the header verbatim from `main`; all future register edits in this arc use a header-preserving splice, not a full-file dump.
- **Convergence called here (advisor()-confirmed).** Round 2's two P2s (findings 1+2 above) are non-regression residuals, not new holes this PR introduces — the round-3 read-only fix causing the round-4 TOCTOU is the same underlying tension (lazy sidecar provisioning) that only a canonical-file-lock redesign resolves, which is exactly why that redesign is its own scoped follow-on (`B-46`) rather than a fourth round on this PR. No further out-of-family review round was run on this disposition-only commit — registering + documenting a known, narrow, non-regression residual is the resolution, not a new substantive change needing re-review.

### B-46 · Cross-process ledger lock has a first-write TOCTOU on lazy sidecar provisioning *(surfaced at B-40's own PR, out-of-family Codex round 4 + advisor(), 2026-07-15; CLOSED 2026-07-18 — PR #1052)*
- **CLOSED 2026-07-18 (PR #1052; bullet refreshed at merge-gate round-1 to shipped HEAD)** — canonical-file lock redesign hardened through 9 codex rounds: writers dir(EXCLUSIVE, per-thread-reentrant)→file-EX non-blocking-probe handoff; lock never creates the canonical (round-36 guard preserved); readers dir-first, never create/mkdir; inode-stability verify-retry; `cross_process_replace_lock` for rollback's inode swap; ONE global lock order (cross-process before `_WRITE_LOCK`); transitional mixed-version coexistence (writers PROVISION + lock the legacy sidecar, O_NOFOLLOW + alias checks, loud non-regular refusal). Residuals per the module docstring: absent parent dir at read; new-reader vs fresh-old-writer first sidecar creation (torn read, loud). 20+ witnesses incl. two-process fork-based dir-flock coverage; all mutation classes probe-killed.
- **What it is.** `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock`'s lock sidecar (B-40) is provisioned lazily by whichever writer touches a ledger first. A reader racing that very first writer can pass `cross_process_read_lock`'s "sidecar doesn't exist yet" existence check before the writer creates it, then read the ledger unguarded while that writer is mid-append — defeating the torn-read protection for exactly the first write/read race against a brand-new ledger. The identical gap exists in `jsonl_event_ledger_lifecycle.py`'s initialization probe (a fresh ledger can take the missing-file branch and return `entry_count=0` without waiting, even while another process already holds the writer lock but hasn't yet created/populated the file).
- **Current state.** Registered, not built — advisor()-confirmed non-regression (every read was unguarded, always, before B-40 existed; this is a residual, not a new hole) and narrow in practice (once any writer has ever created the sidecar for a given ledger, all subsequent reads/writes correctly serialize — the window is exactly the first concurrent write+read pair against a not-yet-touched ledger, which degrades to a loud parse error in an already-degenerate scenario, not silent corruption). This finding and B-40's round-3 P1 fix (readers must never create the sidecar or `mkdir`, to preserve `harness-inspect`'s read-only-CLI contract) are in direct tension under a lazily-created sidecar — no fix satisfies both without changing the lock target.
- **Close-out steps.** Lock the CANONICAL ledger file directly (a separate fd opened purely for `flock`, independent of any fd used for actual I/O — POSIX `flock` is per open-file-description, so this is safe) instead of a lazily-created sidecar; when the canonical file doesn't exist, there is genuinely nothing to read yet (no race is possible on a file that isn't there); when it does exist, lock it read-only (`O_RDONLY`, no `O_CREAT` needed since it already exists) or read-write (`O_CREAT|O_RDWR`, needed once, for the writer that creates it). This removes the provisioning race entirely but touches the write path adjacent to hash-chain integrity and the `harness-inspect` read-only contract — its own scoped arc, not bolted onto B-40's PR. **Council: no** — mechanical redesign once scoped, same implementation-discretion category as `B-45`; not an open spec question.

### B-45 · Cross-process ledger lock has no genuine Windows serialization backend *(surfaced at B-40's own PR, out-of-family Codex + advisor(), 2026-07-15)*
- **What it is.** `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock` (B-40) degrades to a no-op on Windows (`sys.platform == "win32"`) rather than providing real cross-process serialization there — Windows sits at exact pre-B-40 parity (same as before the module existed), not at feature parity with POSIX. The identical gap already exists, unregistered until now, at `harness_runtime.lifecycle.reconciler_pause_resume_substrate`'s own unconditional `fcntl` import (that module isn't eagerly imported at package `__init__`, so it doesn't break basic import on Windows, but its lock is equally POSIX-only).
- **Current state.** Registered, not built — a deliberate scope call (advisor()-reviewed during B-40's own PR), not an oversight. `msvcrt.locking` is byte-range and exclusive-only; it does not cleanly provide the shared-reader/exclusive-writer (`LOCK_SH`/`LOCK_EX`) split both `cross_process_ledger_lock` and `reconciler_pause_resume_substrate` rely on. A correct cross-platform abstraction preserving those semantics (e.g. a small shared/exclusive lock facade backed by `fcntl` on POSIX and a genuine Windows-native primitive, or a portable third-party lock library) is its own scoped arc, not a hasty `sys.platform` branch bolted onto a PR already touching hash-chain integrity.
- **Close-out steps.** (1) Pick a Windows backend (`msvcrt`-based exclusive-only lock with a documented downgrade of the shared-read guarantee, or a vetted portable library — a real design choice, council-eligible if the tradeoff is non-obvious); (2) wire it in behind the `_IS_WINDOWS` no-op branches of the canonical-file/directory lock context managers and `_DirLock` (the pre-B-46 `_flock` symbol was deleted at PR #1052), also covering the transitional legacy-sidecar protocol retirement once no pre-B-46 writers remain; (3) apply the same fix to `reconciler_pause_resume_substrate`'s `_workflow_lock` so both same-host lock primitives in this codebase reach genuine Windows parity together, not one at a time. No Windows CI exists today, so this is currently untested-by-CI on that platform regardless — low urgency until Windows becomes an active target, not blocking. **Council:** no for now — narrow implementation-discretion follow-on per C-IS-09 §9.4's own deferred-mechanism framing; council-eligible AT BUILD TIME only if the msvcrt-exclusive-only-lock tradeoff (weaker-than-POSIX shared-read guarantee on Windows) turns out non-obvious once actually scoped.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-27 (grounding pass, re-verified at HEAD `25b44127`) — STILL DORMANT; premise intact, scope WIDER than registered.** Premise re-verified verbatim: `cross_process_ledger_lock.py:80` still defines `_IS_WINDOWS = sys.platform == "win32"`; the three lock context managers still open with a bare `yield`/`return` no-op on Windows (`:289-292`, `:409-412`, `:491-494`); no `msvcrt` reference exists anywhere in the repo (0 hits across all non-worktree `.py` files); no Windows CI exists.
- **(a) A THIRD POSIX-only same-host lock primitive landed since this row was written.** `B-73` (PR #1120, 2026-07-26) added `protected_result_store.py`'s `_cross_process_lock()` (no-op guard at `:339-342`), whose docstring (`:334-337`) explicitly adopts this carve-out — by module name ("matching `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock`'s own Windows carve-out"), not by the `B-45` id. It is **exclusive-only** (`fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)` at `:347`), so it maps CLEANLY onto `msvcrt.locking` and does **NOT** add to this row's hard part (the `LOCK_SH`/`LOCK_EX` shared-reader/exclusive-writer split). Close-out step (3)'s "**both** same-host lock primitives" is therefore now understated — widen it to the **three** lock sites: `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock`, `harness_runtime.lifecycle.reconciler_pause_resume_substrate._workflow_lock` (`:271`, lock body `:290-300`, with its unguarded top-level `import fcntl` at `:106`), and `harness_runtime.lifecycle.protected_result_store._cross_process_lock`.
- **(b) B-45 has become the codebase's NAMED Windows-platform posture**, cited from 6+ production/test surfaces rather than remaining one module's local carve-out: `audit_writer.py:783` (no `O_NOFOLLOW`/uid validation on win32), `audit_writer.py:1074` (parent-dir fsync skipped), `audit_writer.py:1527` (`tenant_transaction`'s cross-process half a win32 no-op), `redaction_token_audit_map.py:112`, `shutdown.py:117`, `harness-is/tests/test_cross_process_ledger_lock.py:24`, plus three skip-reasons at `harness-runtime/tests/test_u_rt_134_audit_signing_fail_closed.py:1106/1205/1263`. Retiring this row is correspondingly a multi-surface doc sweep, not a one-module change.
- **(c) Blast-radius nuance.** `reconciler_pause_resume_substrate` is imported **module-level** by `bootstrap/factories/r_cxa_2_producer_loop_factory.py:36`, so on Windows that one bootstrap path is an **import-time `ModuleNotFoundError: fcntl` crash**, not merely an unavailable lock. (The package `__init__` remains unaffected, so this row's original "doesn't break basic import" statement is still true as written.) **Council: unchanged — no for now.**
- **UPDATE 2026-07-30 (post-#1159 gate-log LOW batch) — TWO corrections, neither restructuring the row above.**
- **(d) A FOURTH Windows no-op surface, inside an already-counted module.** Recounted by direct read at HEAD: **three** modules carry same-host POSIX `flock` locks — unchanged, and correctly recorded at (a) above — but they expose **five** distinct `_IS_WINDOWS` no-op branches, because the count above is of *modules* while what a Windows backend must cover is *surfaces*: `cross_process_write_lock` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/cross_process_ledger_lock.py:358`), `cross_process_replace_lock` (`:478`), `cross_process_read_lock` (`:560`), **`cross_process_scope_lock` (`:205`)**, and `protected_result_store._cross_process_lock` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/protected_result_store.py:339`). `cross_process_scope_lock` — added at **U-MEM-27** (PR #1159) for the memory store's per-root guarded-write hold — is the one this row has never named: it is neither a canonical-file lock nor a directory lock (it locks a *provisioned lock file* under a scope root), so the close-out's "canonical-file/directory lock context managers and `_DirLock`" phrasing does not reach it. It is **exclusive-only** (it builds on `_DirLock`'s `LOCK_EX` face), so it maps cleanly onto `msvcrt.locking` and does **not** add to the `LOCK_SH`/`LOCK_EX` hard part. `_DirLock` itself has no guard of its own (callers gate); `reconciler_pause_resume_substrate` still has none either — its unconditional `fcntl` import is an *import-time* failure, not a no-op, already recorded at (c).
- **(e) The "parity-only" framing is no longer whole — U-MEM-27 made the no-op withhold a CORRECTNESS guarantee.** "Windows sits at exact pre-B-40 parity" holds while the no-op only withholds an *improvement*. It no longer does. `CanonicalMemoryStore.write_record_guarded` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_store.py`) documents its compare-and-commit binding as **cross-process** in its own SCOPE paragraph, and that claim is witnessed against a genuine second OS process (`harness-is/tests/test_memory_store_cross_process_guard.py`) — the binding rests entirely on `cross_process_scope_lock`. On Windows that lock yields immediately, so the binding **silently degrades to in-process-only**: a second same-host process (the documented `harness run` alongside `harness daemon` case) can append superseding provenance between the precondition read and the write it authorizes, and a **promotion activation commits against provenance already superseded**. That is a wrong durable write, not a missing optimization. Still **not blocking** — no Windows CI, no active Windows target — but the failure mode to state at build time is now **data correctness**, not parity, and it raises the floor on how carefully the Windows backend's guarantees must be documented.
- **Cross-ref (2026-07-30).** Sibling lock-substrate row **B-93** (no deadline on any of the blocking-`flock` acquisition sites) registers against the *same* primitive set and is B-45's natural co-rider — see B-93 for the grounded eight-site enumeration.

### B-41 · `_classify_provider_exception` misroutes AuthenticationError to TRANSIENT_RETRY *(surfaced at B-38 grounding, 2026-07-15, advisor()-flagged)*
- **What it is.** `_classify_provider_exception` (`retry_breaker_fallback.py:265-286`) returned `ValidatorRetryExitClass.TRANSIENT_RETRY` for any exception other than `LLMDispatchProviderUnreachableError`/`LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` — including an SDK `AuthenticationError` (`anthropic`/`openai` `.status_code` 401/403) — running the full retry staircase before eventually exhausting, contradicting the function's own docstring's claim that "AUTH ... are fail-fast."
- **Close-out.** Verified against `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` v1.99 (canonical HEAD) directly, not from memory: §14.5's C-RT-15 fail-class table states `RT-FAIL-PROVIDER-AUTH` (the raw SDK auth error, 401/403 — NOT a harness wrapper type) is "Raise unmodified"; §14.6 D2's fork-resolution text and its own narrative both independently say AUTH is fail-fast. Three independent spec statements plus the function's own docstring all agreed — this was a straightforward conformance fix, not an open design decision, so no fork was needed. Added a `status_code in (401, 403)` duck-typed guard to `_classify_provider_exception`, placed after the wrapper-type check and before the `TRANSIENT_RETRY` catch-all — deliberately NOT reusing `_classify_breaker_cause` so the two classifiers' control-flow contracts stay independent. This also closes `B-38`'s noted divergence as a side effect (both classifiers now agree on AUTH).
- **Test-witness gap closed.** Added a fail-fast witness test — the naive assertion "exactly 2 total dispatch calls" does NOT discriminate fail-fast (candidate 0 abandoned after 1 call, candidate 1 gets the 2nd) from a buggy retry-then-succeed on candidate 0 (also totals 2 calls); the real witness checks WHICH candidate the 2nd call actually reached. Added a regression-guard test proving 429/5xx still retry (guards against an overly-broad `status_code is not None` mutation, which would wrongly fail-fast rate-limits and server errors). Both mutation-probed against real production-code reversions — the fail-fast test genuinely fails when the guard is reverted, and the regression-guard test genuinely fails when the guard is broadened past 401/403.

### B-42 · `breaker.cause`'s `FIVE_XX_STREAK` is tagged by the terminal exception only, not a verified streak *(surfaced at B-38's own PR, out-of-family Codex round 1, 2026-07-15)*
- **What it is.** `_classify_breaker_cause` tags `FIVE_XX_STREAK` from a single last-attempt exception's `.status_code >= 500` — but `BreakerStateMachine` (`retry_breaker.py`) tracks only an aggregate `fail_count`, not a per-cause failure history. A breaker with `fail_threshold > 1` that trips after a mixed sequence (e.g. a rate-limit failure, then an auth failure, then a terminal 5xx) still reports `FIVE_XX_STREAK` — the name implies N *consecutive* 5xx responses, but only the terminal exception is ever inspected.
- **Close-out.** CLOSED 2026-07-15. Grounded directly against `Spec_Operational_Discipline_v1_32.md` §7.1 (only requires "the classified trip-cause, when known" — no streak-verification mandate) and `BreakerStateMachine`'s own source (only an aggregate `fail_count`, no per-cause history); confirmed no live consumer reads `harness.breaker.cause` today. Chose option (b) from this row's own close-out options: a documentation-only clarification, not new per-cause tracking infrastructure (which would be speculative ahead of any consumer — the same discipline the B-19 redundancy analysis already established). Added a clarifying paragraph to `BreakerCause`'s docstring in `harness_breaker_schema.py` stating `FIVE_XX_STREAK` is tagged from a single terminal 5xx exception, not a verified N-consecutive streak — mirroring the equivalent clarification `_classify_breaker_cause`'s own docstring in `retry_breaker_fallback.py` already carried since `B-38`'s original landing. The spec-committed enum value (`5xx_streak`) is UNCHANGED; only the docstring's description of its derivation is now precise. No behavior/test change; ruff clean; OD breaker test suite (30 tests) still green.
- **Adjacent finding split out, not folded in.** OD spec v1.32 §7.1's own prose still describes `harness.breaker.cause` as "vacuous-today ... absent from every emitted event," which is now stale since `B-38` populated it for 3 of 4 values. Fixing spec prose is a design-substrate edit requiring design-phase posture, not a Phase-7 code fix — registered separately as `B-43` rather than bundled into this row's code-only PR.

### B-43 · OD spec v1.32 §7.1 prose still describes `harness.breaker.cause` as vacuous-today, stale since B-38 *(surfaced at B-42 grounding, 2026-07-15, advisor()-flagged)*
- **CLOSED 2026-07-18 (PR #1050)** — in-place correction of the v1.32 §7.1 `cause` row + change-note historical annotation + `harness-od/CLAUDE.md` mirror, clearance marker `spec-operational-discipline-v1-32-b43-correction-cleared-2026-07-18.md` (ADR-D7 in-place precedent; no contract change).
- **What it is.** `Spec_Operational_Discipline_v1_32.md` §7.1's `harness.breaker.cause` row states: "Vacuous-today, honestly documented ... the attribute is present in the schema (forward-compatible typed slot) and absent from every emitted event until a follow-on classifier arc supplies real signal." `B-38` (PR #1020) landed exactly that follow-on classifier — `_classify_breaker_cause` now populates `RATE_LIMIT`/`AUTH_FAILURE`/`FIVE_XX_STREAK` at all 3 real `record_failure()` call sites and the attribute reaches the real span. The spec prose was never updated to reflect this and is now factually wrong about current runtime behavior for 3 of the 4 enum values (`CAPABILITY_SHORTFALL` remains genuinely vacuous, per `B-38`'s own grounding).
- **Current state.** FIXED at PR #1050 (see the CLOSED line above): the design-phase posture + clearance-marker requirements this row anticipated were satisfied by the correction PR itself; the historical constraint text below is preserved as the registration-era record.
- **Close-out steps (historical — superseded by the in-place correction; the v1.33-delta route was NOT taken because v1.33 was already consumed by the SigningBackend seam and v1.34 is reserved by the pending B-51/B-52/B-54 amendment).** The original plan: a future design-phase session should author a narrow ADDITIVE prose-only delta correcting the §7.1 `cause` row's "vacuous-today" language to reflect `B-38`'s landed classifier, then file the matching clearance marker. The same stale-vacuous framing likely also appears at `harness-od/CLAUDE.md` §1.2 (spec-pointer summary) and §4.1 (graduation-note context) — both mode-agnostic, not design-substrate, so those ARE Phase-7-editable, but were deliberately left untouched here to keep this a pure registration pass; the design-phase pass on the spec itself should sweep all copies at once rather than fixing them piecemeal across postures. **Council: no** — this is a narrow prose-accuracy delta over an already-cleared spec version (v1.32 stays canonical), not a contract change.

Subagent reports supplied close-out *mechanism* detail only — three subagent CXA *status* claims were rejected (CXA-3 "N/A" was a misread; CXA-4 "fully-wired" contradicted R-700 PARTIAL; an "RT-35 PR #52 awaiting merge" claim was stale).
- **Surface IV centerpiece empirically verified this session:** `routing_core_surface.infer()` raises `NotImplementedError` (`:83`/`:97`); `layered_routing_strategy.route()` has zero non-test callers; **but** `retry_breaker_fallback.py` (C-RT-16) *does* implement fallback-chain advancement (the subagent's "fallback not implemented / re-raises" claim was corrected). Accurate framing: provider construction + failure-time fallback wired; capability-aware routing-*selection* stubbed; multi-provider operation **unexercised at MVP**.
