- Grounded in: ADR-F1/F4/F5 + ADR-D2/D3/D5/D6; CP spec v1.30 `C-CP-01..04`; OD spec v1.27 `C-OD-10/13`; AS spec `C-AS-05/13/15`; runtime spec v1.41 `C-RT-16/22/29` + §14.9.8 + §14.C/D; CXA v2.18 §2.3.x; production source at `harness-{cp,runtime,od,as}/src/`.

### B-66 · B-60 fence-consult order at admission discrimination has no single-site witness *(registered at the B-60 close, merge-gate test-witness lens, 2026-07-21)*

- **What it is.** The round-10 consult-before-admission-discrimination order and the `_cancel_worker` pre-marker consult have no single-site behavioral discriminators — outcomes are pinned jointly with the B-61 drain fence and the post-marker sibling respectively (lens-verified non-vacuous: disabling BOTH layers fails the witness).
- **Posture.** Strictly-earlier semantics; in-code order comments at all four sites are the guard; an implementation-order spy would be a change-detector, not a witness — build only if a regression occurs. Accepted at merge-gate round-2 APPROVE.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-27 (grounding pass, re-verified at HEAD `25b44127`) — STILL DORMANT; every load-bearing claim holds.** The round-10 consult-before-admission-discrimination order is intact at BOTH proceed sites: `_consult_dispatch_fence()` at `workflow_driver.py:9078` precedes the `isinstance(_admission, SubAgentDispatchCapacityError)` discrimination at `:9083` in `_proceed_branch`, and `:13540` precedes `:13545` in `_proceed_worker`. The B-61 per-append drain fence is live (`:2003-2027` — each physical append guarded by its own `token.effect_entry()`). All **23** `test_b60_*` witnesses are unchanged and green across their 7 carrier test modules.
- **Explicitly recorded to prevent a future false-positive.** B-80's `compute_effect_fence_tree_wide_abort_present` (`workflow_driver.py:3009-3058`, landed PR #1131) is a **RESUME-time `EffectFenceResolution.ABORT`** mechanism, unrelated to B-60/B-66's **dispatch-time cooperative cancel token** — two mechanisms sharing the word "fence", with no interaction. Verified structurally rather than by reading alone: the combined B-79/B-80 source diff (`f4696aa0~1..25b44127` over `harness-*/src`; 548 added / 6 removed lines) contains **ZERO** hits on `B-60`, `B-66`, `B-61`, `_consult_dispatch_fence`, `_DISPATCH_CANCEL_TOKEN_VAR`, `effect_entry`, `_cancel_branch`, `_cancel_worker`, `wait_ack`, or `drain_branch_buffers`.
- **Minor row imprecision corrected in passing.** Claim (2) names only `_cancel_worker`'s pre-marker consult, but the "four sites" the posture cites also include `_cancel_branch`'s structurally-identical pre-marker consult at `:9532` (its post-marker sibling is `:9547`; `_cancel_worker`'s pair is `:13945`/`:13960`). The four order-bearing sites are therefore `_proceed_branch:9078`, `_proceed_worker:13540`, `_cancel_branch:9532`, `_cancel_worker:13945`. **Posture unchanged — accepted residual. Council: no.**

### B-67 · Protected result store's TTL GC sweep has no shutdown-time hook — bootstrap half only *(registered at the B-65-A impl arc, out-of-family Codex round 1 [P1], 2026-07-22; CLOSED at round 4 correction, same date)*

- **What it is.** CORRECTED at out-of-family Codex round 4: this row's own premise ("this runtime has no graceful-teardown chain to hook") was FALSE. `shutdown(ctx)` at `harness_runtime/shutdown.py` IS that chain — it is invoked unconditionally in `run()`'s/`resume()`'s `finally` block regardless of workflow outcome (verified by direct read of `api.py` lines ~671/941). The row's earlier reasoning conflated this with the SEPARATE signal-handler drain path (`admin/shutdown_cli.py`, which only sets `drained_flag`) — that path is real but immaterial here: `shutdown()`'s own step 1 sets `drained_flag` too, so whether a signal pre-emptively flips it or not, `shutdown()` still runs once `run()`/`resume()` unwinds.
- **Fix landed.** A new step 5b in `shutdown()`: a bounded, best-effort `ctx.protected_result_store.gc_sweep()` off the default executor via `asyncio.to_thread` — safe here specifically because `shutdown()` only runs AFTER `execute_workflow` has already returned, so no sync driver can be occupying the default pool concurrently (unlike the request-path raise sites this same round also fixed to avoid that exact deadlock class). The `stage_4_od.py` comment carrying the false premise was corrected in the same commit. Both halves are now wired: bootstrap reaps a PRIOR process's abandoned entries; shutdown reaps THIS process's own before exit. Residual: only a hard kill (SIGKILL/segfault) that bypasses Python's `finally` entirely still relies on the next process's bootstrap sweep — the same case the bootstrap half was always designed for.
- **Council: no** — corrective wiring fix once the false premise was caught by review, not a design fork.

### B-68 · Protected result store's opportunistic GC can race a concurrent in-flight publication under a short TTL *(registered at the B-65-A impl arc, out-of-family Codex round 11 [P2], 2026-07-22; CLOSED 2026-07-25 -- in-process synchronization scope; cross-process residual split to B-73)*

- **What it is.** `write_once()` captures `_StoredEnvelope.written_at` via `time.time()` BEFORE serialization/encryption/`_publish_atomic`'s fsync+`os.link` commit — a real, non-instantaneous gap. A CONCURRENT sweep (a different in-process opportunistic call from another request, or the bootstrap/shutdown-half sweep from a co-resident process sharing the same store directory) that runs during that gap decrypts the envelope, computes `current_time - written_at`, and — if the deployment-configured `ttl_seconds` (operator-settable, `gt=0.0`, no floor) is shorter than the write's own encrypt+fsync latency — can judge the just-published entry already expired and unlink it before `write_once()` even returns. The caller then holds a "live-looking" `composite_key` whose first `read()` raises an unhandled `FileNotFoundError` instead of the typed `ProtectedStoreCrossTenantError`/`ProtectedStoreTamperError` refusals the store otherwise guarantees.
- **Fix landed (2026-07-25, branch `b68-protected-result-store-gc-race-mtime-authority`, 5 rounds of out-of-family Codex review).** (1) `gc_sweep()`'s age authority switched from the pre-publish `_StoredEnvelope.written_at` field to the committed entry file's own filesystem mtime, explicitly refreshed via `os.utime` + its own fsync AFTER the durable `os.link` commit — closing the original timing gap. (2) A process-wide `threading.Lock` registry keyed by the store root's RESOLVED path (`_root_locks`, module-level, not per-instance) now serializes `_publish_atomic`'s entire publish critical section against `gc_sweep`'s entire scan — closing same-process races, including a co-resident concurrent-INSTANCE race (e.g. across bootstrap/resume cycles) the original registration did not anticipate; the lock key uses the root's filesystem identity (`(st_dev, st_ino)`, falling back to the resolved path only before the directory exists) so filesystem-equivalent path aliases (e.g. a `..`-containing spelling, or a case-folded spelling on a case-insensitive volume) share the same lock, and the temp-file's full lifetime (creation through the post-commit mtime-refresh fsync) is inside the lock so a sweep cannot reclaim an in-flight temp file mid-publish. (3) `read()` degrades a missing entry to a typed `ProtectedStoreEntryNotFoundError(FileNotFoundError)` — the optional interim hardening this row's close_out named — instead of a raw `FileNotFoundError`. Each fix is witnessed by a deterministic (non-flaky, mechanism-level) mutation-probed test: reverting the fix makes the corresponding test fail; restoring it passes. 10 new tests added across all rounds; full `harness-runtime` + `harness-cp` suites green.
- **Frequency correction (merge-gate round-1 concurrency lens, PR #1078, 2026-07-22).** Independently confirmed the race is real and reachable via genuine multi-threading, and sharpened this writeup's own framing: `shutdown()` step 5b fires `gc_sweep()` UNCONDITIONALLY once per `run()`/`resume()` call, with no interval throttle — unlike the in-write opportunistic trigger's `_OPPORTUNISTIC_GC_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 300.0` gate. On a daemon serving many concurrent short-lived workflow runs against the SAME shared store instance, concurrent-sweep frequency is materially higher than a reading based only on the 300s opportunistic throttle would suggest — the fix above closes the race regardless of frequency for the same-process case.
- **Residual split out (round 5 convergence check).** Out-of-family Codex round 5 confirmed the in-process fix is sound but restated the SAME cross-PROCESS gap this row's fix comments always disclosed ("a genuinely separate OS process sharing the store directory remains an open residual, registered, not silently assumed closed") — two co-resident daemon PROCESSES each hold their own independent `_root_locks` registry, so a `threading.Lock` alone cannot exclude them. Not a defect introduced by this fix; split to **B-73** as its own registered row rather than expanding this arc's scope, per the terminal register discipline (B-64/B-67 precedents).
- **Council: no** — narrow TTL-dependent race with a documented reachability precondition; the in-process fix was a known primitive (a resolved-path-keyed lock), not a judgment call.

### B-69 · `resume_handle` crash-recovery resume path cannot address individual paused children for multi-child HITL delivery *(registered at the B-39 spec-leg PR #1092, out-of-family Codex round 3 (branch-vs-main), 2026-07-23; council convened + spec leg applied 2026-07-30)*

- **STATUS 2026-07-31 — Status flipped `open` → `closed`. IMPL LEG LANDED (PR #1166).** U-RT-148 (Runtime plan v2.55) + the U-CP-64 amendment (CP plan v2.47) + NEW **U-OD-57** (OD plan **v2.31** — the delta OD spec v1.36 §30.5.2 held for the impl leg; carrier **option (b), a sibling payload type**, selected). All **16** U-RT-148 ACs and all **10** U-CP-64 ACs met **by EXECUTION**; the A′ ordering honored at **commit** granularity (the fence lands before the accessor is reachable at any commit); W1 / W2 / W2′ / W3 / W4 all landed, with **W3 mutation-probed green**. **Both conjuncts of the closure criterion below are therefore satisfied.** Residuals surfaced and **REGISTERED rather than absorbed**: `B-100` (the empty-key carve-out does not reach the LINEAR/orchestrator fence carriers) and `B-101` (accessor-derived provenance is lost through base-typed Pydantic serialization). `pr: '#pending'` is reconciled at the next register touch post-merge.
- **STATUS 2026-07-30 — `registered_finding` → `open`. SPEC LEG APPLIED.** Council record `.harness/council-b69-pause-state-accessor-2026-07-30.md` (C10 + C11 + C9, converged, `CLEAR-TO-COMMIT`); **operator ratified OPTION A′ — CO-REQUISITE, SEQUENCED — 2026-07-30** (record §14). Landed: Runtime spec v1.106→**v1.107** (NEW §14.14.9 accessor + closed four-variant projection DTO + five-member cause attribution + declared postures; NEW §30 refusal-only staleness precondition + cause attribution on the EXISTING `RT-FAIL-RESUME-HANDLE-UNKNOWN`, **no new peer class**; the `:3274` scope-limit sentence **discharged** to a cross-reference; NEW §14.14.8 **append-only/never-truncated** substrate invariant); CP spec v1.111→**v1.112** (REQUIRED non-downgradable `ResumeContext` response-provenance carrier; NEW public projection-returning surface over the two private resume-tree walks; two registered scope-limit lifts); OD spec v1.35→**v1.36** (NEW §C-OD-30.5 emission home); CXA v2.22→**v2.23** (classification-only: **NO new row**, aggregate FROZEN at 111, payload widening recorded); plans Runtime **v2.55** (NEW U-RT-148) + CP **v2.47** (U-CP-64 amended).
- **Closure criterion — X-AL-2 conjunctive form, verbatim.** ***B-69 closes iff (accessor landed) ∧ (precondition landed ∧ exercised by the W3 mutation-probe); partial is non-closure.*** Spec surfaces and impl units MAY land in separate merges within the arc, subject to one ordering constraint: **the staleness precondition lands first or simultaneously — never after.** No interval may exist in which an unfenced accessor is reachable on `main`.
- **Why the accessor may not ship alone.** `resume()` re-derives the **snapshot** from the journal but never re-derives the operator's **response map** — two different objects, only one is re-derived — and CP spec v1.106 §1.2 property 4's sole-member carve-out **permits** a uniform `hitl_response` to resolve the single unaddressed gate-owning branch of the live cycle. A superseded read can therefore land a response on a branch the operator never saw, reachable through the **1→1 single-location happy path**.
- **Routed OUT, not absorbed:** `B-97` (pause-journal tenant binding + unserialized `_append`), `B-98` (gate description absent from durable state), `B-99` (the `summary_text` reopening condition). Folded INTO the impl arc: the Class 3 cite-hygiene fix for **three** stale docstrings in `pause_resume_protocol_types.py`.
- **What it is.** `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §0's `ResumeContext.hitl_responses` (keyed by a paused child's own `run_id`, read off `PausedChildBranchResumeState.child_snapshot.run_id`) requires the caller to already possess a prior `RunResult.pause_snapshot` object — true on `api.resume()`'s `pause_snapshot`-supplied resume mode. `api.resume()`'s OTHER supported mode, `resume_handle` (crash-recovery: the caller supplies only a `workflow_id`; the harness reads the latest durably-journaled snapshot itself from its own `JournalWorkflowPauseStore`, `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` §14.14.8), gives the caller NO prior snapshot to read a paused child's `run_id` from BEFORE `resume_context` must be constructed and passed to the SAME `resume()` call — there is no intermediate step where the caller can inspect which children are paused and by what `run_id` before committing to a `resume_context` value.
- **⚠️ HISTORICAL — the three bullets below (`Current state`, `Close-out steps`, `Council: TBD`) describe the PRE-2026-07-30 state and are SUPERSEDED by the STATUS bullet at the top of this section. They are preserved verbatim as provenance, NOT as operative instructions.** The accessor has been **designed and specified**; the council **has run and converged**; the exposure tradeoff they anticipate as the likely operator question **was found not to be one** (the accessor grants a strict subset of the authority `resume()` already grants the identical caller). **Do not re-open the design or re-convene the council on the strength of these bullets** — the live next step is the impl arc (U-RT-148 + the U-CP-64 amendment).
- **Current state** *(historical, pre-2026-07-30)*. Registered, not built (narrowed out of the B-39 spec leg's scope per advisor()-recommended narrowing, round 3 correction — solving this would require designing a new read-accessor, a materially bigger surface than the spec leg's addressing-scheme fix). `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §0 states this as an explicit scope limit: `hitl_responses` multi-child addressing works only on the `pause_snapshot`-supplied resume path; a `resume_handle` caller is limited to the single-paused-child case (the uniform `hitl_response` field only, valid per CP spec v1.106 §1.2 property 4 when exactly one child is paused this cycle). `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md`'s `resume()` invariants (the "Resume-context one-shot delivery" bullet) carry the matching Runtime-owned cross-reference. **GENERALIZED 2026-07-24** (out-of-family Codex, `B-70` spec-leg round 5): `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_107.md` §1's `resume_handle` scope-limit note for effect-fence pauses cites this row as already covering the gap, but this row as previously written named only HITL child `run_id`s, not effect-fence `idempotency_key`s — the accessor's design scope is widened here (durably-journaled `run_id`s AND `idempotency_key`s) so that citation is accurate rather than merely asserted.
- **Close-out steps** *(historical, pre-2026-07-30 — DISCHARGED by the spec leg; the live close-out is the X-AL-2 conjunctive criterion in the STATUS bullet above)*. Design a durable-pause-state read accessor (Runtime-owned, since it reads `JournalWorkflowPauseStore`, §14.14.8) that a `resume_handle` caller can invoke BEFORE constructing `resume_context`, to learn the currently-paused children's `run_id`s (and, ideally, enough context per child — e.g. the gate description/workflow position — to compose a sensible operator response) for the SAME `workflow_id` the caller intends to resume. This is a genuine new API surface, not a mechanical patch — likely council-eligible if the tradeoff between exposing partial durable state pre-resume vs. keeping `resume()` a single atomic call is non-obvious once scoped. **Council: TBD at build time** — narrow implementation-discretion follow-on in scope shape, but the read-accessor's own API design (a new function? an optional `resume()` pre-flight mode? a field on `RunResult`?) is a real design choice worth a dyadic council pass if it turns out non-obvious.

### B-70 · `effect_fence_resolution_for`'s uniform-fallback semantics share B-39's cross-branch-misapplication hazard, unfixed in the already-shipped mechanism *(registered at the B-39 spec-leg PR #1092, out-of-family Codex round 3 (branch-vs-main), 2026-07-23; GROUNDED + fork filed 2026-07-24; SPEC LEG LANDED 2026-07-24 — CP spec v1.107 + plan v2.43; IMPL LEG LANDED 2026-07-25 — PR #1101, CLOSED)*

- **What it is.** `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §1.2 property 4 (round-3 correction) closes a safety gap in the NEW `ResumeContext.hitl_responses`/`hitl_response_for` mechanism: when 2+ children are concurrently paused, an operator supplying only the uniform `hitl_response` field (no keyed map) must NOT have that response silently applied to every unaddressed child — the resolver must instead leave unaddressed children paused/INERT. The SHIPPED, ALREADY-CLEARED sibling mechanism this pattern was modeled on, `ResumeContext.effect_fence_resolutions`/`effect_fence_resolution_for` (§26.8.1, unamended by the B-39 arc), has the IDENTICAL uniform-fallback shape and the IDENTICAL gap: with 2+ children concurrently effect-fence-paused, a uniform `effect_fence_resolution` (no keyed map) resolves EVERY unaddressed child to the SAME SKIP_AS_FIRED/RE_FIRE/ABORT judgment, which can be wrong for some of them (e.g. one child's non-idempotent effect genuinely fired and needs SKIP_AS_FIRED while a sibling's did not and needs RE_FIRE).
- **Current state.** GROUNDED 2026-07-24 (autonomous-loop grounding pass, `.harness/class_2_fork_b70_effect_fence_resolution_uniform_fallback.md`) — direct read of `workflow_driver.py` confirms the mechanism (the singular `resume_context.effect_fence_resolution` is consulted independently at every `execute_workflow` recursion depth whose own `resume_snapshot.effect_fence_resume` is set, with no cross-depth uniqueness check) and resolves the two open grounding questions the register originally deferred: (1) NO container/gate-owning split is needed (unlike HITL) — `EffectFencePausedBranchResumeState`'s own docstring + `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §2 (REMOVED) both confirm effect-fence carriers never cross a recursion boundary the way HITL child-pause delivery does (a nested descendant's own effect-fence pause propagates via the generic `PausedChildBranchResumeState`/`SubAgentChildPausedError` path, never via `EffectFencePausedBranchResumeState`, so every captured fence-pause entry is inherently gate-owning and already `idempotency_key`-unique across depth); (2) the fix DOES need its own CP spec property (a §26.8.1 companion mirroring §1.2 property 4's safety+liveness shape, simpler — no property-5 equivalent) before code, since restricting the auto-fallback is an observable CONTRACT change to a cleared mechanism, not pure impl-to-spec. **SPEC LEG LANDED 2026-07-24** (operator ratified "open the spec-leg now" via `AskUserQuestion`) — `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_107.md` NEW §1 (the safety+liveness invariant, sharpened across 5 out-of-family review rounds from the fork's §3 draft — round 1 added the previously-omitted ORCHESTRATOR carrier; round 2 fixed a gap where the LINEAR consume site never calls `effect_fence_resolution_for` at all, but its own fix over-corrected; round 3 found round 2's fix would permanently livelock any 2+-simultaneous-LINEAR-pause resume, corrected to genuine map-addressability via the LINEAR carrier's already-existing `idempotency_key` field; round 4 added a `resume_handle` scope-limit note mirroring `B-69`; no property-5 equivalent still holds per the grounding above) + `Implementation_Plan_Control_Plane_v2_43.md` (ONE deferred coverage-matrix row, zero unit amendment — mirrors how v2.42 §5 deferred the parallel `hitl_responses` property 4/5 invariants) + 2 clearance markers. Status flipped `registered_finding` → `design_substrate_gated` → `open` (spec-leg gate cleared; impl leg — the resolver's tree-walk enumeration of the unaddressed effect-fence-pause set, wired at the LINEAR + ORCHESTRATOR + two fan-out consume sites — is the next buildable slice, NOT yet opened, per the B-33/B-39/B-59 precedent). `council: no` unchanged (mechanical parity, no cross-domain tension). **IMPL LEG LANDED 2026-07-25** (PR #1101) — `compute_effect_fence_uniform_fallback_eligible_key`/`_collect_effect_fence_idempotency_keys` (a tree-walk across all three carriers) + `_resolve_effect_fence_gated` (a genuine caller of the existing `effect_fence_resolution_for`, per a merge-gate spec-conformance BLOCK caught pre-merge — an earlier draft reimplemented its logic inline) wired at the LINEAR site (round-3-corrected to become map-addressable), the ORCHESTRATOR's own dispatch, and both fan-out consume sites; `effect_fence_uniform_fallback_eligible_key` threaded end-to-end `mcp_server.py` → `execute_workflow` → `StepExecutionContext` → `sub_agent_dispatch.py` → `child_workflow_runner.py`, mirroring the existing `hitl_uniform_fallback_eligible_run_id` wiring. 2 merge-gate rounds (round 1 BLOCK on spec-conformance + test-witness, both fixed; round 2 all-approve); 1767 harness-cp + 3046 harness-runtime tests green. **Status flipped `open` → `closed`.**
- **Close-out steps (round-7-corrected — do NOT copy the round-3-vintage phrasing below verbatim; see the correction note).** Extend `effect_fence_resolution_for`'s CONSUMPTION contract (not necessarily the field/method signature itself) with the SAME KIND of multi-branch fallback-safety invariant B-39's `hitl_response_for` now carries at its FINAL, round-7-corrected shape (`Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §1.2 property 4) — a deterministic SAFETY rule (uniform fallback resolves a branch only when it is the sole unaddressed member of the relevant set this cycle) stated as an invariant on the OUTCOME, not a prescribed counting mechanism. **Correction note:** an earlier draft of this row said "the resolver must count concurrently-effect-fence-paused children first and apply the uniform fallback only when exactly one is paused" without B-39's own gate-owning-vs-transitively-paused-container distinction (property 5) — B-39 itself REJECTED an unscoped version of that exact count-based framing at its own round 4 (it strands container branches). Before implementing this row's fix: FIRST determine whether effect-fence pauses have an analogous transitively-paused-container concept under `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` recursion (unresolved as of this registration — `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §2's REMOVED-section text claims "the effect-fence mechanism does not cross a recursion boundary the way HITL child-pause delivery does," but this claim was never re-verified against the ACTUAL `EffectFencePausedBranchResumeState`/`effect_fence_paused_branches` carrier code — grep `harness-cp/src/harness_cp/pause_resume_protocol_types.py` before assuming either way). If containers exist for effect-fence too, mirror B-39's gate-owning/property-5 split exactly; if not, the plain count-based rule (no container exclusion needed) is safe as originally drafted. Requires a small design-substrate amendment to CP spec §26.8.1 (a Class 2/3 fork, likely — mechanical parity fix once the container question is resolved) plus updating the callers of `effect_fence_resolution_for` (the SAME `workflow_driver.py` peek-reader sites B-39's own `resume_context_holder` retirement touches — see the B-39 impl leg's own scope-discovery pass) to enforce the gate. **Council: no** — parity fix mirroring an already-ratified pattern (B-39's own property 4), not a fresh open design question.

### B-71 · Operator-facing HITL escalation requests carry no run-instance-distinct correlation identifier, making `hitl_responses` addressing impossible for peer branches sharing a `child_workflow_id` *(registered at the B-39 spec-leg PR #1092, out-of-family Codex round 6 (branch-vs-main), 2026-07-23)*

- **What it is.** `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §0's `ResumeContext.hitl_responses` (keyed by a paused child's own `run_id`) lets a caller who already KNOWS a paused child's `run_id` construct a correctly-keyed response — but it does not solve how the caller LEARNS which `run_id` a given HITL escalation request corresponds to. The existing operator-facing request types this arc does not touch — `HITLEscalationBrief` (C-CP-28 §25.2, `harness_cp/validator_framework_types.py`: `parent_step_id`, `parent_action_id`, `fail_class`, `fail_detail_hash`, `escalation_reason`, `proposed_response_palette` — no `run_id` field) and the webhook delivery payload the HITL gate composer sends — carry `parent_action_id` and the `hitl:`-prefixed `compose_hitl_action_id(parent_action_id, placement_position)` (`harness-runtime/lifecycle/hitl_gate_composer.py:425-437`: `f"hitl:{parent_action_id}:{placement_position.value}"`), both derived from the SAME workflow_id-scoped, non-run-instance identifier this arc's own `branch_path` keying-defect note already found collides across repeated same-`child_workflow_id` dispatch. Empirically confirmed (direct read of both files, 2026-07-23): neither type carries a `run_id`. Concretely — when two peer branches dispatch the SAME `child_workflow_id` and BOTH hit the identical internal HITL gate, their two escalation requests/webhook payloads are byte-identical on every field an operator or webhook consumer can observe, so there is structurally no way to tell them apart — a human or automated consumer cannot know which `child_run_id` to key a `hitl_responses` entry under for either request.
- **Current state.** Registered, not built — narrowed out of the B-39 spec leg's scope (advisor()-pattern narrowing, matching `B-69`'s precedent): `HITLEscalationBrief`/the webhook payload are an EXISTING C-CP-28 contract this delta does not touch, and fixing them is a genuinely separate carrier/design surface from this delta's `ResumeContext` addressing fix. `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §0 states this as an explicit scope limit.
- **Close-out steps.** Amend `HITLEscalationBrief` (and the equivalent webhook payload type, if distinct) to carry a run-instance-distinct correlation identifier — most naturally the paused child's own `run_id` (the SAME value `hitl_responses` already keys by), threaded through at HITL-gate composition time (the composer already has access to the executing step's `run_id` at dispatch). This is a C-CP-28 contract amendment (NEW field, additive/byte-compat if optional) — likely mechanical once scoped, but touches an operator-facing/webhook-external payload shape, so any downstream webhook consumer contract implications should be checked before landing. **Council: TBD at build time** — likely narrow (adding a field mirroring an established pattern), but the operator-facing/webhook-external surface warrants a quick check before assuming so.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-27 (roadmap-continue, `advisor()`-directed premise falsification — DOC-ONLY, no spec/plan change landed, prior draft reverted pre-merge):** the "likely mechanical" framing above is wrong. A `run_id`-threading fix was authored across both CP and Runtime axes (`StepExecutionContext`/`HITLEscalationBrief` gain `run_id`; the webhook idempotency key and payload incorporate it) and taken through 5 rounds of out-of-family `just codex-review-uncommitted`, each catching a genuine defect (Optional-vs-required breakage against a 6th out-of-driver `StepExecutionContext(...)` constructor at `r_cxa_2_producer_loop_factory.py`; a missing `None`-guard that would silently recreate the exact collision for any context lacking `run_id`; a new exception not classified terminal by the driver's `RT-FAIL-HITL-GATE-` prefix nor added to `retry_breaker_fallback.py`'s allowlist; wrong unit ownership, U-RT-58 vs U-RT-94). **Round 5 found the fix's premise itself is false:** `RuntimeHITLGateComposer` fires `_escalate_to_secondary_channel` INSIDE the PARENT's dispatch of a `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step, BEFORE `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher` mints any child `run_id` — `execute_workflow`'s `run_id` parameter is per-PARENT-RUN, not per-branch, so every peer branch of one fan-out shares the identical value. A `...:{run_id}` idempotency-key/payload suffix therefore does NOT disambiguate B-71's actual collision — it only disambiguates across distinct parent runs, not peers within one. `advisor()` confirmed this against the full transcript and directed a full revert rather than a 6th patch: 5 consecutive rounds, each fixing a different layer of the same wrong approach, is the non-convergence signal (mirrors `[[reviewer-oscillation-register-and-hold]]`), not an invitation to keep iterating. **This is the SAME pre-dispatch-identity problem `B-72` CP spec v1.108 property 6 already owns** — a `branch_index`-derived tree-wide-unique INTERNAL identity, deliberately NOT `hitl_responses`/`run_id`-addressable, per v1.108 §1.1(b): "no `child_run_id` exists to key by." The real fix needs a branch-distinct identity co-designed with B-72's mechanism, not a mechanical field-add — and it touches an operator-facing/webhook-EXTERNAL payload, which is itself a design decision, not impl discretion. **Third falsified premise on this row** (registration-time "run_id already accessible at the composer"; round-1 draft's "required field is safe"; round-5's "run_id disambiguates peers") — the pattern across all three is the same: assuming a value threaded at ONE call-graph layer is available/meaningful at a DIFFERENT layer without tracing the actual dispatch path first. **Council flips from "TBD, likely narrow" to genuinely OPEN** — needs a real convening (C1 orchestration + C9/C10 blast-radius/action-safety) co-designed with B-72 property 6's mechanism before the next build attempt. Register status stays `registered_finding` (not `open` — no Claude-closeable slice was actually landed this pass; all drafted artifacts reverted, `git status` confirmed clean of this arc's files before this update was written).

### B-72 · A `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step's own `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` HITL gate can re-fire on every resume-forward of an already-dispatched, deeper-paused child, discarding the child's `paused_child_branches` cursor *(registered at the B-39 impl-leg Slice B branch `b39-impl-leg-slice-b-hitl-delivery-mechanism`, out-of-family Codex round 4 (branch-vs-main), 2026-07-24)*

- **What it is.** `RuntimeHITLGateComposer.dispatch()`'s Step 0 one-shot short-circuit (`hitl_gate_composer.py` ~1652-1730) only bypasses the gate when `step_context.hitl_delivery_holder.consume_and_clear()` is non-`None`. This session's B-39 Slice B delivery mechanism populates `hitl_delivery_holder` ONLY at the linear `resume_at` reconstruction site in `workflow_driver.py` — it is never threaded to fan-out branch composition (`_execute_parallelization` / `_execute_orchestrator_workers`, which build each branch's `StepExecutionContext` via `compose_branch_child_context` and only pass `resume_context`/`child_resume_snapshot` through, never `hitl_delivery_holder`). So when a fan-out branch's own `step_kind` is `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` AND that step carries its own `HITLPlacement(position=SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY)` declaration, EVERY re-dispatch of that branch (including a pure resume-forward whose sole purpose is carrying `child_resume_snapshot` down to an already-paused descendant) runs Step 0 → finds `hitl_delivery_holder is None` → falls through to full gate re-evaluation. `HITLPlacementTrigger` for `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` (`hitl_placement.py:128-138`) states applicability as "All cells; sub-agent interrupt stranding mitigated via cascade-timeout per C-CP-21 §21.3" — confirming `DURABLE_ASYNC` synchrony is spec-reachable for this placement, and that the spec's own anticipated mitigation for this exact stranding class is a cascade-timeout, not cursor-preservation. If the ancestor's OWN gate re-raises `HITLPauseRequestedSignal` on this re-evaluation (never reaching `inner.dispatch()`, so `child_resume_snapshot` is never forwarded to `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher`), the fan-out branch-failure capture logic builds a FRESH `PausedChildBranchResumeState` for this cycle representing "paused at its own gate" — with no evident mechanism to nest or preserve the PRIOR round's already-known deeper `child_snapshot` (the descendant that was already dispatched and is already paused further down), because a fresh signal carries no reference to the previous cycle's branch disposition. This is PRE-EXISTING and orthogonal to B-39's own delivery-cell mechanism, not introduced by it: under the OLD, now-retired ctx-level global `ResumeContextHolder`, the SAME ancestor gate re-fire would ALSO occur on every resume-forward (the composer's Step 0 had no first-dispatch-vs-resume-forward distinction then either) — but the OLD holder was tree-wide-shared, so ANY non-`None` operator-supplied value (even one addressed to a different, deeper branch) would satisfy the ancestor's Step 0 and let it proceed to `inner.dispatch()`, accidentally forwarding the child cursor correctly (while risking the OLD mechanism's own already-documented misapplication hazard). B-39's move to per-branch, non-globally-shared delivery removes that accidental masking, making the orphaning failure mode newly *visible*, but the root defect — the composer's dispatch entrypoint cannot distinguish "first dispatch of this branch" from "forwarding an already-dispatched, already-paused-deeper branch" — predates this arc.
- **Current state.** Registered, not built. Reachability confirmed via direct spec-text read (not merely reasoned) — `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY`'s "all cells" applicability plus the composer's `applicable_placements={SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY}` single-instance-per-step_kind binding (`hitl_gate_composer.py:834-849`). NOT built into the B-39 Slice B delivery-mechanism PR — genuinely bigger scope than this arc's delivery-cell mechanism (touches the composer's own dispatch entrypoint and the fan-out branch-failure capture path, not the `ResumeContext`/`HITLDeliveryCell` surface this arc owns), and per advisor()'s framing this is a pre-existing structural gap this arc's B-39 changes make more visible rather than introduce — so it does not block this arc's PR.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-25 (roadmap-continue, fresh empirical grounding pass on the fan-out branch-failure capture logic, no code change owed):** the close-out steps' own open question — "confirm whether a fresh `HITLPauseRequestedSignal` genuinely drops the prior cycle's `child_snapshot` cursor" — is now answered via direct trace, not reasoning: (1) `HITLPauseRequestedSignal` inherits `BaseException` directly, not `Exception` (`workflow_driver.py:~5079-5081`'s own comment on the LINEAR path); (2) the fan-out branch-dispatch try/except chain (`ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS` ~8680-8800, `PARALLELIZATION` ~12620+) catches only `CancelledError`, `SubAgentChildPausedError`, `SubAgentDispatchCapacityError`, and a bare `except Exception as _branch_exc:` — none of which match a `BaseException`-only signal, so a re-fired ancestor-gate pause is NOT caught by any handler in that chain and propagates out of the branch coroutine; (3) `asyncio.gather(*branches, return_exceptions=True)` DOES capture `BaseException` subclasses as ordinary result-list entries — confirmed by this same file's own B-60 comment at `workflow_driver.py:1891-1898` ("`return_exceptions=True` captures BaseExceptions — including `DispatchFenceTrippedSignal` — as ordinary results"), which is the PRECEDENT this workspace already built a fix for; (4) the only post-gather rescue, `_reraise_captured_fence_signal()` (`workflow_driver.py:1891`, called at ~8906/8928/8963/12858/12880/12911), scans EXCLUSIVELY for `_DispatchFenceTrippedSignal` (+ its `BaseExceptionGroup`-wrapped form) — it has no `HITLPauseRequestedSignal` case, so a re-fired ancestor-gate signal passes through un-re-raised and lands as a plain object in `results_by_ordinal[plan[0]]`; (5) `any_failed = any(isinstance(r, BaseException) for r in results)` (`workflow_driver.py:9011`/`12953`) DOES correctly classify it as a failure (this is `BaseException`-checked, not `Exception`-checked, so it is not silently read as success) — but since no `SubAgentChildPausedError` was raised this cycle (the ancestor's OWN gate fired, not the descendant's), `paused_child_dispositions` gets no entry for this branch, and under `proceed` the run finishes `_finish(RunStatus.PARTIAL, fail_class=None, salvage=True)` with the deeper descendant's `child_snapshot` cursor nowhere captured. **This confirms the register's suspected mechanism is structurally sound** (not merely plausible) for the PROCEED-tier fan-out path; the CASCADE_CANCEL/PAUSE-tier TaskGroup path (which the B-60 comment also flags for `BaseExceptionGroup`-wrapped delivery) was traced only for the `_DispatchFenceTrippedSignal` case, not re-verified here for `HITLPauseRequestedSignal` specifically — that remains open for whoever builds the reproduction. **Reproduction is still NOT built** — this pass was static-trace-only, deliberately stopping short of the ~700-line real-stack scaffold `test_u_rt_95_hitl_resume_consume_cycle_e2e.py` implies, per this session's own multi-arc length this wake (B-30 ship + a 9-row grounding sweep already landed) and the standing discipline against designing a fix on unverified footing. Next session should build the reproduction directly — this grounding pass narrows exactly what it needs to assert: a fan-out branch whose `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step has its own `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` gate, a resume-forward carrying a real `child_resume_snapshot`, and an assertion that the run ends `PARTIAL`/cursor-lost rather than correctly re-entering the deeper paused child.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-25 (round 2, roadmap-continue, structural reframe — verified, not just reasoned):** `hitl_response_for`'s own docstring (`pause_resume_protocol_types.py:~1069-1098`) states directly that "HOW the resolved per-branch answer physically reaches the resumed step's gate composer (which parameter, which call site, at which recursion level) is deliberately UNSPECIFIED here" and that a container/ancestor branch "is simply re-entered/recursed into, unconditionally, by whatever mechanism the impl leg lands (**deferred, §1.3**)." Cross-checked against the code, not just the docstring's claim: `grep -n 'HITLDeliveryCell(' harness-cp/src/harness_cp/workflow_driver.py` returns exactly ONE construction site, `workflow_driver.py:4777`, inside `_execute_workflow_body` (`workflow_driver.py:3126`, single call site at line 3072 — the `SINGLE_THREADED_LINEAR`-only per-step loop). No equivalent construction exists in `_execute_parallelization`/`_execute_orchestrator_workers`/`_execute_hierarchical_delegation`. **This reframes B-72's disposition:** it is not a surprising, accidentally-discovered re-fire bug — it is the CP spec's OWN acknowledged, explicitly-deferred fan-out delivery-mechanism gap (§1.3, "impl discretion"). B-39 Slice B built the CARRIER (`ResumeContext.hitl_responses`/`hitl_response_for`) but explicitly scoped OUT the WIRING that would call `hitl_response_for(child_run_id)` at the right fan-out dispatch site and construct a `HITLDeliveryCell`-equivalent for a branch's `StepExecutionContext` — B-72 IS that deferred wiring, not a separate correctness bug found alongside it. **Retiring the "skip-a-gate vs. strand-a-descendant" council framing** — the spec's mechanism ROUTES a resolved answer to the correct branch via `hitl_response_for(child_run_id)`; it does not skip or bypass gate evaluation. A branch with no resolved answer yet SHOULD re-fire (correct behavior, not a bug); the actual gap is that even a branch WITH a resolved answer waiting has no delivery path, since no `HITLDeliveryCell`-equivalent construction exists for any fan-out topology. **The design-risk flag relocates, it does not disappear:** the same docstring spends a full paragraph on property 4 (multi-child fallback-safety, a black-box invariant the deferred §1.3 resolver must independently satisfy) and explicitly states a PRIOR DRAFT's counting/INERT mechanism was found by out-of-family review to strand a transitively-paused container branch — this is genuine, already-once-falsified design subtlety, not a solved problem. **What remains genuinely unverified (NOT resolved by this citation, still reproduction-owed):** the OBSERVABLE runtime failure shape — whether a realistic persona/blast-radius config actually reaches gate re-evaluation rather than auto-approving it away first (`test_u_rt_95`'s own SOLO-persona lesson: `solo_persona_floor_auto` silently auto-approves before synchrony is even evaluated), whether the PROCEED-tier `PARTIAL`/cursor-lost outcome traced statically in the round-1 update above is what actually happens at runtime, and what the CASCADE_CANCEL/PAUSE-tier TaskGroup path does (untraced for this signal type). Keep these as predicted-not-confirmed until the reproduction runs.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-25 (round 3, roadmap-continue, EMPIRICALLY CONFIRMED — reproduction built and run):** `harness-runtime/tests/integration/test_b72_fanout_sub_agent_dispatch_hitl_gate_resume.py` (2 tests, both PASS — pin the confirmed broken state directly, not via `xfail`). Deliberately simpler than the original "forwarding an already-paused descendant's cursor" framing — no nested child pause needed to observe the gap, since round 2's grounding found the delivery-mechanism absence is unconditional for ANY fan-out branch resume, not specific to cursor-forwarding. Test 1 (precondition) confirms a `PARALLELIZATION` workflow's single branch, `step_kind=SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` carrying `HITLPlacement(position=SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY)`, correctly PAUSES on first dispatch via the REAL `RuntimeHITLGateComposer` (confirms `fold_step_hitl_placements` reaches fan-out branches — the placement producer is NOT the gap). Test 2 pins the core gap with 4 independent oracles: `api.resume(..., resume_context=ResumeContext(hitl_response=APPROVE))` does NOT reach `completed` — `resumed.status=='paused'`, `failure_cause is None`, 2 webhook POSTs (re-escalated, didn't consume), and a DIRECT spy on `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher.dispatch` (`sub_agent_dispatcher_calls == []`) proving the real dispatcher was never reached, not just that the aggregate status looks wrong. **Refines the observable failure shape** vs. round 1's PROCEED-tier `PARTIAL`/cursor-lost static prediction: this reproduction used `TEAM_BINDING` (→ `cascade_policy=pause`), and the observed shape is cleaner than "lost" — the run correctly reports `paused` again (no state corruption), it simply NEVER converges no matter how many times a resolved answer is delivered, because the delivered value is never consumed. This is effectively an infinite re-pause for any fan-out branch carrying its own HITL gate. The exact terminal status a genuine fix produces is deliberately NOT documented as `completed` — codex round 2 empirically bypassed `RuntimeHITLGateComposer.dispatch` entirely and found the run STILL ends `paused` (`pause_reason='explicit_operator'`) for this test's specific child shape (a separate, not-yet-understood quirk unrelated to the HITL gap, confirmed both by codex's bypass and an independent bypass I ran myself), even though the real dispatcher WAS reached and the branch's own terminal disposition came back `completed`. The dispatcher-spy oracle is therefore the load-bearing, confirmed-safe assertion — not the run's eventual terminal status. The PROCEED-tier `PARTIAL` shape from round 1 remains untested by this reproduction (this test uses `pause` cascade policy only) — a `proceed`-tier variant is a natural follow-on but not required to confirm the core gap. Full harness-runtime suite re-run after adding this test: 3069 passed, 40 skipped, 0 xfailed, 0 unexpected failures — no regressions (`just check`, full monorepo: 6682 passed, 10 skipped, 36 deselected, 0 xfailed). **4 rounds of codex out-of-family review on this PR, all real, all fixed:** (1) a blanket `@pytest.mark.xfail` would have swallowed ANY failure, not just the confirmed one — replaced with direct oracles; (2) asserting only aggregate `status`, and separately proxying "was the child dispatched" via the Anthropic client's call count, were both too weak — codex empirically bypassed the gate and showed the Anthropic-client proxy doesn't discriminate for this child's engine class (`PURE_PATTERN_NO_ENGINE`); replaced with a direct spy on `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher.dispatch`, independently mutation-probed (0 calls before resume, jumps to 1 when the gate is bypassed — confirmed non-vacuous); (3) this register text itself went stale after fix (2), still describing the retired Anthropic-client oracle — corrected; (4) the test never asserted `resumed.pause_snapshot is not None` — a re-fire that silently dropped the resumable snapshot (leaving the caller stuck, `status='paused'` with `pause_snapshot=None`) would have passed every prior assertion, contradicting the documented "clean, indefinitely resumable" shape; added that assertion plus a workflow-identity check. Final oracle count: 6 (`status`, `failure_cause`, `pause_snapshot is not None`, `workflow_id` preserved, webhook count, dispatcher-spy). **A separate, still-open finding narrows what this reproduction scopes for the multi-branch case (see close-out steps below).**
- **Close-out steps.** Build the deferred fan-out HITL delivery-mechanism wiring: at the fan-out branch dispatch site (or an equivalent seam in `_execute_parallelization`/`_execute_orchestrator_workers`/`_execute_hierarchical_delegation`), call `resume_context.hitl_response_for(child_run_id)` for a gate-owning branch and construct a `HITLDeliveryCell`-equivalent on that branch's `StepExecutionContext` (mirroring `workflow_driver.py:4777`'s LINEAR-path construction) so the composer's Step 0 can consume it — while independently satisfying property 4's multi-child fallback-safety invariant per the deferred §1.3 resolver contract (a real design task, not mechanical — a prior draft's counting approach already failed review here). **Codex's third round-1 finding (NOT yet addressed — genuinely open):** in this reproduction the gate fires BEFORE `_dispatch_inner` ever creates a child run, so the resulting `PauseSnapshot` carries no child run ID or branch-specific identity — `hitl_response_for(child_run_id)` cannot by itself distinguish two PEER gate-owning branches in this exact pre-dispatch-gate shape (neither would have a child run yet either). This single-branch reproduction confirms the LIVENESS symptom (gate never consumes, infinite re-pause) but does NOT by itself fully scope the branch-addressing mechanism for 2+ concurrently-paused gate-owning branches — either a multi-peer-branch reproduction, or grounding on how a branch's OWN pre-dispatch identity would be exposed for addressing (distinct from a recursively-dispatched child's `run_id`), is additional groundwork owed before the fix is genuinely scoped. Do not treat "the fix is scoped" as settled — treat it as "the single-branch liveness gap is scoped; the multi-branch addressing question is still open." **Council:** the property-4-safe resolver design (not a gate-skip tradeoff) is worth a dyadic pass before implementing the fix — likely C10 (blast-radius/gate-integrity, given the prior falsified draft) paired with whichever voice owns liveness/fallback-safety composition; the multi-branch addressing gap above should be part of that same council pass, not resolved unilaterally.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-25 (round 4, roadmap-continue, `advisor()`-checked twice — a scoping trap caught before being written up as a finding, not a new build):** re-confirmed by fresh direct read (not re-derivation) that `hitl_delivery_holder=`/`hitl_delivery_cell`/`HITLDeliveryCell(` appear in `workflow_driver.py` EXACTLY where round 2 already found them — all three strictly inside `_execute_workflow_body` (def at line 3126); `_execute_parallelization` (def at line 7399) and `_execute_orchestrator_workers` (def at line 10759) reference none of them. This is a reconfirmation of round 2's finding, not a new one. **The genuinely new, still-unresolved observation this pass surfaces: the register's own two framings of "what kind of bug B-72 is" don't reconcile, and nobody has named that yet.** Round 1's "What it is" text frames this as a **dispatch-idempotency** defect — "the composer's dispatch entrypoint cannot distinguish 'first dispatch of this branch' from 'forwarding an already-dispatched, already-paused-deeper branch'" (a fix needs NO addressing key at all — just a first-dispatch-vs-resume-forward check before the gate re-evaluates). Round 2's reframe describes an **addressing/delivery** defect — "a branch WITH a resolved answer waiting has no delivery path" (a fix needs `hitl_response_for` wired to a per-branch key, exactly the property-4 resolver problem). These are different fixes with different shapes and different council scope. **This session almost committed the mistake this note exists to prevent:** mid-grounding, `branch_path` (`compose_branch_path`, `{parent_action_id}:{branch_index}`) was floated as a candidate addressing key for category-(a), reasoning that its documented collision (`pause_resume_protocol_types.py`'s keying-defect note, ~line 1031) only applies to NESTED grandchildren under two different peer children, not to peer branches themselves. `advisor()` correctly rejected this before it was written up: (1) the (a)-vs-(b) bug category is unsettled, so blessing an addressing *key* presupposes an answer to a question that hasn't been answered; (2) this single-branch reproduction (round 3) exercises ZERO multi-branch addressing, so "collision only hits grandchildren, not peers" is an untested hypothesis, not a finding — and it is *specifically* the reading the code's own keying-defect note says was "recorded so the `branch_path` reading is not reinvented." **`branch_path`-as-addressing-key is explicitly NOT a finding of this pass — flagged here only so a future session doesn't independently re-derive and trust it without the same warning.** **First question the next session (or the council pass already recommended above) must resolve, before any design or code: is B-72 a dispatch-idempotency defect (b) or an addressing/delivery defect (a)?** The answer determines whether a key is needed at all, and if so, whether `branch_path`, `run_id`, or something new is safe to use for it — none of that is decided by this pass.
- **UPDATE 2026-07-25 (round 5, roadmap-continue, `systems-architect` tension-resolution mode, `advisor()`-corrected before write-up — ROUND-3 REPRODUCTION'S CATEGORY RESOLVED; B-72'S ORIGINAL REGISTERED SCENARIO STILL UNCLASSIFIED, addressing key still open):** **Scope correction up front (out-of-family Codex [P2], caught before merge):** an earlier draft of this update's heading and early conclusion overstated this pass as resolving "B-72" wholesale. It does not. What is resolved below is the category of the round-3 REPRODUCTION's narrower single-dispatch scenario only. B-72's own ORIGINAL "What it is" text (lines 601-603, round 1) describes a DIFFERENT, hybrid scenario (a branch that already dispatched into its own gate, got approved, and is now resume-forwarded toward its self-dispatched, deeper-paused descendant) that this same pass's own later correction (below) admits neither property 5 nor the round-3 repro settles. Do not read this update as closing B-72's category question in general — only the repro's narrower shape is closed; B-72's original scenario remains open, and the council/reproduction work below must not be narrowed on the false premise that it is settled. With that scope correction stated: round 4's own framing of the (a)-vs-(b) split was the wrong axis. Direct read of the canonical spec text, `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §1.2 properties 4-5 (the highest artifact in the authority chain that speaks to this, both cited verbatim, not paraphrased) — **not two competing bug categories for the SAME branch, but two DIFFERENT branch roles the spec already distinguishes and already prescribes different treatment for**: property 5 states "Transitively-paused container/ancestor branches are not gate-owning... it never itself dispatched into a HITL gate, never itself needs a `hitl_responses` key, and MUST always be traversable (re-entered/recursed into) regardless of what `hitl_responses` does or does not contain"; property 4 states its safety/liveness invariant "applies ONLY among gate-owning branches — never to a container/ancestor branch's own re-entry." So: a **gate-owning branch** (its own `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` placement is what paused it) needs a *delivered* answer — a delivery-CELL construction, mirroring LINEAR's, that the composer's Step 0 can consume — category (a); a **transitively-paused container/ancestor branch** (paused only because a descendant is paused, forwarding `child_resume_snapshot` down) is re-entered *unconditionally*, no answer, no delivery — category (b), and the spec already mandates this treatment, it is not an open question. Round 1's "What it is" text describes the container case ("forwarding an already-dispatched, already-paused-deeper branch"); round 3's reproduction pins the gate-owning case — confirmed directly from the test file (`test_b72_fanout_sub_agent_dispatch_hitl_gate_resume.py:17-19,240,249`), a single `PARALLELIZATION` branch whose own `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step carries its own `HITLPlacement(position=SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY)` — no `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION`, no nested/recursive child pause, i.e. genuinely gate-owning, not a container. **B-72 as empirically pinned by the round-3 reproduction is therefore category (a) — but "category (a)" is DELIVERY-mechanism-construction, not necessarily KEYED addressing, and conflating the two is itself a correction owed here (out-of-family Codex [P2], caught before merge, same pass):** an earlier draft of this update said a gate-owning branch "needs an *addressed* answer" — too strong. The round-3 test itself resumes via `resume_context = ResumeContext(hitl_response=...)` (`test_b72_fanout_sub_agent_dispatch_hitl_gate_resume.py:573`) — the UNIFORM field, not a keyed `hitl_responses` map — and property 4 explicitly permits the uniform fallback to resolve a gate-owning branch when it is the SOLE member of the unaddressed gate-owning set this cycle (exactly this repro's shape: one branch, no peers). So the round-3 repro's confirmed gap is purely the ABSENCE of any delivery-cell construction for fan-out branches (mirroring round 2's original framing, `workflow_driver.py:4777`'s LINEAR-only construction) — wiring that alone, with no keying/addressing logic at all, would already fix this specific reproduction via the uniform fallback. **Keyed multi-peer addressing (2+ concurrently unaddressed gate-owning branches) is a SEPARATE, harder, still fully open question the round-3 repro does not exercise even partially** — not "category (a) settled, addressing key still open" as an earlier draft implied, but "delivery-construction resolved for the single-branch case; keyed addressing for the multi-peer case is untouched by any evidence gathered so far." **Correction (same pass, out-of-family Codex [P2], caught before merge):** round 1's actual "What it is" scenario is NOT the clean property-5 container case, and an earlier draft of this update wrongly declared it settled by property 5 — property 5's text is explicit that it covers a branch that "never itself dispatched into a HITL gate" (`Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §1.2 property 5, verbatim), scoped to `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` ancestors. Round 1's branch is a fan-out `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step that DID dispatch into its OWN `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` gate — that is how it got approved and recursed into the child that is now paused deeper — and is only later, on the resume-forward cycle, acting in a container-like role toward that self-dispatched descendant. Property 5 does not speak to this branch at all; it is neither the round-3 repro's clean single-dispatch gate-owning case nor property 5's pure never-gate-owning ancestor — it is a **third, hybrid case** (previously-gate-owning, now forwarding past its own already-once-satisfied gate) that this pass does NOT resolve. **This hybrid case stays OPEN, unresolved, and in scope** — it is exercised by zero of the round-3 repro (same epistemic status as the still-open multi-branch addressing gap at the close-out steps above) and needs its own reproduction/grounding before any claim about its correct treatment, likely as its own register row once scoped (mirroring how B-73–B-77 were split from B-68 at close) rather than staying conflated into B-72 as-is. Supporting evidence that a keyless idempotency-only fix could not be the answer FOR THE GATE-OWNING CASE regardless: `hitl_gate_composer.py:1684-1689`'s Step 0 has exactly one bypass path and it requires a delivered value (`consume_and_clear()` non-`None`); there is no "already-fired-this-branch" keyless bypass in the current contract, and inventing one would let the gate proceed with no operator answer — the exact fail-safe violation round 2's "a branch with no resolved answer yet SHOULD re-fire" and the B-EDIT-CARRIER response-routing history both rule out. **What this pass explicitly does NOT resolve, per `advisor()`'s caution against blessing a key as a side effect of settling the category (mirrors round 4's own rejection of `branch_path`):** `hitl_response_for(child_run_id)` cannot be restated as "the wiring" — the register's own already-standing finding (close-out steps above) that the gate fires BEFORE `_dispatch_inner` ever creates a child run stands unchanged, now cited against the correct call path (an earlier draft mis-cited this at `hitl_gate_composer.py:864`, which is unrelated code — corrected, out-of-family Codex [P3]): `_dispatch_inner` is defined at `hitl_gate_composer.py:1015` and calls `self.inner.dispatch(...)` at `hitl_gate_composer.py:1035` (i.e. only after Step 0's bypass-or-refire decision); the actual child run — and its `run_id` — does not exist until `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher`'s own dispatch path constructs `_child_run_id_seed` and invokes `self.child_workflow_runner(...)` at `sub_agent_dispatch.py:1078-1108`, strictly downstream of the gate's own re-evaluation: a fan-out gate-owning branch is a STEP inside the parent run, not a run itself (contrast LINEAR's `run_id`, `workflow_driver.py:4716-4719`, "this call's own run identifier"), so `run_id` is identical across peer gate-owning branches and cannot address them individually — the addressing key for the gate-owning case remains genuinely open. **Tiebreaker for the next session (unresolved by this pass, and corrected same-pass, out-of-family Codex [P2], caught before merge — the tiebreaker itself was mis-scoped):** property 1 (`Spec_Control_Plane_v1_106.md` §1.2, verbatim) does NOT leave the `hitl_responses` KEY SHAPE to impl discretion — it normatively FIXES it: "keyed by the paused child's own `run_id`, not `branch_path`." So finding *any* pre-dispatch identity is not by itself sufficient for Class 3 — reusing a non-`run_id`-shaped identity (`branch_index`, `parent_action_id`, or similar) as the `hitl_responses` key would itself change property 1's fixed carrier semantics, which is a spec amendment, not impl discretion, regardless of whether the identity happens to already exist in code. The real tiebreaker: does any mechanism exist (or can one be added without changing PUBLIC carrier semantics) to make the child's `run_id` itself known/assignable BEFORE `_dispatch_inner` fires — e.g. reserving/minting the child run's `run_id` earlier in the dispatch sequence, still keyed the same way property 1 already fixes? **This tiebreaker itself needs a further correction (out-of-family Codex [P2], caught before merge, same pass): even a YES answer does not clear this to Class 3 on its own.** Internally minting the child's `run_id` earlier only solves half the problem — the OPERATOR (the external caller constructing `resume_context.hitl_responses`) has no public way to learn that minted `run_id` before responding. This is exactly `B-71`'s own already-registered, separate scope (`.harness/forward-register.yaml` id `B-71`, filed independently of this pass): `HITLEscalationBrief` and the HITL webhook payload derive their identifier from a workflow_id-scoped `compose_hitl_action_id`, NOT `run_id` — the public escalation surface exposes no run-instance-distinct identifier at all, and B-71's own close-out already calls the fix a `C-CP-28` contract amendment (additive, but still a public-carrier change, not silent impl discretion). So: **even if `run_id` can be minted early internally (YES branch), the gate-owning addressing fix is still GATED on B-71 landing (or being co-designed with it)** — a caller cannot address a keyed response to a branch it was never told the identifier of. Class 3 (pure impl discretion, no design-artifact revision) would require BOTH an internally-mintable-early `run_id` AND that identifier already being exposed on the public escalation payload without further change — the second condition is currently FALSE per B-71, so this tiebreaker's honest current answer leans toward Class 1 / B-71-dependent, not Class 3, until B-71 is scoped or landed. If NO (no `run_id`-compatible carrier is available pre-dispatch at all, only a differently-shaped identity), satisfying property 4 for the gate-owning case requires either changing property 1's fixed keying contract (a spec amendment) or a new carrier §1.3 does not define — a design gap, Class 1 per this skill's §4A.4 (inventing an ad hoc identity unilaterally would extend the H_T design, `CLAUDE.md` I-2/X-AL-3). This pass does not fully resolve either branch of that tiebreaker, and explicitly retracts the earlier, too-permissive framing that any pre-dispatch identity (or an early-mintable `run_id` alone) would qualify as Class 3 — B-71 is a load-bearing dependency for the keyed-addressing side of this fix that any council pass must account for. **Council recommendation at the close-out steps above stays alive and is NOT narrowed by this pass** — it now covers: (1) the delivery-cell-construction fix for the round-3-confirmed single-branch gate-owning case (no keying needed, per the correction above), (2) the keyed multi-peer addressing design, gated on `B-71` landing or being co-designed with it (per the tiebreaker correction above), (3) property 4's resolver set-membership mechanism, and (4) the round-1 hybrid case (previously-gate-owning, now forwarding past its own already-satisfied gate) this correction re-opened — property 5 does not settle it, so it is not safely retirable from scope. **Operator decides:** whether the round-1 hybrid case is worth a dedicated reproduction before the council convenes (it is currently untested by any existing repro), and whether the tiebreaker check is worth its own grounding pass first. **Close-out caveat (out-of-family Codex [P2], NOT independently verified this pass — flagged so whoever builds the fix checks it, not asserted as a finding):** `_collect_gate_owning_run_ids` (`workflow_driver.py:2665-2698`) treats any node carrying `fan_out_resume`/`peer_fan_out_resume` as an unconditional container regardless of its own `pause_reason` (the function's own docstring states this is "checked FIRST, regardless of its own pause_reason"), and `PeerFanOutResumeState` is constructed whenever any branch fails/pauses in a `PARALLELIZATION` run (`workflow_driver.py:9722`) — including, plausibly, a branch paused at its own gate with zero dispatched children. If so, a branch in exactly the round-3 repro's shape could compute to an EMPTY gate-owning set via `compute_hitl_uniform_fallback_eligible_run_id`, meaning delivery-cell construction alone may not suffice — gate-ownership detection may also need a fix for the pre-dispatch (own-gate, zero-children) case. This is unverified against the repro's actual captured snapshot fields this pass; verify before building on it.

**Net position after all corrections above (read this, not the correction trail, for the current disposition):** (1) the round-3 reproduction's branch is confirmed gate-owning; its fix needs delivery-cell construction (mirroring LINEAR's), not necessarily a keyed map — the uniform fallback should suffice once wired, MODULO the unverified gate-ownership-detection caveat directly above. (2) Whether KEYED multi-peer addressing is separately needed remains fully open, and any such design is gated on `B-71` (landing, or being co-designed with it) — B-71 is not optional groundwork, it is a hard dependency. (3) B-72's own original registered scenario (a branch already-gate-owning once, now resume-forwarded toward its self-dispatched deeper-paused descendant) is a third, hybrid case neither property 5 nor the round-3 repro resolves — it stays open and untested. (4) Council scope covers all four items above (delivery-cell fix, B-71-gated keyed addressing, property 4's resolver mechanism, the hybrid case) — none are retired from scope by this pass.

Fixed by restoring `step_kind` as a CAPTURED field (compared for equality on resume), not a constant check — the `PausedChildBranchResumeState` (B-31) precedent round 4 mirrored does NOT apply here, since that carrier IS uniquely `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` by construction while this one is genuinely multi-kind. `child_workflow_id` is unaffected (already defaulted to `None` via a try/except for non-`SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` payloads). NEW regression test reproduces the exact scenario codex flagged: a `PRE_ACTION`-gated `INFERENCE_STEP` branch pre-dispatch-pauses, and resuming with the UNCHANGED workflow now succeeds instead of failing closed on a bogus kind-changed material diff. Full harness-cp + harness-runtime targeted suites re-run clean (157 + 2 passed, +1 from this round); ruff + pyright strict clean on all touched files. **5 rounds of out-of-family `just codex-review` total to convergence for the impl leg.**

### B-73 · Protected result store's GC/publish synchronization is in-process only -- two co-resident OS processes sharing one store root are not mutually excluded *(split from B-68 at its close, out-of-family Codex round 5, 2026-07-25; CLOSED 2026-07-26 -- PR #1120)*

- **What it is.** Split out of B-68 at its close (2026-07-25). B-68's fix serializes `gc_sweep()` against `_publish_atomic()` via a `threading.Lock` keyed by the store root's resolved path, held in a module-level `_root_locks` registry — this closes every same-process race, including multiple `ProtectedResultStore` instances constructed against the same root within one process (e.g. across bootstrap/resume cycles). Out-of-family Codex round 5 (the B-68 fix's convergence check) confirmed the registry does NOT extend across a process boundary: two separate daemon PROCESSES sharing one store directory each hold their own independent `_root_locks` dict, so one process's bootstrap/shutdown sweep can still unlink another process's just-published entry between its `os.link()` commit and mtime refresh, if the deployment-configured TTL is shorter than that window.
- **Current state.** Registered, not built. This is the SAME reachability precondition B-68's original registration already named (TTL shorter than single-digit-ms publish latency — roughly seven orders of magnitude below the factory 86400s default) and the same residual B-68's own code comments and commit history disclosed throughout its 5 review rounds ("a genuinely separate OS process sharing the store directory remains an open residual, registered, not silently assumed closed"). Not a defect introduced by B-68's fix — an inherent limitation of an in-process primitive.
- **Close-out steps.** Add an OS-level advisory lock (`fcntl.flock` on a dedicated lockfile under the store root, or equivalent) wrapping the same `_publish_atomic`/`gc_sweep` critical sections the in-process `threading.Lock` already wraps, acquired/released around the existing lock so both layers compose without a nested-lock ordering hazard. Witness with a genuine multi-PROCESS test (e.g. `multiprocessing` workers, not threads) pinning a TTL shorter than an injected publication delay in one process while a second process sweeps concurrently, asserting the entry survives.
- **Council: no** — narrow TTL-dependent cross-process race with a documented, unchanged reachability precondition; the fix is a known primitive (`fcntl.flock`), not a judgment call.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-26 (PR #1120).** `_cross_process_lock()` (an `fcntl.flock` on a dedicated `.cross_process.lock` file, disjoint from both `gc_sweep` glob patterns) is now composed INSIDE the existing in-process `self._publish_lock` at both `_publish_atomic` and `gc_sweep`'s candidate-selection phase — a single, fixed nesting order everywhere, no lock-ordering hazard. Guarded on Windows (`_IS_WINDOWS = sys.platform == "win32"`) per C-STK-10, mirroring the same carve-out already shipped at `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock`; `fcntl` is imported lazily inside the lock method, never at module scope. **4 rounds of out-of-family `just codex-review main` to clean convergence:** round 1 caught a [P1] (the unguarded top-level `import fcntl` would have broken `import harness_runtime` entirely on Windows, since `harness_runtime.types` imports `ProtectedResultStore` unconditionally) plus a [P2] (a fresh child store's opportunistic pre-write GC sweep already acquires the same lock, so the write-once regression test passed even with `_publish_atomic`'s own lock removed alone — fixed by suppressing the opportunistic sweep in the test's child process); round 2 caught 2 further [P2]s (the new tests would spuriously fail on Windows, where the lock intentionally no-ops — added `skipif`; the readiness marker was written before `flock` was ever attempted, leaving a scheduling window); round 3 found the same underlying concern persisted (writing the marker "right before" the contending call still is not proof of being AT the `flock` attempt, since real serialization + Fernet encryption happen first) — fixed by wrapping `fcntl.flock` itself in each child script so the marker fires at the actual syscall attempt; round 4 clean. Each fix mutation-probe-verified (revert, confirm the regression test fails; restore, confirm green) before landing. Witnessed with two genuine-subprocess regression tests (`subprocess`, not `multiprocessing` — avoids both the fork-after-lock-held deadlock hazard and spawn's pickle-by-module-name ambiguity in this monorepo's several same-named `tests` packages) asserting cross-process blocking via `store._cross_process_lock()` held directly in the parent — a deliberate, reasoned deviation from the originally-envisioned TTL-race-survival recipe (lock-identity blocking is the more deterministic, non-flaky witness shape for this claim). `merge-gate` 3-lens review: concurrency + test-witness APPROVE at round 1; spec-conformance BLOCKED once (this register row was not updated in the same PR, diverging from the B-68 precedent) — fixed by this same update.

### B-74 · Protected result store's mtime-based GC age authority can under-report publish time below a filesystem's timestamp granularity *(surfaced at the B-68 impl arc, out-of-family Codex round 7 [P2], 2026-07-25)*

- **What it is.** `_publish_atomic`'s `os.utime(entry_path, None)` refresh sets the entry's mtime to the current system time, but the underlying filesystem is what actually STORES that value, at whatever timestamp resolution it supports — on a coarse-granularity filesystem (e.g. legacy FAT32, or certain network filesystem configurations; NOT the modern POSIX filesystems — ext4/APFS/NTFS — this workspace's committed local-development/self-hosted-server/managed-cloud deployment surfaces default to, which offer sub-millisecond resolution), the stored mtime can round DOWN by up to the filesystem's own granularity (e.g. up to ~1 second on a 1-second-resolution filesystem). If the deployment-configured `ttl_seconds` is SHORTER than that rounding error, `gc_sweep()` can classify a just-published, genuinely-live entry as already expired and reclaim it — even though the B-68 mtime-authority fix (round 1) is working exactly as designed; the filesystem itself cannot represent the precision the fix relies on.
- **Current state.** NARROWED (not closed) 2026-07-26 (PR #1123): the "likely ONE finding" framing below was directionally right but not literal — `B-77` and `B-74` share raw mtime as an untrustworthy age authority, but `B-77`'s reorder fix only removes the DATA write+fsync duration from its OWN crash window, not the whole window (see `B-77`'s row — a residual metadata-fsync+commit tail remains, reproduced by out-of-family Codex round 9). This row's own concern (filesystem storage-resolution rounding) is UNTOUCHED by that reorder either way — it was never about ordering, only about what a coarse filesystem stores once a value is fsync'd, so it stays fully in scope, not reduced. Two same-arc attempts to dispose of this row were both wrong and reverted: PR #1123 round 1 tried "no realistic operator would configure a below-floor TTL" (rejected by Codex — `protected_result_store_ttl_seconds` enforces only `gt=0.0`, no floor, and the env loader + factory pass an override through unchanged, so this is a SUPPORTED configuration, not an unreachable one); a later round considered adding that same TTL floor as a mechanical fix and advisor() rejected it too — it is this row's own close_out option ("reject TTLs below a guaranteed timestamp resolution"), already flagged as a genuine design decision, and the field is a spec'd deployment-configurable surface (Runtime v1.103 §14.8.11 AC 7) Phase-7 impl discretion should not narrow unilaterally. Remains open, not dormant, not built this arc.

  **NARROWED AGAIN 2026-07-30 (dated re-check against the landed `B-77` fix, PR `#pending`).** `B-77`'s first-observation grace covers this row's reachable loss case but does NOT subsume it. **Now covered:** a granularity error up to ONE sweep interval — the sweep that FIRST observes a coarse-stamped, genuinely-live entry can no longer reclaim it, which is the bootstrap-sweep-immediately-after-publication path (witnessed at `test_coarse_filesystem_mtime_granularity_does_not_lose_a_live_entry_on_sight`, whose first assertion passes only because of the grace). **Surviving residue — this row's whole remaining scope:** a rounding error EXCEEDING the gap between two consecutive sweeps. That gap is not TTL-bound — a bootstrap sweep (`stage_4_od.py`) and an explicit or shutdown-step-5b sweep (`shutdown.py`) can land milliseconds apart, well inside a 1-second filesystem granularity — so a live entry can still be lost on such a volume. That exact residue is PINNED as an executable assertion in the same witness (its second `gc_sweep` reclaims the live entry, labelled REGISTERED RESIDUAL in the test body), so a future fix must come back and update it rather than silently diverge. Not closed as subsumed-by-`B-77`; the close-out below is unchanged and still the fix.

  **Cross-reference (advisor(), B-76 arc, 2026-07-26):** likely ONE finding with `B-77` — both concern raw mtime as an untrustworthy age authority (this row: filesystem timestamp-granularity rounding; `B-77`: crash-window non-atomicity). A fix that moves the age authority off raw mtime would likely close both; ground as a single "does the age authority stay mtime?" question before building either in isolation.
- **Close-out steps.** Design a TTL-floor validation or a probe-based age-authority selection that accounts for the deployment filesystem's actual timestamp resolution — e.g. probe the store root's filesystem granularity at construction/first-write time (write a marker file, read back its mtime precision) and either refuse a TTL below that floor with a typed error, or fall back to a higher-resolution age signal (e.g. a companion sidecar file recording a monotonic sequence number or a separate high-resolution timestamp store) when the TTL is below the floor. Witness with a test that simulates a coarse-mtime filesystem (e.g. via a monkeypatched `os.stat`/`os.utime` rounding to whole seconds) and a TTL below that resolution, asserting the store either refuses the TTL or does not lose the live entry.
- **Council: no** — narrow, filesystem-dependent TTL-precondition gap; the fix requires picking a concrete resolution floor which is a design decision, not an immediate judgment call.

### B-75 · Protected result store's gc_sweep candidate-selection scan can itself stall inside the publish lock under a pathological backlog or a hanging filesystem *(surfaced at the B-68 impl arc, out-of-family Codex round 8 [P1], 2026-07-25; FIXED 2026-07-26 -- PR #1122, CLOSED)*

- **What it is.** Surfaced at round 8 — a finding directly about round 7's OWN fix, the third consecutive round to surface an issue in the immediately-prior round's change (round 6→7→8). Round 7 narrowed `gc_sweep`'s lock to a fast, `stat()`-only candidate-selection phase specifically to bound lock-hold time against a slow DECRYPT phase (see B-68's closed row). Codex round 8 observes that even this narrowed phase — `glob()` + `stat()` over every `*.entry`/`.tmp-*` file, still inside the lock — is itself unbounded in a MORE EXTREME scenario: a pathologically large backlog (far larger than what makes decrypt slow, since `stat()` is orders of magnitude cheaper per file), or a filesystem mount that hangs/stalls on `stat()` itself (e.g. a dead network mount). Under `run_off_loop_detach_on_cancel`'s detach semantics, a shutdown sweep stuck in THIS phase would ALSO get detached while still holding the lock, reopening the same class of liveness hazard round 7 closed for the decrypt phase specifically.
- **Current state.** CLOSED. Registered, not built — was the state through round 8's own registration — per `advisor()`'s explicit round-8 guidance: three consecutive rounds each surfacing a defect the PRIOR round's own fix introduced is the non-convergent-adversarial-hardening-arms-race signature (this workspace's own standing discipline) — stop patching, register, and ship rather than open a round 9. Reachability is genuinely narrower than round 7's finding (needs either an extreme file count or a hanging/failing filesystem mount, not merely a large-but-normal backlog), and a failing filesystem mount would likely be causing failures well beyond this store already. **Escalation (advisor(), B-76 arc, 2026-07-26):** `B-73`'s cross-process `flock` is now ALSO held across this candidate-selection phase, so a stalled `glob()`/`stat()` scan blocks other OS PROCESSES sharing the store root, not just other threads in this process — worse than this row's original P1 framing. Own arc, not a continuation of B-76's.
