- **Close-out steps.** Bound the candidate-selection phase itself — e.g. batch the `glob()`/`stat()` scan with a periodic lock release/re-acquire (yielding between batches so a stalled scan cannot monopolize the lock indefinitely), or move the scan itself off-lock using a snapshot-then-verify pattern (enumerate + stat unlocked, then re-verify each candidate's mtime under a BRIEF lock hold immediately before acting on it — mirroring how phase 2's unlink already trusts a monotonic once-confirmed-expired fact, but applied one level earlier). Witness with a test that stalls `glob()`/`stat()` itself (not decrypt) and asserts a concurrent write still completes within a short bounded window.
- **Council: no** — narrow liveness-hardening follow-on to an already-shipped fix; the fix shape (batch/yield or snapshot-then-verify) is a known pattern, not an open design question.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-26 (PR #1122).** Selected via `advisor()` over the still-open `B-74`/`B-77` pair — its close_out already named a known fix shape (no open design question), it's the P1, and `B-73` escalated it to cross-process blast radius. Candidate ENUMERATION (`glob()`/`stat()` over `*.entry`/`.tmp-*`) now runs fully unlocked, producing PROVISIONAL candidates; each is re-verified in a single brief COMBINED lock acquisition before being trusted — never one acquisition per candidate (would turn `_cross_process_lock`'s per-call `fcntl.flock` into N kernel round-trips). Resolved advisor()'s flagged asymmetry (does the fix cover `.tmp-*`, or scope to `*.entry` only?) by hand: a `.tmp-*` file's mtime is not a trustworthy "definitely done" signal unlocked (it could be mid-write) the way an entry's is (monotonic expiry + fresh uuid4 keys) — but since `tempfile.mkstemp` itself only ever runs INSIDE the lock, a temp-file candidate that survives to be re-observed once the sweep holds the lock can only be a genuine crash orphan (its creator either already finished and already unlinked it, or crashed while holding a now-free lock). Same unlocked-scan-then-brief-relock-reverify pattern is correct and safe for both file types — not scoped to `*.entry` only. Two new regression tests: a structural witness recording whether `_publish_lock.locked()` is true at each `glob()` call (asserts unheld, not a wall-clock check per `[[verification-shape-sharpened-grep-vs-e2e]]`), and a stalled-enumeration concurrent-write test mirroring the existing stalled-decrypt pattern. Both mutation-probe-verified. Hand-traced the fix against the two most load-bearing pre-existing lock tests before writing code to confirm neither would regress — both pass unchanged. Full `harness-runtime` suite: 3087 passed, 10 skipped; ruff + pyright strict clean.

### B-76 · Protected result store's lock-key registry can fail open on a transient stat() failure during root-identity resolution *(surfaced at the B-68 impl arc, out-of-family Codex round 8 [P2], 2026-07-25; FIXED 2026-07-26 -- PR #1121, CLOSED)*

- **What it is.** `_lock_for_root` keys the process-wide lock registry on `(st_dev, st_ino)` when `resolved.stat()` succeeds, falling back to the resolved-path STRING when it raises `OSError` (a defensive branch added at round 6 for the not-yet-created-directory case, now effectively unreachable in normal operation since round 6's own fix made `_lock_for_root` unconditionally `mkdir` the directory before stat'ing). Codex observes the fallback is still live for a TRANSIENT `stat()` failure (not "doesn't exist yet", but a genuine intermittent I/O error) — if one concurrent caller's `stat()` transiently fails while another's (near-simultaneous) call succeeds, the two callers key DIFFERENT locks for the SAME logical root (one string-keyed, one inode-keyed), silently reopening the exact aliasing race the identity-based key exists to close.
- **Current state.** CLOSED. Registered, not built — was the state through the B-68 arc's round 8. Narrow: requires a genuinely intermittent filesystem error at the precise moment of a concurrent lock-key computation, not a sustained failure (a sustained failure would surface elsewhere — e.g. the `mkdir` immediately preceding this stat would itself already be failing loudly).
- **Close-out steps.** Do not silently fall back to a different key namespace on a `stat()` failure after a successful `mkdir` — propagate the `OSError` (or retry with a bounded backoff before propagating) so a transient failure surfaces as a typed refusal rather than a silent identity-namespace split. Witness with a test that monkeypatches `Path.stat` to fail once then succeed, asserting the lock registry does not admit two distinct lock objects for the same resolved root across that transient failure.
- **Council: no** — narrow transient-I/O-error handling gap; propagate-vs-fallback is a correctness fix, not an open design question.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-26 (PR #1121).** Selected via `advisor()` as the cleanest of the 4 findings surfaced at the same round (`B-74`/`B-75`/`B-76`/`B-77`, all in this file) — a genuinely mechanical propagate-vs-fallback fix with no interaction with `B-73`'s new cross-process flock and no reopening of the phase-1/phase-2 GC-sweep safety argument. Removed the `except OSError: key = str(resolved)` fallback; `resolved.stat()`'s `OSError` now propagates. Verified all 3 direct/indirect callers of `_publish_lock`/`_lock_for_root` already tolerate the raised error by direct grep (not just the docstring's own claim): `write_once` folds it to `UnresolvableResultRef`; the bootstrap (`stage_4_od.py`) and shutdown-step-5b (`shutdown.py`) `gc_sweep()` callers each wrap it in `except Exception`; `_maybe_opportunistic_gc_sweep` does too. Witnessed with a frame-targeted `Path.stat` monkeypatch (checks the immediate caller is `_lock_for_root` specifically) rather than a blind global intercept — `Path.resolve()`/`Path.mkdir()` both call `.stat()` internally in this pathlib version and each already swallows its own `OSError`, so a naive intercept gets absorbed there instead of ever reaching the line under test. Mutation-probe-verified: reverting the fix makes the new test fail (`DID NOT RAISE`); restoring it passes. `advisor()` additionally flagged that `B-74`/`B-77` are likely ONE finding (both concern raw mtime as an untrustworthy age authority — worth a combined grounding pass, not two separate builds) and that `B-73` made `B-75` WORSE (candidate selection now also holds the cross-process flock, so a stalled scan blocks other PROCESSES, not just threads) — both recorded at their own rows, not built here. Full `harness-runtime` suite: 3085 passed, 10 skipped; ruff + pyright strict clean.

### B-77 · Protected result store's mtime refresh is not durable atomically with the publish commit, leaving a crash window where a recovered entry looks pre-aged *(surfaced at the B-68 impl arc, out-of-family Codex round 8 [P2], 2026-07-25; CLOSED 2026-07-30 -- first-observation grace)*

- **What it is.** `_publish_atomic` makes the entry durable in two separate steps: the `os.link` commit + its directory fsync (durability step 1), THEN a separate `os.utime` mtime refresh + its own fsync (durability step 2). If the process crashes BETWEEN these two steps (after the entry is durably linked and visible, but before its mtime is refreshed), the entry that survives the crash retains the TEMP FILE's original, pre-fsync-latency mtime — the exact stale timestamp the B-68 round-1 fix exists to move away from. The NEXT process's bootstrap sweep, reading this crash-recovered entry, would then see the OLD (potentially already-TTL-exceeded) mtime and could immediately reclaim an entry that in fact just barely finished publishing before the crash.
- **Current state.** NARROWED TWICE, not closed, 2026-07-26 (PR #1123). This PR went through THREE wrong claims before landing here, all caught by out-of-family Codex: round 1 tried to classify B-77 as dormant via a "sub-millisecond `os.link`-to-`os.utime` window" argument (wrong — the real pre-fix window was the FULL pre-link write+fsync duration, unbounded). Round 2 moved the SOLE mtime refresh from post-commit to pre-commit and claimed this "closes the window structurally" (wrong — Codex round 9 [P1] reproduced a DIFFERENT, normal-path regression against `main`: with the stamp now before the metadata-fsync+`os.link`+directory-fsync tail instead of after, a successful uninterrupted write could return a reference an IMMEDIATE sweep reclaims — `ttl_seconds=0.02` + a 100ms-delayed directory fsync — something that could never happen on `main`, where the stamp already ran after that tail). Round 3 (current): `_publish_atomic` now stamps TWICE — once pre-commit (on the temp file, for crash recovery) AND once post-commit (on the entry, restoring `main`'s exact original normal-path behavior) — rather than moving the single stamp. Strict improvement over `main` on both axes, no reopened ground. The residual crash window is now `os.link` → the post-commit refresh completing — the SAME shape B-77 originally named, starting from the fresher pre-commit baseline rather than write-time. Restoring the post-commit block also reintroduces a pre-existing, out-of-scope inconsistency the round-2 reorder had incidentally fixed: if the post-commit refresh's own fsync fails, `write_once` reports `UnresolvableResultRef` even though `entry_path` is already durably linked and readable — matches `main`'s original semantics, intentionally not fixed here. Mutation-probe-verified regression tests added for BOTH the crash-recovery path (pre-commit stamp) and the normal-path regression Codex round 9 found (post-commit stamp); the residual is registered, not tested — no fix for it has landed. `merge-gate`'s 3-lens review approved the round-2 diff; only the concurrency lens was re-run for round 3 (the durability-step change is inside the same lock its reasoning already covered) per advisor()-directed scope.
- **Close-out steps.** advisor()-directed: do NOT add a TTL floor — that is `B-74`'s own already-declined close_out option ("reject TTLs below a guaranteed timestamp resolution... a genuine design decision"), and the field is a spec'd deployment-configurable surface (Runtime v1.103 §14.8.11 AC 7) Phase-7 impl discretion should not narrow. The actual fix is an age authority that cannot predate publication by construction, not a further reordering of a two-stamp pipeline: `gc_sweep` should not reclaim an entry it has not observed in a PRIOR sweep (a grace period) — bounded to one sweep interval, immune to arbitrary I/O latency in the publish path, implementable entirely inside `gc_sweep`/`_publish_atomic` without touching the config contract. Witness with a test that simulates a crash strictly BETWEEN `os.link` and the post-commit refresh completing (e.g. a monkeypatched post-commit `os.utime` that raises once) and asserts a subsequent bootstrap-style sweep does not reclaim the crash-recovered entry immediately.
- **Council: no** — the grace-period fix shape is a known pattern (first-observation gating), not an open design question; narrow implementation-discretion follow-on once picked up.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-30 (PR `#pending`, branch `b77-gc-first-observation-grace-20260730`).** The close_out above landed verbatim as a FIRST-OBSERVATION GRACE inside `gc_sweep` — no fourth reordering of the two-stamp pipeline, no TTL floor, no change to the `ttl_seconds` config contract. `gc_sweep` now records the filenames it verified past TTL under its own lock (`_observe_expired`) and reclaims only those a PRIOR sweep of the same root already recorded. **Persistence judgment (the central design call):** the record is module-wide, keyed by the root's filesystem identity (`_root_identity_key`, extracted from `_lock_for_root` so both key identically) — NOT per-instance. Grounded against the real call graph rather than the abstract pattern: the composition root builds a FRESH `ProtectedResultStore` per `run()`/`resume()` bootstrap (`bootstrap/factories/protected_result_store_factory.py:78` ← `bootstrap/stage_4_od.py:123`, which sweeps immediately), so a per-instance record would hand EVERY bootstrap sweep a fresh grace and defer reclaim to shutdown indefinitely, weakening the spec's own bounded-retention term. A fresh PROCESS legitimately starts with an empty registry — it cannot tell a genuine age from a crash-window artifact, so granting the grace is the safe direction, and that IS precisely the crash-recovery case this row names. Four mutation-probed witnesses: `test_crash_between_link_and_post_commit_refresh_survives_a_fresh_bootstrap_sweep` (the close_out's own recipe), `test_uninterrupted_write_survives_an_immediate_sweep_past_the_grace` (the PR #1123 round-2 normal-path regression class, swept twice so the grace cannot mask it), `test_genuinely_expired_entry_is_reclaimed_at_the_second_sweep_not_the_first` (liveness — one interval, not immortality), and `test_coarse_filesystem_mtime_granularity_does_not_lose_a_live_entry_on_sight` (the `B-74` re-assessment). Pre-existing single-sweep witnesses were updated to sweep twice at one pinned `now` (`_sweep_past_grace`) so their original TTL-classification discrimination is preserved rather than masked. Spec check before building: Runtime v1.106 §14.8.11's bounded-retention term requires GC sweeps at bootstrap/shutdown plus a periodic-or-opportunistic runtime sweep and a typed expiry report line — it states no reclaim-on-first-observation timing, so the grace violates no AC. The out-of-scope inconsistency this row also recorded (a failing post-commit refresh fsync reporting `UnresolvableResultRef` for an already-durably-linked entry) is UNCHANGED and still not fixed.

### B-78 · A `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step's own `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` gate under `EVALUATOR_OPTIMIZER`/`DECENTRALIZED_HANDOFF` can exhibit the same pre-dispatch re-fire livelock `B-72` property 6 closes for the fan-out topologies, ungrounded by any reproduction *(split from B-72's spec leg, out-of-family Codex round 5+7, 2026-07-25; GROUNDED + IMPL LEG LANDED 2026-07-26 — PR #1117, CLOSED)*

- **What it is.** `B-72`'s CP spec v1.108 §1.2b scopes property 6 to ONLY the `PARALLELIZATION`/`ORCHESTRATOR_WORKERS`/`HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` topologies — the ones whose gate-owning-branch pause state is captured via the `PeerFanOutResumeState`/`FanOutResumeState` carrier family (`HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` included on carrier-identity grounds per round 6, since it recursively reuses `_execute_orchestrator_workers` per U-CP-88/89, not a separate implementation). A `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step carrying its own `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` placement under `EVALUATOR_OPTIMIZER` or `DECENTRALIZED_HANDOFF` is captured into a DIFFERENT carrier family instead (`EvaluatorOptimizerResumeState`/`HandoffResumeState`) and can plausibly exhibit the SAME re-fire-on-resume symptom `B-72`'s round-3 reproduction pins for the fan-out case.
- **Current state.** CLOSED. Registered, not built, not even reproduced — was the state through round 7. This has never been directly grounded against those carriers' own code paths — round 5 first flagged it as an explicit exclusion in `B-72`'s spec leg; round 7 (out-of-family Codex, [P2]) flagged that the exclusion needed its own durably-tracked register row, not just correction-history prose buried inside `B-72`.
- **Close-out steps.** A future arc's own reproduction-first grounding pass (mirroring how `B-72`'s round-3 repro preceded its own spec leg): construct a real `EVALUATOR_OPTIMIZER` or `DECENTRALIZED_HANDOFF` workflow whose `SUB_AGENT_DISPATCH` step carries `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY`, confirm (or falsify) that its own pre-dispatch gate re-fires identically on resume, and only then determine whether a property-6-shaped fix (or a materially different one, given the different carrier shape) is warranted — not yet designed, not yet specified.
- **Council: no** — narrow topology-specific livelock candidate with a clear grounding-first next step (reproduce before designing), mirroring `B-72`'s own precedent; not a judgment call yet.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-26 (PR #1117).** The reproduction-first grounding pass confirmed a real defect WORSE than the registered livelock hypothesis: a genuine `HITLPauseRequestedSignal` (a deliberate `BaseException`, not `Exception`) raised from the dispatch path inside `_execute_evaluator_optimizer`/`_execute_decentralized_handoff` was UNCAUGHT — crashing the run outright rather than pausing it — and separately, neither topology constructed a `HITLDeliveryCell` at its own resumed-step dispatch site, so even a caught pause could never deliver a resolved operator response on resume. Fixed by mirroring the already-cleared LINEAR delivery-cell mechanism (CP spec v1.108 §1.2, line 37) at both topologies' resumed-step dispatch sites — a name-matched `except BaseException` catch + `HITLDeliveryCell` construction, identical resolution shape to LINEAR/fan-out. NO new CP-spec carrier field needed (EO/DH have no branch-ordinal ambiguity property 6's field was built for) — plain Phase-7 impl work, not a Class 1 change. **6 rounds of out-of-family `just codex-review main` to convergence:** rounds 1-3 each fixed a genuine regression this arc's own prior fix introduced (REJECT-must-be-terminal; the terminal carve-out over-matched non-HITL `rt_fail_class`es; the narrowed carve-out was checked ahead of `cascade_policy`'s PROCEED branch in DH, wrongly overriding its PARTIAL-salvage disposition); round 4's finding (an unanswered `HITL_PENDING` resume proceeds to dispatch rather than staying inert) was confirmed, via direct grep, to be the identical pre-existing shape already at the shipped LINEAR site — folded into `B-79`'s scope (broadened, not duplicated) rather than fixed here; round 5 found nothing new; round 6 (a `merge-gate` spec-conformance-lens-driven pass) caught + fixed a stale-carry-text defect this same PR's own CP spec/plan correction left behind (see `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_109.md`/`Implementation_Plan_Control_Plane_v2_45.md`) plus a genuine [P2] audit-completeness gap — the new EO/DH pause handlers omitted the `PAUSE_CAPTURED` CP→IS state-ledger emission LINEAR's own HITL branch fires, fixed by mirroring it at both sites with mutation-probed regression tests. **`merge-gate` 3-lens review:** concurrency + test-witness APPROVE round 1 (unchanged across rounds); spec-conformance BLOCKED twice on stale-carry-text (round 1: CP spec/plan still asserting the gap "ungrounded"; round 2: the round-1 fix's own filing footer + this file's own B-78 section left stale) before all-approve.

### B-79 · A resumed branch's `HITLDeliveryCell` is constructed from `step_id` identity alone, so a HITL placement/gate-configuration change between pause and resume is not detected as a material diff *(surfaced at the B-72 impl leg, out-of-family Codex round 3 (branch-vs-main), 2026-07-25; SPEC LEG + IMPL LEG SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-07-27 — PR #1132; IMPL LEG SLICE 2 LANDED 2026-07-27 — PR #1133, CLOSED)*

- **What it is.** Out-of-family Codex round 3 (branch-vs-main, B-72 impl leg PR #1116, 2026-07-25). The B-72 impl leg's `_resume_body_mismatch` guard (mirroring the pre-existing `effect_fence_paused_branches`/`paused_child_branches` precedent) validates a pre-dispatch gate-owning branch's `step_id` identity before delivering the operator's resolved response, but does not validate whether the branch's HITL placement (e.g. `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY`) or `step_kind` still applies to the resumed step. If a workflow is edited between pause and resume while preserving the branch's `step_id` — removing its `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` placement, for instance — the guard still accepts the resume and `RuntimeHITLGateComposer`'s Step 0 unconditionally consumes and applies the stored `HITLDeliveryCell` (e.g. a REJECT) to the now-ungated branch, before any current-placement evaluation runs. Confirmed PRE-EXISTING and NOT introduced by the B-72 impl leg — the identical unconditional construction (no placement-identity check at all) already exists at the already-shipped LINEAR `resume_at` delivery-cell site (`workflow_driver.py` ~4748-4777, landed at the B-39 impl leg), which B-72's fan-out wiring mirrors verbatim — confirmed by direct grep/read of that site before registering this row, per this workspace's own `[[codex-finding-scope-verify-sibling-pattern]]` discipline.
- **Current state.** Registered, not built.
- **Close-out steps.** Design a HITL placement/gate-configuration identity check (e.g. persisting the resolved set of applicable HITL placements, or a content hash of the step's HITL-relevant fields, alongside `step_id` at pause-capture time) and validate it at BOTH the LINEAR `resume_at` site and the fan-out `_resume_body_mismatch` closures (`_execute_parallelization`/`_execute_orchestrator_workers`) before a `HITLDeliveryCell` is constructed — reject the resume as a material diff if the check fails, mirroring the existing step_id/step_kind precedent. This is a genuine design task (what identity to persist, and whether the composer's own persona/engine-class placement-resolution logic — which `harness-cp` does not have visibility into, that logic being Runtime-owned — needs a new cross-axis seam to expose it), not a mechanical patch. Witness with a test that edits a paused step's HITL placement (or step_kind) between pause and resume while preserving `step_id`, and asserts the resume is rejected as a material diff rather than silently delivering the stale response.
- **Council: likely.** A dyadic pass (a design task touching a Runtime-owned cross-axis seam, not mechanical) — C10 (blast-radius/gate-integrity, mirroring the B-72 spec-leg's own prior falsified-draft caution) paired with whichever voice owns the CP↔Runtime HITL-placement-resolution boundary. Not urgent — the reachability precondition (an operator edits a workflow's HITL placements between an in-flight pause and its resume) is narrow, and this gap is symmetric with an already-shipped LINEAR mechanism that has not surfaced in production.
- **CONVENED 2026-07-27 (roadmap-continue, dyadic `council-orchestrator` pass, C10 + C1 co-primary; C11 considered and recused — the register's "HITL" phrasing invited it by association, but C1's own SKILL.md draws the boundary explicitly: "C1 owns *where* HITL plugs in; C11 owns the *primitive*," and this question is squarely placement, not primitive).** The convened agent ran the orchestrator's mandatory probe-first discipline BEFORE any deliberation, and the probe **falsified this row's own premise**: "the composer's own persona/engine-class placement-resolution logic... is Runtime-owned, and `harness-cp` does not have visibility into it" is false for the scenario in scope. Direct read confirmed `step_context.hitl_placements` (via `fold_step_hitl_placements`, computed at every dispatch/resume site e.g. `workflow_driver.py` lines 5107-5114, 7885-7890, 8051-8060, 11832-11840) and `StepEffectiveBinding.removed_placements` (`per_step_override_evaluator.py:259-362`) are BOTH CP-native, deterministic folds already available at pause-capture time — `hitl_gate_composer.py:1752-1762`'s own inline comment confirms the Runtime composer only *consumes* `step_context.hitl_placements`, which is "surfaced onto the per-step `StepExecutionContext` at workflow-binding time by the CP driver." Only the gate-*level* computation (persona_tier × blast_radius × mcp_trust_tier) is genuinely Runtime-resolved, and that's a function of deployment configuration, not the workflow-body edit this row's reachability precondition names — **so no new cross-axis seam is needed**, contrary to the close-out steps' own framing above. One TENSION surfaced and probe-resolved: C1 initially proposed hashing only `HITLPlacement.position` (narrower, cheaper); C10 wanted the full applicability surface covered (a `tool_filter`/`cascade_policy`/`timeout` edit on the same placement kind would otherwise slip through). Re-checking the evidence found `HITLPlacement` (`hitl_placement.py:176-197`) is itself a frozen 4-field Pydantic model already inside the SAME tuple C1 proposed hashing — hashing all 4 fields costs nothing extra over the narrow framing, so both voices converged on the broader hash with zero additional plumbing cost. **Recommendation:** persist a SHA-256 content hash over `(ordered HITLPlacement tuple, sorted removed_placements)` on the existing pause-capture carriers (`PausedChildBranchResumeState`/`PreDispatchGateOwningBranchResumeState`, drop-when-`None` byte-compat mirroring the B-31 `child_workflow_id`/B-72 `step_kind` precedent exactly) plus a new analogous carrier for the LINEAR `resume_at` site — the probe found that site has NO per-step identity check at all today, not even `step_id`, a slightly larger gap than the fan-out sites this row originally scoped; a companion fix is also owed at `hitl_gate_composer.py`'s Step 0 short-circuit (~1684-1750), which never gates delivery on placement drift today. **Fix size:** mechanical-with-a-thin-design-decision, not a genuine architectural task. **Classification: Class 1** — every prior material-diff-identity addition to these same carriers (`step_kind`; `child_workflow_id` at B-31/v1.99; the B-72 pre-dispatch-gate-owning identity itself at v1.107→v1.108) went through a CP spec-leg amendment before impl per `C-CP-26` PauseResumeProtocol authority; this is architecturally identical in kind, not a bug-fix-shaped impl-discretion call. **Next action:** open a CP spec-leg (fork doc, or a B-72-lineage follow-on) proposing the additive field(s) + a new §1.x invariant mirroring property 6's shape, before any impl. Not yet opened this pass — this update is doc-only.
- **SPEC LEG LANDED 2026-07-27 — `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_110.md` §1 (property 7) + `Implementation_Plan_Control_Plane_v2_46.md`, co-designed with `B-80`.** Before drafting, every load-bearing call-graph claim from the convening above was independently re-verified against current HEAD (per this same session's own `B-71` arc lesson about a spec delta built on an unverified premise): `PreDispatchGateOwningBranchResumeState`'s exact field shape (`branch_index`/`step_id`/`step_kind`/`child_workflow_id`, `pause_resume_protocol_types.py:208-252`); `HITLPlacement`'s exact 4-field, frozen, hashable shape (`hitl_placement.py`); `fold_step_hitl_placements`'s CP-native fold (`workflow_driver_types.py`); `removed_placements`' CP-native origin (`per_step_override_evaluator.py`, `hitl_placement.py`, `workflow_manifest_entry.py`); and the Runtime composer's Step 0 unconditional-delivery shape (`hitl_gate_composer.py:1684-1750`, confirming `resumed_placements`/`resumed_matching` are computed ONLY for the audit entry, never gating delivery) — all confirmed exactly as the convening found. Property 7 requires the gate-configuration identity be validated unchanged at the fan-out closure's pre-dispatch-gate-owning delivery site, the LINEAR `resume_at` site, the `EVALUATOR_OPTIMIZER` site, and the `DECENTRALIZED_HANDOFF` site (all 3 sequential sites carry no per-step identity check of any kind today; a net-new carrier, shared or per-site, is authorized); a Runtime-side defense-in-depth check at Step 0 is left to impl discretion, not required, since a rejected CP-side resume never constructs a `HITLDeliveryCell` for the paths this property covers. **4 out-of-family codex findings caught before merge across 2 rounds, all fixed:** (1) [P1] round 1: an initial draft ALSO authorized this field on `PausedChildBranchResumeState` — WRONG, confirmed by direct read (`workflow_driver.py:8016-8058`) that carrier only forwards `child_resume_snapshot` to a recursively-dispatched child's own, separate `execute_workflow()` call and never itself constructs a `HITLDeliveryCell` (that only happens for `branch_index in _recovered_pre_dispatch_gate_owning`, `workflow_driver.py:8036-8045`) — a hash there would neither close a real gap NOR be neutral, it would incorrectly reject a valid resume whenever the PARENT's own unrelated placement config changed; corrected to scope §1.3a's carrier authorization to `PreDispatchGateOwningBranchResumeState` only (the child's own gate identity is covered recursively at its own `execute_workflow()` invocation); (2) [P2] round 1: §1.1(c) falsely claimed the rejection emits a C-CP-20 audit-ledger entry — confirmed by direct read that `_resume_body_mismatch`'s actual existing behavior is `RunResult(status=FAILED, fail_class=...)` only, no audit-ledger write; corrected to match; (3) [P2] round 1: the plan's "entirely CP-native, zero cross-axis cascade" claim was wrong for property 8 (see B-80's own update below); (4) [P1] round 2: property 7's scope entirely OMITTED the `EVALUATOR_OPTIMIZER` (`workflow_driver.py:10789-10806`) and `DECENTRALIZED_HANDOFF` (`workflow_driver.py:14803-14820`) delivery-cell construction sites — both landed later by the `B-78` impl leg (PR #1117) and confirmed by their own inline comments to mirror the LINEAR mechanism EXACTLY, sharing the identical no-per-step-identity-check gap; corrected to cover all 3 sequential sites uniformly, one shared net-new carrier or 3 distinct ones at impl discretion. Clearance at `.harness/clearance/spec-control-plane-v1-110-cleared-2026-07-27.md` + `.harness/clearance/implementation-plan-control-plane-v2-46-cleared-2026-07-27.md`. Register status flips `registered_finding` → `open` (the design-extension blocker is cleared; the impl leg — the hashing scheme, the new fields, the comparison wiring, the witness tests at all 4 sites — is a separate follow-on arc, not yet built).

### B-80 · The `_any_fence_abort` HITL-delivery suppression guard is scoped to the current fan-out level, not the true root of a nested resume tree *(surfaced at the B-72 impl leg, out-of-family Codex round 6 (branch-vs-main), 2026-07-25)*

- **What it is.** Out-of-family Codex round 6 (branch-vs-main, B-72 impl leg PR #1116, 2026-07-25). The round-4 fix suppresses a pre-dispatch gate-owning peer's `HITLDeliveryCell` construction when `_any_fence_abort` is true, but that value is computed from `_recovered_effect_fence_paused`, which is scoped to the CURRENT fan-out level's own recovered branches only. When an effect-fence ABORT targets a DIFFERENT, nested pause subtree (e.g. a deeper `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` level, or a sibling peer's recursively paused child), a shallower level's own `_any_fence_abort` stays false and its pre-dispatch gate-owning peer can still receive a delivery cell and dispatch before the run-level abort is honored — resumed branches are processed sequentially, so an earlier pre-dispatch branch can consume its HITL response and execute ahead of a later subtree processing the abort. Confirmed PRE-EXISTING and NOT introduced by the B-72 impl leg — `_any_fence_abort` itself was computed fan-out-locally by the B-70 impl leg (CP spec v1.107), and the pre-existing effect-fence-directive peer suppression already consumes that same narrow value; B-72's round-4 guard mirrors the established pattern verbatim. Affects BOTH the pre-existing effect-fence directive suppression and the new B-72 HITL delivery gate, at all four fan-out dispatch sites (`_execute_parallelization` and `_execute_orchestrator_workers`, including `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION`'s recursive reuse).
- **Current state.** Registered, not built.
- **Close-out steps.** Compute the abort guard from the true root of the resume tree (or thread a run-level abort signal down through the recursive `_execute_orchestrator_workers`/`HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` calls) rather than from the current level's own `_recovered_effect_fence_paused`, and consume it uniformly at both the pre-existing effect-fence-directive suppression site and the B-72 pre-dispatch HITL delivery-cell gate. This is a genuine design task (what "true root" means for a peer fan-out vs. a recursively nested `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` tree, and how to thread it without a new cross-axis seam), not a mechanical patch. Witness with a test where a nested/deeper subtree's effect-fence ABORT races a shallower sibling subtree's pre-dispatch HITL delivery in the same resume round, and assert the shallower delivery is suppressed.
- **Council: likely.** A dyadic pass (a design task touching cross-recursion-boundary abort propagation, not mechanical) — C1 (orchestration/topology, owns `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION`'s recursive reuse) paired with C10 (blast-radius/gate-integrity, mirroring B-79's own reasoning). Not urgent — the reachability precondition (a nested subtree ABORT racing a shallower pre-dispatch HITL delivery in the SAME resume round) is narrow, and the suppression gap is symmetric with an already-shipped effect-fence-directive mechanism that has not surfaced in production.
- **CONVENED 2026-07-27 (roadmap-continue, dyadic `council-orchestrator` pass, C1 + C10 co-primary).** The convened agent's probe-first check confirmed `HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION`'s "recursion" is NOT same-stack-frame Python recursion — each nesting level re-enters via a FRESH `execute_workflow()` call across the CP→Runtime→CP boundary (`RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher` → `ChildWorkflowRunner` → `execute_workflow`), and TWO existing sibling values — `hitl_uniform_fallback_eligible_run_id` (B-39) and `effect_fence_uniform_fallback_eligible_key` (B-70, `workflow_driver.py:2873-2908`) — are ALREADY computed once at the true depth-0 root and threaded VERBATIM through this exact crossing (`execute_workflow()`'s signature → `_execute_orchestrator_workers()`/`_execute_parallelization()` → `child_workflow_runner.py`'s `ChildWorkflowRunner` Protocol), which is precisely B-70's own already-solved instance of this problem shape. **Recommendation:** a THIRD sibling value, `effect_fence_tree_wide_abort_present: bool`, computed once at depth-0 via a ~5-line helper reusing the EXISTING `_collect_effect_fence_idempotency_keys` tree walk + `_resolve_effect_fence_gated` resolver, threaded through the IDENTICAL channel as the two siblings, **OR**ed (not replacing) into the level-local `_any_fence_abort` at both consume sites — no new cross-axis seam, no new carrier field, satisfies this row's own registered constraint exactly. **TENSION probe-resolved** (blanket tree-wide boolean vs. a precise `cascade_policy`-chain walk, in favor of the blanket reading): (a) the already-shipped local-level rule this arc extends is itself `cascade_policy`-blind ("tier-agnostic FAILED" per CP spec v1.107 §1.1) — a `cascade_policy`-aware tree-wide extension would be MORE discriminating than the shipped convention it extends, out of family; (b) `PauseSnapshot` carries ZERO `cascade_policy` field anywhere in its schema (verified directly) — the precise reading would need a NEW durable carrier field, a strictly bigger, separately-scoped task. C1's precision concern is registered as a named, deliberately-declined refinement, not silently dropped — revisit only if a real production case demonstrates non-negligible over-suppression cost under `cascade_policy=proceed` nesting. **A genuine new finding, not resolvable as pure impl discretion:** the fix surfaces an undocumented interaction between TWO existing CP-spec invariants — §1.1's abort-suppression rule and §1.2 property 6's MANDATORY delivery for the sole-unaddressed pre-dispatch gate-owning branch (v1.108) — since widening `_any_fence_abort` tree-wide widens the circumstances under which that mandatory delivery is skipped. Traced empirically: this does NOT reintroduce a livelock (suppression only delays a peer by one resume round; the run either terminates `FAILED` via the abort's own path regardless, or a later round recovers the peer if the run turns out not doomed) — but the spec text as written does not currently reconcile §1.1 against §1.2 property 6 for the tree-wide case. **Fix size:** mechanical (one new pure function reusing two existing utilities + a parameter threaded through an existing channel + a 2-line `OR` at each of 2 consume sites + the already-specified witness test). **Classification: Class 1, not Class 3** — mirrors the B-70/B-72 precedent exactly (both routed through a CP spec delta + clearance marker + plan coverage-row before impl), and additionally owes the §1.1/§1.2-property-6 reconciliation as new spec prose (likely a new §1.1(c) or similar, noting the tree-wide suppression rule takes precedence over property 6's mandatory-delivery obligation, with the delayed-not-lost liveness rationale this convening worked out). **Next action:** route to a CP spec leg before any impl, co-designable with B-79 (both touch the same `_resume_body_mismatch`/pre-dispatch-carrier family). Not yet opened this pass — this update is doc-only.
- **SPEC LEG LANDED 2026-07-27 — `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_110.md` §2 (property 8) + `Implementation_Plan_Control_Plane_v2_46.md`, co-designed with `B-79`.** Before drafting, every load-bearing call-graph claim from the convening above was independently re-verified against current HEAD (per this same session's own `B-71` arc lesson): `execute_workflow()`'s own signature threading `effect_fence_uniform_fallback_eligible_key` through `_execute_orchestrator_workers`/`_execute_parallelization` and `child_workflow_runner.py`'s `ChildWorkflowRunner` Protocol at lines 88-89/185-186/258-259; `_any_fence_abort`'s exact consume sites at `workflow_driver.py:7999`/`8037` and `12257`/`12281`; and — the single most load-bearing check — that `compute_effect_fence_uniform_fallback_eligible_key` (`workflow_driver.py:2873-2908`) is a PURE COMPUTED VALUE derived fresh from the already-persisted `root_snapshot` at each resume call, never itself stored in `PauseSnapshot` — confirming property 8 needs NO new durable carrier, unlike property 6/7. §2.3 states the genuine new spec-prose reconciliation: property 8's tree-wide signal, once true, TAKES PRECEDENCE over property 6's §1.1(b) mandatory-delivery outcome — property 6's TEXT is unamended, only the circumstances under which it fires are now conditioned. **Out-of-family codex [P2] caught before merge:** an initial draft's plan text claimed "zero cross-axis cascade" for this property — WRONG, the new parameter's threading DOES touch the Runtime-owned `ChildWorkflowRunner` Protocol (`child_workflow_runner.py`) exactly like its two sibling parameters already do; corrected to state accurately: no NEW crossing point is introduced (the SAME established CP→Runtime→CP boundary the two siblings already use), but the touch itself is real, not absent — confirmed at this correction that neither sibling parameter is documented anywhere in `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` today, a pre-existing Runtime-spec coverage gap this delta does not attempt to backfill. Clearance at `.harness/clearance/spec-control-plane-v1-110-cleared-2026-07-27.md` + `.harness/clearance/implementation-plan-control-plane-v2-46-cleared-2026-07-27.md`. Register status flips `registered_finding` → `open` (the design-extension blocker is cleared; the impl leg — the new function, the parameter thread, the two `OR`-sites, the §1.1(b) precedence wiring, the witness test — is a separate follow-on arc, not yet built).

### B-81 · OW/HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION test parity gaps remain for the B-72 pre-dispatch gate-owning carrier's exclusion conjuncts and resume-side material-diff validation *(surfaced at the B-72 impl leg, merge-gate 3-lens review round 2, 2026-07-25; CLOSED 2026-07-26 -- PR #1118 + PR #1119)*

- **What it is.** merge-gate 3-lens review round 2 (test-witness lens, B-72 impl leg PR #1116, 2026-07-25) found two coverage gaps after round 1's OW `HITLDeliveryCell`-construction gap was closed by a dedicated regression test (`test_ow_worker_resume_delivers_and_excludes_resolved_pre_dispatch_gate_owner` in `test_workflow_driver_fanout_pause.py`): (1) the OW/`HIERARCHICAL_DELEGATION` mirror of `_resume_body_mismatch`'s pre-dispatch-gate-owning material-diff validation (`workflow_driver.py`, the `FanOutResumeState`-consuming closure near the PARALLELIZATION site's ~7772-7831) has zero test coverage — PARALLELIZATION got three dedicated witnesses (`test_peer_resume_rejects_pre_dispatch_gate_owning_kind_changed`, `test_peer_resume_rejects_pre_dispatch_gate_owning_workflow_id_swap`, plus the INFERENCE_STEP round-5 regression test) but the OW mirror has none; (2) the new exclusion tests (both the PARALLELIZATION and OW variants) only exercise the completed/`terminal_dispositions` arm of the carry-forward filter's three exclusion conjuncts — the `paused_child_dispositions` and `effect_fence_paused_dispositions` arms remain completely unwitnessed at BOTH construction sites (`workflow_driver.py` ~9958-9965 and ~14036-14043). Per `advisor()` consultation during the merge-gate loop: these are guard-hardening on symmetric, already-reviewed code (confirmed structurally identical to the tested PARALLELIZATION paths by the concurrency lens across 2 rounds), not the load-bearing delivery outcome round 1's finding was — registered as a follow-on rather than blocking this PR's merge a third time.
- **Current state.** CLOSED.
- **Close-out steps.** (1) Port `test_peer_resume_rejects_pre_dispatch_gate_owning_kind_changed` + `test_peer_resume_rejects_pre_dispatch_gate_owning_workflow_id_swap` + the INFERENCE_STEP round-5 regression test to `test_workflow_driver_fanout_pause.py` against `_execute_orchestrator_workers`'s own `_resume_body_mismatch` closure (`worker_steps`-based, not `steps`-based). (2) Add a paused-child-resolves-this-round exclusion test (a recovered pre-dispatch gate-owning branch's delivered cell triggers a REAL nested `execute_workflow` dispatch that itself pauses, landing the branch in `paused_child_dispositions` instead of `terminal_dispositions`) and an effect-fence-resolves-this-round exclusion test (the branch's delivered cell leads to an effect-fence-ambiguous dispatch), at BOTH the `PeerFanOutResumeState` and `FanOutResumeState` construction sites — 4 new tests total. Mutation-probe each by deleting the specific exclusion conjunct it targets, mirroring the discipline already applied to the completed-arm tests this PR shipped.
- **Council: no.** Mechanical test-coverage parity against an already-established pattern (mirror an existing PARALLELIZATION test to its OW twin; add the 2 remaining exclusion-conjunct arms using the same shape as the completed-arm test this PR already ships). Not a design decision.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-26 (PR #1118 + PR #1119).** Step 1/2 (PR #1118, all-approve merge-gate round 1, clean codex-review round 1): ported the 3 PARALLELIZATION material-diff tests to their OW mirror. Step 2/2 (PR #1119, all-approve merge-gate round 1, clean codex-review round 1): added the 4 remaining exclusion-conjunct tests at both construction sites, each driving a recovered pre-dispatch gate-owning branch's delivered cell into a real nested-child pause (`SubAgentChildPausedError` via a faithful `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher` double) or an effect-fence-ambiguous outcome instead of a clean complete, proving exclusion from `pre_dispatch_gate_owning_branches`. Before starting step 2, grepped both construction sites and confirmed the exclusion-conjunct filter is byte-identical — no production asymmetry, safe as pure test-parity work. All 7 tests mutation-probe-verified (a naive single-marker `str.replace` correctly raised an ambiguity error when probing the workflow-id-swap guard, since PARALLELIZATION's and OW's guard text is byte-identical at two locations in the same file — disambiguated via a unique nearby anchor; sharpening added to `mutation-probe-load-bearing-witness` memory). merge-gate test-witness lens on PR #1119 found the paused-child dispatcher docstrings overclaimed which exception-catch path the tests exercise (own-dispatch vs. in-flight-cancellation-race) — both write the same disposition dict identically, so mutation-probe validity was unaffected; fixed inline, no re-gate needed.

### B-82 · Ollama dispatch branch never serializes the standard memory tools its selected access mode promises *(surfaced at the gate-(d) R-300 live re-confirmation, 2026-07-27; CLOSED 2026-07-27 — PR #1140)*

- **What it is.** `reflect_memory_provider_capabilities` reports `supports_standard_memory_tools=True` for `ollama` (verified empirically 2026-07-27), so `select_memory_access_mode` (`harness-cp/src/harness_cp/memory_access_mode.py:190-200`) returns `MemoryAccessMode.STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` for an ollama binding under a permissive policy. But the ollama arm of `RuntimeLLMDispatcher.dispatch` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/llm_dispatch.py`, the `elif provider_name == "ollama":` branch) has no standard-memory-tools counterpart to the openai arm's `_dispatch_openai_with_standard_memory_tools` — it calls the bare `_dispatch_ollama` unconditionally. The five C-MEM-14 tools the selected access mode promises are therefore never serialized into the ollama request, and no continuation loop exists to execute a returned tool call: **the selected mode silently degrades to no memory access at all.**
- **Why it is coupled to the OpenAI wire-name fix.** This is NOT itself a wire-name defect (`ollama.chat` was never handed a dotted `function.name` because it was never handed a memory tool at all). But fixing it *independently* of the wire-name map would **extend the OpenAI HTTP-400 failure class** — a dotted `tools[].function.name` rejected by `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$` — to any name-validating ollama build or downstream OpenAI-compatible shim. Any ollama injection must route through an adapter-owned wire encoding from the start.
- **Current state.** CLOSED. Discovered 2026-07-27 during the gate-(d) R-300 live re-confirmation, while diagnosing the OpenAI 400 that the wire-name map closes; deliberately left out of that fix's scope to keep the surgical diff surgical.
- **Class 3 doc-clarity observation (same discovery).** C-MEM-14's tool-name table (`Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md` lines 485-497) reads as **identity-normative**, and the per-provider **wire encoding** of those identities is **adapter-owned** — not a spec-level rename. The `MemoryToolName` enum stays dotted and provider-neutral (`memory.search` ...); only the OpenAI adapter boundary re-spells it (`memory_search` ...). Recorded here so a future reader does not mistake the wire map for a C-MEM-14 divergence.
- **Close-out steps.** (1) Decide the ollama shape: `ollama.chat` accepts an OpenAI-compatible `tools=` array, so the most likely fix is an `_dispatch_ollama_with_standard_memory_tools` sibling reusing the same tool-schema builder + continuation loop, with the wire-name map (or an ollama-specific equivalent) applied at serialization AND at inbound resolve. Ground the actual ollama tool-call response shape against a real local daemon before writing the loop — do not assume it is byte-identical to OpenAI's `choices[].message.tool_calls[]`. (2) Alternatively, if standard memory tools are deliberately out of scope for ollama, the FIX IS IN THE OTHER DIRECTION: `reflect_memory_provider_capabilities` must stop reporting `supports_standard_memory_tools=True` for ollama so the selection degrades explicitly to `PROMPT_EXTENSION_PACKET` rather than silently to nothing. Either way the current state — a selected mode the dispatch branch does not serve — is a fail-silent gap. (3) Witness with a deterministic test that captures the kwargs handed to a fake `client.chat` and asserts either the tools are present under provider-safe names, or that the selected access mode is not `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` for an ollama binding.
- **Council: mixed.** No for the mechanical half (mirroring an existing, already-reviewed openai dispatch arm). YES if grounding shows option (2) is the right answer — narrowing a declared provider capability changes which memory access mode an entire provider family receives, which is a C-MEM-13 selection-semantics decision, not impl discretion.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-27 (PR #1140) — direction (1) built; the wire-name coupling DISSOLVED by grounding, so the council gate this row raised never fired.** A live probe against the local ollama daemon (`llama3.2:3b`, client `ollama==0.6.2`) confirmed `/api/chat` accepts the DOTTED C-MEM-14 provider-neutral identities (`memory.search` ...) verbatim in `tools[].function.name` and echoes them back unchanged in `message.tool_calls[].function.name` — ollama imposes NO `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$` name grammar, so **there is no wire-name map on this path**. The "coupled to the OpenAI wire-name fix" framing above is therefore superseded: adding an ollama wire-name map would encode a **false capability claim** about the provider, and would additionally require an inbound re-resolve for names ollama never mangles. Direction (2) — narrowing `reflect_memory_provider_capabilities` — was REJECTED on the same grounding: ollama genuinely supports tool calling, so declaring otherwise would amputate the WRITE half of C-MEM-14 (`memory.write_note` / `memory.propose_promotion` / `memory.request_redaction` are unreachable through a prompt-extension packet) for the entire local-open-weight family — a C-MEM-13 selection-semantics regression, not a fix. Because grounding settled direction (1) unambiguously, the row's own "YES if grounding shows option (2)" council condition was not met.
- **What shipped (PR #1140).** `_dispatch_ollama_with_standard_memory_tools` + 6 sibling helpers in `harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/llm_dispatch.py`, gated by a branch guard byte-mirroring the OpenAI arm's predicate (`standard_memory_tool_executor is not None and memory_context is not None and memory_context.access_mode is STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS`). The loop mirrors the OpenAI arm's SHAPE (same `max_iterations=16` bound, same `_standard_memory_tool_context` scope_ref/policy_ref binding, same deepcopy-before-mutate schema discipline guarding the shared module-level `MEMORY_TOOL_CONTRACTS` against concurrent-dispatch aliasing, same `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` fail-loud convention) against ollama's DIFFERENT wire shapes: **no `choices[]` envelope** (the assistant turn is top-level `ChatResponse.message`, `ollama/_types.py:418`); **`arguments` arrives ALREADY DECODED** as a `Mapping[str, Any]` (`_types.py:346`), so the happy path does not `json.loads` (a JSON string is still tolerated defensively for hand-rolled stubs; junk and non-object payloads fail loud); and the typed client carries **no `tool_call_id` at all** — `Message`'s fields are `role`/`content`/`thinking`/`images`/`tool_name`/`tool_calls` (`_types.py:304-355`), and the daemon's tool-call id is dropped before it reaches Python — so the tool-result turn correlates BY NAME as `{"role": "tool", "tool_name": "<dotted identity>", "content": "<json>"}`; an extra `tool_call_id` key would be silently dropped by the pydantic model (default `extra='ignore'`), so it is deliberately not emitted. **Malformed-call semantics deliberately MIRROR OpenAI: fail-loud** — `_standard_memory_tool_context` raises `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` on a scope_ref mismatch and it propagates exactly as on the OpenAI path; a feed-the-error-back-as-a-tool-turn recovery mechanism was considered and rejected as a per-provider semantic divergence, i.e. a design extension. One adjacent rename: `_openai_function_tool_name` → `_function_tool_name` (the `{"type":"function","function":{"name"}}` envelope reader is genuinely provider-neutral; both arms now use it). **Witnesses:** 6 new deterministic tests in `harness-runtime/tests/test_lifecycle_llm_dispatch.py` — a serialization witness asserting all five DOTTED names plus a caller-supplied dotted duplicate deduped under a `len()` check (a set comparison alone cannot see a surviving duplicate) and the per-dispatch scope_ref/policy_ref binding leaving the shared contract schema unmutated; a loop witness asserting the executor receives the provider-neutral `MemoryToolName`, the tool-result turn's exact ollama shape, and usage read off the FINAL turn's `prompt_eval_count`/`eval_count` with no `gen_ai.response.id`; an unknown-name raise (the OpenAI wire spelling `memory_search` is NOT tolerated here — it is a name this path never advertises); a malformed-arguments raise; a scope_ref-mismatch fail-loud; and a bare-path over-fire guard proving a `PROMPT_EXTENSION_PACKET` context injects no tools. Both mutation probes confirmed: reverting the branch guard fails 3 of them, dropping the tool-result-turn append fails the loop witness. The Class 3 doc-clarity observation above stands and is now sharper: the wire encoding is adapter-owned **per adapter** — the OpenAI boundary re-spells, the ollama boundary does not.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 1 — two durable-double-write defects, BOTH pre-existing in the OpenAI sibling, fixed symmetrically in the same PR.** The mandatory sibling check found the OpenAI loop has shared both shapes since it shipped, so fixing only the new ollama arm would have left the higher-traffic provider defective *and* introduced asymmetric semantics between two loops that are meant to mirror. **[P1] batch pre-validation.** Both loops resolved-and-executed each tool call lazily inside a `messages.extend(generator)`, so with a multi-call response an earlier — possibly `memory.write_note` — call committed *before* a later call was validated (unknown name / malformed arguments / `scope_ref` mismatch). Both loops now resolve and fully validate the COMPLETE batch first, via `_openai_prepared_memory_tool_calls` / `_ollama_prepared_memory_tool_calls` returning a `tuple[_PreparedMemoryToolCall, ...]`, and only then execute in order through `_openai_memory_tool_result_messages` / `_ollama_memory_tool_result_messages`. Fail-loud semantics are unchanged — any invalid call still fails the dispatch, just before side effects instead of mid-batch. **[P2] iteration bound.** Both loops let the 16th iteration execute its batch and only *then* raised loop-exhausted, committing a terminal write for a dispatch that reports failure. Both now check the bound BEFORE executing: if a response carries tool calls and no iteration budget remains to send their results back, the exhaustion error raises with nothing executed. **Why these were P1/P2 rather than cosmetic:** both aborts classify `TRANSIENT_RETRY` — `_classify_provider_exception` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/retry_breaker_fallback.py:299-304`) fail-fasts only `LLMDispatchProviderUnreachableError` / `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` / HTTP 401-403 and maps everything else, including bare `RuntimeError` and `MemoryToolExecutionInputError`, to the transient staircase — so `RetryBreakerFallbackDispatcher` re-ran the whole dispatch from the top and the already-committed call executed a second time. **The exhaustion error type is deliberately unchanged** (the same generic `RuntimeError` both loops always raised, now built by the shared `_memory_tool_loop_exhausted` helper so the two messages cannot drift): re-typing it would change how retry routes loop exhaustion, which is a separate decision from the execute-before-raise defect and was explicitly out of scope. **4 further witnesses, one pair per provider** (a two-call batch whose second call is invalid → the executor receives ZERO calls; an all-tool-calls script driven to the bound → exactly 15 executions, 16 provider calls, exhaustion raised). Both new mutation probes confirmed: making the prepare functions lazy generators again fails both batch witnesses (the failure diff shows the leaked `MemoryToolName.WRITE_NOTE` request — the double-write class, made visible), and moving the bound check back after execution fails both iteration-bound witnesses.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 2 — two more [P1]s, again BOTH pre-existing on the OpenAI sibling, again fixed symmetrically.** **[P1-a] three of the five advertised tools were unreachable.** The dispatch-side `_standard_memory_tool_context` required `arguments["scope_ref"]` for every call, but only `memory.search` and `memory.write_note` declare `scope_ref`; `memory.read` / `memory.propose_promotion` / `memory.request_redaction` declare none **and** set `additionalProperties: false` (`harness-as/src/harness_as/memory_tool_contracts.py` — READ at `:181-217`, PROPOSE_PROMOTION at `:250-283`, REQUEST_REDACTION at `:284-307`), so a schema-conformant model *cannot* send one. Those three therefore failed at the dispatch boundary before ever reaching the executor. **Grounding verdict on OpenAI history:** latently broken there since the memory loop shipped — pre-round-1 `_openai_memory_tool_result_message` called `_standard_memory_tool_context(arguments, ...)` unconditionally with no per-tool branch, and the existing tests only ever exercised `memory.search`, which is one of the two tools that *does* declare the field. The check is now scoped to the declaring tools via `_MEMORY_TOOLS_DECLARING_SCOPE_REF`, derived from the contracts at import — mirroring **both** `_bind_schema_fixed_value`'s properties-presence authority **and** the executor's own partition, since `StandardMemoryToolExecutor._require_scope_ref` is reached only from `_search` and `_write_note` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_tool_executor.py:195` / `:262`). Nothing is weakened: the authoritative `scope_ref` on `MemoryToolExecutionContext` was always `RuntimeMemoryContext.scope_ref`, with the argument serving only as a cross-check — which is exactly why it can be scoped to the tools that carry one. **[P1-b] automatic memory broke ordinary tool workflows.** With standard-memory mode active, a caller's legitimate non-memory tool survives the dedup filter and is advertised — but a model call to it hit the memory resolver and raised `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError`. Both loops now classify the batch before touching it: if **any** call names something outside our advertised memory identities, the whole response is returned to the caller unprocessed with **zero** memory calls executed. **Grounding verdict on hallucinated names:** the bare paths (`_dispatch_openai` / `_dispatch_ollama`) return every `tool_call` to the caller untouched whatever it names — they never inspect names at all — so hallucinated names were always the caller's to handle. Returning them (rather than raising) is therefore bare-path parity, and raising would be a *new* failure mode that appears only once automatic memory is enabled — precisely the regression class this finding names. **Raises are now reserved for memory-named calls with malformed payloads**, i.e. a tool we advertised, called wrong. The choice also preserves round-1's batch atomicity: a mixed batch cannot half-commit memory before control returns. One adjacent simplification: the two round-2 accessors collapsed into the existing provider-neutral `_function_tool_name`, which reads the same `{"type":"function","function":{"name"}}` envelope for both the outbound `tools[]` array and the inbound `tool_calls[]` list. **6 further witnesses** (a `memory.read` executing end-to-end with no `scope_ref` argument; a mixed memory+caller batch returned whole with the executor untouched; a caller-only batch returned — one triple per provider), and one pre-existing witness was re-pointed: its "invalid second call" is now a *memory*-named call with malformed arguments, since a non-memory name is no longer an error. Both fixes mutation-probed — re-requiring `scope_ref` for every tool fails both `memory.read` witnesses, and dropping the caller-owned-batch return fails all four caller-tool witnesses with the exact `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` the finding reported.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 3 — two bounded [P2]s, again symmetric across both loops.** **[P2-a] the retryable exhaustion error masked a fail-fast one.** Round 2 placed the iteration-bound check before batch preparation, so a *malformed* memory call arriving in the **terminal** batch raised the generic exhaustion `RuntimeError` — which classifies `TRANSIENT_RETRY` — instead of `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError`, which is fail-fast. A request that can never succeed would therefore replay up to 16 provider calls per attempt, plus whatever writes the preceding 15 valid turns committed. Both loops now order the three guards correctly: caller-owned return → **validate the whole batch** → bound check → execute. Validation is pure, so moving it earlier costs nothing and cannot itself commit. **[P2-b] four fixtures used the wrong required key.** `memory.write_note` requires `note` (`harness-as/src/harness_as/memory_tool_contracts.py:218-249`), not `text`; all four corrected. **Grounding — where required-key validation actually lives:** the **executor**, not the dispatch layer. `StandardMemoryToolExecutor._write_note` reads it via `_string_arg(args, "note")` at `harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_tool_executor.py:266`, and `_string_arg` raises `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` at `:549-553`. The dispatch-side `_standard_memory_tool_context` validates only `scope_ref`/`policy_ref` presence-and-match — so **no** dispatch-side required-key validation was added (that would have been new semantics this arc has no authority for). Instead one witness now runs through a **real** `StandardMemoryToolExecutor` over a `tmp_path` filesystem store, constructed exactly the way `tests/test_memory_tool_executor.py::_executor` constructs it and pinned to the same `policy:u-mem-16` / `scope:u-mem-16` refs the dispatch fixtures already use — the fake executor validates nothing, so only a real one can witness this class at all. **4 further witnesses** (a malformed *terminal* batch failing fast rather than as exhaustion, one per loop; a real-executor `memory.write_note` round trip whose tool-result turn carries a genuine `memory_ref`/`operation_ref`, proving the write committed; the same call with `text` failing loud). Both fixes mutation-probed: reverting the reorder reproduces the masking `RuntimeError: … exceeded 16 continuation turns` verbatim on both loops, and restoring the `text` key fails at `memory_tool_executor.py:552`. A first draft of the terminal-batch witnesses was **found inadequate by its own probe** — they used a repeating canned response, so they raised on turn 1 and never reached the bound; the probe passed, which is what exposed them. Rewritten to script 15 valid turns followed by one malformed turn exactly at the bound (asserting 16 provider calls, 15 executions, and that the message is not the exhaustion text), after which the probe correctly failed.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 4 — one [P2] fixed, one bounded residual registered as `B-84`.** **[P2] usage was bundled from the final response only.** Both loops returned `_provider_response_bundle(final_response)`, whose usage reads that one response — so every continuation turn's tokens vanished from turn capture, `gen_ai.usage.*`, and cost attribution. **Sibling verdict: the OpenAI arm shared the shape verbatim** — `_openai_response_bundle` read `getattr(response, "usage", None)` off whichever single response it was handed, exactly like `_ollama_response_bundle` read that response's `prompt_eval_count`/`eval_count`. Both now thread a running `_UsageAttrs` accumulator through the loop, folding each turn in immediately after the provider call and *before* any exit path, so the caller-owned-batch return and the no-tool-calls return both carry full totals. `None` is treated as **absent, not zero** (a turn the provider reported nothing for must neither invent a `0` total nor erase counts already accumulated), and `response_id` is deliberately **not** accumulated — it identifies one response, so the bundle keeps the final turn's, unchanged. Witnessed by extending both existing loop tests rather than adding parallel ones: their two calls already carry distinct counts (10/4 then 12/5 on OpenAI; 20/8 then 31/9 on ollama), so the asserted sums are discriminating against *both* a final-turn-only reading and a first-turn-only one. Mutation-probed — collapsing the accumulator back to the per-turn value fails both, with `assert 12 == (10 + 12)` and `assert 31 == (20 + 31)`. **The registered residual (`B-84`):** an executor-level argument failure — e.g. `memory.read` missing the contract-required `memory_ref` — still occurs *mid-batch* during sequential execution, so a valid earlier write commits before the later call raises, and the transient classification then re-runs the dispatch and duplicates the write. Deliberately **not** fixed here: the naive fix (full per-tool contract prevalidation at the dispatch layer) would mirror-copy the executor's required-key and policy semantics into a second location — the drift class this workspace treats as a defect in its own right, and directly against round 3's grounded boundary that required-key enforcement lives at the executor.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 5 — non-tool-template ollama models were regressed by the injection itself.** `reflect_provider_capabilities` returns `supports_tools=True` for **every** ollama model string (`harness-cp/src/harness_cp/provider_capabilities.py:127`, pre-existing), so `select_memory_access_mode` picks `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` even for a model with no tool template — e.g. `llava-llama3` — and the new branch then sends `tools=`, which the daemon rejects with a 400. A dispatch that worked as plain text before B-82 started failing. **Fixed narrowly, with the daemon as the authority rather than a capability table** (a per-model table is a real design question and deliberately out of scope): on the **first** chat call of the memory loop only, a tool-unsupported rejection re-dispatches through the bare `_dispatch_ollama` **without** tools, byte-restoring the pre-B-82 path for exactly those models. *(Superseded at B-83 / PR #1142: the retry remains tool-free — the daemon fix is intact — but now carries the C-MEM-12 packet folded into the single system source when the B-83 repair is authorized; the byte-restore property was itself B-83's defect on this path.)* First call only — a mid-loop rejection would mean the model changed identity between continuation turns, which stays fail-loud. **Both SDK facts were probed live rather than assumed:** `ollama.ResponseError` (`ollama/_types.py:632-651`) carries only `.error` (str) and `.status_code`, giving no machine-readable discriminator, and against a local daemon on ollama 0.6.2 the tool rejection is `.error = 'registry.ollama.ai/library/llava-llama3:latest does not support tools'` with `.status_code = 400`, while a model-not-found is the *same type* with `.error = "model 'x' not found"` and `.status_code = 404` — so a blanket `except ResponseError` would have swallowed unrelated faults, and the message substring really is the only available discriminator. **The brittleness is documented at the catch site**: matching an upstream string means an upstream rewording stops the match — but it then fails **closed** (a loud dispatch failure), never silently absorbing a different error. The predicate duck-types on the class name plus the `.error` substring rather than importing `ollama`, keeping this module free of provider-SDK imports like every other dispatch branch; the *tests* use the real `ollama.ResponseError`, so the shape stays faithful. **The OpenAI loop needs no equivalent and none was added** — every OpenAI chat model supports tools, so the failure mode does not exist there and a mirrored catch would be dead code. **3 witnesses, each mutation-probed against a distinct conjunct:** removing the fallback fails the fallback witness; widening the catch to any `ResponseError` fails the model-not-found propagation witness; dropping the first-call restriction fails the mid-loop fail-loud witness. A **fourth** issue Codex raised in the same round — the SDK silently dropping declared schema constraints — was verified, judged an aggravator of an existing registered class rather than a new defect, and **recorded on `B-84`** instead of fixed here. Full harness-runtime non-e2e suite green (**3123 passed / 10 skipped**).

### B-83 · No dispatch-side guard that the selected `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` access mode is actually served by the provider arm reached *(surfaced by the B-82 fix, 2026-07-27; CLOSED 2026-07-28 — PR #1142)*

- **What it is.** B-82 was one instance of a general shape: `select_memory_access_mode` (`harness-cp/src/harness_cp/memory_access_mode.py:158-245`) picks an access mode from *declared provider capabilities*, and `RuntimeLLMDispatcher.dispatch` then routes to a *provider arm* — but nothing checks that the arm reached actually serves the mode selected. With B-82 closed, both arms that `reflect_memory_provider_capabilities` can select `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` for (openai, ollama) now serve it, so the defect is not currently reachable through the DEFAULT capability reflection. It remains reachable two ways: **(a)** a caller supplying `MemoryAccessModeRequest.provider_capabilities` explicitly (the `request.provider_capabilities or reflect_...` short-circuit at `memory_access_mode.py:163-166`) can declare `supports_standard_memory_tools=True` for anthropic or an external-CLI binding, whose dispatch arms ignore memory tools entirely — the anthropic arm branches only on `step_has_memory_tool(payload.tools)`/`hitl_tool_loop`, never on access mode, and `_payload_to_external_cli_prompt` is text-only by contract; **(b)** more importantly, a **statically injected** `RuntimeLLMDispatcher.memory_context` (the no-`memory_runtime` path at `llm_dispatch.py:1302`) is composed ONCE and is NOT recomposed when `RetryBreakerFallbackDispatcher` rebinds the provider per attempt (`retry_breaker_fallback.py:188-191` constructs a fresh `StepEffectiveBinding` with a different provider) — so a context composed for openai under `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` can be dispatched against an anthropic or external-CLI fallback and degrade to zero memory access, silently. The `memory_runtime`-bound path is NOT affected (it recomposes per attempt via `compose_for_dispatch`).
- **Grounding note — what this row is NOT.** The B-82 grounding pass also checked whether C-MEM-13's `no_memory_access is a valid policy outcome and must be ledgered when memory was requested` (`Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md:463`) lacks a ledgering witness. It does NOT: `MemoryContextComposer.compose_run_start` ledgers the denial via `_write_injection_decision` and emits a `DENIAL` telemetry span with `POLICY_DENIAL` failure class (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_context.py:165-205`), and a direct witness already exists at `harness-runtime/tests/test_memory_context.py:364-388` (`test_no_memory_access_denial_is_explicit_and_ledgered_without_retrieval`). That candidate finding is recorded here as CHECKED-AND-CLOSED rather than registered, so a future reader does not re-open it.
- **Reachability (c) — DEFAULT-reachable, added 2026-07-27 correcting this row's own earlier claim.** This row originally asserted the defect was "not reachable through the default capability reflection". **That is false, and was falsified by the B-82 round-5 fallback filed the same day.** `reflect_provider_capabilities` returns `supports_tools=True` for every ollama model string (`harness-cp/src/harness_cp/provider_capabilities.py:127`), so a model with no tool template — `llava-llama3`, say — selects `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` through **plain default reflection**, and the round-5 fallback then deliberately re-dispatches it bare, with no memory access at all and **no ledger trace of the degradation**. That is precisely this row's shape: a selected access mode the reached provider path does not serve, degrading silently. The fallback is still the right call for its own problem (it byte-restores a previously-working text dispatch, and the daemon is the only authority on which models take tools) — it simply makes B-83 reachable on the default path rather than only via the two exotic routes below, which raises this row's priority. Recorded here rather than fixed because the honest fix is the same posture decision (1) already owes.
- **Adjacent, same family — the caller-owned mixed-batch return.** When a response mixes a memory call with a caller-advertised tool call, both loops return the whole response unprocessed (B-82 round 2, for batch atomicity + bare-path parity). The memory call the model asked for is therefore **not served, and nothing records that it wasn't** — the caller cannot execute it either, since it does not own the memory tools. Same fail-silent family as this row, and it is a *consequence of a deliberate tradeoff* rather than an independent defect, so it is recorded here under this row's scope rather than registered separately: whatever posture (1) settles on — fail-loud, or explicit ledgered degradation — should cover this exit path too.
- **Current state.** **CLOSED 2026-07-28 (PR #1142)** — dispatch-time serve-check + repair-to-packet, covering (a), (b) and (c) from one authority.
- **What was built.** `_standard_memory_tools_context` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/llm_dispatch.py`) is now the single predicate answering *"will THIS dispatch inject the C-MEM-14 tools?"*, read by both provider arm guards **and** by the serve-check, so the branch taken and the check applied cannot disagree. Its provider-identity conjunct (`selection.selected_provider != provider_name`) **detects** **(b)** — `MemoryAccessModeSelection.selected_provider` is `request.model_binding.provider` verbatim (`harness-cp/src/harness_cp/memory_access_mode.py:372`), the same string that reaches the dispatch, and the `memory_runtime`-bound path recomposes per attempt so the conjunct is inert there. When `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` was selected and the arm will not serve it — anthropic, external-CLI, or an openai/ollama dispatch with no bound executor — the already-retrieved, already-policy-checked C-MEM-12 packet is rendered into the effective system prompt through a new `render_packet_for_degraded_dispatch` sibling in `memory_context.py` (identical body to `render_prompt_extension_packet` via a shared `_render_packet`; only the access-mode gate is dropped, so every existing caller is byte-identical) and appended through the shared `append_rendered_memory_packet` seam. Reachability **(c)** repairs at its own site inside the ollama loop and reports the repair back on a third return element, so one emitter covers all three.
- **Two dispositions — repair vs. report-only** *(split at out-of-family Codex round 1, [P1-a])*. **(a)** and **(c)** are **repaired**: the context was composed *for the dispatched provider*, so its packet is provider-matched by construction and discloses nothing the selection had not already cleared. **(b)** is **report-only** — neither the tools *nor the packet* reach the rebound provider. Retrieval and injection enforce **provider-family scope before ranking** (`Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md:481`) and the packet was assembled under provider X's `MemoryScope.provider_family`, so forwarding its *text* to a Y-family provider bypasses that scope boundary — a **disclosure** leak, which read-only framing does not fix and which the write-authority argument this row originally gave does not cover. Recomposition is impossible on that path by construction (it is definitionally the `memory_runtime`-**unbound** configuration) and fail-closed would convert a silent degradation into a run failure, so the degradation is made **visible** in telemetry rather than repaired.
- **Telemetry.** Exactly one `memory.operation` span per degraded dispatch, with every attribute reporting what *actually* happened so the two dispositions are distinguishable without parsing a reason string. **Repaired** — `operation_name=injection`, `operation_kind=inject`, `access_mode=prompt_extension_packet`, the *served* `packet_hash` + `record_count`, and **no** `failure_class` (a successful injection through a different seam, not a failure). **Report-only** — `operation_name=denial`, `operation_kind=inject`, `access_mode=no_memory_access`, `record_count=0`, and **no** `packet_hash` (claiming a served hash for a withheld packet would be a lie); this mirrors `compose_run_start`'s own DENIAL+INJECT shape for the no-memory outcome. `failure_class` is deliberately unset on both: no member of the closed `MemoryTelemetryFailureClass` describes a provider-scope mismatch, and reusing `POLICY_DENIAL` would assert a decision no resolver made. The B-83 detail (`memory.degraded_serve` + `.reason` — `arm_unservable` | `provider_scope_mismatch` — + `.selected_access_mode`) lands on the memory tracer's span only, never on the `gen_ai` span, per C-OD-04 §4.3 namespace discipline; the reasons are span-attribute **values**, never new enum members.
- **Posture chosen — repair, not fail-loud.** Close-out step (1) leaned toward the fail-loud raise. Grounding reversed it: a raise turns a currently-silent degradation into a **run failure** for any deployment on the injected-context + fallback shape, while C-MEM-13 (`Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md:460` — "Providers without usable tool support may receive prompt-extension packets") already sanctions serving the packet. C-MEM-14 (`:489`) states the exposure as a present-tense obligation, so serving *something* is the contract-faithful reading. Both cites verified byte-exact at the arc.
- **NOT built, deliberately — recorded so nobody silently absorbs it.** The literal *"explicit **ledgered** degradation"* half of close-out step (1) was **not** implemented. Writing a degradation *entry* (as opposed to the span above) is fork-shaped: the load-bearing blocker is the **absent store carrier on the dispatch path** (the memory-operation sink is reachable only via the composer/executor privates; a dispatcher-visible carrier is a new Protocol/field surface), i.e. an X-AL-3 design extension routed through back-flow, not impl discretion. *(Qualified at the merge-gate: an entry reusing the run-start DENIAL+INJECT shape needs no new enum members — `compose_run_start` already ledgers a no-memory outcome with existing members; new members on the three closed enums — `MemoryOperationKind`, `MemoryTelemetryOperationName`, `MemoryTelemetryFailureClass` — would be required only for a dispatch-time-DISTINCT operation kind, if one were wanted.)*
- **Behavior change, flagged.** The B-82 round-5 ollama fallback **no longer byte-restores the pre-B-82 bare dispatch**. The retry still drops the *tools* (which is what the daemon rejected, so the daemon fix is intact) but now carries the packet as read-only prompt text. The R5 comment block states this explicitly, and its witness was updated to the new contract and renamed `test_ollama_tool_unsupported_model_falls_back_to_the_tool_free_path` rather than deleted.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 7 (final fix round before merge-gate) — one [P2] at the intersection of the authorization and observability surfaces.** **[P2-e]** the span always reported `packet_policy_denied` whenever the packet fallback was ineligible, because the carrier was a **boolean**. A run denied by an **empty token budget**, or by an **absent provider capability**, was therefore reported as an operator **policy** decision — a false accusation about a configuration the operator never made, and one that points any investigation at the wrong file. Fixed by replacing `RuntimeMemoryContext.prompt_packet_fallback_eligible: bool` with `prompt_packet_fallback_denial: str | None` — `None` means authorized; otherwise the counterfactual selector's **own** `MemoryAccessModeDenialReason` value, carried verbatim to the span.
- **Grounded correction to the round-7 brief's own hypothesis.** The brief anticipated three distinguishable causes (policy / budget / capability). The selector yields only **two** reachable values: `token_budget_empty` for the budget gate, and `no_supported_mode` for **both** a denying `injection_access` *and* an absent `supports_prompt_extension_packet` — it does not separate those two once past its packet branch. Verified empirically across all five configurations before writing a line.
