The fix therefore removes the **false** distinction without inventing one the selector cannot observe, and a witness asserts that non-distinction explicitly so a later reader does not mistake it for a gap. The fail-safe default is a `not_derived` sentinel, asserted **disjoint** from every real `MemoryAccessModeDenialReason` value, so an underived context is never mistaken for a gate that actually closed.
- **Recorded residual of the observability surface → B-87.** The `finally` emission introduced at round 6 over-reports a **pre-wire** translation failure as a completed injection — packet_hash present, `operation.name=injection`, and **zero adapter calls made**. Registered rather than fixed: the imprecision **over**-reports (it claims a disclosure that never left the process, so an investigator over-responds rather than misses a leak), and this arc reached its fix-round cap at Codex R7. *(Superseded — B-87 CLOSED 2026-07-29 at PR #1149; see its own row.)*
- **Out-of-family Codex round 6 — one [P2], a fourth defect shape: the *observability* of the decision.** **[P2-d]** the degraded-serve span was emitted from a **post-dispatch** block, so whenever the provider call raised — timeout, adapter error, or the bare ollama retry itself failing — control left before emission and the only B-83-specific signal vanished. By then the packet had already been **sent** (the disclosure happened) or already been **withheld** (the denial happened): precisely the cases where the signal matters most. Fixed by moving emission into a `finally` around the whole arm-dispatch chain, with `memory.degraded_serve.dispatch_outcome` (`completed` | `provider_error` | `cancelled`) plus `memory.degraded_serve.provider_error_type` on failure. Cancellation is labelled separately rather than blamed on the provider. The enclosing `gen_ai` span already records the exception itself through OTel's `record_exception` / `set_status_on_exception` defaults — which this module relies on everywhere rather than calling explicitly — so the short degraded span carries the class as an attribute instead.
- **The round-2 return-bundle choice had to be reversed.** The R5 fallback decides its degradation *inside* the arm call and reported it as a tuple element — which travels **only on success**. An R5 retry that itself failed therefore lost the report entirely, and the arm site's own local was `None` on that path (the serve-check returns `None` for a servable arm). Replaced with a `_DegradedMemoryServeSink` single-slot carrier, written the moment the disposition is settled and **before** the retry it describes. Both halves — the `finally` and the publish-before-retry ordering — are separately load-bearing and separately probed.
- **Residual exit paths, stated plainly.** `asyncio.CancelledError` propagates *through* `finally`, so the span is still emitted — witnessed, not assumed — and the same holds for any `BaseException`. The only remaining losses are process-level: `SIGKILL`, a hard interpreter crash, or an OTel exporter dropping the span downstream. No in-process placement can address those, and all of them lose the enclosing `gen_ai` span too, so the degraded span is no weaker than the dispatch telemetry it accompanies.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 5 — one [P1], a third defect shape: the repair's own *authorization*.** **[P1-e]** the repair rendered the packet **without evaluating the prompt-packet mode's own gates**. `select_memory_access_mode` returns at the *first* mode whose gates pass, so a `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` selection proves **nothing** about `PROMPT_EXTENSION_PACKET` — that branch was never evaluated. A policy allowing `standard_tool_access` while `injection_access` denies prompt packets is legal and expressible (`DENY` is the field default, and the in-repo `test_external_cli_route_metadata_composes_with_standard_tool_context` already builds exactly that shape), and the packet branch additionally requires `token_budget > 0` which the tools branch does not. The degradation could therefore disclose memory **through a mode the operator switched off**, on an unbudgeted run.
- **Authority gap, stated plainly.** The needed inputs — `workflow_policy` / `step_policy` / `token_budget` / `provider_capabilities` — live on the **composition request** and are on *neither* `RuntimeMemoryContext` *nor* `MemoryAccessModeSelection`. The dispatcher genuinely cannot answer the question at dispatch time. Rather than mirror-copying policy predicates into harness-runtime (the drift class this workspace treats as a defect in its own right) or parsing `decision_trace` strings, the answer is settled at **composition**, where the inputs are in scope, by **re-running the real `select_memory_access_mode`** with native + standard-tools capabilities masked off — the exact counterfactual the repair faces — and carried forward on a new additive `RuntimeMemoryContext.prompt_packet_fallback_eligible`. The probe is pure (the selector is documented "without touching credentials") and composes the whole decision tree in canonical order with **zero duplicated logic**. The field **defaults False**: a context assembled anywhere but `compose_run_start` has not proven eligibility, and unproven reads as ineligible. `compose_run_start` is the *only* production producer (the static `RuntimeLLMDispatcher.memory_context` seam has no production wiring — `materialize_llm_dispatcher_stage` defaults it `None`), so production coverage is total while hand-built contexts fail safe.
- **Fixture honesty.** The B-83 dispatch fixtures **were** silently relying on the unchecked path: their implied `policy:u-mem-16` document leaves `injection_access` at its `DENY` default. The flag is now an **explicit** fixture parameter (default `True`, i.e. those witnesses model an `injection_access=PROMPT_PACKET` policy with a non-empty budget) instead of an unstated assumption.
- **The authorization set is exhaustive against the selector's own decision tree.** The repair requires **(1)** provider identity, **(2)** provider-family scope, **(3)** prompt-packet eligibility — and (3) composes *every* gate on the path to `PROMPT_EXTENSION_PACKET`: `policy.enabled`, `retrieval_access` DENY, external-CLI route/auth denial, `supports_prompt_extension_packet`, `injection_access` ≠ `PROMPT_PACKET`, and `token_budget <= 0`. Verified mechanically by driving each of those six gates negative one at a time: three (`enabled`, `retrieval_access`, external-CLI auth) collapse the selection to `NO_MEMORY_ACCESS`, where `_packet_matches_mode` forbids a packet at all and the repair is unreachable **by construction**; the other three leave `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` selected (repair reachable) and the eligibility flag correctly reads `False`. There is no seventh gate on that path.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 4 — one [P2], the anthropic member of the fold-shape family.** **[P2-c]** the fold asked *"is there an existing system source?"* by **truthiness**, while the conflict check asks it by **presence** (`"system" in kwargs`). Two *valid* shapes slipped through the gap — the SDK content-block **array** (the same `[{"type": "text", ...}]` form `_anthropic_system_cache_block` itself emits) and the **empty string** — landing in the `system`-variable branch, where the still-present `params["system"]` then collided and raised `PromptInjectionConflictError`: the repair converting a *valid* dispatch into a failure. Fixed by keying the fold on `"system" in payload.params`, so **the fold predicate and the conflict predicate agree on what "present" means**, and by routing the value through round 3's `_content_with_appended_packet` — whose sequence branch appends a `{"type": "text", ...}` block, a valid Anthropic `TextBlockParam` (`cache_control` is optional) — so no divergent serialization was introduced.
- **Completeness sweep — the fold family is closed.** Run through the *real* translators across **17 arm × shape combinations**. For each of the four arms the **fold predicate now mirrors that arm's own conflict predicate**: anthropic on `"system" in payload.params`; openai/ollama on a leading `role:"system"` in the **post-`params`-merge** `kwargs["messages"]` (so a `params["messages"]` override is covered, matching `_inject_leading_system_message`); external-CLI on a leading `role:"system"` in `payload.messages` (that renderer ignores `params` for messages, so there is no post-merge divergence to cover). Every value shape reaching a fold is handled totally — `_content_with_appended_packet` (str / `None` / sequence / other) for anthropic+openai+ollama, `_external_cli_message_content` (str / other) for the CLI. All 17 combinations kept the original text **and** carried the packet, with no raise and exactly one system source. Ollama's reachable set is narrower by construction: `Message.content` is `Optional[str]`.
- **One residual, characterized rather than fixed.** The "exactly one system source" property is **index-0-scoped** on openai/ollama — a `role:"system"` message at index > 0 is seen by neither the conflict check nor the fold, so the fold prepends a leading one and the wire carries two system messages. That is the **pre-existing** R-PM-1 conflict-check semantics, not a B-83 regression; it raises nothing, replaces nothing, and OpenAI accepts multiple system messages.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 3 — two bounded [P2]s, both inside the fold helpers round 2 introduced.** **[P2-a]** OpenAI accepts a system-message `content` as either a plain string *or* a content-part **array**, and the fold coerced it through `str()` before appending — putting a Python repr (`"[{'type': 'text', ...}]"`, reproduced verbatim at the probe) on the wire and destroying the parts. Fixed with `_content_with_appended_packet`, which folds each shape in its own terms: string/`None` → string append; a parts **sequence** → one **additional** `{"type": "text", "text": ...}` part (the same part shape this module already emits at the Anthropic system block and the external-CLI response) with every original part preserved in order; any other shape → JSON-serialized (`sort_keys` + `default=str`, matching `_inject_upstream_context_message`) then string-appended, so an unexpected shape is neither mangled nor raised on. **Grounded and skipped for ollama:** `ollama._types.Message.content` is `Optional[str]` (`ollama/_types.py:312`) — str-only, so a parts array cannot reach the daemon through the typed client; ollama shares the helper but can only ever take the string branch. **[P2-b]** the external-CLI fold passed `None` to the append helper for any non-string content, **replacing** the step's own system instructions with the packet alone — and since the external-CLI arm is text-only it *always* degrades `standard_memory_tools`, making that the **common** path there rather than an edge case. Fixed by extracting the renderer's own serialization into `_external_cli_message_content` and reusing it at **both** the render loop and the fold, so a repaired dispatch can never represent the same content differently from an unrepaired one.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 2 — the same two defect shapes, one layer deeper.** **[P1-c]** the round-1 scope conjunct was **identity**-level while the disclosure boundary is **family**-level. On the `memory_runtime` path a cross-family fallback *recomposes* the context for the current candidate — so `selection.selected_provider` matches and the identity conjunct passes — while `compose_for_dispatch` keeps deriving `record_scope.provider_family` from `fallback_chain.primary.family` (`automatic_memory.py:191-199`), and `MemoryRetriever._scope_mismatch` (`memory_retrieval.py:371-384`) filters records on exactly that field. A **primary-family** packet therefore reached the ollama R5 retry and was rendered. Fixed with a family conjunct keyed on the packet's *actual* `record_scope.provider_family` versus `provider_family_for_provider(provider)` (`cross_family_cost_tag.py:82` — the workspace's existing provider→family authority; a second mapping here would be the mirror-copy drift class), reason `provider_family_scope_mismatch`, with both conjuncts now living in one shared `_degraded_serve_disposition` read by the arm-selection check *and* the R5 site. An absent scope or absent family counts as **not** matching: `_scope_mismatch` treats a `None` request family as "no constraint", so the packet may hold records of any family — unverifiable is not safe. This also exposed a **fixture lie**: the test helper hardcoded `provider_family=OPENAI` for every context regardless of provider, so the anthropic/ollama witnesses were asserting a scope their provider did not have; the fixture now derives family from provider through the same authority. **[P1-d]** the *arm-selection* repair still appended the packet as a **second** system source — the exact class fixed at the R5 site in round 2 — so a payload carrying its own system prompt raised `PromptInjectionConflictError` across all four translators. Fixed by threading the packet as a `memory_packet` parameter so **each translator folds it into its own single effective system source** (Anthropic's `params["system"]`-or-kwarg, folded *before* the cache-breakpoint decision so the block covers the merged text; OpenAI/Ollama's leading `role:"system"` message post-`params`-merge; the external CLI's system section) rather than duplicating each translator's rule at the call site. A genuine **two-base** collision (active prompt *and* payload system) still fails loud — witnessed.
- **Grounding finding, registered not absorbed → B-86.** The *servable* tools path crosses the same family boundary with strictly greater reach: the injected schemas bind `scope_ref`/`policy_ref` as fixed values, so a cross-family candidate can `memory.search` across **and** `memory.write_note` into the primary family's scope — read disclosure *and* write attribution, where the packet leak was read-only. Pre-existing since U-MEM-16/B-82 and gated on a C-MEM-11/13 scope-semantics decision, so it is registered rather than absorbed here.
- **Out-of-family Codex round 1 — two real [P1]s, both fixed on this branch, both reproduced empirically before fixing.** **[P1-a]** the (b) repair forwarded provider X's packet to a Y-family provider — the scope leak above; (b) became report-only. **[P1-b]** the R5 retry supplied the packet as a **second** system source, so `_inject_leading_system_message` raised `PromptInjectionConflictError` for any payload already leading with a `{"role":"system"}` message — i.e. the previously-working tool-free fallback hard-failed for the idiomatic self-system-prompt step shape. Closed by folding the packet into the **one** effective system source (`_fold_memory_packet_into_system`, plus a defaulted `memory_packet` kwarg on `_dispatch_ollama` so every other caller stays byte-identical), operating on the **finished kwargs** post-`params`-merge for exactly the reason `_inject_leading_system_message` does — a `params["messages"]` override can hide the real leading system message, so a payload-level merge would reintroduce the collision.
- **Witnesses.** 27 deterministic tests (anthropic override; external-CLI arm; provider-rebound context report-only on a tool-capable openai arm; cross-family recomposed context report-only through the R5 path; ollama tools-unsupported retry; the R5 retry against a payload-carried system message; the arm-selection repair against a payload-carried system message; the arm-selection repair against Anthropic's `params["system"]`; a two-base collision still failing loud; a servable-path over-fire guard; an OpenAI content-part-array system message; an external-CLI mapping-content system message; an anthropic `params["system"]` block array; an empty-string `params["system"]`; a packet-policy-denied context at the arm site; the same at the R5 site; two composer-level eligibility-derivation tests against the real selector; a repair-carrying dispatch whose provider call raises; a report-only dispatch whose provider call raises; an R5 retry that itself fails; a cancelled dispatch; a success-path outcome assertion; an underived-eligibility context reporting `not_derived`; a sentinel-disjointness check; active-prompt composition ordering) and 16 mutation probes: disabling the serve-check fails 4; reverting the R5 packet injection fails only (c); dropping the provider-identity conjunct fails only (b); *repairing* the provider mismatch fails only the (b) report-only witness; reverting to the two-source retry shape reproduces `PromptInjectionConflictError` at the P1-b witness; dropping the family conjunct fails only the cross-family witness; reverting the arm-selection repair to append-as-second-source reproduces `PromptInjectionConflictError` at both P1-d witnesses; reverting the OpenAI content fold to `str()` fails the parts-array witness on a Python repr; reverting the external-CLI fold to the `None`-pass fails the mapping-content witness with the step's directive entirely absent from the prompt; reverting the anthropic fold to the truthiness key reproduces `PromptInjectionConflictError` at **both** the block-array and empty-string witnesses; dropping the eligibility conjunct fails both packet-policy-denied witnesses (arm site *and* R5 site); stubbing the composition-time derivation to always-eligible fails both composer-level derivation witnesses; moving the emission back post-dispatch fails all four exception-path witnesses; publishing the R5 sink *after* its retry instead of before fails the R5-retry-failure witness alone; collapsing the composer derivation back to one label fails both composer-level precise-reason witnesses; collapsing the span reason at the disposition fails all three dispatch-level precise-reason witnesses.
- **Scope split.** The mixed memory+caller batch case, previously recorded under this row's "adjacent, same family" note, is genuinely unrepairable at its site and moved to **B-85**.
- **Close-out steps.** (1) Decide the failure posture: a fail-loud typed error at the dispatch boundary when `memory_context.access_mode is STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` and the resolved provider arm has no standard-memory-tools sibling, versus an explicit, ledgered degradation to `PROMPT_EXTENSION_PACKET`/`NO_MEMORY_ACCESS` (which C-MEM-13:463 would then require be ledgered — the check-and-closed note above confirms the ledgering mechanism already exists, only the dispatch-side re-composition trigger would be new). Fail-loud is the cheaper and more consistent reading given the surrounding `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError`/`MemoryToolExecutionInputError` conventions. (2) Separately decide whether a statically injected `memory_context` should be re-composed (or invalidated) on a fallback provider rebinding, or whether that configuration should simply be refused at bind time when a `fallback_chain` is also bound — the latter is a much smaller surface. (3) Witness with two deterministic tests: an explicit-capability-override dispatch against the anthropic arm, and a rebinding test where a `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` context composed for openai is dispatched through a cross-family fallback to anthropic.
- **Council: likely for step (1), no for step (3).** Choosing fail-loud versus explicit-ledgered-degradation is a C-MEM-13 selection-semantics decision with a blast-radius dimension (a fail-loud raise turns a currently-silent degradation into a run failure for any deployment relying on the injected-context + fallback configuration) — C10 (blast radius) paired with whichever voice owns the memory access-mode selection semantics. Step (2)'s bind-time refusal is mechanical once (1) is settled.

### B-84 · Memory tool batch: an executor-level argument failure mid-batch can commit earlier writes, then retry-duplicate them *(surfaced by out-of-family Codex round 4 on the B-82 arc, 2026-07-27)*

- **What it is.** The B-82 arc's round-1 fix made both memory tool loops validate the COMPLETE batch before executing any of it, which closed the *dispatch-side* half of the partial-batch problem. A second half remains, one layer down: validation the DISPATCH layer legitimately does not perform still happens mid-execution, at the executor. Concretely — a batch of `[memory.write_note (valid), memory.read (missing the contract-required `memory_ref`)]` passes every dispatch-side check, so `_openai_memory_tool_result_messages` / `_ollama_memory_tool_result_messages` execute the calls in order: the write COMMITS a durable record, then the read raises `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` from `_string_arg` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_tool_executor.py:549-553`). That error **was** retry-classified TRANSIENT — `_classify_provider_exception` fail-fasted only `LLMDispatchProviderUnreachableError` / `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` / HTTP 401-403 and mapped everything else to the staircase — so `RetryBreakerFallbackDispatcher` re-ran the whole dispatch from the top and the already-committed write executed a second time. Same double-write class as the round-1 [P1], reached through a different door. **PARTIAL MITIGATION LANDED (2026-07-28)** — `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` now joins the fail-fast branch of `_classify_provider_exception` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/retry_breaker_fallback.py:326-334`), so the retry staircase no longer re-runs the dispatch for this class: on the default single-candidate chain a mid-batch executor input failure now consumes exactly ONE dispatch instead of `max_attempts`. **The row stays `registered_finding`** — the retry-duplication half is closed, the partial-commit half is not: the earlier write still commits before the later call raises, and a MULTI-candidate fallback chain still re-dispatches the whole batch on each remaining candidate (one extra copy per candidate, versus `max_attempts` × candidates before).
- **What IS already prevalidated (so the residual is genuinely narrow).** The batch pre-pass built at B-82 rounds 1/3 resolves and checks, for EVERY call, before anything executes: the tool_call wire shape (`function` is a mapping; OpenAI's `id` present and non-empty); the name resolving to a `MemoryToolName` (via the wire map on the OpenAI arm, dotted-only on the ollama arm); the `arguments` payload normalizing to a mapping (already-decoded on ollama, JSON-decoded on OpenAI, fail-loud otherwise); and the `RuntimeMemoryContext` checks — `record_scope`/`scope_ref` present, and the `scope_ref` argument matching the context for the two tools whose contracts declare it. What it does NOT check is per-tool required-argument keys (`note` for `write_note`, `memory_ref` for `read`/`propose_promotion`/`request_redaction`, `query` for `search`, …) or per-tool policy gates — because those are the EXECUTOR's semantics, not the dispatch layer's.
- **Why it was not fixed in the B-82 arc.** The obvious fix — add full per-tool contract prevalidation at the dispatch layer — would **mirror-copy the executor's own required-key and policy semantics into a second location**, which is precisely the drift class this workspace treats as a defect in its own right (see `deferred-mechanism-spec-leg-exit-on-soundness` / the one-source-of-truth discipline): the copy silently rots the first time a contract's required set changes, and the two copies then disagree about which calls are legal. Round 3 established the grounded boundary empirically — required-key enforcement lives at `memory_tool_executor.py:266` -> `:549-553`, and the dispatch layer validates only `scope_ref`/`policy_ref` — so widening the dispatch layer here would be inventing new dispatch semantics, not honoring existing ones. Registered rather than fixed unilaterally.
- **AGGRAVATOR — the ollama SDK silently drops the schema constraints the contracts declare** *(out-of-family Codex round 5; verified empirically at registration time against the locked `ollama==0.6.2`)*. The client **revalidates** caller-supplied tool mappings as its own `ollama.Tool` model before posting to `/api/chat` — `ChatRequest.tools: Optional[Sequence[Tool]]` (`ollama/_types.py:400`) — and that model is a **closed field subset** layered over a plain pydantic `BaseModel`: `SubscriptableBaseModel` (`_types.py:19`) sets no `model_config`, so pydantic's default `extra='ignore'` applies; `Tool.Function.Parameters` (`_types.py:365-371`) declares only `type` / `$defs` / `items` / `required` / `properties`, and `Parameters.Property` (`_types.py:372-379`) declares only `type` / `items` / `description` / `enum`. Every other JSON-Schema keyword is therefore discarded en route to the daemon, with no error and no warning. Measured directly against the real C-MEM-14 contracts by round-tripping them through `Tool.model_validate`: `memory.search` loses top-level `additionalProperties: false`, `limit.minimum: 1`, and `query.minLength: 1`; `memory.write_note` loses `note.minLength: 1`. The model consequently never sees the constraints the contracts declare, which **raises the odds it emits an argument payload the executor then rejects** — feeding precisely this row's mid-batch / transient-retry class. This is an aggravator only: it changes the likelihood, not the mechanism, and the mitigation menu below is unchanged. (Orthogonal to the separate, already-fixed B-82 round-5 issue of non-tool-template models 400-ing on `tools=` at all.)
- **Current state.** REGISTERED, partially mitigated (the fail-fast reclassification below landed 2026-07-28; the partial-commit half is what keeps this row open). Reachability requires a multi-call batch in which an earlier call is a valid write AND a later call is dispatch-valid but executor-invalid. Narrower than the round-1 [P1] it descends from (which needed only a malformed later call of any kind), and unreachable for single-call batches, which is the common shape.
- **Close-out steps.** Two candidate directions, both real, neither obviously dominant: **(1) an executor-exposed pure `validate()` pre-pass** — the executor grows a side-effect-free method that runs exactly the argument/policy checks `_execute_authorized` would run, and the dispatch batch pre-pass calls it for every call before executing any. Keeps ONE source of truth (the executor still owns the semantics; dispatch only asks) at the cost of a new public surface on the executor and a discipline that every future check lands in the shared path rather than inline. **(2) transactional / journaled batch execution at the executor** — the executor accepts a batch and either commits all durable operations or none, via the existing memory-operation ledger as the journal. Strictly stronger (it also covers a mid-batch failure that no pre-pass could predict, e.g. a policy resolver flipping or an I/O error) but a materially larger change touching durable-write ordering, and it needs a decision about what "rollback" means for an append-only canonical store — likely compensating ledger entries rather than deletion. **The third, cheaper mitigation is LANDED (2026-07-28) and does not close this row:** executor-level input errors are now fail-FAST rather than transient. **Grounding verdict before building — IMPL-DISCRETION, not spec-enumerated.** Runtime spec §14.6 D2 (`design-substrate/Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md:3796`) states the predicate as *"Retry-eligible iff the provider exception matches CP §21.2 transient staircase (`REFLEXION` / `RETRY_WITH_BACKOFF` stages); fail-fast otherwise (AUTH, payload-shape, shutdown)"* — fail-fast is the RESIDUAL class and the parenthetical is illustrative, not a closed set. What IS closed is the §14.5 C-RT-15 fail-class table (`:3678-3685`) and CP C-CP-21 §21.1's five-value `ValidatorRetryExitClass` (`design-substrate/Spec_Control_Plane_v1_2.md:1843-1878`, cardinality-5 closed); neither enumerates which concrete Python exception types map to which class — that mapping is the classifier's, and its own docstring already declared itself *"MVP discrimination (conservative)"*. A contract-invalid C-MEM-14 memory tool call matches nothing in §21.2 (network / rate-limit / 5xx) and is the same permanent malformed-input class as `RT-FAIL-PAYLOAD-SHAPE` raised one layer deeper, so the reclassification moves the impl TOWARD the spec predicate rather than extending it (no X-AL-3 surface). **One correction to this row's own earlier rationale:** "the same arguments will fail identically" is imprecise — a retry re-dispatches to the provider and re-samples the tool-call arguments, so a retry *could* emit valid ones. The defensible rationale is the spec predicate + the `RT-FAIL-PAYLOAD-SHAPE` parity, and what fail-fast removes is the duplicate durable commit, not merely wasted attempts. **Scope — `MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError` deliberately EXCLUDED:** `_read_record_by_ref` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_tool_executor.py:446-449`) wraps ANY store exception, including genuinely transient I/O, into the denied class, and `_allowed_index_entry` (`:424-435`) raises it off mutable index/store state — fail-fasting the `MemoryToolExecutionError` base would kill retries for real flakes; and whether a policy DENIAL is permanently terminal is a C-MEM policy-semantics decision this narrow change does not take. Fail-fast also still permits the outer candidate advance, so a rebinding-recomposed context is not foreclosed either way. **Witnesses** (`harness-runtime/tests/test_lifecycle_retry_breaker_fallback.py`): a fail-fast candidate-advance witness mirroring the B-41 auth test's discriminating shape (which candidate got call 2) plus the `permanent-fail-exit` / `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` span attributes; a single-candidate exactly-one-dispatch witness; a denied-still-retries scope guard. Both probes confirmed: reverting the `isinstance` addition fails the first two (the second reports `assert 3 == 1` — the three-copy duplication made visible), and widening it to the `MemoryToolExecutionError` base fails the third. **Out-of-family Codex round 1 on this arc — one real [P1], fixed here.** `_allowed_kinds` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_tool_executor.py:574-598`; the bare conversion sat at pre-fix `:584`) raised a **bare** `ValueError` from `MemoryRecordKind(item)` on an unknown `allowed_kinds` string — and since `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` *subclasses* `ValueError` rather than the reverse, a bare `ValueError` misses the fail-fast isinstance check entirely, so a `memory.search` with a bad kind following a committed `memory.write_note` still replayed transient. Wrapped to raise the typed input error naming the offending value + the accepted kinds. **Mandatory sibling sweep — this was the only gap.** Every model-supplied argument in the executor reaches exactly five helpers, all already typed: `_string_arg` (`:549-553`), `_optional_string_arg` (`:556-562`), `_positive_int_arg` (`:565-571`), `_allowed_kinds` (`:574-598`, the fixed site), `_promotion_kind` (`:654-669`, `KeyError` already wrapped); `_kind_from_memory_ref` (`:635-642`) already wraps its enum conversion. `MemoryID` / `Identifier` are `NewType` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_record_envelope.py:23`, `state_ledger_entry_schema.py:33`) — zero runtime validation, not raise sites. The pydantic constructions (`MemoryRetrievalRequest` / `PromotionCandidate` / `MemoryOperationPayload`) carry no constraint reachable from a model argument. `MemoryToolExecutionContext._non_empty_string` (`:113-118`) raises a bare `ValueError` but validates HARNESS-supplied context strings, never model input. Dispatch side: both `MemoryToolName(name)` conversions (`llm_dispatch.py:3562` OpenAI, `:4160` ollama) and every other wire-shape check already raise `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError`, already fail-fast. Witness at `harness-runtime/tests/test_memory_tool_executor.py` — a real-executor `memory.search` with an unknown `allowed_kind`, asserting the typed error, its message content, and (cross-layer, on the REAL exception instance) `_classify_provider_exception(...) is None`. Mutation-probed: unwrapping the conversion fails the witness with `ValueError: 'not_a_real_kind' is not a valid MemoryRecordKind`, and that same exception then classifies `transient-retry` — the replay. **Adjacent row:** typing this site also *shifted* its emitted telemetry class — the pre-fix bare `ValueError` classified `provider_adapter_failure`, the typed error classifies `io_failure`; neither is correct, and the substring-keyed `classify_memory_failure` defect behind it is registered as **B-88**.
- **Council: likely.** Direction (2) is a durable-write-semantics decision (all-or-nothing across a batch, and what compensation means for an append-only store) with a blast-radius dimension — C10 paired with whichever voice owns the IS-side memory operation ledger. Direction (1) and the fail-fast reclassification are closer to mechanical once the direction is chosen, but the choice between (1) and (2) is not: it is the difference between "prevent the predictable case" and "make the whole batch atomic," and picking (1) forecloses nothing while picking (2) subsumes it.
- **2026-07-30 re-price note (post-#1159/#1160, mirrors the YAML summary).** Direction (2)'s cost assessment predates U-MEM-27: the generic `write_record_guarded` compare-and-commit seam (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_store.py`) and the reentrant per-root `cross_process_scope_lock` now exist, so a batch could hold the root scope across multiple writes without self-deadlock — the (1)-vs-(2) tradeoff leans differently than the recorded lean. Defect premise unchanged. Re-price BOTH directions at build time rather than reading the recorded lean.


### B-85 · A mixed memory+caller tool batch returns the advertised memory call unserved and un-ledgered *(surfaced at the B-83 fix, 2026-07-28)*

- **What it is.** Both standard-memory-tool continuation loops, on seeing a response whose `tool_calls[]` mixes a C-MEM-14 memory call with a caller-advertised tool call, hand the **whole response back unprocessed** and execute nothing from it. That is a deliberate B-82 round-2 choice, for batch atomicity + bare-path parity: a mixed batch must not half-commit memory before control returns to the caller. The consequence is this row: the memory call the model explicitly asked for is **never served, and nothing records that it wasn't** — no span, no ledger entry, no error. The caller cannot serve it either, since it does not own the memory tools.
- **Reachability.** `harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/llm_dispatch.py:3389-3401` (openai arm) and `:3979-3987` (ollama arm) — line numbers at the B-83 arc's final HEAD (they drifted across the arc's later rounds; corrected at the merge-gate) — the two `if not _all_calls_are_memory_tools(...)` early returns. Both sites carry an inline `B-85` marker, which is the durable anchor if lines drift again.
- **Why it was not fixed at the B-83 arc.** B-83's repair is *prompt composition*, applied **before** the provider call. This exit happens **after** a response is already composed, and a prompt packet cannot retroactively answer a tool call the model has already emitted. There is no mechanical repair to apply — only a posture to choose. Note this is **not** a defect in the atomicity choice, which remains correct.
- **Current state — CLOSED 2026-07-28 (Round 37); posture (3) RECORD landed.** Split out of B-83 rather than absorbed by it, precisely because the B-83 repair provably cannot reach it.
- **Close-out steps.** Choose the posture; the mechanics follow trivially. **(1) FAIL** — raise at the mixed-batch detection so the unserved memory call is loud rather than silent; consistent with the surrounding `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` conventions, but it turns a currently-working caller-tool dispatch into a failure. **(2) SPLIT** — execute the memory calls and return the caller's calls unprocessed; restores service but reopens exactly the half-commit window B-82 round 2 closed (the memory writes commit, the caller's handling can then fail, and the whole dispatch retries under the TRANSIENT classification). **(3) REPAIR/RECORD** — keep the return-whole semantics and emit a degraded/unserved telemetry span at the exit; cheapest, preserves atomicity, but leaves the model's request genuinely unanswered. (3)'s span is emittable with the existing closed enums; a **ledgered** entry is not, under the same X-AL-3 constraint recorded at B-83's close-out.
- **Council: likely.** This is the same C-MEM-13/14 serve-semantics decision B-83 answered for the pre-call case, now for the post-response case, and each option trades a different harm: (1) blast radius on working dispatches, (2) reopens a durable double-write window, (3) accepts a silent-to-the-model outcome. C10 (blast radius) paired with whichever voice owns the memory access-mode selection semantics.
- **CLOSED 2026-07-28 (Round 37).** Landed in the same PR as the B-86 Class 1 fork filing.
  - **Posture (3) RECORD chosen, and grounding is what chose it — the spec is genuinely silent.** C-MEM-14 (`design-substrate/Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md:489`) obliges tool **exposure**, not service. C-MEM-13's must-ledger clause (`:463`) binds only `no_memory_access` transitions, which this exit is not. And C-MEM-08's **closed** 12-kind operation enum (`:247` / `:251`) has no way to express *requested-but-unserved* — which is precisely what makes the **ledger** half X-AL-3-blocked and the **span** half emittable, exactly as this row's own close-out predicted.
  - **Emission shape — a SEPARATE single-slot sink + emitter, deliberately not the degraded-serve one.** The degraded-serve emitter's `rendered=None` shape hardcodes `DENIAL` / `no_memory_access` / `degraded_serve=True`; all three are **false** at iteration k>0, where the loop has already served k rounds of memory calls. The new span carries `operation_name=standard_tool_call`, `access_mode=standard_memory_tools`, `record_count=0`, **no** packet_hash, **no** failure_class, `reason=mixed_batch_caller_owned`, plus a quantitative `memory.unserved_tool_call.count` — every one of them an attribute **value** per the B-83 precedent, never a new member of a closed enum. Return-whole semantics are byte-identical, and a zero-memory-call caller-only batch deliberately emits **nothing** (negative-asserted, so the row cannot drift into emitting on every mixed batch).
  - **Postures (1) and (2) rejected as Class-2-shaped, not as unworkable.** **(1) FAIL** inverts the witnessed bare-path-parity invariant — enabling memory must never turn a working caller-tool dispatch into a failure — and pays fail-fast candidate abandonment for what is an *observability* gap. **(2) SPLIT** reopens exactly the duplicate-durable-write window B-82 round 2 and B-84 / PR #1143 closed, on a path where the harness cannot serve the caller half anyway.
  - **A fourth option was enumerated and rejected.** Withhold the memory tools whenever the step advertises caller tools, making the mixed batch *unrepresentable*. Genuinely preventive rather than observational — but it disables memory on every tool-declaring step and changes **which** memory a run reaches, which is B-86's territory, not this row's.
  - **Stated honestly.** The span may fire 0..N times across retry attempts, because model-emitted batches are nondeterministic; that is the same property the B-83 span has, and the witnesses do not assert exactly-once.
  - **Not foreclosing.** Advisor and grounding both record that choosing (3) leaves (1) and (2) available later as a Class 2 fork, and that (3) is the option which preserves the committed B-82-round-2 atomicity decision.


### B-86 · Cross-family fallback serves the chain primary's memory scope to a different-family provider on the SERVABLE tools path *(surfaced while grounding B-83 [P1-c], 2026-07-28; CLOSED 2026-07-28 — U-MEM-26 impl leg)*

- **What it is.** The B-83 fix stops the degraded-serve *packet* from crossing a provider-family boundary. The **servable tools path** crosses the same boundary and was deliberately left untouched. On the `memory_runtime` path a cross-family fallback *recomposes* the context for the current candidate, so `select_memory_access_mode` re-picks `STANDARD_MEMORY_TOOLS` from the **candidate's** capabilities and the tools are injected — but `compose_for_dispatch` derives `record_scope.provider_family` from `fallback_chain.primary.family` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/automatic_memory.py:191-199`), **not** from the candidate. The injected schemas bind `scope_ref` (a hash of that primary-family scope) and `policy_ref` as fixed enum values (`_openai_standard_memory_tools` / `_ollama_standard_memory_tools`), so the candidate can `memory.search` **across**, and `memory.write_note` **into**, a scope belonging to a different provider family — read disclosure *and* write attribution, where B-83's packet leak was read-only.
- **The field is a real boundary, not decoration.** `MemoryRetriever._scope_mismatch` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_retrieval.py:371-384`) filters candidate records on `provider_family`, which is what makes the primary-derived scope load-bearing in the first place.
- **Residual, same family.** `provider_family_for_provider` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/cross_family_cost_tag.py:82`) falls back to `LOCAL_OPEN_WEIGHT` for an **unknown** provider key, so an unregistered provider compares equal to an ollama-scoped packet at B-83's new family conjunct — conservative for its original cost-attribution use, wrong for a scope-boundary check.
- **Current state — `closed` (2026-07-28, at the U-MEM-26 impl leg; `registered_finding` → `design_substrate_gated` at the fork filing, → `open` at the spec leg, → `closed` here — all three transits on the same day).** The **C-MEM-13 withhold guard landed** as the **sixth conjunct** of `_standard_memory_tools_context` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/llm_dispatch.py`), sharing `_packet_scope_matches_dispatch_family` **verbatim** with the predicate `_degraded_serve_disposition` already reads — deliberately *one* predicate, not a second cross-family test: a divergent test would let a cross-family dispatch fail the arm guard, fall through to `_degraded_serve_for_unservable_arm`, pass disposition conjunct 2, and land on the `arm_unservable` **repair** branch, serving the same cross-family packet as prompt text. Downstream is by construction — the guard returns `None`, the disposition reports `provider_family_scope_mismatch` with `rendered=None`, the plain arm dispatches with no schemas / no `scope_ref` / no packet, and the degraded span carries DENIAL / `no_memory_access` / `record_count=0` / no `packet_hash`. The dispatch still completes. **Two defect-pinning tests were inverted** with contract cites rather than deleted: the B-83-era R5 witness (which required the tools to be *armed* cross-family so the daemon could refuse them) is now `test_u_mem_26_cross_family_ollama_dispatch_withholds_tools_and_packet`, and the r300 `api.run` integration test — which asserted the five C-MEM-14 wire names **do** reach the cross-family openai leg — now asserts the opposite at `test_r300_deterministic_cross_family_fallback_through_production_path`, so the withholding is proven on the **canonical production path**, not only at the unit boundary. Net posture is the report-only **denial**, with **harness-authored capture unaffected** (C3's condition of concurrence), witnessed with the run's composed scope. **Pre-existing** — the tools path had behaved this way since U-MEM-16 / B-82. The close-out steps below are **history (pre-ratification)**.
- **Why it was scoped out of the B-83 arc.** Closing it means either disabling memory entirely on every cross-family fallback, or recomposing the scope per candidate. Both change *which memory a run can reach* — a C-MEM-11/13 scope-semantics decision, not the dispatch-side serve-check B-83 owns.
- **Close-out steps.** **(1)** Decide whether `record_scope.provider_family` should track the **dispatched candidate** rather than the chain primary. Ground it first: does a per-candidate scope *fragment* memory across a fallback chain — each family reading a disjoint record set, so a fallback silently loses the run's accumulated memory — and is that preferable to the current cross-family reach? This is the load-bearing question and it is not mechanical. **(2)** If the scope stays primary-derived, decide the dispatch-side posture for a cross-family *servable* dispatch: withhold the tools (memory off for the fallback leg, consistent with B-83's packet posture), or keep them and accept documented cross-family reach. **(3)** Independently and cheaply: make the unknown-provider fallback explicit at B-83's call site — an unknown key should not silently compare equal to `LOCAL_OPEN_WEIGHT` for a scope-boundary decision; a `None`/unknown sentinel forcing report-only is the conservative shape and does not disturb the cost-attribution caller.
- **Council: likely, for (1) and (2).** Whether memory scope follows the chain primary or the dispatched candidate decides which records a run can read and write across a fallback, trading a disclosure/attribution boundary against memory continuity on fallback — C10 (blast radius) paired with whichever voice owns C-MEM-11/13 retrieval scope semantics. (3) is mechanical once (1) is settled and could land ahead of it.
- **GROUNDED + COUNCIL-CONVENED 2026-07-28 (Round 37); flipped `registered_finding` → `design_substrate_gated`.** Filed as `.harness/class_1_fork_b86_memory_scope_provider_family_keying.md` per the **B-70 discriminator**: a real gap that needs spec-level CONTRACT text before code. No `design-substrate/**` file is edited by the filing; the spec-leg is a separate follow-on arc per the B-33 / B-59 / B-70 / B-72 precedent.
  - **The council (C10 + C3 co-primary, C6 consultant) probe-RESOLVED the Q1 tension.** The leak's carrier is the **unconditional `scope_ref` binding at the injection site**, not the scope's *key*. Probe E is the dissolving finding: the **writer is already candidate-derived today** (`memory_capture.py:585-591`) and the disclosure occurs anyway — so re-keying the scope would suppress it only as a side effect of shattering the partition. Neither voice conceded its axis; the mechanism was the conflation.
  - **Recommendations (operator holds decision authority).** **Q1** — key `record_scope.provider_family` to the **chain primary** (a run-level partition), stated in the spec so it stops being an accident. **Q2** — **withhold** the standard memory tools on a cross-family *servable* dispatch, reporting a named denial reason (the B-83 disposition shape), with C3's condition of concurrence that **harness-authored automatic capture is unaffected**.
  - **Stated limit (C6), recorded as a non-claim.** Family equality is *necessary but not sufficient*: `local_terminal` sits **within** `local_open_weight` while carrying the slate's most restrictive trust posture. Addressed outside this contract, and needs its own pass post-spec-leg.
  - **What the spec-leg owes** (drafting targets at fork doc §6): a **C-MEM-03 value domain** plus explicit `null` semantics (the wildcard-on-`None` behaviour is load-bearing and currently undocumented), the **derivation rule** (run-level, composed once, capture writes under the run's composed record scope), a **C-MEM-13 withhold invariant**, and a **C-MEM-14 exposure qualification**. Threat model `:481` needs no amendment — B-86 is *conformance repair to a cleared invariant*, which is what keeps the spec-leg X-AL-3-clean.
  - **Item (3) LANDED in the same PR as the filing.** `provider_family_for_scope_check` now fails **closed** (`None`) on unregistered provider keys at `_packet_scope_matches_dispatch_family`, so an unknown key no longer compares equal to a `local_open_weight`-scoped packet. Previously the packet got **repaired** — a live leak; it is now report-only, and mutation-probed.
  - **Forward items carried by the filing.** The direction consequence for the producer key-vs-value asymmetry is recorded at **B-89**; the adjacent tenant / `workload_class` omission is registered as **B-90**.
- **SPEC-LEG LANDED 2026-07-28.** `Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md` v1 → v1.1 (C-MEM-03 value domain + `null` semantics + derivation rule; C-MEM-13 cross-family withhold invariant; C-MEM-14 exposure qualification; the threat-model `:481` invariant unchanged and standing as the authority, which is what keeps the leg X-AL-3-clean) plus the paired plan delta `Implementation_Plan_Memory_Substrate_v1.md` v1 → v1.1 (**NEW U-MEM-26**). Clearance markers at `.harness/clearance/spec-memory-substrate-v1-1-cleared-2026-07-28.md` + `.harness/clearance/implementation-plan-memory-substrate-v1-1-cleared-2026-07-28.md`. The **impl leg** — the C-MEM-13 withhold guard, the `B-89` writer-side repair, and the incidental `B-90` fold-in, all decomposed at U-MEM-26 — is the remaining gate on this row.
- **IMPL-LEG LANDED 2026-07-28 — row CLOSED.** U-MEM-26 shipped in four slices: the `harness-is` read boundary (null asymmetry, the `B-90` predicate trio, the request-side value domain), the `B-89` writer repair plus write-boundary enforcement at all scope-authoring surfaces, the C-MEM-13 withhold guard with its production-wired witnesses, and the docs/register refresh. Wire-name / HTTP-400 coverage displaced by the r300 inversion is preserved on the same-family paths that still inject. Mutation probes: dropping the conjunct reddens all three withhold witnesses; swapping it for a raw provider-name `!=` reddens 18 same-family ollama tests — the shared-predicate discipline is load-bearing, not cosmetic. The C6 stated limit (family equality is *necessary but not sufficient*; `local_terminal` sits within `local_open_weight`) is explicitly **out of scope** for U-MEM-26 and still needs its own pass. Arc record at `.harness/u-mem-26-impl-leg-close-2026-07-28.md`.


### B-87 · Degraded-serve span over-reports pre-wire failures as completed injections *(surfaced by out-of-family Codex round 8 on the B-83 arc, 2026-07-28; CLOSED 2026-07-29 — PR #1149)*

- **What it is.** B-83 round 6 moved the degraded-serve span emission into a `finally` around the whole arm-dispatch chain, so a repair that was *sent* and then failed still reports. That correctly covers **post-wire** failures. It over-covers **pre-wire** ones: when the repair is authorized, `_DegradedMemoryServe.rendered` is populated at the serve-check — *before* any translation runs — and the per-arm translators can raise before the provider adapter is ever called. The `finally` then emits `operation_name=injection` + `access_mode=prompt_extension_packet` + the packet_hash + a non-zero record_count: the span asserts the packet **was injected**, when nothing left the process.
- **Verified empirically at registration time, not hypothesised.** Driving the residual two-base case — an `active_system_prompt` bound *and* a payload-owned `params["system"]`, which B-83 round 4 deliberately leaves as a fail-loud `PromptInjectionConflictError` — through the real dispatcher on a repair-eligible context produced **zero adapter calls** and yet a span carrying `memory.operation.name=injection`, `memory.access_mode=prompt_extension_packet`, `memory.packet_hash=cccc…`, `memory.record_count=1`, `dispatch_outcome=provider_error`, `provider_error_type=PromptInjectionConflictError`.
- **Reachability.** Any translation-layer raise on a **repaired** dispatch: the two-base `PromptInjectionConflictError` residual (reachable on all four arms), `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` from the external-CLI text-only tools rejection, and any future pre-adapter validation. **Not** reachable for report-only dispositions (`rendered is None` already emits `denial` / `no_memory_access` / no packet_hash), nor when the repair is unauthorized. Note that `test_b83_two_base_system_sources_still_fail_loud_through_the_repair` already exercises this exact path but asserts only the raise, not the span — the suite passes *over* the defect rather than covering it.
- **Candidate fix.** A reached-the-wire marker set at the provider-call boundary — the one place per arm where the adapter is actually invoked — with the `finally` emitting a **distinct** classification when the marker is unset: pre-wire failures reported as e.g. `denial` plus a `dispatch_outcome="translation_error"` value and **no** packet_hash, since no packet reached a provider. Mechanically small; the care is in threading the marker to every arm's true adapter-call site (including the ollama R5 bare retry) without reintroducing the round-6 loss on genuine post-wire failures.
- **Current state.** **CLOSED 2026-07-29 at PR #1149** (branch `b87-reached-the-wire-prewire-marker`) — all three close-out steps land.
  - **Mechanism.** A frame-local `_ProviderWireReachedSink` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/llm_dispatch.py`) is constructed once per dispatch and threaded to every arm; `_emit_degraded_memory_serve_span` gained a `reached_wire` parameter and branches on it. Marker **set** → today's post-wire shape **verbatim** (`injection` + packet_hash + record_count, `completed` or `provider_error`). Marker **unset** → `dispatch_outcome="pre_wire_failure"` plus a new `memory.degraded_serve.pre_wire_error_type` attribute, with **no** packet_hash and no `injection` operation name (the existing closed-enum member `packet_assembly` is emitted instead).
  - **The candidate name `translation_error` above was rejected**, at out-of-family Codex R1 [P2], as over-claiming: *any* pre-wire phase can fail — payload-shape rejection, adapter selection, a repair-authorization raise — not only translation. The shipped value is phase-neutral by construction and names the **boundary**, not the phase.
  - **Placement, per arm.** Direct-SDK arms construct the awaitable, mark, *then* await — so a call-time **synchronous** `TypeError` from the SDK is correctly pre-wire (R7). The external-CLI arm fires **inside** the subprocess runner after `create_subprocess_exec` returns — the semantic anchor is that a child process now exists that can observe the payload, so a spawn failure stays pre-wire (R2/R3). The anthropic-memory arm fires once inside `execute_with_memory_callbacks` before its first SDK call (R5), since that helper owns a tool loop the dispatcher cannot see into.
  - **Two declines, on the record.** (a) **In-coroutine vendor-side validation** that raises after the awaitable is entered is **post-wire by design** — resolving it would need SDK-internal hooks that do not exist. (b) Consequently the SDK arms' boundary is **our last observable point**, deliberately; this row does not claim byte-on-the-wire fidelity.
  - **Compatibility tiers.** An adapter or runner that does not **declare** an explicit `on_wire` parameter (a bare `**kwargs` deliberately does *not* count, R4) keeps the pre-B-87 marker placement, discriminated by the single shared `accepts_explicit_on_wire` predicate; the public runner seam statically admits **both** shapes (R6).
  - **Robustness.** An `on_wire` callback that raises after spawn kills and reaps the child, and the orphaned coroutine is closed rather than leaked (R7).
  - **Witnesses.** Step (2) landed as specified — `test_b83_two_base_system_sources_still_fail_loud_through_the_repair` now asserts the **span** as well as the raise. Step (3) resolved as `dispatch_outcome`, an attribute **value**; no member added to any closed C-MEM-19 enum. 8 out-of-family Codex rounds (9 real findings), ~25 mutation-probed witnesses across 4 test files, `just check` 7014 passed.
- **Why registered rather than fixed now — direction rationale.** Two reasons, both deliberate. **(1) The imprecision over-reports, not under-reports.** It claims a disclosure that never occurred; it never hides one that did. An investigator reading this span over-responds — checks a provider that was never called — rather than missing a real leak. That is the fail-safe direction for a telemetry defect on a disclosure-adjacent surface, and it is what makes deferral tolerable at all. **(2) This arc reached its fix-round cap at Codex R7** per the workspace's non-convergent-adversarial-hardening discipline: eight review rounds on a single repair, each finding a narrower instance of an already-characterized surface, is the point at which continued in-arc fixing costs more than it returns. Registering keeps the finding live and specified rather than absorbed.
- **Close-out steps.** **(1)** Add the reached-the-wire marker and branch the `finally` emission on it — marker set → today's behavior verbatim; marker unset → the pre-wire classification with no packet_hash and no `injection` operation name, since claiming a packet_hash for a packet no provider ever saw *is* the defect. Ground the exact adapter-call sites first (there is more than one per arm: anthropic has three variants, ollama has the standard-tools loop *and* its R5 bare retry), and mind that the marker must be set **before** the awaited call, not after — otherwise a post-wire failure regresses to being reported as pre-wire, the mirror image of this row and the exact ordering trap B-83 round 6 [P2-d] already hit once with the R5 sink. **(2)** Extend the existing two-base witness to assert the **span** as well as the raise; the path is already covered, only the assertion is missing. **(3)** Decide whether `dispatch_outcome` gains a `translation_error` value or whether pre-wire failures ride the `reason` axis — either is an attribute **value**, never a new member of the closed C-MEM-19 enums.
- **Council: no.** A telemetry-accuracy fix inside an already-settled span contract, with the classification axis (attribute values, not enum members) established by B-83 rounds 5–7. No cross-domain tension to surface: nothing about *which* memory is served or *who* may see it changes — only whether the span truthfully reports that a disclosure occurred. The one judgement call (`dispatch_outcome` vs `reason`) is impl discretion once the marker exists.

### B-88 · Memory failure telemetry: substring-keyed `classify_memory_failure` collapses the typed input-error family — and two real policy denials — to `io_failure` *(surfaced by the merge-gate lens-2 sweep on the B-84 arc, 2026-07-28)*

- **What it is.** `classify_memory_failure` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_observability.py:150-163`) classifies into the C-MEM-19 failure vocabulary by matching **substrings** of the exception message and of the lowercased type name. Its third rule is `isinstance(exc, (OSError, IOError)) or "io" in exc_name` — and `"io"` is a substring of **"execut·io·n"**. Every member of the `MemoryToolExecutionError` family therefore matches: `memorytoolexecutioninputerror`, `memorytoolexecutiondeniederror`, and the base `memorytoolexecutionerror` all contain `"io"`. The rules are ordered, so a member escapes only if an EARLIER message rule happens to fire first. That is the whole of what keeps policy denials out of the IO bucket today — an accident of wording, not a type distinction.
- **Empirical evidence (probed at registration time, not inferred).** Fed real exception instances:

  | exception | message | emitted `memory.failure_class` |
  |---|---|---|
  | `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` | `unsupported allowed_kinds entry …` | `io_failure` |
  | `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` | `memory tool argument 'note' must be a string` | `io_failure` |
  | `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` | `memory tool argument 'limit' must be >= 1` | `io_failure` |
  | `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` | `unsupported promotion target_kind …` | `io_failure` |
  | `MemoryToolExecutionError` (base) | `write_note capture failed` | `io_failure` |
  | **`MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError`** | `memory ref mem:x is **superseded**` (`memory_tool_executor.py:433`) | **`io_failure`** |
  | **`MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError`** | `memory ref mem:x is **not retrievable**` (`:435`) | **`io_failure`** |
  | `MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError` | `memory ref mem:x denied by policy` | `policy_denial` (correct) |
  | bare `ValueError` (the pre-B-84 `_allowed_kinds` shape) | `'x' is not a valid MemoryRecordKind` | `provider_adapter_failure` |

  Note the last row: the SAME logical failure changed telemetry class as a side effect of B-84 typing it (`provider_adapter_failure` → `io_failure`). Neither value is right — which is the point.
- **Why it is a defect and not a nit.** `Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md:641` (C-MEM-19 Invariants) requires: *"Failure telemetry must distinguish policy denial, path violation, IO failure, serialization failure, provider adapter failure, and retrieval empty-result."* Two genuine policy denials emitting `io_failure` violates that invariant **on an axis the vocabulary already has** — an operator triaging memory IO failures sees records that were merely superseded or unretrievable. **A second, spec-side half:** the six-value vocabulary has **no input-validation class**, so even a correct classifier has nowhere truthful to put a malformed-argument failure. Whether to add one is a C-MEM-19 amendment — design-substrate, not impl — and must route as back-flow, not be absorbed here.
- **Reachability.** Every standard-memory-tool failure path. `StandardMemoryToolExecutor.execute` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_tool_executor.py:167-175`) calls `classify_memory_failure(exc)` in its blanket `except`, so the attribute is emitted on every raise, including the now-fail-fast B-84 class. Pre-existing and latent — it predates B-84 and is orthogonal to it, but B-84 is what made it visible (and shifted one class).
- **Adjacent, same family — the fail-fast breaker-cause emission is unwitnessed for this class** *(merge-gate lens 3)*. On the fail-fast exit, `retry_breaker_fallback.py` calls `breaker.record_failure(cause=_classify_breaker_cause(exc))`, which returns `None` for an exception with no `.status_code` — correct, but nothing pins it: a mutation hardcoding `BreakerCause.AUTH_FAILURE` there **survives** the B-84 witnesses. Same telemetry-precision family (a failure emitting a confidently wrong classification), same fix shape (assert the emitted classification, not just the control flow), so it is recorded here rather than as a separate row.
- **Current state — IMPL HALF LANDED 2026-07-28; the SPEC half REMAINS, so this row stays `registered_finding`.** Classification is now **type-keyed**: `classify_memory_failure` first reads a `memory_failure_class` class attribute declared at the exception's own definition site (`MEMORY_FAILURE_CLASS_ATTRIBUTE`, `harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_observability.py`), and that declaration wins outright; only a real `MemoryTelemetryFailureClass` member is honoured, so a typo or a string cannot invent a class outside the closed vocabulary. `getattr` walks the MRO, so subclasses inherit for free. The message/type-name heuristics survive ONLY as the residual for types that declare nothing, and the `"io" in exc_name` clause is **deleted** — `isinstance(exc, OSError)` is the whole IO rule now (`IOError` is an alias of `OSError`, so the old tuple was redundant).
  - **Shape chosen — declaration-at-the-definition-site, not a name list in the classifier.** The alternative (a frozen exact-type-name set inside `harness-is`) would be a SECOND authority for a hierarchy `harness-is` cannot import (`harness-runtime` imports `harness-is`, never the reverse), i.e. exactly the mirror-copy drift class this workspace already treats as a defect in its own right (see B-82/B-84's own close-out reasoning). The declaration keeps ONE source of truth: the exception says what it is. In-repo precedent: `memory_tool_executor.py:170-172` already keys `memory.policy.decision` on `isinstance(exc, MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError)` — `failure_class` on the very next line was the only substring-keyed attribute on that span, and the two could disagree.
  - **Populations table (old → new).** Every change is deliberate:

    | population (raise sites) | before | after | why |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError`, all **14** raise sites | `policy_denial` OR `io_failure` by wording | **`policy_denial`** always | declared. FOUR sites actually changed, not the two this row named: "memory promotion review is forbidden", "memory ref X is superseded", "memory ref X is not retrievable", "memory tool scope_ref does not match context" (named by message, not by line offset, which this arc's own edits shift) |
    | `MemoryToolExecutionInputError`, all 10 sites | `io_failure` (and `policy_denial` for the `'policy_ref'`-named argument message) | **`provider_adapter_failure`** | LEAST-WRONG stopgap; see the choice rationale below. Also kills the `policy_ref` miss, where a malformed argument asserted a policy decision no resolver made |
    | `write_note` durable-write failure (capture `status=FAILED`) | `io_failure` | **`io_failure`** (via NEW `MemoryToolExecutionStoreError`) | **round 2, closing a regression round 1 introduced.** Round 1's unconditional base declaration reported a disk-full store write as `provider_adapter_failure`. Discriminated on the capture API's **closed two-value status enum** — never on `failure_reason` wording — because `FAILED` is produced at exactly ONE place, `EpisodicMemoryCapture._capture`'s `except` around `write_record` + `append_memory_operation` (`memory_capture.py:541-552`), which classifies its own span `io_failure`. The subtype ADOPTS that classification rather than inventing a third answer |
    | `MemoryToolExecutionError` base | `io_failure` | **`provider_adapter_failure`** | after round 2 the base's ONLY raise site is `_write_note`'s defensive `memory_id is None` branch, unreachable through the real capture API (a `CAPTURED` result always sets `memory_id`) — the type determines nothing, so it declares the residual. A future subclass that declares nothing inherits the residual, never a confidently wrong class |
    | undeclared harness types whose NAME contains "io" but whose failure is not IO — `MemoryOperationIdempotencyConflictError` / `…ProjectionMismatchError` / `…RedactionEventMismatchError` (reachable at the `memory_redaction.py:260` site), pydantic `ValidationError` ("validat·io·n") | `io_failure` | **`provider_adapter_failure`** | the collision class this row named; an idempotency conflict is not an IO failure |
    | `MemoryPathViolationError` | `path_violation` (every message contains "path") | `path_violation` (declared) | unchanged value, now by construction |
    | `MemoryCallbackIOError` non-policy sites (not-found / out-of-range / content_b64 decode) | `io_failure` (via the `"io"` substring) | `io_failure` (declared) | unchanged value. Without the declaration these EIGHT sites would have regressed to the residual once the substring rule died — the type *is* the C-RT-15 `RT-FAIL-MEMORY-CALLBACK-IO` storage-backend I/O failure |
    | native-adapter POLICY denials — `_require_native_access`, `_require_capture_allowed`, and both `_require_retrieval_allowed` branches (redaction-state + resolver DENY) | `policy_denial` by wording | `policy_denial` by construction | the C-MEM-15 callback surface offers exactly ONE failure carrier, so a denial must be raised as a `MemoryCallbackIOError`; a **module-private** `_NativeMemoryPolicyDeniedError(MemoryCallbackIOError)` now carries them. `isinstance`, every `except`, every `pytest.raises`, and the transient `RT-FAIL-MEMORY-CALLBACK-IO` mapping are all unchanged — it is a telemetry discriminator, NOT an addition to the §14.12.1 typed-exception surface |
    | everything else undeclared (`MemoryStoreRecordUnavailableError`, raw `OSError`, stdlib) | — | unchanged | residual rules preserved |

  - **The input-validation choice (`provider_adapter_failure`), stated for the spec half to overturn.** (i) The fault originates in a provider/model-emitted tool call arriving through the adapter, not in the memory substrate's own IO or record codecs. (ii) `retry_breaker_fallback._classify_provider_exception` already groups `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` with `LLMDispatchPayloadShapeError` — the provider-payload-shape family — so this reads the same failure the same way at both layers. (iii) It RESTORES the value this population emitted before B-84 typed `_allowed_kinds` (a bare `ValueError` fell to the same residual), so B-84's incidental `provider_adapter_failure` → `io_failure` shift is reverted rather than entrenched. (iv) `serialization_failure` is reserved for record-codec faults; using it here would poison that bucket for triage. The declaration is written explicitly on `MemoryToolExecutionInputError` (not inherited) precisely so it is the single flip site when the vocabulary gains an input class.
  - **Residual, stated honestly.** Undeclared types are still classified by message wording (`policy`/`denied`/`unavailable` → `policy_denial`; `path`/`traversal`/`prefix` → `path_violation`; `json`/`serial`/`decode` → `serialization_failure`). That is wording-luck for e.g. `MemoryStoreRecordUnavailableError`, and it is left alone: those types have no vocabulary-determined class, and inventing one for them is outside this row.
  - **Base-type raise-site sweep (round 2, mirroring the B-84 R1 sweep).** `raise MemoryToolExecutionError(` occurs at **exactly one** site repo-wide, so the round-1 mislabel class was confined to that site and is now closed. Store exceptions do NOT otherwise reach the base: `_read_record_by_ref` wraps them into `MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError`, and any un-wrapped store exception propagates raw through `execute`'s blanket `except` and is classified by the residual (`isinstance(exc, OSError)` → `io_failure`), which was already correct.
  - **Adjacent, untouched (recorded, not fixed).** `memory_capture.py:545` hardcodes `IO_FAILURE` for ANY exception from the `write_record` + `append_memory_operation` pair. Round 2 makes the executor **adopt** that classification instead of contradicting it, so the coarseness is now inherited by one more span: a non-IO exception escaping that pair is reported `io_failure` at both layers. That is the capture layer's pre-existing choice — changing it is a separate judgement about whether a capture-layer span may assert IO without evidence, and it cannot be made at the executor, which receives only a formatted reason STRING (`f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"`) with no exception retained on `MemoryCaptureResult`.
  - **Merge-gate round 3 (test-witness lens BLOCK, closed).** The lens found the three *other* `_NativeMemoryPolicyDeniedError` raise sites unwitnessed: their covering tests asserted only `pytest.raises(MemoryCallbackIOError, ...)`, which the subtype satisfies identically, so reverting any one of them to the base type left the suite green while re-emitting `io_failure` on a genuine denial. All three are now span-pinned (`_require_capture_allowed`; `_require_retrieval_allowed`'s resolver-DENY branch; and its redaction-state branch, which previously had NO test at all), each probed individually: reverting each site fails exactly its own test with `assert 'io_failure' == 'policy_denial'`. The same round added the third consumer's failure-path pin (`memory_redaction`), wording-CONFLICTING rows so the base and path declarations are no longer deletable-green, and fixed two count claims (14 denial raise sites, not 12) plus an inaccurate test comment.
  - **Witnesses.** `harness-runtime/tests/test_memory_failure_classification.py` — THE population table, 37 parametrized rows over real exception instances carrying their actual raise-site messages, plus a pin that `memory.policy.decision` and `failure_class` agree on the four previously-disagreeing denials, plus a pin that the private discriminator is still a `MemoryCallbackIOError`. `harness-is/tests/test_memory_observability.py` — the declaration mechanism (wins over conflicting wording; inherited; overridable; a non-member declaration is IGNORED) and the residual rules. `harness-runtime/tests/test_memory_tool_executor.py::test_write_note_store_failure_emits_io_failure_class` — round 2 END-TO-END through the real executor with `store.write_record` raising `OSError("disk full")`, asserting the emitted `memory.failure_class == "io_failure"` on the outer `memory.tool_call` span (grounded there: `_write_note` does NOT thread the executor's tracer into `EpisodicMemoryCapture`, so on this path the outer span is the ONLY telemetry record of the failure). `harness-runtime/tests/test_lifecycle_retry_breaker_fallback.py::test_memory_tool_input_error_fail_fast_trip_carries_no_breaker_cause` — the lens-3 half below. `harness-runtime/tests/test_native_memory_adapter.py` — all four native-adapter denial sites span-pinned (the fourth, `_require_native_access`, was already pinned). `harness-is/tests/test_memory_redaction.py::test_redaction_write_failure_emits_classified_failure_class` — the THIRD consumer, two rows with DIFFERENT expected classes (`OSError` → `io_failure`; `MemoryOperationIdempotencyConflictError` → `provider_adapter_failure`) so the assertion pins the classification rather than a constant.
  - **Mutation probes (all five fail post-fix, restored after).** (a) Restoring `or "io" in exc_name`: 2 rows fail (`MemoryOperationIdempotencyConflictError`, and the test's own `_BogusDeclarationError` — "declarat·io·n"). (b) Removing `MemoryToolExecutionDeniedError`'s declaration: 11 failures, every denial row plus the agreement pin. (c) Hardcoding `BreakerCause.AUTH_FAILURE` at the fail-fast `record_failure`: 1 failure — the NEW test only, confirming this row's claim that the B-84 witnesses survive it. (d) **Round 2 — restoring the regression** (store failure raises the base again): the end-to-end witness fails. (e) **Round 2 — removing `MemoryToolExecutionStoreError`'s `IO_FAILURE` declaration** (so it inherits the base residual): 4 failures — the end-to-end span assertion (`assert 'provider_adapter_failure' == 'io_failure'`) plus the 3 store-subtype table rows. (f) **Round 3 — reverting each of the three newly-pinned native-adapter denial sites to `MemoryCallbackIOError`, one at a time**: each run fails exactly ONE test, its own, with `AssertionError: assert 'io_failure' == 'policy_denial'`. (g) **Round 3 — hardcoding `failure_class="io_failure"` at `memory_redaction`'s emission site**: fails the non-IO row only (`assert 'io_failure' == 'provider_adapter_failure'`), which is the correct discriminating shape — the IO row still passes, proving the failure is about classification and not about the site being exercised.
- **Adjacent lens-3 half — CLOSED at the same arc.** The fail-fast breaker-cause emission is now witnessed end-to-end: `fail_threshold=1` so the single fail-fast failure trips a real breaker, a spying registry captures the emitted `BreakerTransition`, and the test asserts `transition.cause is None` after asserting the transition exists (positive control, so the assertion is never vacuous).
- **Close-out steps.** (1) **Type-keyed classification — LANDED** (2026-07-28) as described above. (2) **Decide the input-validation class — REMAINS OPEN, spec-side.** The impl half took the least-wrong existing value with the rationale recorded above; adding a member to the closed C-MEM-19 vocabulary is a **Class 1 back-flow to `Spec_Memory_Substrate_v1.md` C-MEM-19, not absorbable at Phase 7 per X-AL-3**. When it lands, the single flip site is `MemoryToolExecutionInputError.memory_failure_class`. (3) **Witnesses + mutation probes — LANDED**, including the breaker-cause pin.
- **Council: likely, for step (2) only.** Adding a member to a closed C-MEM-19 enum is a spec amendment with an observability-vocabulary blast radius (every consumer switching on the value) — C7/C8 (observability) paired with whoever owns the memory-substrate contract. Step (1) is mechanical once (2) is settled; step (3) follows.
- **Cross-ref.** Surfaced by the merge-gate lens-2 sweep on the **B-84** arc; the `provider_adapter_failure` → `io_failure` shift is a direct consequence of B-84 typing `_allowed_kinds`. The bullet above folds in the lens-3 breaker-cause observation.


### B-89 · Memory-scope producer asymmetry: capture stamps raw provider KEYS while retrieval requests carry family VALUES *(surfaced by the B-86 grounding pass, 2026-07-28; CLOSED 2026-07-28 — U-MEM-26 impl leg)*

- **What it is.** The two producers of a `MemoryScope` disagree about the *vocabulary* of `provider_family`. The capture path constructs its **own** scope with `provider_family=provider` — the raw per-turn dispatched provider **KEY** (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_capture.py:585-591`; key vocabulary `claude_code` / `codex` / `gemini` / `antigravity` / `ollama` / `anthropic` / `openai` / `google` at `harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/lifecycle/cross_family_cost_tag.py:60-69`) — while retrieval requests carry the composed `record_scope`'s `ProviderFamily` **VALUE** (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/automatic_memory.py:197`). All three enforcement sites compare raw strings with **no normalization**: `MemoryRetriever._scope_mismatch` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_retrieval.py:371-384`), the index `_scope_matches` (`memory_retrieval_index.py:379-396`), and `memory_policy._scope_not_broader` (`memory_policy.py:336-359`).
- **Empirically confirmed at registration, not inferred.** An `ollama`-written note is invisible even to a `local_open_weight`-scoped request **of its own family**. Tool-written notes are therefore **write-only for 5 of the 8 registered provider keys**; only `anthropic` / `openai` / `google` coincide with a family value, and they do so by accident. The practical consequence: "memory continuity" under primary-derived keying is largely **notional** today — the continuity side of B-86's Q1 tension only becomes real once this row lands.
- **Existence is independent of B-86's Q1; direction is not.** Both Q1 readings leave the `ollama` note invisible — Q1 asks *primary-or-candidate*, this row asks *key-or-value*; orthogonal axes. But the **direction** is determined by the B-86 council record (fork doc §5-Q1): the **writer adopts the reader's key** — the capture path stops constructing an independent `MemoryScope` and consumes the run's composed `record_scope`. That is the larger of the two candidate repairs, correct on one-source-of-truth grounds, and it will incidentally close **B-90** when it lands.
- **Intrinsic evidence for the family reading.** The field is literally named `provider_family` (`harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_record_envelope.py:120`) while C-MEM-03 declares **no value domain** at all. That is evidence for the fork to weigh — **not** an impl licence to normalize ahead of the contract text.
- **Hash-inert; blast radius low.** `MemoryScope` is not folded into `memory_id` (content-hash + run_id only, `memory_capture.py:574-575`), so a writer-side normalization moves **no identities**.
- **Migration residual — forward-only and permanent.** Records already written with raw provider keys stay invisible under family-value requests. That is **not a regression** (they are invisible today), but the spec-leg must **state** it rather than leave it implied, alongside the C-MEM-03 value domain it belongs to.
- **Current state.** `closed` 2026-07-28 at the U-MEM-26 impl leg (`design_substrate_gated` → `open` at the B-86 spec leg → `closed` here). `EpisodicMemoryCapture` gained a **`record_scope` parameter** and writes records under the run's composed scope verbatim; **both construction sites pass it**. The `provider` argument is unchanged and still feeds the C-MEM-08 payload and the C-MEM-19 span **as the raw key it is** — only the *record* scope stops deriving from it; the residual self-construction remains for callers with no composed scope and is bound by the C-MEM-03 value domain instead.
