### B-93 · Whole-root memory-write serialization has no deadline — an unbounded guarded precondition stalls every memory writer on the host *(surfaced by the merge-gate concurrency lens on the U-MEM-27 impl leg, PR #1159, 2026-07-29; classified REGISTER at the post-#1159 gate-log grounding pass, 2026-07-30)*

- **What it is.** Every `CanonicalMemoryStore` write takes `cross_process_scope_lock` over the whole memory **root** (`_write_scope`, `harness-is/src/harness_is/memory_store.py`) — deliberately, because the section `write_record_guarded` wraps re-reads a caller-chosen set of *other* records before writing this one, and a per-file lock cannot exclude a writer of a file the section has not named yet. That lock is a plain **blocking** `flock(LOCK_EX)` with no timeout (`_DirLock.acquire`, `harness-is/src/harness_is/cross_process_ledger_lock.py`). `write_record_guarded` additionally holds it **across a caller-supplied precondition callable**, so the hold time of a root-wide exclusive lock is set by code the store does not control and cannot bound.
- **No live hazard at HEAD.** The only precondition in the tree is `_provenance_unchanged` (`harness-runtime/src/harness_runtime/memory_promotion.py`), bounded at one `read_record` per cited source memory ref — a small N of local file reads, no network, no operator wait, no unbounded loop. This row is registered against a **future** precondition, not a present stall.
- **Why it is worth registering anyway.** The seam is **generic by design** — it "knows nothing about what the precondition tests" — which is exactly what makes the next precondition author unaware that they are holding a host-wide lock. An operator confirmation, a provider round-trip, or a retrieval sweep inside a precondition blocks **every same-host memory writer**: a `harness run` alongside a `harness daemon` is the shared-root case the seam's own SCOPE paragraph documents, and it would block with no deadline and no diagnostic.
- **No convention to copy — which is why this is a scoped arc, not a one-liner.** Counted by direct read at HEAD, this codebase has **eight blocking-`flock` acquisition sites** across **three** modules, reachable through **six** entry surfaces — and *not one of them* takes a timeout. In `harness-is/src/harness_is/cross_process_ledger_lock.py` (**six** sites): `_DirLock.acquire` `:133` (`LOCK_EX`), the shared engine behind **all four** of that module's entry surfaces (`_dir_lock_for` callers at `:213` scope / `:365` write / `:484` replace / `:572` read); `cross_process_write_lock` `:406` (`LOCK_EX`); `cross_process_replace_lock` `:507` (`LOCK_EX`); `cross_process_read_lock` `:605` (**`LOCK_SH`** — so "they are all `LOCK_EX`" is false); `_acquire_legacy_sidecar_for_writer` `:274` (`LOCK_EX`); `_acquire_legacy_sidecar_if_present` `:330` (`LOCK_EX`/`LOCK_SH`). In `harness-runtime` (**two** sites): `reconciler_pause_resume_substrate._workflow_lock` `:294` (`LOCK_EX`) and `protected_result_store._cross_process_lock` `:347` (`LOCK_EX`). A deadline means `LOCK_NB` + retry + a new typed exception at each, so putting one on a single site would itself be the inconsistent surface.
- **Close-out steps.** **(1)** Pick the deadline shape — `LOCK_NB` + bounded retry with backoff (portable, and composes with B-45's Windows backend) or a watchdog around the blocking call. **(2)** Choose the timeout's semantics and its exception: a **new** typed error distinct from `MemoryStoreGuardedWriteConflictError`, because a refused commit is a *precondition* outcome and a timeout is a *liveness* one — collapsing them would let a caller retry-loop against a stuck host. **(3)** Apply it consistently across **all eight acquisition sites** above — the acquisition site, not the entry surface, is where a deadline goes. **Two traps** a naive impl falls into. *First*, one entry surface can block at up to **three** distinct sites in sequence (dir lock → legacy sidecar → file lock), so a deadline bolted onto `cross_process_write_lock`'s own `flock` leaves the other two unbounded and the entry surface still has no end-to-end bound. *Second*, the three `LOCK_NB` calls (`:402`, `:498`, `:599`) are contention **probes** that fall back to a blocking wait on the very next line — they are not deadlines and must not be counted as coverage. **(4)** Document the hold-time contract at `write_record_guarded` so a precondition author meets the constraint at the seam rather than in production.
- **Natural co-rider on B-45.** Both arcs traverse the same set of lock primitives — B-45 to give them a Windows backend, this row to give them a deadline — so doing them together costs one traversal instead of two.
- **Council: no.** An implementation-level liveness bound on an already-cleared lock mechanism (C-IS-09 §9.4 defers the mechanism to implementation discretion), with no cross-domain tension to name. Step (2) does carry a real design choice, so it is not mechanical — `advisor()` + out-of-family review at build time is the right weight. Council-eligible only if taken **with** B-45 and the Windows tradeoff makes the deadline semantics non-obvious.
- **Trigger for re-check.** The **second** precondition. The row should be re-read at every new `write_record_guarded` caller, since the first unbounded one converts this from latent to live.
- **Cross-ref.** Surfaced by merge-gate lens 1 on PR #1159 (U-MEM-27 impl leg). Sibling lock-substrate row: **B-45** (Windows backend for the same primitives, whose enumeration this arc's `cross_process_scope_lock` extends to four).

### B-94 · Derived-index auto-refresh rebuild overlap race — two concurrent rebuilds can leave the OLDER snapshot last and reading fresh *(surfaced by out-of-family Codex R5 on the B-93 gate-log arc, 2026-07-30; classified PRE-EXISTING by git-show verification against fc40fb2b)*

- **What it is.** `DerivedRetrievalIndexStore.rebuild` computes its entry set from the filesystem and *then* appends it, holding **no lock across the two** (`_append_jsonl` is a bare `open("ab")` + write). Two overlapping rebuilds interleave as: X reads the record set → a commit lands → Y reads the **newer** set and appends it → X appends its **older** set **last**. `read_current` resolves an untokenized ledger by **position** (last rebuild wins), so it returns X's stale snapshot with `stale=False`: a committed record missing from an index reported as current.
- **Pre-existing, and NOT widened by the B-93 arc — verified by `git show fc40fb2b`.** Pre-branch `rebuild` (`memory_retrieval_index.py:177-200`) computed entries at `:180` and appended at `:199` with no lock; `_append_jsonl` (`:571-574`) took none either; `read_current` (`:210-227`) was purely positional last-wins; and `AutoRefreshingDerivedRetrievalIndexStore.read_current` (`automatic_memory.py:110-114`) rebuilt on missing/stale with no coordination. The same interleaving produces the same wrong outcome on the pre-branch code. An all-untokenized ledger takes the identical positional path before and after the arc.
- **What the B-93 arc covered, and why this sits outside it.** That arc added a commit-ordering protocol — `commit_seq` stamped inside the memory store's write hold, plus a `rebuilt_coverage` event binding a rebuild to the commit it answers — which orders **tokenized** rebuilds: the hook path, where the caller has an invalidation event to quote. The **production auto-refresh path is untokenized by design**: it rebuilds because a read found the index missing or stale, not in response to a specific write, so it has no token to pass and correctly falls to the legacy positional rule. The protocol does not reach this race.
- **Candidate fix shape** *(Codex R5's direction, recorded not built)*. Derive a coverage token on the auto-refresh path from **the ledger length captured at the read that decided to rebuild**. That read already walks the whole ledger, so its byte length at that moment is free — and it is exactly the monotonic quantity `_next_commit_seq` uses. It names *"the state I found stale"*, which is the honest under-claiming attribution the rebuild contract already requires. The subclass would capture it in its `except` branch and pass it to `rebuild(commit_seq=...)`; the existing ordering rules then reject the older overlapping snapshot with no change to `read_current`.
- **Open questions for build time.** **(1)** The capture must happen *inside* the failed read or it re-races — `read_current` returns nothing on the raise, so the token needs a carrier on the exception or a private read variant. **(2)** Whether the fix belongs at the `harness-runtime` subclass (where auto-refresh policy lives) or the IS-axis base (where token semantics live) — the IS axis has **zero outbound cross-axis edges**, so a base-class change must not import runtime concerns. **(3)** Whether a losing rebuild should be **pruned** rather than merely ignored, since the ledger still grows by a full snapshot per overlap.
- **Not urgent.** The outcome is a **stale-but-valid** index — a retrieval base that under-serves, never a wrong record — the path self-corrects at the next canonical write's stale marker, and single-process local development (the default posture) does not overlap rebuilds at all.
- **Council: no.** A bounded concurrency fix inside an already-cleared mechanism, with the ordering protocol it would extend already built and witnessed. No cross-domain tension: the question is *where the token is captured*, not whether the index should be ordered. Open question (2) is a real placement decision, so `advisor()` + out-of-family review at build time is the right weight.
- **Cross-ref.** Surfaced by Codex R5 on the B-93 gate-log arc. Sibling: **B-93** (no deadline on the same substrate's blocking locks) — both re-check on the same trigger, a second concurrent memory consumer becoming routine.

### B-95 · Mixed-version ordering bound — a live PRE-B-93 **writer**'s untokenized stale markers cannot be ordered against a covered rebuild *(surfaced by out-of-family Codex R6 on the B-93 gate-log arc, 2026-07-30; REGISTER-AND-HOLD under the arc's own stop rule)*

- **What it is.** During a mixed-version window a **new** hook captures snapshot A; a **pre-B-93 writer** commits B and appends an **untokenized** stale marker; the hook then appends A's rebuild pair last. In `read_current` the untokenized marker has no `commit_seq`, so it is excluded from `max_stale_seq`, and accepting the covered rebuild resets the positional `stale_after_current` flag — the A-only index reads **fresh** with B missing. Codex reproduced it deterministically.
- **Precondition triple — each verified by direct read at HEAD, each false today.** **(a)** A live pre-B-93 **writer**, not merely an old *reader* (whose parse compatibility the R3 fix already covers): at HEAD `commit_seq` is a **required** field on `DerivedIndexInvalidation`, so every B-93+ writer stamps every marker, and an untokenized marker can only originate from an older writer process. **(b)** A **wired** `derived_index_hook` — re-confirmed **zero production consumers** at HEAD; the only wirings are `harness-is/tests/test_memory_store.py` and `harness-is/tests/test_memory_redaction.py`. **(c)** An overlapping write + hook-rebuild interleaving.
- **Sharpening found while verifying.** Historical untokenized lines written *before* an upgrade are **benign** — a later rebuild reads the whole filesystem and genuinely covers them. Only a **concurrent** untokenized write, interleaved with an in-flight rebuild, is harmful. The bound is about the live window, not about legacy ledger content.
- **The information-theoretic limit, precisely.** A legacy stale marker carries **no commit token by definition**, so no read-side rule can retroactively order it against a covered rebuild — the information the comparison needs was never written. This is not a missing check; it is **absent data**. Hence only three choices exist:
  - **(1) Serialize notifications during a detected mixed window.** Reintroduces exactly the callback-deadlock surface the arc's first commit removed (a hook holding the notify turn while its inner thread's write waits behind it) — trading a latent ordering gap for a latent hang. Rejected unless the window can be *detected* and the serialization *scoped* to it.
  - **(2) Version-fence the ledger.** Have B-93+ writers refuse to share a ledger they can detect an older writer is also appending to (a writer-version marker line, or a fence file beside the ledger), converting a silent misorder into a **loud refusal** during the upgrade window. Costs a new failure mode operators must understand, plus a decision about what a fenced writer should *do*.
  - **(3) Accept the bound and document it.** **Taken here** — all three preconditions are counterfactual at HEAD.
- **HOLD rationale.** This is the **third consecutive self-inflicted `read_current` finding** (R4 caused by R3's carrier; R5's [P2] by R3/R4's two-pass read; this by R2's ordering protocol). That is the arms-race stop rule the arc pre-declared at round 4, firing exactly as written. **No further carrier change is made.**
- **Documented in code, not witnessed.** The bound is stated in the `memory_retrieval_index` module docstring and on `DerivedIndexInvalidation.commit_seq`, both citing this row. The R3 mixed-version witness was **re-scoped** to say it proves reader *parse* compatibility only and explicitly disclaims any ordering guarantee — deliberately no witness for the gap itself, since it is registered rather than fixed.
- **Re-open when** a genuine mixed-version deployment is planned, **or** the first production `derived_index_hook` consumer is wired — whichever comes first, since either alone makes a precondition true.
- **Council: not for the bound itself** — accepting a documented limit needs no deliberation. **If** direction (2) is ever taken it becomes council-eligible: refusing to write during an upgrade window is an operator-visible availability tradeoff (C11 operator-loop / local-deployment against C3 state-and-persistence integrity), which *is* a nameable cross-domain tension.
- **Cross-ref.** Sibling ordering-scope row **B-94** (the untokenized auto-refresh path, pre-existing); substrate sibling **B-93**. All three re-check on overlapping triggers around concurrent memory consumers.

### B-96 · The B-77 first-observation grace is bounded by SWEEP COUNT, not elapsed time -- a short or failing run's own shutdown sweep can reclaim a crash-recovered entry milliseconds after its bootstrap sweep first observed it *(surfaced at the B-77 impl arc, out-of-family Codex round 2 [P2], 2026-07-30)*

- **What it is.** `gc_sweep`'s B-77 first-observation grace refuses to reclaim an entry the store has not already seen past TTL at a PRIOR sweep of the same root. That closes B-77's named defect — a crash-recovered entry carrying only the pre-commit mtime stamp is never reclaimed by the sweep that FIRST sees it, i.e. never by the recovering process's bootstrap sweep. The residue is the grace's **practical duration**: every `run()`/`resume()` sweeps twice by construction — once at `bootstrap/stage_4_od.py:123` and once, unconditionally, at `shutdown.py` step 5b — so the gap between first and second observation is the run's own duration, milliseconds for a short or immediately-failing run. A genuinely-live, crash-recovered entry therefore survives only to that same run's shutdown, not for any wall-clock interval a repair flow can rely on.
- **Current state.** Registered, not built. The sweep-COUNT reading is exactly what `B-77`'s ratified close_out specifies (verbatim: "should not reclaim an entry it has not observed in a PRIOR sweep (a grace period) — bounded to one sweep interval"), and it landed as ratified. It was deliberately NOT redesigned mid-arc: `B-77`'s own row records three prior wrong mechanism claims on this same file, and a fourth unratified attempt is precisely the non-convergent-adversarial-hardening pattern this workspace stops on. The practical bound is stated honestly in `gc_sweep`'s docstring rather than overclaimed. Reachability is the same narrow floor `B-74`/`B-77` share: a deployment `ttl_seconds` short enough that the crash window alone tips a live entry past it (the factory default is 86400s, ~7 orders of magnitude above), AND a crash landing strictly between `os.link` and the post-commit refresh, AND a recovering run short enough that its shutdown sweep fires before any repair read.
- **Close-out steps.** Replace the sweep-COUNT grace with an elapsed-TIME one, using the first observation's wall-clock as the age authority `B-77`'s close_out actually names ("an age authority that cannot predate publication by construction") — record `first_observed_at` alongside the filename in `_root_observed_expired` and reclaim only when BOTH the mtime-derived age AND `now - first_observed_at` exceed `ttl_seconds`. The tradeoff that makes this ratification-owned rather than mechanical: the conservative rule lengthens worst-case retention of a genuine crash orphan by up to one full TTL (a day at the factory default), against the spec's own bounded-retention term (Runtime v1.106 §14.8.11 — "a signing outage must not grow an unbounded store of sensitive payloads"). Do not narrow `ttl_seconds` itself (`B-74`'s declined option). Witness by driving a full bootstrap-then-shutdown pair against a crash-window entry.
- **Council: ⚖️ conditional** — the mechanism is mechanical, but the retention bound it picks is a nameable cross-domain tension (C3 state-and-persistence integrity wanting the conservative age authority ⊥ C7/C8 wanting the bounded-retention floor on sensitive payloads); convene if the elapsed-time variant is taken.
- **Cross-ref.** `B-77` (closed — the grace itself); `B-74` (open — the filesystem-granularity dimension of the same mtime-as-age-authority question). All three re-check together.

### B-97 · The durable pause journal carries no tenant binding *(surfaced at the B-69 council convening as residual R-3, routed OUT of that arc, 2026-07-30; half (b), cross-process append serialization, CLOSED 2026-07-31; half (a), the tenant binding, RATIFIED + SPEC-APPLIED 2026-07-31 as Reading A — per-tenant path segregation — with the impl leg owed)*

- **Half (b) — CLOSED 2026-07-31 (the `B-97`(b) arc).** `JournalWorkflowPauseStore._append` now runs its **entire** body — the `is_new_file` / `needs_leading_newline` / append TOCTOU triple, the write, the `fsync`, and the torn-append self-heal — under `_cross_process_append_lock`: a hand-rolled `fcntl.flock` on a dedicated **per-workflow** `<journal>.lock` file, mirroring the primitive already shipped at `protected_result_store._cross_process_lock`. The **read path deliberately takes no lock**, which is what keeps Runtime spec v1.107 §30's `empty-journal` INDETERMINATE clause exact **as written** — and is why the arc owed **zero spec text**.
- **Granularity is the grounded part.** The house `harness_is.cross_process_ledger_lock.cross_process_scope_lock` (one lock per directory TREE) was implemented **first** and **rejected at out-of-family review round 2 [P2]**: a directory-wide lock makes one workflow's append wait behind **another** workflow's, which contradicts this store's own isolation property (per-workflow files exist so one workflow cannot block another) and — because reads are unlocked — widens the window in which a read of the *queued* workflow reports the `absent` cause that §30 attributes as **PERMANENT**. The per-workflow lock leaves that pre-existing capture-not-yet-landed race exactly the size it already was.
- **Two pre-existing crash windows closed on the way past.** Out-of-family review round 1 **[P1]**: a process that creates the journal directory and dies before writing leaves the dirent **present but not durable**, and a parent-fsync keyed on `dir_is_new` alone then skips it **forever** — a capture returns successfully while the whole `pause-journal` directory stays losable. Round 1 re-keyed it on `is_new_file or dir_is_new`; the **pre-merge 3-lens gate found that form narrows but does not close** the window — a writer whose `path.open("a")` creates the journal file and then dies before the fsync leaves **both** flags False for every successor, so *"a journal file exists ⇒ some earlier writer already fsynced"* is false for that interleaving.
- **Shipped form: both directory fsyncs are UNCONDITIONAL.** The parent (`STATE_LEDGER`) fsync runs after `mkdir` and **before the lock**, so it precedes any statement that could create a journal file; the journal-dir fsync runs after **every** append. True by construction, no interleaving exception — and it **deletes both flags** rather than adding a third. The journal-dir level carried the identical defect one level down (create file, write, die before fsync, every successor reads `is_new_file=False`); that one is **purely pre-existing**, byte-identical to the pre-`B-97` code, and is closed here rather than left as a known twin of the defect being fixed beside it. Cost: two best-effort directory fsyncs on a path that already `fsync`s the record, for a human-latency event.
- **Spec refresh owed at the half-(a) leg.** Two Runtime spec v1.107 §30 sentences go **stale-as-described** once half (b) lands: `:3313` term 5 (*"makes no claim about cross-process write-write serialization of the journal, which remains unguarded"*) and `:3319` (*"cross-process append serialization is explicitly unresolved (registered at `B-97`)"*). **Neither is a Class-1 halt** — both sentences' asserted *properties* still hold (term 5's supersession-window claim is untouched; `:3319`'s `empty-journal` indeterminacy never depended on append serialization); only their parenthetical descriptions of the store are now wrong. The half-(a) leg touches §30 anyway for the sixth cause member, so it carries this refresh.
- **Forward note — blocking `flock` on the event loop** *(out-of-family [P2], pre-merge gate)*. `capture()` is called synchronously from the async `capture_pause_snapshot`, so the `flock` blocks the loop. Precedented — the sibling protected result store grew `resolve_result_ref_off_loop` for exactly this — **deliberately not built in this arc**. **SPLIT to its own row `B-103` at the half-(a) spec leg, 2026-07-31**, per this row's own leg-2 obligation: parking it here would have lost it from `--open` the moment this row closes.
- **Sharpening recorded honestly.** The original finding framed (b) as *interleaved / torn records*. An empirical probe at the arc found that half **not reachable at the shipped record shape**: `_append` emits each record in exactly **one** `write()` syscall (measured on the real `FileIO` → `BufferedWriter` → `TextIOWrapper` stack at 60 KB, at 4 MB, and on the leading-newline self-heal path, whose 1-byte newline is coalesced into the same flush), and `O_APPEND` makes a single write atomic on a local POSIX filesystem — a 2×12 concurrent-append run at 12 KB / 60 KB / 500 KB / 4 MB produced **zero** malformed records with the lock **removed**. What the lock genuinely buys: cross-process mutual exclusion **at all**; independence from an **unspecified** platform guarantee (`O_APPEND` atomicity does not hold on NFS/SMB, and the resolved `STATE_LEDGER` dir is operator-configured); and **soundness** for the self-heal's documented single-writer-only reasoning.
- **Half (a) — RATIFIED and SPEC-APPLIED 2026-07-31; IMPL LEG OWED.** *(The bullets below preserve the finding as filed; this bullet records how it was answered. The row's status moved `registered_finding` → `open`: its design question is settled and what remains is ordinary implementation work.)* **Gate 1 (operator, 2026-07-31): READING A — per-tenant PATH SEGREGATION**, with a contractually injective **CONSTANT** encoding, the **record shape UNCHANGED**, the five-member `PauseJournalReadCause` vocabulary **staying at five**, a **MANDATORY quiesced cutover**, and the `(workflow_id, run_id)` rider **NOT bundled**. **Gate 2 (operator, 2026-07-31), after the C3 ⊥ C11 dyadic:** **(3a) abandon-by-default CONFIRMED**, and **§5.3.1 = (i)+(iii) with (iii) a PRECONDITION of (3a)** — both as the council recommended. **The four decisions enumerated below are answered as:** (1) the **filename**, under a TOTAL injective encoding closing **all three** hazards (branch / delimiter / field-boundary), realized on the in-house length-prefixed canonical-segment shape as a module constant with **no configuration surface**, delegating to the **Runtime-local** `normalize_tenant_scope` and explicitly **not** to OD's; (2) **DISSOLVED, not discharged** — a wrong-tenant read resolves the caller's **own** path and returns the ordinary `absent`, which under segregation is the *truth*, so **no sixth cause member is minted** and decision (2) as posed below is superseded; (3) **(3a) abandon by default** with the failure-mode asymmetry stated as spec text, plus **(3b)** operator-declared relocation under four mechanical terms; (4) **answered NO explicitly.** **Applied at** `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` **v1.108** (§14.14.8 keying + encoding + migration contract + orphan statement + named silence; §14.14.9.1 parallel keying; §30's `absent` row tenant-scoped across three states; §13.4 rows; **NEW §13.7** enumeration surface) and `Implementation_Plan_Harness_Runtime_v2_56.md` **NEW U-RT-149**. **CP / OD / IS / AS and CXA UNCHANGED**, each determined at the leg rather than assumed — the arc is wholly intra-`harness-runtime`.
- **Half (a) — the finding as filed.** `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` §14.8.11 (`:4804` heading, `:4809` tenant-composite key, `:4812` tenant-bound lookup — **cites refreshed at this arc**; the prior `:4636`/`:4639` pair was stale by ~168 lines after v1.107) commits a **full-strength tenant-composite key + a typed cross-tenant refusal** for comparable durable state. `JournalWorkflowPauseStore` (§14.14.8) is **`workflow_id`-keyed, one file per workflow** — and now carries recovered branch outputs. **PRE-EXISTING and explicitly NOT `B-69`'s**: `api.resume()` reads the same key today, and the `B-69` accessor adds **zero** tenant exposure.
- **Why (a) is fork-shaped, not an impl task.** It changes the store's **keying** — capture-side and hash-adjacent (the `B-73`/`B-80` split-out discipline that kept it out of `B-69` in the first place). The chain mirrors `B-65`'s: **Class 2 fork doc → operator ratification → spec leg → impl leg.** Four decisions the fork must put to the operator, none of which an impl leg may settle silently:
  1. **Key composition** — does the tenant scope enter the sha256 **filename** (opaque, but makes an existing journal unaddressable) or ride **in-record** alongside `workflow_id` (readable, but leaves the path tenant-blind)?
  2. **Refusal semantics** — a cross-tenant read must be **REFUSED TYPED** per §14.8.11 `:4812`, which needs a **sixth** cause in the deliberately **closed** five-member `PauseJournalReadCause` vocabulary (Runtime spec §30). Widening a closed vocabulary *is* the spec question.
  3. **Migration** — an existing untenanted journal must be readable or deliberately abandoned, against §14.14.8's **append-only / never-truncated** substrate invariant, which forbids rewriting in place.
  4. **The `(workflow_id, run_id)` rider** — whether the same arc should carry run-instance distinctness, since both are keying changes and a second keying arc would pay the migration cost twice.
- **Discriminator worth preserving.** The two failure modes are **distinct**, and only (b) was a concurrency question. *Sequential interleaving* — one writer at a time, both records well-formed — is the **supersession** problem `B-69`'s staleness precondition already fences, and it needs no concurrency at all: *"it is not writing at the same time; it is writing in between."*
- **Council: ⚖️ conditional** — (a) is C3's surface (state/persistence keying) with a nameable C3 ⊥ C11 tension if a tenant key adds local-deployment config burden. (b) was mechanical and is closed, so the prior *"convene only if (a) is taken without (b)"* condition is now **unconditionally met**: opening (a) means convening.
- **Cross-ref.** `B-69` (closed — the arc that surfaced it and deliberately excluded it); Runtime spec §14.8.11 (the shape to mirror); §14.14.8 (the store); **`B-102`** — (b)'s lock **shrinks but does not close** the window that row names.

### B-98 · Gate description / question text is absent from every durable pause carrier *(surfaced at the B-69 council convening, SEAM 3, routed OUT of that arc, 2026-07-30)*

- **What it is.** An operator inspecting durable pause state can learn **where** a workflow is paused and **through which resolution channel**, but not **what was asked**. Verified across every `PauseSnapshot` field at the `B-69` convening — and **symmetrically** for the closed-vocabulary counter-proposal: `HITLPlacementKind` exists as a `StrEnum`, but **no durable pause carrier declares a field of that type**; every `placement` occurrence in `pause_resume_protocol_types.py` is docstring prose. The same empirical fact defeats both a free-text description and a closed-vocabulary placement field: **both are capture-side scope.**
- **`hitl_gate_config_hash` is not a substitute.** Tested and rejected on its surviving ground: it is a **material-diff identity, not a description** — an opaque digest whose only operator-meaningful semantic is *"the gate config changed since capture."* It discriminates a config **edit**, never a **re-pause**.
- **Current state.** Registered, not built. Explicitly **declined for the `B-69` arc by the ergonomics voice itself** — the projection ships without it, with position + `pause_reason` judged sufficient for the map-composition task.
- **Close-out.** Capture-side, **hash-affecting**, X-AL-3-relevant — materially bigger than a read-only projection. Requires a capture-side carrier decision (which field, on which resume-state carriers, with what hash disposition per the drop-when-empty precedent) **before** any read-side projection field is added.
- **Distinct from `B-71`.** `B-71` adds a run-instance correlation id to `HITLEscalationBrief` / the webhook payload — it is about **disambiguating** two identical-looking escalations. This row is about **describing** one. Adjacent, not the same.
- **Council: ✅ yes if opened** — a nameable **C10 ⊥ C11** tension (action-safety wanting a closed vocabulary and no unbounded free-text channel in durable state, against operator-loop wanting a description a human can act on). The `B-69` convening surfaced exactly that tension without resolving it, because the empirical absence closed the seam first.
- **Cross-ref.** `B-69` (open — the arc that surfaced it); `B-71` (registered — adjacent, distinct); `B-99` (the sibling payload-boundary reopening condition).

### B-99 · The B-69 projection's `summary_text` exclusion must be re-adjudicated when the `pause_context_reader` deferral is discharged *(named reopening condition registered at the B-69 spec leg, 2026-07-30)*

- **What it is.** A **named reopening condition** — not a defect, not a gate — filed so the `B-69` projection's own justification cannot go stale silently. `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` v1.107 §14.14.9.2 excludes `StateSummary.summary_text` from the caller-facing projection. That exclusion originally rested on **two** legs, and the convening's own adversarial pass **falsified one**.
- **The falsified leg.** *"The exclusion is free, since `summary_text` is empty everywhere."* True today — but only because the MVP binding is the injected **placeholder** `pause_context_reader` that §14.14.7 marks explicitly deferred and whose own docstring says its successor *"reads the current ledger head."* The emptiness is a property of a placeholder, not a structural property.
- **The surviving leg, now carrying the argument alone.** The field is an **unbounded forward channel** — a placeholder *for real ledger content*, with no redaction contract anywhere. An exclusion decided while the field is empty is free; the same exclusion after it carries ledger content is a **removal from a shipped caller-facing contract**, which nobody makes. Decided now, and stated as forward-load-bearing.
- **Honest counter, left unsmoothed.** When a real reader is composed, the **ergonomics case for inclusion genuinely reopens.**
- **Trigger.** The discharge of §14.14.7's `pause_context_reader` deferral — i.e. when a real reader is composed and `summary_text` begins carrying ledger content.
- **Obligation at that point.** Re-adjudicate the projection's field set **with a redaction contract as a PRECONDITION of any inclusion**, and re-adjudicate the paired `Spec_Operational_Discipline_v1_36.md` §30.5.4 redaction posture (currently **NOT ENGAGED** precisely because nothing redactable is emitted) **in the same arc**. Also re-examine `Implementation_Plan_Harness_Runtime_v2_55.md` U-RT-148 **AC #5** (the W4 characterization witness), which asserts a **placeholder property** and will fail *for a good reason* once a real reader lands — its correct disposition is then **DELETION or INVERSION, never repair**.
- **No gate is imposed now, deliberately.** The field is excluded, so there is nothing to redact, and gating an absent channel is textbook over-gating — the available over-gate was explicitly declined on the record.
- **Council: ❌ no** — the tension (C10 ⊥ C11 on the payload boundary) was already convened and adjudicated at the `B-69` record; this row records the condition under which that adjudication must be re-run, and the re-run inherits the same voice pair.
- **Cross-ref.** `B-69` (open — the arc whose exclusion this guards); Runtime spec §14.14.7 (the deferral that is the trigger) + v1.107 §14.14.9.2 (the exclusion); OD spec v1.36 §30.5.4 (the paired posture).

### B-100 · The empty-`idempotency_key` key-ABSENT carve-out does not reach the LINEAR and orchestrator fence carriers *(surfaced at the `B-69` impl leg, reported not absorbed, 2026-07-31)*

- **What it is.** `Spec_Control_Plane_v1_112.md` §2.1 and `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` v1.107 §14.14.9.2 both scope the empty-key / key-field-**ABSENT** source shape to `EffectFencePausedBranchResumeState` — the fan-out crash-reconstruction carrier, which the spec leg verified constructs `idempotency_key=""` deliberately. The **LINEAR** and **orchestrator** capture sites admit an empty key too: the LINEAR site constructs `EffectFenceResumeState(idempotency_key=_fence_key)` whenever `isinstance(_fence_key, str)`, which admits `""`.
- **Why it matters.** Both real resume sites consult a fence resolution only when the key is **truthy**, and `compute_effect_fence_tree_wide_abort_present` already filters *all* empty keys regardless of carrier. So an empty key surfaced on either of those two shapes is the **same drop-not-refuse livelock** — the operator's response is silently **DROPPED, not refused** — that the branch-carrier carve-out exists to foreclose, arriving one door over.
- **Why it was filed rather than fixed in place.** The impl leg conforms to the cleared enumeration **exactly**. Adding keyless sibling source shapes for the LINEAR and orchestrator carriers would extend the cleared eight-shape enumeration — an **X-AL-3 design extension an impl leg must not make silently**, and the spec leg reversed a *fifth variant* on exactly this kind of reasoning at its round 11.
- **Severity, stated honestly.** The exposure is **defensive-only** at both sites today (the key is read by name off a runtime error; no production path is known to yield `""`), so this is a **soundness gap, not a live defect** — which is why it is registered rather than treated as a halt.
- **Close-out.** Two separable dispositions: **(a) spec-side** — widen the key-ABSENT treatment to *any* effect-fence source shape whose captured key is empty (the variant set stays at FOUR; only the SOURCE column widens, exactly as the spec leg's own rounds 1 and 2 widenings did); **(b) capture-side** — refuse an empty key at the LINEAR/orchestrator capture sites so the read side never has to represent it (cheaper in type surface, but capture-side and therefore hash-adjacent, which is why it is not the default recommendation).
- **Council.** No — the tension was already convened and adjudicated at the `B-69` record; this row only asks whether the *same* adjudicated rule reaches two more source carriers.

### B-101 · Accessor-derived provenance is lost through base-typed Pydantic serialization *(surfaced at the `B-69` impl leg, out-of-family review round 3, reported not absorbed, 2026-07-31)*

- **What it is.** `AccessorDerivedResumeContext` is a **subclass** of the CP-owned `ResumeContext`, and Pydantic v2 serializes a subclass through a base-typed field or `TypeAdapter` using the **base's** schema. A round-trip through any `ResumeContext`-typed boundary therefore emits only the four legacy fields and silently **drops `pause_state`** — after which `api.resume()`'s variant check sees a legacy context and the §30 staleness fence does not fire.
- **Severity, stated honestly.** **Not reachable on any shipped path today**: `api.resume` takes the object directly, and the in-process MCP handoff stores it without serializing. A latent hazard, not a live defect.
- **Why it is filed rather than fixed.** It **cannot be closed from the subclass** — the behavior is controlled by the base type's schema, and CP spec v1.112 requires `ResumeContext`'s field set be **PRESERVED VERBATIM**, so adding a variant discriminator to it is a **spec change an impl leg must not make**.
- **Relation to the cleared residual.** CP spec v1.112 §1.2 already states *"a caller who takes an accessor read and then, independently, constructs a legacy `ResumeContext` is unfenced … the type system cannot track a side effect that happened before construction."* This is an instance of that — but it arrives through **ordinary serialization** rather than a deliberate act, which is the sharper form and the reason it is registered.
- **Pinned, not just described.** `test_provenance_is_lost_through_base_typed_serialization_registered_b101` asserts the **current** behavior, so the boundary is visible and any future change to it is deliberate and reviewed.
- **Nearest-approach vector** *(scoped re-gate at the `B-97`(b) arc, 2026-07-31)*. `ResumeContext` is **already** a base-typed field of `StepExecutionContext` (`workflow_driver_types.py:460`). It is **not dumped today** — only `HandoffContext` is `model_dump_json`'d, `model_copy` preserves object references rather than revalidating, and `revalidate_instances` defaults to `never` — so the not-reachable-today claim **stands**. But the hazard is **one `model_dump` away on an existing carrier**, not a hypothetical future boundary, and disposition **(b)** (documenting the boundary as out of scope) would **not** close that vector.
- **Close-out.** Both dispositions are spec-side: **(a)** amend CP spec §1 so `ResumeContext` carries a closed variant discriminator and the two variants become an explicit discriminated union — strictly stronger, but it breaks the field-set-preserved-verbatim guarantee §0.4 makes; **(b)** declare the serialization boundary formally out of the fence's scope, extending §1.2's stated residual to name it. **(b)** is the cheaper and more honest default; **(a)** is warranted only if a serialization boundary ever appears on a real resume path.
- **Council.** No — a scoping question about an already-adjudicated residual.

### B-102 · `corrupt-latest` is presented as decisive, but a torn in-flight append tail is cross-process reachable exactly as `empty-journal` is *(surfaced at the `B-97`(b) arc, lens-1 F1, 2026-07-31; spec half DISCHARGED at Runtime spec v1.108, 2026-07-31 — the read-side attribution flip rides the `B-97`(a) impl leg)*

- **SPEC HALF DISCHARGED at the `B-97`(a) spec leg, 2026-07-31.** Runtime spec **v1.108** amends §30's cause table so **`absent` (`:3318`) and `corrupt-latest` (`:3321`) each carry the SAME reachability qualification `empty-journal` (`:3319`) already carried** — permanent for the single-process deployment where it is decisive, **INDETERMINATE across processes**, and *an implementation MUST NOT present either as decisive loss where a second writer is reachable*. **All three permanent-absence causes are now qualified rather than one of three**, and the `RT-FAIL-RESUME-HANDLE-UNKNOWN` taxonomy row plus §14.14.9.4's routing clause were refreshed in the same edit so **no surface flattens the table**. This is the row's own disposition **(a)**, and it needed **no new cause member** — which is why it composed cleanly with `B-97` half (a) rather than competing with it. *(The bullets below preserve the finding as filed.)*
- **IMPL HALF OUTSTANDING — the whole remainder, and it is NOT independently scheduled.** Flip the **shipped** read-side attribution to the indeterminate disposition for `absent` and `corrupt-latest` where a second writer is reachable, matching what `empty-journal` already reports (`journal_workflow_pause_store.py` hard-codes `retryable=False, indeterminate=False` for both today). That flip **rides `Implementation_Plan_Harness_Runtime_v2_56.md` U-RT-149 AC #4(c)** at the `B-97`(a) impl leg — both touch the same attribution site, and two arcs editing one function is the duplication this register exists to prevent.
- **Why NARROWED rather than CLOSED.** Closing while half the row's own close-out is unshipped would present unshipped work as done, and would leave the contract and the code disagreeing with nothing tracking the gap. The stale cross-reference in `B-97`'s own text — that half (a) *"needs a sixth cause member"* — is corrected at that row: **Reading A mints none.**

- **What it is.** `Spec_Harness_Runtime_v1.md` v1.107 §30's cause table scopes its MUST-NOT-present-as-decisive clause to **one row of three**. `empty-journal` (`:3319`) carries *"INDETERMINATE across processes — a concurrent `capture()` may complete immediately after this read"* plus *"an implementation MUST NOT present it as decisive loss where a second writer is reachable."* The `corrupt-latest` row (`:3321`) says only **"permanent"**, and the shipped read hard-codes `retryable=False, indeterminate=False` for it (`journal_workflow_pause_store.py`, the non-empty branch of `read_latest_attributed`).
- **Why the same argument applies.** A torn trailing line is **exactly what an in-flight append looks like from a reader**. A read that lands mid-append attributes `corrupt-latest` to a record that is about to become well-formed — and where a second writer is reachable, that outcome is **indeterminate, not decisive loss**. The two rows differ only in *where* the concurrent writer is caught (before its first byte vs. between its bytes), which is not a difference the operator-repair column can act on.
- **The same omission reaches `absent`** *(found independently by out-of-family review round 2 at this arc)*. `absent` (`:3318`) is likewise flat **"permanent"**, yet a read of a workflow whose capture has been dispatched but has not yet created its journal file reports it with `indeterminate=False` — and that capture may complete moments later. So §30 qualifies **one** of the three permanent-absence causes and leaves the other two unqualified on the same reachability.
- **`B-97`(b) shrinks the window; it does not close it.** Concurrent **appends** are now serialized, so a torn tail can no longer be produced by two live writers racing. But a writer **killed mid-append** still leaves one, and a reader racing a **single** live writer is unaffected by a lock the reader deliberately does not take.
- **Severity, stated honestly.** Not a live defect on the shipped single-process deployment. Filed as a **soundness gap in the attribution contract**, exactly as `B-100` was.
- **Close-out — spec-touch owed.** An impl leg must not widen a cleared cause table on its own authority (X-AL-3). **(a)** Amend §30's cause table so `corrupt-latest` **and** `absent` carry the same reachability qualification `empty-journal` already carries, then flip the read-side attribution to `indeterminate=True` for those causes where a second writer is reachable. **(b)** The narrower alternative: leave the table alone and document why they *are* decisive despite the symmetry — viable only if someone can produce the asymmetry, which this row could not. **Prefer (a).** The fix is **attribution-only**: the fail-closed disposition is unchanged either way, and **no new cause member is needed** — which keeps it independent of `B-97` half (a), whose refusal semantics *do* need a sixth member.
- **Council.** No — this asks whether an already-adjudicated qualification reaches one more row of the same table; a scoping question for a spec-writer apply pass, not a fresh cross-domain tension.
- **Cross-ref.** `B-97` (half (a) touches the same closed vocabulary; half (b) is the reachability amplifier that motivated this row); `B-69` (closed — the arc that shipped the five-cause attribution).

### B-103 · The `B-97`(b) cross-process append `flock` is a BLOCKING syscall taken on the event loop *(surfaced at the `B-97`(b) pre-merge gate as an out-of-family [P2], 2026-07-31; split to its own row at the `B-97`(a) spec leg, 2026-07-31)*

- **What it is.** `JournalWorkflowPauseStore.capture()` is called **synchronously** from the async `capture_pause_snapshot`, so the per-workflow `fcntl.flock` `B-97`(b) introduced is a **blocking syscall taken on the event loop**. A contended acquisition stalls the whole loop — every other workflow, every pending timer — for the duration of another process's append.
- **Precedented, which is why it is registered rather than argued.** The sibling protected result store grew an off-loop resolution path (`resolve_result_ref_off_loop`) for exactly this shape.
- **Why it was split rather than folded into the keying arc.** It is a **mechanism / latency** question with **no keying content and no spec text**; folding it into `B-97` half (a) would have mixed two unrelated review surfaces. Parking it in `B-97`'s prose would have lost it from `--open` the moment that row closes — or, if the row were held open for it, would have falsely implied half (a) was unfinished. `[[spine-ledger-forward-arc-registration]]`.
- **Severity, stated honestly.** A durable pause is a **human-latency** event and the lock is held for one append, so the contention window is small and **no live stall has been observed**. This is a soundness / latency gap, **not a live defect**.
- **Close-out.** Move the blocking acquisition off the event loop on the sibling's own precedent (an `asyncio.to_thread`-shaped offload of the locked append, or an equivalent off-loop resolution path), and **witness it by execution**: an unrelated concurrent workflow **and** a pending loop timer demonstrably advance while a second process holds the journal lock — with the **mutation probe** being the direct synchronous call, under which that witness must fail.
- **Two things this row MUST NOT do.** It must **not** change the lock's **granularity** — per-workflow was grounded and defended at the `B-97`(b) arc against a directory-tree alternative, and reopening it here relitigates a settled decision. It must **not** change the **read** path, which deliberately takes no lock — that no-lock property is what keeps Runtime spec §30's `empty-journal` **INDETERMINATE** clause exact as written.
- **Spec expectation.** **No spec text is expected to be owed** — the offload is an execution-venue mechanism and §14.8.1's wrap-asymmetry lineage already covers the class. **Confirm that before building, and file a fork if it turns out otherwise rather than absorbing it.**
- **Council: ❌ no** — a single-axis execution-venue question with no nameable cross-domain tension. The granularity decision that *would* have carried one is settled and explicitly out of scope; route to **C9** (reliability / recovery) directly, plus `advisor()` if the offload shape interacts with drain or shutdown ordering.
- **Cross-ref.** `B-97` (half (b) introduced the lock; half (a)'s spec leg split this row out); `B-102` (the adjacent attribution row from the same arc).

### B-104 · The pause journal writes no pause-RESOLVED marker, so the §13.7 enumeration can only bound the at-risk set from ABOVE *(surfaced at the `B-97`(a) spec leg, out-of-family review round 1 [P1], reported not absorbed, 2026-07-31)*

- **What it is.** The pause journal is **append-only and never truncated** (Runtime spec §14.14.8's v1.107 substrate invariant), and **nothing is written when a resume succeeds** — no resolved-marker, no status field, no removal path anywhere in the store. A journal whose pause was successfully resumed is therefore **byte-indistinguishable** from one whose pause is still outstanding.
- **Why it surfaced now.** Runtime spec **v1.108 §13.7** declares the operator enumeration surface that makes **(3a) abandon-by-default** ratifiable, and council condition `K-10(c)` asks that surface to report *"how much would be LOST"*. **It cannot.** It can only bound the at-risk set **from above**.
- **How the spec leg handled it, and why that is the right disposition.** `K-10`'s **mechanism** was applied unchanged; only its **claim** was scoped, at §13.7 term **3-bis** — the enumeration is an **upper bound**, it cannot discriminate outstanding from resolved, and §13.7 **MUST NOT be cited as proof a cutover is safe**. That is the same discipline `K-1` established for quiescence (state the negative fact rather than a precondition you cannot discharge), applied one condition over.
- **Severity, stated honestly.** **Not a live defect** — nothing miscomputes, and nothing is lost that would otherwise survive. It is a **ceiling on what the operator recourse can tell a human**, and it makes *"drain pauses before upgrading"* a procedure the operator must verify by their own means rather than read off the harness.
- **Close-out — capture-side, therefore fork-shaped.** Any resolved-marker is a write the store does not make today, on a record shape whose hash disposition is load-bearing — the `B-73`/`B-80` split-out discipline that kept the tenant binding itself out of the read-only `B-69` arc. **Two candidate shapes, neither chosen:** **(a)** a **resolved sentinel record** appended at successful resume — cheapest against the append-only invariant, since an append rewrites and truncates nothing and the latest-record read would then report the journal as resolved; it needs a decision on whether that sentinel is a `PauseSnapshot` at all, and on what the **closed five-member cause vocabulary** reports for a resolved journal, which an impl leg must not settle. **(b)** an out-of-band per-journal **marker file** — avoids the record shape entirely but adds a **second authority** over one journal's state, the exact defect the no-fallback-read rule exists to prevent. **Prefer (a) if taken** — but ground it first: check whether an operator holding §13.7's upper bound **plus** their own drain procedure actually needs the discrimination, because a marker bought for a need nobody has is a capture-side change against a hash-adjacent record for nothing.
- **Do NOT bundle into the `B-97`(a) impl leg.** That leg is read-side and keying-side; this is capture-side.
- **Council: ⚖️ conditional** — if opened it carries a nameable **C3 ⊥ C11** tension (state/persistence integrity wanting the record shape and the append-only invariant left alone, against operator-loop wanting an answerable *"is anything actually outstanding"*), the same pair the `B-97`(a) dyadic convened. Convene **only if shape (a) is taken**, since that is where the closed-vocabulary and record-shape questions bite; a pure documentation close needs no convening.
- **Cross-ref.** `B-97` half (a) (the arc whose §13.7 surface this bounds); `B-98` (the sibling capture-side pause-carrier gap — gate description absence — same class, same reason it is not absorbed).
